# Qualified ACE behaviours

ACE behaviours qualified for the reference build under The Plan §8.1: Office Click-to-Run 16.0.20527.20196 x64,
the native raw IDispatch lane, unless a note says otherwise. A note is written as soon as the records support its
claim. Its open follow-up questions do not block it, and a later contradicting record reopens it. A difference found
on another ACE build is recorded beside the note, with both builds' records.

Each note has this form:

- **Claim**, in one or two sentences: what ACE does.
- **Conditions**: formats, routes and lanes it was asked through.
- **Records**: the supporting records, and the discriminating ones that ruled out competing explanations.
- **Open**: follow-up questions, each a candidate probe.
- **Other builds**: only when a difference has been found.

The notes below were drafted by Sonnet sub-agents from the retained records, completed with the follow-up probes of the
qualification rounds (`evidence/r-q-*`, `evidence/r-q2-*`, `evidence/r-ops`, `evidence/r-sample-rec`, 2026-11-17), and
spot-checked against the records. The ETW recorder (`evidence/r-etw-rec`) and the owned Access host
(`evidence/r-access-host`) added or extended the CRASH-ORDER, TXN-FLUSH, RO-NATIVE, EXPR-SANDBOX and R-UDF-1 notes. Each section's branch status
table (Q qualified, Q+T qualified with a troublesome remainder, F follow-up needed, T troublesome, N not an ACE question)
is summarised in [qualification-report.md](qualification-report.md).

## R-ORACLE-1, R-STRING-1 and R-ACT-1

### R-ORACLE-1 OR-IDENT: the loaded ACE modules are identified by path, version and hash, stably, on the DAO and ADO lanes
- **Claim.** In every capture the module snapshots identify the loaded ACE binaries through public OS enumeration: `ACEDAO.DLL` 16.0.20527.20196 (sha256 prefix 335ca6761d67f6e2), `ACECORE.DLL`,
  `ACEES.DLL`, `ACEWSTR.DLL` 16.0.20527.20196, `ACEOLEDB.DLL` 16.0.20527.20196 (71cc23e41a82141e), `ACEERR.DLL` 16.0.20527.20016 and `ACEINTL.DLL` 16.0.20326.20022. Nothing of ACE is loaded before
  activation (59 modules, 0 identified). DAO: creating DBEngine loads `ACEDAO.DLL` (82 modules, 14 identified). ADO: creating `ADODB.Connection` loads only `msado15.dll` (65, 1 identified), assigning the Provider loads
  nothing more, `Open` loads `ACEOLEDB.DLL`, `ACECORE.DLL`, `ACEWSTR.DLL` (150, 18), a first read loads `ACEES.DLL` (155, 22), `Close` unloads nothing, and creating DAO.DBEngine.120 afterwards in the same process loads
  `ACEDAO.DLL` (156, 23). A connectionless `ADODB.Recordset` loads no ACE module (70 loaded, 2 identified).
- **Conditions.** Native raw IDispatch lane, .NET 11 worker, locale 7177, ACCDB.
- **Records.** `r-oracle-1/create-fixture-t1`, `failed-call-t1`, `table-read-t1`; `r-q-wire/ident-ado-lazy-load/`, `r-q-wire/ado-connectionless-recordset/`; the `modules` events of all later records. Truncation control: the
  `deliberate-truncation` event, `complete=False`, "buffer smaller than module list", in every record.
- **Open.** Access lane (needs the Access host: T); the owner's 20016 DAO report against the 20196 module.

### R-ORACLE-1 OR-WIRE: a Decimal column made by DAO `CreateField(name, 20)` has Precision 38 Scale 0 and misreads; with explicit precision every route is exact
- **Claim.** Columns made by ADO DDL as `DECIMAL(28,0)` and `DECIMAL(18,4)` store and return every tested value exactly, with all three 32-bit words, through DAO `Field.Value`, SQL `CStr`/`CDbl`, filters and
  aggregates, ADO `Value` and `GetRows`; the 29-digit maximum is refused with 3761 (DAO `0x800A0EB1`, ADO `0x80040E21`). Columns made by DAO `TableDef.CreateField` without precision report Precision 38 and Scale 0: SQL literal
  inserts succeed, DAO `Field.Value` of a non-Null value fails 3000 "Reserved error (-1038)" (`0x800A0BB8`), DAO puts fail, every ADO put fails, and ADO reads return the stored integer with `Lo32` moved into `Mid32`
  (7 reads as 30064771072) and fractions truncated at scale 0.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB and MDB alike; writes by SQL literal, DAO put and ADO put; reads by five routes; values with distinct words.
- **Records.** `r-dec-read/{accdb,mdb}-{sql-literal,dao-put,ado-put}/` (exact); `r-dec-read/{accdb,mdb}-daofield-{sql-literal,dao-put,ado-put}/` (misread); `r-fix-wire-ado/create-fdec-dao-put`, `create-fdec-sql-literal`.
  Discriminator: precision is the only difference between the two groups, and the same ADO route decodes the explicit-precision columns exactly, which rules out a capture or ADO-decode artefact.
- **Open.** Which layer moves the words (not separable through the API); a DAO route to a Decimal column with a chosen precision.

### R-ORACLE-1 OR-WIRE: Decimal precision 1 to 28, scale at most the precision; out-of-range values are refused, words and scale are exact
- **Claim.** ADO DDL accepts `DECIMAL(28,0)`, `(1,1)`, `(18,18)`, and refuses `(29,0)`, `(38,0)` and `(0,0)` with "Invalid precision for decimal data type" (SQLState 3700) and `(28,29)` with "Invalid scale" (3701). On
  `DECIMAL(1,0)`, `(5,2)`, `(28,28)` and `(28,0)` columns the stored value keeps its column scale (-0.5 in `(28,28)` reads scale 28, sign 0x80, exact words) and DAO `Value`, SQL `CStr` and ADO `Value` agree. A SQL literal beyond
  the column precision is refused under dbFailOnError with 3761 (`0x800A0EB1`: 10 in `(1,0)`, 1000 in `(5,2)`, 1 in `(28,28)`); a 29-digit literal into `(28,0)` fails `0x800A0006` Overflow. 0.005 and 0.004 into `(5,2)` and a 29th
  decimal place into `(28,28)` are stored as zero without error.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB; ADO DDL; SQL literals under dbFailOnError; DAO and ADO reads.
- **Records.** `r-q-wire/dec-ddl-precision-limits/`, `r-q-wire/dec-scale-endpoints/`.
- **Open.** Truncation or banker's rounding at a scale tie (the only tie asked, 0.005 into `(5,2)`, gives 0.00 under both); MDB.
- **Built (`w15-ado-batch-err-schema`, `d2b21142`, 2026-11-27):** `AdoSqlErrors.TryMap` maps ad-hoc `Connection.Execute` `CREATE TABLE` ACE `3700`/`3701` to `0x80004005`, `helpContext` `5003700`/`5003701`, `SQLState` `"3700"`/`"3701"`, `NativeError = null`, `IID_ICommandText` (`r-q-wire/dec-ddl-precision-limits`).

### R-ORACLE-1 OR-WIRE / R-STRING-1: a leading U+FEFF in Text and Memo is lost or re-decoded by a rule that depends on column, length and content; U+FFFE, U+FFFF and U+FFFD are kept
- **Claim.** After the value is stored and the database reopened, a Text or Memo value whose first unit is U+FEFF reads back changed in these cases, and unchanged in the others (ACCDB and MDB alike, DAO and ADO reads alike):
  *TEXT(255), U+FEFF then n ASCII x*: lost for n = 0 (`""`), 1, 2, 3 (the x's remain, `Len` n); kept for every n from 4 to 100 (`Len` n + 1). *MEMO, same strings*: lost for n up to 4, kept from n = 5 (so the threshold
  differs by one unit between the two column types: row n = 4 reads `Len(T)` 5 and `Len(M)` 4). *Other content*: U+FEFF then three U+00E9 is lost (`Len` 3) but 20 and 100 U+00E9 are kept; U+FEFF then 3 euro is lost and reads as
  the pair U+00AC U+0020 repeated three times (the two bytes AC 20 of U+20AC read as Latin-1; `Len` 6), and so do 20 euro in TEXT and in MEMO and 100 euro in TEXT (`Len` 40, 40 and 200), while 100 euro in MEMO is kept (`Len` 101); `x` euro `x` is lost
  (`Len` 3); 99 x then euro and euro then 99 x are kept. A non-leading U+FEFF, two leading U+FEFF, and U+FEFF U+FFFE, each followed by 100 x, are kept. Every Memo of 255, 256, 500, 1000, 4000 and 8000 characters after the U+FEFF is
  kept, as are 500 euro and 100 x then euro. Short values (up to a few units) always show the decode: exactly U+FEFF gives `""`; U+FEFF `ab` gives `ab`; U+FEFF e-acute gives U+00E9; U+FEFF U+FEFF gives U+00FF U+00FE; U+FEFF U+FFFE gives
  U+00FE U+00FF. A U+FEFF in the middle or at the end is always kept. U+FFFE alone, leading, trailing, inside and doubled, U+FFFF and U+FFFD are kept in every route. The changed outcomes agree with decoding the bytes after a stored
  FF FE (the Unicode-compression marker) as a compressed stream (single bytes as Latin-1, a 00 byte toggling to UTF-16 units); the trigger (which values are decoded that way) is not a length rule alone and is not explained by the
  records. Inside the writing session, a re-fetch of the same open dynaset returned every U+FEFF value unchanged; the engine's own comparison and copy see only the decoded value: after reopening, `T = 'ab'` and `T = ChrW(65279) & 'ab'` both
  match the row written as U+FEFF `ab` (the U+FEFF is collation-ignorable), `StrComp` in binary mode reads it as `ab`, `INSERT ... SELECT` copies `ab`, and `UPDATE T = T & 'z'` gives `abz`.
- **Conditions.** Values written by six routes (DAO dynaset put, SQL literal, SQL `ChrW(65279)` expression, QueryDef parameter, ADO recordset put, ADO SQL insert) and read by a DAO snapshot (Value, `Len`, `AscW`) and an ADO recordset; lengths
  and contents by DAO put. ACCDB for all, MDB for the DAO put route and for the sixteen ASCII lengths (identical to ACCDB). A column made by ADO DDL `WITH COMPRESSION` (accepted) behaves like one without.
- **Records.** `r-text-fffe/{accdb,mdb}-{dao-put,sql-literal}/` (U+FFFE, U+FFFF, U+FFFD, doubled and lone rows, read directly); `r-q-wire/feff-accdb-dao-put/`, `feff-accdb-sql-literal/`, `feff-accdb-chrw-expr/`, `feff-accdb-param/`,
  `feff-accdb-ado-put/`, `feff-accdb-ado-sql/`, `feff-mdb-dao-put/`, `feff-accdb-dao-put-refetch/`, `feff-accdb-compression-and-properties/`; `r-q2-wire/feff-len-ascii-accdb/`, `feff-len-ascii-mdb/` (sixteen lengths),
  `feff-len-content/`, `feff-memo-long/`, `feff-compare-copy/`. Discriminators: every write route and both read routes agree, so the change is not in the DAO string path or one API layer; the euro and e-acute rows show that later
  stored bytes are re-decoded, which a writer that simply dropped a BOM would not do; the one-unit shift between TEXT and MEMO, and the euro against e-acute difference, show the trigger depends on column type and content; compressed
  against uncompressed column shows no difference.
- **Corrected.** The first draft said only a value that is exactly U+FEFF is lost; the second draft said the loss held "for short strings" and that 100 x survives. Now: a leading U+FEFF is affected whatever follows when the value is
  short (at most four units in TEXT, five in MEMO for ASCII), and for longer values depends on content (euro kept only in a long MEMO, e-acute kept from 20 units).
- **Open.** The rule behind the trigger and whether the loss is at write or read need the stored bytes (W-04, T); the `UnicodeCompression` flag is not among the 24 DAO field properties.

### R-ORACLE-1 OR-WIRE: Single and Double values are preserved bit for bit through DAO put and read; SQL literals are not bit-exact
- **Claim.** DAO `Field.Value` puts of Single and Double keep -0, subnormals, ±Inf, quiet and signalling NaN and NaN payloads exactly (for example R4 `4523c17f`, R8 `230100000000f87f`) through to the DAO read and `GetRows`; ADO returns the same
  cell values. A SQL literal `-0.0` stores +0 (SQL text cannot carry negative zero, NaN or Inf).
- **Conditions.** DAO dynaset put into SINGLE and DOUBLE fields; ACCDB.
- **Records.** `r-wire/put-tags-single/`, `put-tags-double/`, `read-dao/`, `ado-getrows-tags/`; the SQL contrast `r-wire/create-sql/`, `read-sql/`.
- **Open.** MDB repeat; whether the SQL parser folds `-0.0` as the negation of an integer zero is not separated from a storage effect.

### R-ORACLE-1 OR-WIRE / R-STRING-1: an odd-byte BSTR is refused for Text and Memo puts, truncated for parameters, and stored byte-exact in Binary and OLE
- **Claim.** A BSTR whose byte count is odd put into a Text field through DAO fails `0x80020009` with EXCEPINFO `0x800A0FCB` "Number of bytes of a UNICODE string is not even"; into a Memo, EXCEPINFO `0x800A0E76`; through ADO `0x80040E21`.
  A QueryDef Text or Memo parameter silently truncates the BSTR to an even length (1 byte gives `""`, 3 bytes give "A"). Into BINARY and LONGBINARY fields through `Field.Value` the BSTR payload is stored as its bytes with its exact length:
  1 to 5-byte BSTRs store 1 to 5 bytes (`41`, `4100`, `410042`, `41004200`, `4100420043`), BINARY(16) pads with zero bytes to 16, and the DAO and ADO reads agree; a typed by-reference BSTR of 3 bytes stores `410042` too (BINARY(8) padded).
  A BSTR text (`"AB"`) stores its UTF-16 bytes (`41004200`; U+0141 U+0242 stores `41014202`). NUL and lone surrogates are stored in Text. After a failed put, `Update` still succeeds and leaves a Null cell (a zero-length string for Memo).
- **Corrected (earlier round).** The 4-byte figure for an odd 3-byte BSTR belongs to the `AppendChunk` route, not the `Field.Value` put.
- **Conditions.** Raw BSTR with exact bytes; DAO put, ADO put, QueryDef parameter, AppendChunk; ACCDB.
- **Records.** `r-wire/put-tags-text/`, `put-tags-memo/` (read directly), `create-dao/`, `put-tags-binary/`, `put-tags-ole/`, `param-select-text/`, `chunk-memo-text-append/`; `r-fix-wire-ado/ado-write-values-text-binary`, `chunk-ole-append-fieldsize`,
  `chunk-memo-text-append-fieldsize`; `r-q-wire/str-odd-bstr-into-binary/`; `r-q2-wire/byref-dao-put-values/`.
- **Open.** Text coercion of an odd BSTR inside expressions; the Memo parameter order dependence recorded as a defect.

### R-ORACLE-1 OR-WIRE: integer admission through SQL literals: Byte wraps modulo 256, Integer and Long refuse out-of-range, fractions round half to even
- **Claim.** `INSERT` of a literal under dbFailOnError: into BYTE, 256, 257, 511, 512, -1 and -2 store 0, 1, 255, 0, 255 and 254 (wrapped modulo 256, no error); 255.4 stores 255 and 255.5 stores 0. Into SHORT, 32768, 32769, 65535, 65536,
  -32769 and -65536 fail `0x800A0006` Overflow and the row is not inserted; 32767 and -32768 store. Into LONG, 2147483648, 4294967295, 4294967296 and -2147483649 fail `0x800A0D88` "Data type mismatch"; 2147483647 and -2147483648 store.
  Fractions round half to even in all three: 0.5 stores 0, 1.5 stores 2, 2.5 stores 2. (Without dbFailOnError, `r-wire/create-sql` shows Null for out-of-range SHORT and LONG literals; not re-asked.)
- **Conditions.** ACCDB; DAO `Execute` with dbFailOnError; one literal per statement.
- **Records.** `r-q-wire/int-sql-literal-byte/`, `int-sql-literal-integer/`, `int-sql-literal-long/`; `r-wire/create-sql/`, `read-sql/`.
- **Open.** Single, Currency and Date literals; the non-FailOnError Null path per type; the ADO command-parameter route (`r-fix-wire-ado/ado-command-parameters-fresh` refuses range with `0x8002000A`).

### R-ORACLE-1 OR-WIRE: OpenDatabase optional arguments, with the name given
- **Claim.** With a real path, `OpenDatabase` opens with `Errors.Count` 0 for: name only; `Options` or `ReadOnly` Missing (also both, and with `Connect` Missing); `Connect` Missing, Empty, Null or `""`; `ReadOnly` Empty or Null with `Options` Missing;
  all three Empty. `Updatable` is -1 for all of them, so `ReadOnly` is ignored when `Options` is Missing. `(False, True)` and `(I2 0, I2 -1)` give `Updatable` 0. `Options` as Null, with or without `ReadOnly` True, or as BSTR "x" fails `0x80020009`,
  EXCEPINFO `0x800A0D5D` ("Data type conversion error", source `DAO.Workspace`), `DBEngine.Errors` 1 × 3421, with no IErrorInfo.
- **Conditions.** One-attempt variants; the name is never Empty, Null or Missing (those open a modal ODBC dialog).
- **Records.** `r-fix-wire-ado/opt-opendatabase-*` (15 records), `r-q-wire/opt-opendatabase-options-null-alone/`.
- **Open.** The Empty, Null and Missing name variants are interactive (ODBC dialog) and are not to be asked outside a dialog-handling route (T).

### R-ORACLE-1 OR-WIRE: Rnd is deterministic after a negative seed, reproducible across queries and processes, and evaluated once per query
- **Claim.** `Rnd(-1)` returns 0.2240070104598999 and the following `Rnd(1)` calls 0.035845816135406494, 0.08635234832763672, and `Rnd()` then 0.16426390409469604; `Rnd(0)` returns the last value; `Rnd(-5)` then `Rnd(1)` returns
  0.8383256793022156 then 0.2874332666397095. The same sequence comes back in a second query of the same process and equals the value seen in an earlier independent process (`r-wire/expr-clock-random`). With constant arguments every row of a
  query gets the same value (evaluated once per query).
- **Conditions.** Snapshots of a 3-row table; constant arguments; ACCDB.
- **Records.** `r-q-wire/rnd-seeded-repeat/`; `r-wire/expr-clock-random/`.
- **Open.** `Now()` and `Timer()` are observed once and not repeated; per-row Rnd with a column argument.

### R-ORACLE-1 OR-OBS: DAO `Errors` holds the latest failure and is never cleared by a success; ADO `Errors` is cleared by the next successful operation; enumerating `Errors` equals indexing it; the channels depend on the call
- **Claim.** DAO: after a failure `DBEngine.Errors` has Count 1 holding that failure (3376, then 3011, then 3078, then 3192, then 3376, each replacing the previous); a following successful `OpenRecordset`, `Execute` or `OpenDatabase`
  leaves the old entry in place. Enumerating the collection through `_NewEnum`/`IEnumVARIANT` (the `For Each` route) yields the same single Error as `Item(0)` (Number 3376, 3011), leaves Count at 1, does not clear it, and a later success leaves
  3011 in place; its enumerator answers `Next` past the end S_FALSE, `Reset` S_OK, `Skip(1)` E_FAIL (0x80004005) and `Clone` S_OK. ADO: `Connection.Errors` holds 1 after a failed `Open` (SQLState 3044) and after a failed `Execute`
  (SQLState 3000), and is 0 after a successful `Open` and after every successful `Execute`. Channels in the raw lane: DAO engine failures (missing table 3011, `DROP TABLE` of a missing table, bad SQL, a bad `Options` type, any member call on a
  retained object after Close except the `Field.Value` get) carry an EXCEPINFO with source `DAO.<class>` and no IErrorInfo (`GetErrorInfo` S_FALSE); failures of the `Field.Value` get carry EXCEPINFO and an IErrorInfo with a zero GUID, whether the
  error is an expression error (`AscW("")`, `0x800A0005`) or 3420 on a retained Field; the same code `0x800A0005` raised at `OpenRecordset` (`Sqr(-1)` in the projection) carries no IErrorInfo. ADO failures carry both, with the provider GUID `{0C733A..}`.
- **Conditions.** Raw IDispatch; the worker captures EXCEPINFO and `GetErrorInfo` straight after each failing call; a marker failure precedes each DAO step.
- **Records.** `r-q-wire/obs-errors-replace-persist/`, `obs-ado-errors-clear/`, `opt-opendatabase-options-null-alone/`, `obs-engine-versus-expression-errors/`; `r-q2-wire/obs-errors-foreach-enumeration/`; `r-ops/enum-dao-collections/`; `r-text-fffe/*`;
  `r-q-wire/retain-recordset-field-after-db-close/`; `r-str-ado-act/str-schema-change`, `r-fix-wire-ado/str-schema-change-isolated`.
- **Open.** The variant "diagnostic getter first against interfering getter or cleanup" is not asked (the worker reads the channels at fixed points); VBA, managed and vtable lanes (T). The IErrorInfo rule (a `Field.Value` get has one, other DAO
  calls do not) rests on records from different probes, because the probe meant to read `Field.Value` expression errors beside engine errors stopped at `OpenRecordset`.
- **Built (`w15-ado-batch-err-schema`, `d2b21142`, 2026-11-27):** `ConnectionObject.Open` applies `Properties("Data Source")` over stale failed-`Open(openText)` `_connectionString` when `openText` is omitted and clears `Errors` on success (`r-q-wire/obs-ado-errors-clear`).

### R-STRING-1 STR-BYREF (and R-ORACLE-1 OR-WIRE by-reference inputs): DAO dereferences a by-reference argument and writes nothing back; ADO writes `RecordsAffected` back
- **Claim.** DAO: `Database.OpenRecordset(Name, Type, Options)` with the Name as `VT_BYREF|VT_VARIANT` holding a BSTR, the Type as typed `VT_BYREF|VT_I4`, and all three by reference (typed BSTR, variant I4 2, typed I4 0) behaves as by value (the recordset opens with
  Type 4 or 2) and the referenced values are unchanged after the call. A by-reference Empty Type fails 3001 (`0x800A0BB9`) and a by-reference Null fails 3421 (`0x800A0D5D`), as by value. `Field.Value` puts through references (variant and typed
  BSTR, I4, R8; a typed by-reference 3-byte odd BSTR into BINARY(8)) store and read back exactly as by value (the odd BSTR stores `410042` plus zero padding); a referenced Null stores Null, a referenced Empty into a Long stores Null. ADO:
  `Connection.Execute(CommandText, RecordsAffected, Options)` writes the affected-row count back into the by-reference argument: UPDATE of 2 rows gives 2, UPDATE of 0 rows 0, INSERT 1, DELETE 1, SELECT 0 and DDL `CREATE TABLE` 0, whatever the
  initial value (Empty or -7), into a variant or a typed `VT_I4`; a typed by-reference BSTR "x" receives the BSTR "2". `Command.Execute` writes the count (2) back for Parameters Missing, also with Options 129 (no recordset), and a by-reference
  Empty `Parameters` fails `0x800A0E7C` "Parameter object is improperly defined" with an IErrorInfo.
- **Conditions.** Raw IDispatch; `VT_BYREF|VT_VARIANT` and typed `VT_BYREF` of scalars and BSTR; ACCDB; the value after the call captured by the worker.
- **Records.** `r-q2-wire/byref-dao-openrecordset-args/`, `byref-dao-put-values/`, `byref-ado-records-affected-counts/`, `byref-ado-command-execute/`; `r-ops/byref-ado-execute-records-affected/`.
- **Also (task 3m35, probe round r-q35-byref-args, ACEDAO 16.0.20625.20044).** The same holds, typed `VT_BYREF|<type>` and
  `VT_BYREF|VT_VARIANT`, for every type each member admits by value: DAO SELECT `Field.GetChunk` Offset and Bytes (ACCDB
  and MDB), `Recordset.PercentPosition` put, `FindLast`, `FindNext` and `FindPrevious` Criteria (all four Find methods
  alike), `Workspace.CommitTrans` and `DBEngine.CommitTrans` Option, `DBEngine.CreateDatabase` and
  `Workspace.CreateDatabase` Name and Locale; ADO `Connection.Open` ConnectionString, UserID, Password and Options,
  `Recordset.Sort` put, `Recordset.Find` Criteria and SkipRecords, `Field.GetChunk` Length (server forward-only and client
  static). The value, the error, the EXCEPINFO, IErrorInfo, the Errors collection and the state equal the by-value
  answer, `*puArgErr` stays unset and nothing is written back. Records: `r-q35-byref-args/` dao-select-getchunk-accdb,
  -mdb, dao-rs-percentposition, dao-rs-findlast, dao-ws-committrans, dao-eng-createdatabase, ado-conn-open, ado-rs-sort,
  ado-rs-find, ado-field-getchunk, each asked twice with identical answers; the by-value records are those of ptrj's
  report (r-q32 q3-lv-getchunk-args, r-cursor-1b percent-assign, r-txn-1b commit-option-vartypes, r-wire
  opt-createdatabase, r-ado-1b conn-open-args, view-sort-grammar, view-find-client, r-oledb-odbc stream-getchunk). The
  organizer read the PercentPosition calls (ByRef 50 and 37.5 give AbsolutePosition 10 and 7, as by value).
- **Open.** The same variable passed twice (aliases) and other members with output arguments (the harness builds one variable per argument); VBA and managed callers (T); ByRef forms of types a member does not admit by value; CreateDatabase Option and ADO Find SearchDirection and Start by reference; MDB for the members asked on ACCDB only.
- **Built (`w15-ado-batch-err-schema`, `d2b21142`, 2026-11-27):** `CommandObject.Execute` dereferences `ByRef` `Parameters` (`VT_BYREF|VT_VARIANT`) and returns `0x800A0E7C` (`ADODB.Command`) for `VT_EMPTY` `Parameters` on non-`?` SQL while leaving ByRef args untouched (`r-q2-wire/byref-ado-command-execute`).

### R-ACT-1 ACT-ABI: named arguments resolve by one `GetIDsOfNames`; skipped optionals, reordering and by-reference named arguments work; an unknown name is DISP_E_UNKNOWNNAME and a repeated one is 3001
- **Claim.** DAO dispids: `Database.Execute` Query 0, Options 1; `DBEngine.CreateWorkspace` Name 0, UserName 1, Password 2; `DBEngine.CreateDatabase` Name 0, Locale 1, Option 2; `Database.OpenRecordset` Name 0, Type 1, Options 2 (`r-ops`). Named
  arguments in any order bind by name (CreateWorkspace UserName, Name, Password gives the right Workspace; CreateDatabase Option, Name, Locale creates the file). A misspelled name fails in `GetIDsOfNames` with `0x80020006`
  (dispid -1) before the call; naming Options twice fails 3001 (`0x800A0BB9`). ADO dispids: `Connection.Execute` CommandText 0, RecordsAffected 1, Options 2; `Recordset.Open` Source 0, ActiveConnection 1, CursorType 2, LockType 3, Options 4. Named
  skipping of `RecordsAffected` works, a named by-reference `RecordsAffected` works, `Recordset.Open` fully named in a different order opens (State 1, rows read), and `ActiveConn` fails `0x80020006`.
- **Conditions.** Raw IDispatch; positional arguments followed by named ones; ACCDB.
- **Records.** `r-q2-wire/named-dao-methods/`, `named-ado-methods/`; `r-ops/named-dao-openrecordset/`; `r-wire/opt-*`.
- **Open.** Default member (DISPID 0) invocation; exact vtable sizes and offsets and independently compiled declarations (need a native caller: T).

### R-STRING-1 STR-READ: a DAO Text or Memo value reads identically by ordinal, by name, from a retained Field, across projections and after revisits
- **Claim.** For the eleven-row fixture (empty string, Null, embedded NUL, a NUL alone, 4,000-unit Memos with NUL, a 70,000-unit Memo, a supplementary character, mixed Latin-1 and wide text) the VT tag, BSTR byte length and text of each value are
  identical by ordinal and by name, between a retained Field object and a fresh one, with one or nine projected columns, with a duplicated column, on repeat reads and after re-finding a row; the ADO read of the same rows returns the same tags and
  `ActualSize`. Embedded NUL is kept (`Len` counts it, `InStr(T, Chr(0))` finds it). `""` and Null stay distinct in DAO and ADO `Value`. After EOF a retained Field fails `0x800A0BCD`; after `Recordset.Close` it fails `0x800A0D5C`.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB for all probes; MDB for the ordinal pass (identical to ACCDB); snapshot recordsets; DAO-written `S` and ADO-written compressed `C` tables.
- **Records.** `r-str-ado-act/str-ordinal-accdb/`, `str-ordinal-mdb/`, `str-named-retained/`, `str-projection/`, `str-revisit/`, `str-after-eof-close/`, `str-ado-read/`.
- **Open.** MDB for the named, projection and revisit probes; native compression forms and page and cache effects (W-04); no allocation or timing claim. Note the U+FEFF note above: a leading U+FEFF in stored text does not read back unchanged.

### R-STRING-1 STR-LV: `FieldSize` of a Memo is in bytes, `GetChunk` offsets and sizes are in characters, on every recordset type
- **Claim.** For Memo values `abcde`, 3,000 `q`, `a` NUL `b` and euro a-umlaut, a property-get `FieldSize` returns 10, 6000, 6 and 4 (twice the character count: 5, 3000, 3 and 2 characters) and `GetChunk(0,4)` and `GetChunk(1,4)` return the expected slices
  (`abcd`, `bcde`; `a` NUL `b`, NUL `b`), identically through a snapshot, a dynaset, a table-type recordset and a forward-only recordset. A Text field refuses both (`0x800A0CBB`).
- **Conditions.** ACCDB; property get of `FieldSize` (the method-call form is refused `0x800A0CB3`).
- **Records.** `r-q-wire/str-memo-fieldsize-by-recordset-type/`; `r-fix-wire-ado/str-memo-fieldsize/`, `r-str-ado-act/str-memo-chunks/`.
- **Open.** Values near 8,000 and 1 GB; the native storage forms behind them (W-04).

### R-STRING-1 STR-LV, R-ORACLE-1 OR-WIRE: DAO SELECT recordsets read Binary, OLE and Decimal(38) alike on every cursor, format and route; FieldSize, GetChunk and GetRows errors by state (task 63z8)
- **Claim.** One answer holds on ACCDB and Jet 4 MDB, on the dynaset, the snapshot and forward-only, and through `OpenRecordset("SELECT ...")`, a saved select query opened by name and `QueryDef.OpenRecordset`: every member below gives the same value or error on all three routes.
  - *Binary(16)* (Type 9, Size 16, Attributes 1): `Field.Value` and `Collect` give `VT_NULL` for Null and for a 0-byte value. Otherwise they give a 16-byte `VT_ARRAY|VT_UI1`, zero-padded on the right (5 bytes written read as 5 bytes and 11 zeros). *OLE* (Type 11, Size 0, Attributes 2): `VT_NULL` for Null and 0 bytes, otherwise the exact bytes.
  - *Decimal(38)* (Type 20, Size 20, Attributes 1): Null reads `VT_NULL`. A non-Null `Field.Value` fails 3000 ("Reserved error (-1038); there is no message for this error.", 5003000) from `DAO.Field`, with IErrorInfo. `Collect` fails the same 3000 from `DAO.Recordset`, without IErrorInfo. Both leave one `Errors` entry.
  - *FieldSize* (a property get) gives twice the character count for a Memo and the byte count for OLE, 0 for Null and empty. *GetChunk(offset, bytes)*:
    - On a Memo it gives a `VT_BSTR` slice, `""` for `bytes = 0` or `offset >= len`.
    - On OLE it gives a `VT_ARRAY|VT_UI1` slice. For `bytes = 0`, `offset = len` and `offset > len` it gives a `VT_ARRAY|VT_UI1` with a null SAFEARRAY.
    - Null gives `VT_NULL` on both.
  - *Errors by state* (FieldSize and GetChunk; all from `DAO.Field`, no IErrorInfo, one `Errors` entry):
    - After `Close`: 3267 on every field type, not 3420. It wins over 3259.
    - On a non-long-value field (Binary, Long, Text, Double, Decimal): 3259 "Invalid field data type." It wins over 3021 at BOF, EOF or on an empty recordset, and over a deleted row.
    - At BOF, EOF and on an empty recordset: 3021.
    - On a row deleted through the recordset: 3021. On a row deleted by another statement: 3167.
  - *GetChunk arguments*:
    - A wrong argument count (0, 1 or 3), `Missing` in either place, or a Double beyond the Long range fails 3001. A non-numeric String or Null fails 3421. Both come with IErrorInfo, ahead of the 3267 and 3259 checks.
    - Doubles and numeric Strings convert to Long rounding half to even: (0.5, 2.5) reads as (0, 2), ("2.5", "3.5") as (2, 4). Empty is 0 and True is -1.
    - A negative converted offset or size fails 3001 without IErrorInfo, also on a Null row.
  - *GetRows* (all errors from `DAO.Recordset`, no IErrorInfo, one `Errors` entry):
    - *NumRows*: omitted or `Missing` reads one row; 0, -1, Empty and True fail 3001; "2" and 2.5 read two rows; a non-numeric String and Null fail 3421.
    - *States*: BOF, EOF and an empty recordset fail 3021. After `Close` it fails 3420.
    - *Large NumRows*: `GetRows(1000000)` succeeds. 3035 ("System resource exceeded.", 5003035) starts at these observed pairs of (projected columns, NumRows): (1, 178,956,971), (2, 89,478,486), (3, 59,652,324), (4, 44,739,243). One less succeeds in each pair. The pairs fit `cols * NumRows * 24 > 2^32 - 1`; that is a fit, not an observed mechanism. On 3035 the cursor stays where it was, and 3035 comes before an empty recordset's 3021.
    - *Unshrunk array*: on a 4-row snapshot, (1 column, 89,478,489) returns 4 rows, but (1, 89,478,490) returns an array of all 89,478,490 elements; with 2 columns the step is between 44,739,246 and 44,739,247. Both steps fit `(NumRows - actualRows) * cols * 24 >= 2^31` (a fit).
    - *Row 0*: a non-Null Decimal(38) at row 0, or a row 0 deleted through the recordset or by another statement, returns S_OK with an array of zero rows, `[0+0, 0+cols]`. It leaves BOF and EOF false, the cursor on row 0, and `Errors` empty. A deleted later row is skipped.
- **Conditions.** Fixtures `r-q32-accdb` and `r-q32-mdb`: table T (ID, Binary(16), OLE, Memo, Long, Text(32), Double, Decimal(38,0)), four rows (all Null; empty; short values; full values), saved queries QAll, QDec, QSimple, QEmpty. Raw IDispatch, x64.
- **Records.** `evidence/r-q32-dao-select-lv/`, 40 questions, 17 asked twice and equal: `q1-val-*`, `q1-collect-*`, `q2-dec38-*`, `q3-lv-cursors-*`, `q3-lv-nonlv-*`, `q3-lv-states-*`, `q3-lv-deleted-*`, `q3-lv-getchunk-args-*`, `q4-getrows-*`, `q5-route-saved-*`, `q5-route-querydef-*`. Discriminators:
  - `q3-lv-deleted-*`: self-deleted against externally deleted.
  - `q3-lv-states-*`: 3267 after Close against 3420.
  - `q1-collect-*` against `q2-dec38-*`: IErrorInfo on Field.Value and not on Collect.
  - `q4-getrows-ceiling-cols-accdb`: the 3035 pairs per column count.
  - `q5-route-*`: the routes.
- **Open.** The contents of the unshrunk array beyond the rows read (not captured element by element); column counts above 4 and the 32-bit lanes; GetChunk on Binary of other sizes; ADO is out of this round.

### R-STRING-1 STR-EDIT: NUL in Text and Memo survives Update and Requery() in MDB and ACCDB
- **Claim.** On a dynaset, T = `x` NUL `y` NUL and M = 6000 `e` plus NUL, put, `Update`, `Requery()` with no argument and `MoveFirst`, read back unchanged; a fresh snapshot gives `Len(T)` 4, `Len(M)` 6001, `InStr(T, Chr(0))` 2. A 256-unit T assignment fails
  `0x800A0C5B`; `CancelUpdate` after Update fails `0x800A0BCC` (ACCDB).
- **Conditions.** MDB (new) and ACCDB (earlier); dynaset; no-argument Requery only.
- **Records.** `r-q-wire/str-edit-requery-mdb/`; `r-str-ado-act/str-edit-requery/`.
- **Open.** Edit of an existing long Memo through different storage forms (W-04).

### R-STRING-1 STR-SCHEMA: ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN under an open dynaset fails 3211; closed, it succeeds, and held objects refresh only on request
- **Claim.** `ALTER TABLE S ADD COLUMN X` while a dynaset on `S` is open fails `0x800A0C8B` with `Errors(0)` 3211 "could not lock table"; with nothing open it succeeds, and `DBEngine.Errors` still reports the old 3211 afterwards. A retained Field keeps reading
  after the failed ALTER, and after a successful ALTER `TableDefs Refresh` shows the new Field. Through ADO a keyset recordset blocks the ALTER (3211) while a client-cursor recordset does not, and the client recordset's `Fields.Count` stays 5 until
  `Requery` (6).
- **Conditions.** DAO dynaset and ADO keyset or client static, one element varied (open, closed, keyset only, client only), ACCDB.
- **Records.** `r-fix-wire-ado/str-schema-change-isolated/`, `fields-refresh-ddl-closed/`, `fields-refresh-ddl-keyset-only/`, `fields-refresh-ddl-client-only/`; `r-str-ado-act/str-schema-change/`; `r-q-wire/obs-errors-replace-persist/`.
- **Open.** MDB; other open recordset types.

### R-ORACLE-1 OR-REENTRY (ACE fault): `Recordset.Requery(QueryDef)` kills the process
- **Claim.** `Recordset.Requery` with a QueryDef argument on a dynaset opened from a saved parameter query ends the process with access violation `0xC0000005` while `rs.Requery` is the call in flight, whether the QueryDef is the recordset's own, a second
  instance of the same saved query, or after a parameter change. `Requery()` without argument returns normally and re-reads (one row), and so do table, dynaset, snapshot and forward-only recordsets. Through the DAO PIA (vtable) the same call returns
  `RPC_E_SERVERFAULT`, which places the fault in ACEDAO (informal, not retained).
- **Conditions.** Raw IDispatch; dynaset; saved parameter query `QParam`; ACCDB.
- **Records.** `r-crash-requery/requery-same-qd/`, `requery-other-qd/`, `param-then-requery-qd/` (Uncertain, exit -1073741819, last native call `rs.Requery`) against `open-read/` and `requery-plain/`; `r-cursor-1b/requery-parameter-query/`,
  `r-dao-1-err-1/query-requery/`, `r-cat-1/parameter-values/`, `r-fix-expr-sql/*-requery-no-argument/`, `r-cursor-1b/requery-types-state/`.
- **Open.** Other recordset types with an argument, and non-QueryDef arguments: deliberately not asked (crash hazard); retain the PIA observation formally (task `9fkt`).

### R-ORACLE-1 OR-REENTRY / R-ACT-1 ACT-THREAD (sink entry): ADO events are raised synchronously on the calling thread, in order, before the call returns; a failing call's error survives; an async Execute raised no ExecuteComplete
- **Claim.** With a recording sink on `ConnectionEvents` (`{00001400-...}`) the events arrive, in order and each before the return of the call that caused it: `WillConnect` (dispid 6) and `ConnectComplete` (7) during `Open`; `WillExecute` (4)
  and `ExecuteComplete` (5) around each `Execute`; `BeginTransComplete` (1); `CommitTransComplete` (3); `Disconnect` (8) during `Close`. Every callback runs on the thread that made the call, on the main STA (thread id of the caller) and on a new MTA
  thread (thread id of the MTA block). A failing `Execute` (missing table) raises `WillExecute` then `ExecuteComplete` with status 2 (adStatusErrorsOccurred) and an Error object, and the call still returns `0x80020009` with `Errors` Count 1: the
  original error survives the callback. `Execute` with `adAsyncExecute` returns at once with `State` 5 (open and executing), the state is 1 after 2 s, `Errors` is 0, and no `ExecuteComplete` was delivered in 2 s or by `Close`, on the STA
  (not pumping) or on an MTA thread (4 events: `WillConnect`, `ConnectComplete`, `WillExecute`, `Disconnect`). `RecordsetEvents` (`{00001266-...}`) raise `WillMove`/`MoveComplete` (dispids 15 and 16) around `Open` and every move, `EndOfRecordset`
  (17) between them when moving past the last row, a `WillChangeRecord`/`WillChangeField`/`FieldChangeComplete`/`RecordChangeComplete` set (11, 9, 10, 12) at a field put, a 11/12 pair at `Update`, and dispid 14 at `Close`; all on the calling thread.
- **Conditions.** Raw IDispatch; sink connected before the Provider is set; ACE provider; ACCDB.
- **Records.** `r-q2-wire/events-ado-connection-main-sta/`, `events-ado-connection-new-mta/`, `events-ado-async-main-sta/`, `events-ado-async-new-mta/`, `events-ado-recordset/`; `r-ops/ado-connection-events/`.
- **Open.** Reentrant calls made from inside a callback, deferred EXCEPINFO, aliases and nested failure need a native test object that calls back (T); why no `ExecuteComplete` arrives for the async call (needs a pumping owner or a longer wait).

### R-ACT-1 ACT-THREAD SAME-STA and MTA-CREATE: DAO objects are direct on an STA and proxies from an MTA; ADO objects are not proxies from an MTA; calls and errors are alike
- **Claim.** `DAO.DBEngine.120` (registered Apartment) and its Database, created on the main STA or on a second STA thread, answer `E_NOINTERFACE` for IClientSecurity, IMultiQI and IMarshal: direct objects (also `CoCreateInstance` asked directly for
  IMarshal, IClassFactory or IClientSecurity: `E_NOINTERFACE`). Created from a new MTA thread (`CoGetApartmentType` type 1; `CoInitializeEx` S_FALSE), they answer all three: a COM proxy. `Version`, `OpenDatabase`, a read and a failing
  `OpenRecordset` return the same values and EXCEPINFO (`0x800A0BC3`, source `DAO.Database`) in all three apartments. ADO is different: `ADODB.Connection`, `Recordset` (registered Both), `Command` (Both) and `Stream` (Apartment) answer IMarshal on the
  main STA and on an MTA thread alike (and `CoCreateInstance` for IMarshal succeeds), and none answers IClientSecurity or IMultiQI from either: no proxy, even for the Apartment-registered classes. A Connection used from an MTA thread opens, reads
  (`COUNT(*)` 3, `MAX(ID)` 3) and fails a bad statement exactly as on the STA (0x80040E14, IErrorInfo with the provider GUID, `Errors` 1). IStdMarshalInfo and IAgileObject are answered by none.
- **Conditions.** Thread apartment from `CoGetApartmentType` (type 0 STA, type 1 MTA); no message pump on the second STA; no concurrent callers.
- **Records.** `r-act-1-thread/*`; `r-q-wire/thread-ado-recordset-main-sta/`, `r-q-wire/thread-ado-recordset-new-mta/`, `r-q-wire/thread-ado-command-main-sta/`, `r-q-wire/thread-ado-command-new-mta/`, `r-q-wire/thread-ado-stream-main-sta/`, `r-q-wire/thread-ado-stream-new-mta/`, `r-q-wire/thread-ado-data-read-main-sta/`, `r-q-wire/thread-ado-data-read-new-mta/`, `thread-engine-identity-*` (DAO from MTA and second STA again); `r-q2-wire/act-dao-clsctx-iids/`, `act-ado-clsctx-iids/`.
- **Open.** What makes ADO answer IMarshal everywhere (consistent with a free-threaded marshaler; needs `IMarshal` calls the harness lacks, T); server-entry thread; concurrent callers.

### R-ACT-1 ACT-THREAD ENGINE-IDENTITY: within one apartment every route returns the one engine; whether a DBEngine in another apartment shares its Workspaces is not shown, its Errors are not shared
- **Claim.** On one STA, `CoCreateInstance`, two `CoGetClassObject`/`CreateInstance` calls, the ordinary `Create` and `ITypeInfo::CreateInstance` (twice) all return the same engine: the `Workspaces` collection reads as the same object (one identity
  label) through each, with Count 1 and the name `#Default Workspace#`. Two creations on one STA share Workspaces (`r-str-ado-act/act-two-engines`, Count 2 with the Workspace held). In another apartment (new MTA thread, proxy; or second STA
  thread, direct) a failed `OpenDatabase` of an absent path (3044, `Errors` 1 in that thread's engine) leaves `Errors` at 0 in the main-STA engine, in both orders. A Workspace appended to `Workspaces` (Count 1 to 2) disappears from the collection
  (Count back to 1) once the last reference to the Workspace object is released, even through the same engine on the same STA.
- **Corrected.** The previous draft said a Workspace appended in another apartment "is not seen by the main engine, so engines in different apartments keep separate Workspaces". That inference is withdrawn: the appended Workspace was released at the end of the thread
  block and, as `act-factory-dao-identity` and `act-typecreate-dao-identity` show, such a Workspace vanishes after its last release even within one engine. Only the `Errors` observation stands.
- **Conditions.** Raw IDispatch, ACCDB not involved; objects bound in a thread are released when its block ends.
- **Records.** `r-q2-wire/act-factory-dao-identity/`, `act-typecreate-dao-identity/` (same-apartment identity, Workspace vanishing); `r-q-wire/thread-engine-identity-main-then-mta/`, `main-then-sta2/`, `mta-then-main/` (Errors not shared); `r-str-ado-act/act-two-engines/`; `r-ops/activation-routes/`.
- **Open.** Workspace sharing across apartments (a reference would have to be held while the other apartment reads, which the harness cannot do: T); whether `Errors` is per engine instance or per thread.

### R-ACT-1 ACT-RETAIN: after `Database.Close` or `Workspace.Close` every held child and every enumerator of it is invalid (3420); the Error objects survive; ADO enumerators and held collections follow the Recordset and Connection
- **Claim.** With a snapshot and a dynaset still open, `Database.Close` succeeds; afterwards every member call on the held Recordsets (`EOF`, `Name`, `MoveNext`, `Close`), their Fields collections and Field (`Name`, `Value`, `Count`) fails `0x800A0D5C`
  (`Errors(0)` 3420 "Object invalid or no longer set"). A held TableDef, its Fields and Indexes, an Index, the TableDefs collection, and a saved QueryDef (`Name`, `SQL`, `Type`, `OpenRecordset`) fail the same way. `Workspace.Close` with a Database and a
  recordset open invalidates the Workspace itself (`Name`), the Database and the recordset. DAO enumerators: when `Database.Close` is called inside the loop after the first item of a `TableDefs` enumeration (or `Recordset.Close` after the first Field of a
  `Fields` enumeration), the next `Next` fails `0x800A0D5C` with nothing fetched, `Next` after `Reset` fails the same, `Skip(1)` fails E_FAIL and `Clone` succeeds. A held Errors collection and its Error object stay readable (`Number` 3011) after
  `Database.Close` and after the DBEngine reference is released, and a DBEngine created again still reports `Errors` Count 1. ADO: after `Recordset.Close` a held Field fails (`Name`, `Value`), the held Fields collection still counts 2 and a Fields enumerator
  that is mid-walk still delivers the next Field (`N`), ends S_FALSE, and after `Reset` delivers a Field again; after `Connection.Close` the Fields collection counts 0, `State` is 0 and a mid-walk enumerator ends at once (S_FALSE, nothing fetched,
  also after `Reset`).
- **Conditions.** ACCDB scratch databases; raw IDispatch; one parent closed per probe; enumerators walked with `Next(1)`.
- **Records.** `r-q-wire/retain-recordset-field-after-db-close/`, `retain-tabledef-after-db-close/`, `retain-querydef-after-db-close/`, `retain-database-after-workspace-close/`, `retain-errors-after-engine-release/`, `retain-ado-field-after-recordset-and-connection-close/`;
  `r-q2-wire/retain-enum-dao-after-close/`, `retain-enum-ado-after-close/`; `r-str-ado-act/str-after-eof-close/`, `ado-session`.
- **Open.** Callback sinks and held class factories outliving their parents (a retained factory is not kept by the harness; sink after Close not asked: T).

### R-ACT-1 ACT-ADO-SCOPE (ACE side): a fresh ADO Connection is MSDASQL; reading `Properties` pins the provider; a connectionless Recordset needs no ACE
- **Claim.** A new `ADODB.Connection` reports `Provider` "MSDASQL". Assigning the ACE provider succeeds when it is the first member used (after nothing, a `Provider` read, `State`, `ConnectionString` or `Mode`), and fails `0x800A0C94` once `Properties.Count`
  was read (14 properties for MSDASQL), leaving `Provider` "MSDASQL.1". With the ACE provider first, `Properties` has 31 entries closed and 101 open. Opens with a non-ACE name, or with only a Data Source while `Provider` still reads MSDASQL, go to the ODBC
  driver manager and fail IM002. A connectionless `ADODB.Recordset` (`Fields.Append`, `Open` without a connection, `AddNew`, `Update`, `MoveFirst`) works, reads 7 and "x" back, has a null `ActiveConnection`, and loads no ACE module. Two Connections made through
  the class factory are independent (Provider set on one reads "MSDASQL" on the other).
- **Conditions.** Raw IDispatch, one element varied per probe (the first member touched), ACE provider 12.0.
- **Records.** `r-fix-wire-ado/ado-provider-pin-isolated/`, `ado-open-provider-property-ace-first/`; `r-str-ado-act/ado-provider-non-ace/`, `ado-persist/`; `r-q-wire/ado-connectionless-recordset/`; `r-q2-wire/act-factory-ado-classes/`.
- **Open.** Effective omitted/default provider with a connection string only; the CalcDb routing and the host decision are not ACE questions. The non-ACE records pass through ODBC; no new probe uses it.
- **Built (`w15-ado-batch-err-schema`, `d2b21142`, 2026-11-27):** `AdoFieldsObject.Append` (`adInteger`/`adVarWChar` on closed fresh connectionless `Recordset`) + `OpenFromFabricated` (`ClientRowBuffer.FromFabricated`) supports `AddNew`/`Update`/`MoveFirst`/`Fields`/`ActiveConnection = null`/`Close` (`r-q-wire/ado-connectionless-recordset`), refusing unrecorded types/coercions/metadata/batch operations by name.

### R-ACT-1 ACT-NAME: `DAO.DBEngine.120` and the version-independent ADODB ProgIDs resolve; the version-dependent `ADODB.*.6.1` ProgIDs do not
- **Claim.** `CLSIDFromProgID` resolves `DAO.DBEngine.120` ({CD7791B9-...}, ACEDAO.DLL, Apartment), `ADODB.Connection` ({00000514-...}, Apartment), `ADODB.Recordset` ({00000535-...}, Both), `ADODB.Command` ({00000507-...}, Both) and `ADODB.Stream`
  ({00000566-...}, Apartment), all in `msado15.dll` for ADO. `ADODB.Connection.6.1`, `ADODB.Recordset.6.1`, `ADODB.Command.6.1` and `ADODB.Stream.6.1` fail with `0x800401F3` (invalid class string): those names are not registered here.

### R-ACT-1 ACT-CLSID: ACEDAO and the ADO classes are in-process only; the requested IID and class context decide the result
- **Claim.** `CoCreateInstance` of DAO.DBEngine.120 with `CLSCTX_INPROC_SERVER` answers `_DBEngine` and `IDispatch` and refuses IMarshal, IClassFactory and IClientSecurity with `E_NOINTERFACE` (`0x80004002`); with `CLSCTX_INPROC_HANDLER` alone
  `0x80040154` (class not registered), with `INPROC|HANDLER` (3) and with `0x17` it succeeds, and with `CLSCTX_LOCAL_SERVER` `0x80040154`. ADODB.Connection answers `_Connection`, `Connection15` ({00000515-...}), IDispatch, IUnknown and IMarshal,
  refuses a wrong interface of the right class (`_Recordset`) with `E_NOINTERFACE`, succeeds with `0x17`, and answers `0x80040154` for `CLSCTX_LOCAL_SERVER`; Recordset, Command and Stream answer their own interface IDs. Remote contexts were never requested.
- **Conditions.** Raw `CoCreateInstance` in the worker's STA; x64; classes by ProgID.
- **Records.** `r-q2-wire/act-dao-clsctx-iids/`, `act-ado-clsctx-iids/`; `r-ops/activation-routes/`.
- **Open.** `CoCreateInstanceEx`, the original context flags of unchanged clients and own-versus-compatibility identity (CalcDb side).

### R-ACT-1 ACT-FACTORY: ACEDAO's class factory hands out the one engine; the ADO factories hand out independent objects; LockServer and CreateInstance succeed
- **Claim.** `CoGetClassObject(IClassFactory)` with `LockServer(TRUE)`, `CreateInstance` and `LockServer(FALSE)` all return S_OK for the DBEngine and for ADODB.Connection (twice), Recordset, Command and Stream by CLSID. Two `CreateInstance` calls on
  the DBEngine factory, `CoCreateInstance` and the ordinary `Create` all return the same engine (shared `Workspaces` identity); the two Connections from the ADO factory are independent objects (Provider assigned on one is "MSDASQL" on the other).
- **Conditions.** In-process class context; the worker's STA; no aggregation.
- **Records.** `r-q2-wire/act-factory-dao-identity/`, `act-factory-ado-classes/`; `r-ops/activation-routes/`.
- **Open.** A retained factory after the activating context changes (CalcDb side); server-lock accounting beyond the call results.

### R-ACT-1 ACT-AGGREGATE: ADO classes aggregate only for IID_IUnknown and never query the outer; the DBEngine refuses aggregation
- **Claim.** With a non-null controlled outer, `CoCreateInstance` of ADODB.Connection, Recordset, Command and Stream succeeds for `IID_IUnknown` (the inner then answers IDispatch) and fails `0x80040110` (CLASS_E_NOAGGREGATION) for `IID_IDispatch`;
  the same through the class factory (Connection, Recordset, Command, Stream: IUnknown succeeds; Connection with IDispatch `0x80040110`). The outer recorded no `QueryInterface` in any aggregated creation. DAO.DBEngine.120 refuses an outer for IUnknown
  and IDispatch: `0x80040110` through the class factory when asked in a fresh process, `0x8000FFFF` (E_UNEXPECTED) when the engine already exists in the process (`r-ops/activation-routes`, `CoCreateInstance` and factory alike).
- **Conditions.** `CLSCTX_INPROC_SERVER`; the outer is the worker's recording IUnknown.
- **Records.** `r-q2-wire/act-aggregate-ado-cocreate/`, `act-aggregate-classobject/`; `r-ops/activation-routes/`.
- **Open.** AddRef and Release of the outer are not recorded (only `QueryInterface`); aggregation of other classes in the libraries.

### R-ACT-1 ACT-TYPECREATE: `ITypeInfo::CreateInstance` reaches the same classes, and the DBEngine coclass gives the existing engine
- **Claim.** From a bound object's type information (`GetTypeInfo`, `GetContainingTypeLib`, `GetTypeInfoOfGuid`) the coclasses Connection, Recordset, Command and Stream of ADO each `CreateInstance` for IDispatch (`Version` "10.0", `State` 0, `CommandTimeout` 30) and
  Connection for IUnknown; the DBEngine coclass of the DAO library creates twice and both results share the `Workspaces` identity of the engine already in the process.
- **Conditions.** Raw IDispatch; the worker's STA.
- **Records.** `r-q2-wire/act-typecreate-ado-coclasses/`, `act-typecreate-dao-identity/`; `r-ops/activation-routes/`.
- **Open.** Type information retained across a context change (CalcDb side).

### R-ACT-1 ACT-META: the type libraries report SYS_WIN32 (DAO) and SYS_WIN64 (ADO) with FHASDISKIMAGE; every object answers ISupportErrorInfo, and Workspace, Database, Recordset and the ADO Connection and Recordset answer IConnectionPointContainer
- **Claim.** In an x64 process, the library of DAO.DBEngine.120's type information is DAO 12.0, `sysKind` 1, flags 8,
  77 types, help string "Microsoft Office 16.0 Access database engine Object Library". ADODB.Connection's library is
  ADODB 6.1, `sysKind` 3, flags 8, 120 types. Of ISupportErrorInfo, IProvideClassInfo, IDispatchEx,
  IConnectionPointContainer, IPersist and IProvideClassInfo2, every object asked answers ISupportErrorInfo: DBEngine,
  Workspaces, Workspace, Database, TableDefs, a snapshot Recordset, its Fields and a Field, DBEngine.Errors, and ADO's
  Connection, its Errors, Recordset, Command and Stream. IConnectionPointContainer is answered by Workspace, Database
  and Recordset, and by ADO's Connection and Recordset. None answers the other four.
- **Conditions.** Raw QueryInterface on the worker's main STA. Each object kind is asked once, nothing else is called
  in between, and the ADO objects are never opened.
- **Records.** `r-act-1-meta/support-dao-objects/`, `support-ado-objects/`; `r-str-ado-act/act-dao120/`,
  `ado-describe/`, `act-ado-classes/`.
- **Open.** The per-IID answers of InterfaceSupportsErrorInfo (a vtable call the harness lacks: T). Other DAO objects
  (QueryDef, Index, Relation, Container). Which connection points the containers enumerate (ADO-EVENT, DAO events).

### R-ORACLE-1 OR-FREEZE: a live writer frozen after a return leaves a stable image: an explicit commit is on disk at return, an implicit INSERT reaches disk about half a second after it returned, and Close adds one more change
- **Claim.** With the writer suspended (all threads, 31 to 44 threads) at the named boundary, the database and lock files are byte-identical at 0, 1.5 and 4 s: *after `CommitTrans` returned* the database is already 8,658,944 bytes (hash ee5c7fd421be) and unchanged for 2.5 s after the writer resumes,
  and changes only at `Close` (hash ab4a093dae48); *after an implicit `Execute` of an INSERT returned* the frozen image is the old 196,608-byte file (e8563962cf5a) for the whole 4 s and becomes 8,658,944 bytes 2.5 s after the resume; *after `Close`* the image is 8,658,944 bytes and
  unchanged by freezing and resuming. The 64-byte lock file is identical in every sample and absent after `Close`. Sampled page by page every 37 ms while a writer runs (`r-sample-rec`): an explicit transaction shows new data pages first (pages 49 and up grow the file from 49 to 2,189 pages
  within the first sample), then pages 0, 1 and 45 to 48 and a second wave of data pages in the next sample, and nothing after; an implicit INSERT leaves the file at 129 pages with no page changed for 14 consecutive samples after the statement returned, then rewrites
  2,189 pages (including 0, 1 and data pages) between 0.54 and 0.64 s after the return signal. Freezing adds no Close, flush or lock transition.
- **Conditions.** Participant b suspends participant a (process threads) from outside; the database is closed and reopened shared before the write; ACCDB, 2,048 rows of 2,000 characters.
- **Records.** `r-q2-wire/freeze-after-commit-return/`, `freeze-after-implicit-return/`, `freeze-after-close/` (participant b's capture holds the freezing); `r-sample-rec/insert-explicit/`, `insert-implicit/`, `pages-large-explicit/`, `pages-large-implicit/`; `r-ops/suspend-writer-mid-insert/`.
- **Open.** A process kill is not a power-loss image and every crash-timing claim belongs to R-CRASH-1 (torn writes, reconstruction: T); the within-statement write order of a long implicit INSERT beyond the sampled interval.

## R-FMT-1, R-FMT-2, R-PRESERVE-1 and R-SORT-3

### R-FMT-1 FMT1-CREATE: CreateDatabase's version option selects the format, and only 64, 128 and 256 are accepted

- **Claim**: `DBEngine.CreateDatabase(path, ";LANGID=0x0409;CP=1252;COUNTRY=0", option)` with option 64 gives a Jet 4 MDB (`db.Version` "4.0"), 128 an ACCDB ("12.0"), 256 an ACCDB reporting "14.0"; 32 is refused with 0x800A0C62 ("Could not find installable ISAM"). The locale string needs its leading `;`: `""` gives 3001 (0x800A0BB9), a string without the leading `;` gives 0x800A0C62, `LANGID=0x0000` gives 0x800A0C9C. `;LANGID=0x0409` alone and with `;CP=1252` are accepted. A bare file is 172032 B (ACCDB) or 77824 B (MDB); a table with a Long key adds 12288 B in both.
- **Conditions**: native DAO, `eng.CreateDatabase` only; each option or spelling is its own call, locale and path fixed.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fmt-1/create-accdb-empty/`, `create-mdb-empty/` (and the 50 other creations, same sizes); `evidence/r-sort-3/create-version-32/`, `create-version-256/` (attempt `a2`, "14.0"), `create-locale-spellings/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/v256-corpus-accdb/` (second, independent "14.0" creation, re-read), `v128-corpus-accdb/` ("12.0"), `v64-corpus-mdb/` ("4.0"). Discriminators: option 64 versus 128 versus 256 with the same locale; 32 versus the others; spelling variants with the option fixed.
- **Open**: options 1, 8 and 16 (older Jet) were never asked.

### R-FMT-1 FMT1-CREATE, R-CAT-1 CAT-NEW r-q38 (W-07, task 537t): an empty Jet 4 MDB differs from run to run only in the bytes its creation date derives

- **Claim**: `CreateDatabase(path, locale, 64)` gives a 77,824-byte, 19-page file. Across 10 empty MDBs (two days a week
  apart, ACE 16.0.20527.20196 and 16.0.20625.20044) exactly 160 bytes differ, all derived from the creation date as
  the ACCDB image's are: page 0 decoded `0x42..0x6A`, the integer day truncated (`46350.960005` gives `46350`) as ten
  u32LE; page 0 decoded `0x72..0x7A`, the creation date; page 17 DateCreate and DateUpdate of the 8 MSysObjects rows,
  equal to each other and to it, or 1 ms later where the system clock ticked during CreateDatabase (eng-langid-only
  a1 from row 1, a2 from row 0: ACE reads the clock again, 537t's replay); and the Owner (page 17) and MSysACEs SID (page 18) words under the date's 2-byte SID keystream.
  The header mask over `0x18..0x98` and the SID keystream are the ACCDB ones. Against the 42-page ACCDB image: page 0
  carries `Standard Jet DB` and version byte `0x01`; page 1 frees pages 19 to 511; MSysObjects holds the first 8 of
  the ACCDB rows (no MSysComplexType tables; page 2 counts 8, index leaves on pages 7 and 8), MSysACEs the 16
  non-complex rows on page 18 (page 3 counts 16 and 8, usage maps on page 9, index leaf page 10); pages 4 to 6 and
  11 to 16 are the ACCDB pages byte for byte. Until Close ACE holds the new file without FILE_SHARE_READ (Win32 32)
  and makes no `.ldb`; Close changes no byte.
- **Conditions**: unencrypted Jet 4, General sort; `;LANGID=0x0409;CP=1252;COUNTRY=0` and `;LANGID=0x0409`; the
  DBEngine and `Workspaces(0)` routes; `.mdb` and `.accdb` file names.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q38-mdb-new-image/` (eng-general-full a1 to a3, eng-langid-only a1 and a2,
  ws-general-full a1 and a2; the whole file after CreateDatabase, after `db.Close` and after release); the earlier
  same-day empties `r-fmt-1/create-mdb-empty`, `r-cat-1/create-mdb-empty`, `r-cat-1/create-accdb-ext-v40` (file
  artifacts). `file-compare --repeats` gives the four repeat pairs equal outside the clock fields; the ten-file,
  two-day comparison was the prober's own byte check, not a tool run (the organizer read only the new records'
  pages).
- **Open**: what decides the constant header words the ACCDB code already names (decoded `0x19..0x2C`, `0x6A` 4518,
  `0x98` 0x0654, the `0xE00` table); other locales, code pages and sort orders; `dbEncrypt` (192), passwords; the
  option as another variant type.

### R-FMT-1 FMT1-REOPEN and R-PRESERVE-1 PRES-NOOP: opening, enumerating, reading, edit-and-cancel and closing leave the file bytes unchanged

- **Claim**: a read-only open (`OpenDatabase(path, False, True)`) with metadata enumeration and reads of user tables does not change one byte: the SHA-256 is the same at creation, before the open and after the close, for 52 fixtures. A read/write open does the same, one element at a time: open and close; the same again; open, enumerate TableDefs, fields and QueryDefs; open, read all rows through a dynaset; open, open the table, MoveFirst, Edit and CancelUpdate. While open, a 64-byte lock file exists (`.laccdb` or `.ldb`) and it is gone after close.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB, copies of one fixture each (30 rows, an index, a Memo, a saved query), whole-file hash read after Close with the DBEngine alive, each step its own open and close.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fmt-1/read-*-*/` (52 records, hashes equal; re-read for the empty, counter, long and wide fixtures); `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/noop-rw-accdb/` and `noop-rw-mdb/` (re-read: `files[after-...]` hash and size equal at all five steps and at the repeat, ACCDB 200704 B and MDB 106496 B); `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/pres-noop/`. Discriminators: read-only versus read/write, repeated, with enumeration, with reads, with an Edit cancelled.
- **Open**: an Edit that is not cancelled but updates with the same value changes the file (the `fx-same-value-update` result in r-fmt-2b); lock-file contents are R-LOCK-1/2.

### R-FMT-1 FMT1-SYSTEM-TABLES: with the default Admin user, every system table refuses DAO index and record access

- **Claim**: the TableDefs of a new file list the system tables (ACCDB: MSysACEs, MSysComplexColumns, MSysObjects, MSysQueries, MSysRelationships; MDB the same without MSysComplexColumns), each with `Fields.Count` 0. `Indexes.Count` on any of them fails with 3110 (0x800A0C26), `OpenRecordset` on any of them and SQL `SELECT` of MSysObjects, MSysRelationships or MSysQueries with 3112 (0x800A0C28), and the `Connection` database property's `Value` with 3251 (0x800A0CB3). User tables and their data are unaffected.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB, the default Admin user, no workgroup, DAO enumeration of TableDefs and Databases properties.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fmt-1/read-*-empty/` (11 failures per ACCDB read, 9 per MDB read, same codes in all 52 reads); `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/pres-noop/` and the other `pres-*` records (SQL refusals 3112). Discriminator: user tables enumerated in the same reads succeed.
- **Open**: whether another user or workgroup changes this (out of scope). Catalog-row comparison needs the W-04 byte route.

### R-FMT-1 FMT1-FAMILIES: DAO reports each scalar family with a fixed Type, Size and Attributes and reads the extremes back exactly

- **Claim**: Byte (2, 1), Integer (3, 2), Long (4, 4), Single (6, 4), Double (7, 8), Currency (5, 8), Date/Time (8, 8), Text (10, size as declared), Memo (12), Yes/No (1, 1), Binary (9, size as declared), Long Binary (11), GUID (15, 16) have the same Type, Size and Attributes in ACCDB and MDB. Extremes read back exactly as VT_UI1, I2, I4, R4, R8, CY, DATE; -0 reads back as -0 for Single and Double; a Null reads VT_NULL except Yes/No. An empty byte array stored in Binary or Long Binary reads Null. A GUID reads as the BSTR `{guid {00112233-4455-6677-8899-AABBCCDDEEFF}}` (re-read: ACE's text). AutoNumber has Attributes 17, its primary key reads Primary and Unique, a descending index field reads Attributes 1.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB, one family per fixture, written through DAO, read through DAO after reopen; no Decimal (see NUMERIC).
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fmt-1/read-*-byte/` ... `read-*-guid/` (13 families), `read-*-counter/`, `read-*-index/` and the matching `create-*` records; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/binary-bytes-accdb/` (empty arrays read Null, re-read). Discriminators: one family per fixture; the two formats side by side.
- **Open**: none that block.

### R-FMT-1 FMT1-DECIMAL: Decimal columns come only from ADO DDL (or CreateField), never from DAO DDL

- **Claim**: DAO `Execute` of `CREATE TABLE ... V DECIMAL(18,4)` and of bare `V DECIMAL` is refused in ACCDB and MDB with 0x800A0CDC (3292). The same column made with ADO DDL (Provider Microsoft.ACE.OLEDB.12.0) is accepted in both formats and DAO reports Type 20, Size 16, Attributes 1, and Precision and Scale as declared; DAO reads the values as VT_DECIMAL.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB; DAO DDL, then ADO DDL on the closed file; reads through DAO.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-ddl-limits/accdb-decimal/`, `accdb-decimal-plain/`, `mdb-decimal/`, `mdb-decimal-plain/`; `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/fmt1-accdb-decimal/`, `fmt1-mdb-decimal/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/decimal-grid-accdb/`, `-mdb/` (re-read: Precision and Scale properties read back for (1,0), (2,1), (5,2), (18,4), (28,0), (28,28)). Discriminators: DDL route varied with format and values fixed.
- **Open**: DAO's own `CreateField(name, 20)` gives Precision 38 and an unreadable field (R-DEC-READ).

### R-FMT-1 FMT1-GATED: BIGINT and DATETIME2 exist in ACCDB and are refused in the Jet 4 MDB

- **Claim**: `ALTER TABLE T ADD COLUMN V BIGINT` and `V DATETIME2` are accepted by an ACCDB (BIGINT: Type 16, Size 8, Attributes 2, rows read VT_I8; DATETIME2: Type 26, Size 42, Attributes 1, read as a BSTR with seven fractional digits) and refused by an MDB with 0x800A0BB9 (3001). After the refusal the column does not exist.
- **Conditions**: DAO `Execute` with dbFailOnError, each statement alone.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fmt-1/create-accdb-bigint/`, `create-accdb-datetime2/`; `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/fmt1-mdb-bigint/`, `fmt1-mdb-datetime2/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/scalar-bounds-accdb/` (BigInt) and `scalar-bounds-mdb/` (no Fbig column), `dt2-ticks-accdb/`. Discriminator: the same statement in two formats.
- **Open**: none that block.

### R-FMT-1 FMT1-COLUMN-ORDER and R-FMT-2 Historical row coordinates (public side): column order, rename, drop and append are visible only through OrdinalPosition, and every old value stays with its column across the 8-bit boundaries

- **Claim**: setting `Field.OrdinalPosition` changes the listed order and the order of `SELECT *` without moving any value. After DROP COLUMN of a middle and a final column, old rows read with their remaining columns intact; after ADD COLUMN of a Long, a Text and a Yes/No, old rows read Null in the Long and Text columns and False in the Yes/No column; a renamed column keeps its values. The same holds at 7, 8, 9, 15, 16 and 17 columns: a row with every column set and one row per column with only that column Null read back exactly, and so do those rows after adding a Yes/No, a Long, a Text, dropping column C3, setting the Yes/No True and adding a second Yes/No to the 8-column table and adding a Long and a Yes/No to the 16-column table (the second added Yes/No reads False for old rows).
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB (the mask probes' values are identical in both), DDL and updates through DAO, key plus Long columns.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fmt-1/read-*-ordinal/`, `read-*-renamed-dropped/`; `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/alt-drop-ladder/`, `alt-append-ladder/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/mask-boundary-accdb/` and `-mdb/` (re-read: every Null pattern at 7 to 17 columns and every ALTER step; the two files' value lines are identical). Discriminators: each ALTER its own step; Null in each position separately.
- **Open**: physical column numbers, positions and the mask bytes are W-04 work (T).

### R-FMT-1 FMT1-WIDE: a table holds at most 255 fields and a record at most about 4000 bytes

- **Claim**: a table of 254 or 255 Long columns in total (key included) is created, takes a row and reads it back; a 256-column table is refused with error 3190 ("Too many fields defined"), and later statements on it find no table. A 255-column Yes/No table (key plus 254) reads False everywhere and keeps a single True set in column 100. Text(255) tables with 4 columns take a row of 255-character values; with 8 and 16 columns the table is created but the INSERT is refused with error 3047 ("Record is too large").
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, SQL DDL and INSERT with dbFailOnError and a marked Errors read after each.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/wide-accdb/` (re-read: Fields.Count 254, 255 and 255 for the three Long tables and the Yes/No table; errors 3190 and 3047), `evidence/r-fmt-1/read-*-wide/` (120 columns). Discriminators: column count varied 254, 255, 256 with type fixed; Text column count varied 4, 8, 16.
- **Open**: where between 4 and 8 columns of 255 characters the 3047 boundary lies (the 4000-byte rule is a guess); TableDef continuation bytes are W-04 work (T).

### R-FMT-1 FMT1-CALCULATED and R-FMT-2 Calculated wrappers (public side): calculated columns through DAO are admitted for the numeric, Text, Memo and Date result types with most expressions, are recomputed on every input change, cannot be written, and an input dropped or renamed leaves the table unreadable

- **Claim**: a TableDef made with `CreateTableDef`, whose field has `Expression` put before it is appended, is created for result types Yes/No (`[A] > 3`), Byte (`[A]`), Integer (`[A] * 2`), Long (`[A] * 100000`), Currency (`[A] * 1.5`), Single and Double (`[A] / 3`), Memo (`String(300, 'm')` and `[B] & "x"`), Decimal (`[A] / 3`), Text (`[B] & "-calc"`, `[B] & "-" & [A]`, `UCase([B])`, `Left([B], 1)`; Type 10, Size 243, Attributes 2) and Date (`[D] + 1`, `#1/1/2000# + [A]`, `DateSerial(2000, 1, [A])`; Type 8). Each reads Type = the requested DAO type, an `Expression` property equal to the text and a `ResultType` property (1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 12 for the first eight, 10 for Text, 8 for Date, 16 for Decimal); the result is Null when an input is Null. `Format([A], "000")`, `CStr([A])`, `DateAdd("d", [A], [D])` and `Date()` are refused at `TableDefs.Append` with error 3859 ("cannot be used in a calculated column"). A calculated result is recomputed and stored when its input changes: A 5 to 6 turns `[A] * 2` from 10 to 12, B 'x' to 'y' turns the Text result "x-calc" into "y-calc", B = Null gives "-calc", A = Null gives Null, A = 7 gives 14. A calculated column cannot be written: `UPDATE ... SET C = 'z'` is refused with 3113 and a recordset Edit put with 3164 (the Update after it then answers "without AddNew or Edit"). Putting `Expression` on an existing field is refused with 3191. After `ALTER TABLE ... DROP COLUMN A` (hr 0, RecordsAffected 1) or after renaming A to A2 through `Field.Name`, every read of the table fails with 0x800A0F2B ("This calculated column contains an invalid expression"); dropping the calculated column C afterwards does not repair the other one's table. A Long calculated result that exceeds Long (`[A] * 1000`, `[A] * [A]` for 2147483647) is stored without error and its read fails with 0x800A0006 ("Overflow"); Double `[A] ^ 5` reads 4.5671926060252477E+46, Currency `[A] * 100000` reads 214748364700000, and Decimal `[A] * 100000000000000` reads the 24-digit values exactly as VT_DECIMAL (214748364700000000000000 and its negative, scale 0).
- **Conditions**: ACCDB only (calculated columns), each result type or expression in its own table, each step's Errors read after a marker.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/calc-types-accdb/`; `evidence/r-q2-fmt-sort/calc-text-date-accdb/` (re-read: 12 expressions, 4 refused), `calc-recompute-accdb/` (re-read: the read after every step, errors 3113, 3164, 3191, scode 0x800A0F2B), `calc-magnitudes-accdb/` (re-read: Overflow, Decimal words); `evidence/r-fmt-1/read-accdb-calculated/`. Discriminators: result type varied with the expression form fixed, expression varied with the type fixed, one input change per step.
- **Open**: what a rename does to the stored expression text (the table is unreadable, so it was not read); wrapper bytes are T. In `calc-magnitudes-accdb` two rows share the non-key ID 648, which does not affect the result.

### R-FMT-1 FMT1-INDEX-ENUM: TableDef.Indexes enumerates in name order, case-insensitively, not in creation order

- **Claim**: after creating `Z_ix`, `M_ix`, `a_ix`, `B_ix`, `_first` and `0_ix` in that order on a table whose key index is `PK_T`, the list reads `_first`, `0_ix`, `a_ix`, `B_ix`, `M_ix`, `PK_T`, `Z_ix` (underscore before digit before letters, upper and lower case together), after every creation and after a read-only reopen.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, SQL CREATE INDEX, TableDefs and Indexes refreshed before each read.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/index-enum-order-accdb/` (re-read); `evidence/r-fmt-1/read-*-index/`, `evidence/r-sort-3b/image-build-order-a/`. Discriminator: creation order differing from name order.
- **Open**: names with other punctuation or non-ASCII letters.

### R-FMT-1 FMT1-COLLATING and R-SORT-3 Descriptor dispatch (locale): the LANGID of CreateDatabase selects the collating order, and locale-tailored orders differ in listed ways

- **Claim**: `Database.CollatingOrder` equals the LANGID requested at creation: 0x0409 gives 1033, 0x0809 2057, 0x0407 1031, 0x041D 1053, 0x0405 1029, 0x0411 1041, 0x041F 1055, 0x040A 1034, 0x0419 1049, 0x0408 1032, 0x0406 1030, and a table's Text field reports the same value; it is unchanged by a read-only reopen and kept by CompactDatabase between ACCDB and MDB. On the corpora tried, General, UK, German (0x0407), Russian (0x0419) and Greek (0x0408) give identical equality classes and order, including a corpus with Cyrillic letters (ё, я, Я), Greek letters (final sigma, accented alpha) and German umlaut and sharp-s forms; Danish (0x0406) puts ä, ø, ö, å and "aa" after z; Swedish puts å, ä, ö, ø after z and ü after ue; Czech puts "ch" between è and i; Spanish-traditional puts "cz" before "ch"; Japanese orders Han 一 < 漢 < 国 < 二; Turkish sorts dotless i before {i, İ} and drops the İ row from a unique index.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB for all locales, MDB for general, German and Russian; reads by SQL ORDER BY, GROUP BY, index walk and unique survivors; each locale its own file.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/fmt1-collating-contrast/`; `evidence/r-sort-3b/locale-*/`, `lg-*`, `lu-*`; `evidence/r-sort-3/compact-*/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/locale-extra-accdb/` (re-read: CollatingOrder 1033, 1049, 1032, 1030, 1031; Russian, Greek and German walks equal General's, Danish differs); `evidence/r-q53-conv-abspos-subq-loc/createdb-{swedish-041d,german-0407}-{accdb,mdb}/` (Swedish `0x041D` `CollatingOrder=1053` and German `0x0407` `1031` empty + 1-table+index kept pages on ACCDB & Jet 4 MDB, `0xE02` `00->02->05->07`). Discriminators: locale varied with corpus, version and path fixed; czech, swedish and japanese found independently by compaction and by creation.
- **Open**: a German phonebook order, Windows regional settings and registry sort options (not permitted); General97 and General - Legacy (no selector; T).

### R-FMT-1 FMT1-PROPERTIES: a TableDef Description and a user Database property can be appended and read back

- **Claim**: appending a TableDef property `Description` (Text) returns success and it reads back with Type 10 and its value; a user Database property (`CalcDbProbe`, Text) reads back in the same session and after a read-only reopen, in ACCDB and MDB.
- **Conditions**: DAO CreateProperty and Properties.Append, read through a refreshed collection.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/fmt1-accdb-properties/`, `fmt1-mdb-properties/`; `evidence/r-fmt-1/read-*-properties/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/query-props-accdb/` (re-read: Description and an unknown text property appended to a table without error).
- **Open**: none that block; property bytes are W-04.

### R-FMT-2 Ordinary Boolean / Null: a Yes/No field cannot hold Null, and empty text is distinct from Null

- **Claim**: a Yes/No value is -1 or 0; `UPDATE ... SET Y = Null` returns success and reads back False; an insert that omits the Yes/No column stores False, and a Yes/No column added to an existing table reads False for old rows; a Null Yes/No in a compound key is stored as False and a Seek with a Null Yes/No key component fails with 0x800A0C5A. For Text, `''` and Null are distinct and independent: `V = ''`, `V = Null`, `V = 'filled'` and `N = ''` each read back as written after a read-only reopen. For Binary and Long Binary an empty array reads back as Null.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, SQL UPDATE and recordset writes, read after each step.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/bool-ladder/`; `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/fmt2-var-null-empty-ladder/`; `evidence/r-sort-3b/create-mix/`, `mix-order-b/`, `mix-seek-b/`; `evidence/r-fmt-1/read-*-yesno/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/binary-bytes-accdb/`, `mask-boundary-accdb/` (the appended Yes/No reads False). Discriminators: Null set by UPDATE, omitted by INSERT, attempted in a key, and appended by ALTER.
- **Open**: the stored value-bit versus presence-bit distinction is a byte question (T).

### R-FMT-2 Scalar boundaries: out-of-range values are refused with error 3421, in-range extremes are stored exactly, fractions round half to even, Money rounds half to even for SQL literals

- **Claim**: a VT_R8 put into a Byte, Integer or Long column rounds half to even and refuses what then lies outside the type with 0x800A0D5D (3421, "Data type conversion error"): Byte 0.5, 1.5, 2.5, -0.4, -0.5 store 0, 2, 2, 0, 0; 255.4 stores 255 and 255.5 is refused; Integer 32767.4 stores 32767, 32767.5 and -32768.6 and an I4 of 32768 or -32769 are refused, -32768.5 stores -32768; Long 2147483647.4 stores 2147483647, 2147483647.5, 2147483648 and -2147483648.6 are refused, -2147483648.5 stores -2147483648, and 1.5, 2.5, 3.5 store 2, 2, 4. Single 3.4028235E38 and 1E-45 are stored, 3.5E38 and -3.5E38 are refused, 1E-46 underflows to 0 without error; Double 1.7976931348623157E308 is stored, the BSTR "1e309" and "-1e309" are refused, "5e-325" underflows to 0. BigInt (ACCDB) stores -1, 9223372036854775807 and -9223372036854775808 and refuses 9223372036854775808 and -9223372036854775809. The refused row stays as an ID-only row. NaN and infinities are accepted for Single and Double and kept by bit pattern. A GUID given as an unbraced string or a 16-byte array is refused with 3421. Currency: SQL numeric literals, `CCur(literal)` and the exact Currency range agree: the ties 0.00005, 0.00015, 0.00025, 0.00035, 0.00045, 0.00055 store 0, 0.0002, 0.0002, 0.0004, 0.0004, 0.0006, and -0.00005, -0.00015, -0.00025 store 0, -0.0002, -0.0002 (half to even at the fifth decimal), 0.00004999 stores 0 and 0.00005001 stores 0.0001; 922337203685477.58075 rounds to ...5808 and is refused as out of range, -922337203685477.58085 rounds to ...5808 and is stored. A VT_R8 put of the same amounts stores 0.0001, 0.0001, 0.0003, 0.0003, 0.0004, 0.0006 (not half to even), and refuses both beyond-range amounts.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB (the integer, Single, Double and Byte rows are identical in both; BigInt ACCDB only), table-recordset puts and SQL `INSERT`, each with a marked Errors read.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/scalar-bounds-accdb/` and `-mdb/` (re-read: stored values and 24 refusals each), `money-ties-accdb/` (re-read); `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/scalar-admission/`; `evidence/r-sort-3/create-nums-accdb/`, `create-nums-mdb/`. Discriminators: fraction just below, at and above each tie and each limit, both signs; three routes for Money (literal, CCur, VT_R8).
- **Open**: why the VT_R8 route gives 1, 1, 3, 3, 4, 6 at the fifth decimal (consistent with rounding the binary value, not tested); VT_CY and BSTR routes for Money; the boundary inside Single's subnormal range.

### R-FMT-2 NUMERIC (public side): a Decimal column keeps at most 28 digits, truncates extra scale toward zero and refuses extra integer digits with error 3761

- **Claim**: ADO DDL creates DECIMAL(p,s) for p up to 28 (the forms (1,0), (2,1), (5,2), (18,4), (28,0), (28,28), (28,10) were created) and refuses (29,0), (38,0) and (38,10) in both formats. A value with more scale than the column is not rounded but truncated toward zero: in (1,0) 0.5, 1.5, 2.5, -0.4 store 0, 1, 2, 0; in (2,1) 0.25, 0.35, 0.05, 0.15 store 0.2, 0.3, 0, 0.1; in (5,2) 12.345, 12.355, 12.365, 0.005, 0.015, 0.025, 0.001 store 12.34, 12.35, 12.36, 0, 0.01, 0.02, 0; in (18,4) 0.00005 and 0.00015 store 0 and 0.0001; in (28,10) 0.00000000005 stores 0. A value with too many integer digits is refused with error 3761 ("The decimal field's precision is too small to accept the numeric you attempted to add"): 10 into (1,0), 10.0 into (2,1), 1000 into (5,2), 100000000000000 into (18,4), 1 into (28,28), a 19-digit integer into (28,10), a 29-digit integer and 79228162514264337593543950335 into (28,0); a 28-digit integer 9999999999999999999999999999 is stored. -0.00 reads "0".
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB (the grid records are identical apart from file size), ADO DDL then DAO writes by SQL literal (at most 15 significant digits) and BSTR integer text, each with a marked Errors read.
