- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/decimal-grid-accdb/` and `-mdb/` (re-read: creation results, Precision and Scale properties, the inserts and their errors); `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/fmt2-scalar-admission-decimal/`, `fmt2-fx-fixture/`. Discriminators: precision and scale varied one at a time; value just inside and just outside each limit.
- **Open**: whether a negative zero is stored (CStr cannot show it); input by BSTR text with a decimal point (locale 7177). The 17-byte layout, sign and word order: see the NUMERIC/GUID bytes note under R-SORT-3.

### R-FMT-2 Dates / GUID (public side): DATE doubles are stored and read back bit-exact within DAO, and GUIDs accept only the braced text form

- **Claim**: VT_DATE puts of -657434, -1.5, -1, -0.5, 0, 0.5, 1.5, 2958465.99998843, 2958466, 2958466.5, 2958467, 10000000, -657435 and -700000 are accepted with no error and read back unchanged (values past 9999-12-31 are admitted); -0 and +0 are kept as different values. A GUID field accepts a braced string, reads back as `{guid {...}}`, and refuses an unbraced string and a 16-byte array with 3421.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, DAO VT_DATE and BSTR puts, each its own write.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/fmt2-scalar-admission-date-range/`; `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/scalar-admission/`; `evidence/r-sort-3/create-nums-accdb/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/neg-zero-accdb/` (-0 and +0 are distinct values in GROUP BY, DISTINCT and the index).
- **Open**: `CDbl(T) = 0` on a Date column returned no row in `neg-zero-accdb` (not understood; the Date bits are not in question). GUID word order: see the NUMERIC/GUID bytes note under R-SORT-3.

### R-FMT-2 Extended Date/Time (public side): DATETIME2 keeps seven fractional digits, rounds the eighth half up, and refuses year 0 and year 10000

- **Claim**: a DATETIME2 column accepts ISO strings by a recordset BSTR put and by a SQL string literal, with identical results by both routes. 0001-01-01 00:00:00.0000000 and .0000001 are stored; 9999-12-31 23:59:59.9999999 is stored. The eighth digit rounds half up: .00000005 gives .0000001, .12345665 gives .1234567 (half to even would give ...66), .12345675 gives .1234568, .12345674 gives .1234567, .12345676 gives .1234568; .99999995 carries to the next second (:06.0000000) and .99999994 gives .9999999. 9999-12-31 23:59:59.99999995 and .99999999 are accepted and read back as the out-of-range text "12/31/9999 24:00:00.0000000". The strings for year 0 (0000-12-31 23:59:59.9999999) and year 10000 are refused: the put with 0x80020009 (the row stays Null), the SQL literal with error 3464 ("Data type mismatch in criteria expression"). `CStr(V)` of a DATETIME2 value fails with 0x800A0BFF.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB; the string forms only (no VT_DATE route asked).
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/dt2-ticks-accdb/` (re-read: 13 stored values per route, rows 14, 15 and 114, 115 refused). Discriminators: the eighth digit varied 4, 5, 6 and the ties 5 at even and odd seventh digits; the two input routes.
- **Open**: the index and ORDER BY of DATETIME2 do not give chronological order (see the R-SORT-3 note on typed keys); VT_DATE input; the 42-byte grammar and key transform are bytes (T).

### R-FMT-2 Index page prefix (gqv4): a root page stores prefix 0 until it is full; a leaf made by a split keeps the prefix of the leaf it came from
- **Claim**: The stored prefix of an index page (the bytes every entry of the page omits) depends on how the page came to be, not on its present entries:
  - A tree's root page stores prefix 0, whether it is the only leaf or an intermediate page, while its entries fit under prefix 0. A full root leaf whose entries no longer fit is first rewritten under their common prefix, so it holds more entries before it splits (602 LONG entries in long-split; refined 2026-11-21 by the zfrp stretch-3 replay of r-q7 long-split, pages 44 onward byte for byte). This holds after ascending or descending inserts, for one, two, four or nine entries, after deletes down to one entry, for an index created after its rows, for LONG and TEXT keys, and in a compacted copy. A root leaf whose nine entries share 5 key bytes still stores 0.
  - A leaf made by a split keeps the stored prefix of the leaf it came from, and the half that receives an inserted key below every key of the tree stores 0 (rewritten 2026-11-21 from r-q17-growth, see Checked further; the earlier claim, "stores the common prefix of the entries it receives", is contradicted). In r-q7 the halves store 3 bytes (`7f 80 00`) for LONG keys 1–1200, ascending or descending, and 6 bytes for TEXT keys `abcd0001`–`abcd0700`, because the full root was first rewritten under that common prefix.
  - The prefix is not recomputed later: after deletes leave a split leaf with entries that share 4 bytes, it still stores 3.
  - A key beyond the last leaf's range does not shorten that leaf's prefix. ACE starts a new rightmost leaf holding the one entry, with prefix 0 (`INSERT` of k = 100000 after 1..1200).
  - This explains NativeLeafEncodingTests' 2,316 native pages whose stored prefix is shorter than their common prefix: roots, and split leaves that have since lost entries.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB (General) created by DAO, one table and one index per question, LONG primary keys and non-unique TEXT(20) indexes; pages read with `calcdb-oracle inspect --index-pages`, and the index pages' bytes kept inline in each record (NativeRead with Keep).
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q7-index-prefix/` (14 questions: text-ascending, text-descending, text-one, text-two, text-deleted-to-one, text-index-after-rows, text-no-common-prefix, text-ascending-compacted, long-ascending, long-split, long-split-descending, long-split-delete-narrow, long-split-insert-wider, text-split). Discriminators: insertion order, entry count, deletes before and after a split, index built before and after the rows, key type, compaction, an insert past the last leaf.
- **Open**: an insert below a split leaf's first entry that shares fewer bytes than its prefix (with an existing leaf to the left); a second-level split (intermediate pages under the root); where ACE splits (602 and 598 entries for LONG keys, 401 and 299 for the text keys); whether an intermediate page that is not the root stores a prefix.
- **Checked further (2026-11-21, r-q17-growth; contradicts the second bullet, which this reopens).** Records `evidence/r-q17-growth/split-inner-below/`, `split-inner-middle/` (statements in the exclusive CreateDatabase session; reads inside it failed with sharing violation 32, so only the pages after Close count), their `-sync` repeats (a shared session under `SetOption(dbImplicitCommitSync, "Yes")`, the first 28 bytes of pages 44 and 48-55 after every statement), `split-long-descending-sync` and `split-ix-mid-insert-302`. Both routes give the same index pages after Close. long-split-descending-sync also gives the same structure as r-q7 long-split-descending, and split-ix-mid-insert-302 gives r-fmt-2b ix-mid-insert-at-602's after-hash (c8cc0170).
  - **A split leaf does not store its entries' common prefix.** Each half keeps the full leaf's stored prefix, except one case: the half whose first entry is the inserted key, when that key is below every key of the tree, stores 0.
    - inner-below: leaf 48 stored 2 while its 516 keys shared 3 bytes. After the split, 48 (259 entries, free 1285) stores 0 and new page 51 (258 entries, free 1808) stores 2.
    - inner-middle: 48 (258 entries, free 1808) and 51 (259 entries, free 1801) both store 2.
    - ix-mid: the full root stored 3. Its halves 122 (keys 1-301) and 123 (keys 302-603, the new key first) both store 3.
    - long-descending: at key 598 and again at key 297, the left half 48 (302 entries, free 898) stores 0 and the right half (49, later 51; 301 entries, free 1807) stores 3.
    - The earlier records (3 for LONG, 6 for TEXT) are cases where the stored prefix already equalled the common one.
  - **Rewrite when full.** A leaf whose stored prefix is 0 is rewritten under its entries' common prefix when it fills, then splits only when that is full: the root at its 402nd entry (key 799: prefix 3, free 1201), and leaf 48 after the first descending split (prefix 0 at key 598; prefix 3 with 602 entries and 1 byte free at key 298). A leaf with prefix 2 whose entries share 3 bytes is not rewritten: in both inner probes leaf 48 is full at 516 entries (prefix 2, free 2) after fill insert 216 and splits at the 217th, where 602 entries would fit under prefix 3.
  - **Split point.** The n entries of the full leaf are halved, n/2 each (n = 602 or 516 here), and the new entry joins the half its key falls in: 302/301 (descending, new key in the left half), 301/302 (ix-mid, key 302 in the right half), 259/258 (inner-below), 258/259 (inner-middle). An append past the last leaf's last key is the exception already recorded: a new rightmost leaf holds the one entry.
  - **Pages.**
    - A root split keeps the root page as the intermediate. The halves go to two newly taken pages, the lower half on the lower page: ix-mid 122 (the free page inside the file) and 123 (new; the file grows 4096 bytes), long-descending 48 and 49. ix-mid changes pages 0 (3586 +2), 1 (4047 `fc` > `f0`), 68 (bytes 16 and 67 `5a` > `5b`), 69 (3909 `00` > `0c`), 70, 73 (the row), 122 and 123 only.
    - A leaf split keeps the lower half on the old page and links a new page for the upper half after it: 48's next (bytes 16-19) goes from 49 to 51, and 49's prev (bytes 12-15) from 48 to 51. The parent gains one entry (free 3603 > 3590).
  - **Open.**
    - Answered by r-q19-growth-open (the index-split notes further below): the prefix-0 half is the half whose first entry is the inserted key, also in a leaf that is not leftmost; an odd n gives the lower half floor(n/2); a key the stored prefix does not cover splits the leaf at once.
    - TEXT keys in these shapes; a split of an intermediate page (one observation, split-text-deep-descending).

### R-FMT-2 Overflow / deletion, Map ownership and growth (W-05): a grown row that no longer fits moves to the first FreeMap page with room, else to the lowest free page; its root becomes a 4-byte locator
- **Claim**: When an UPDATE grows a row past its page's free space, ACE keeps a 4-byte root on the page and writes the whole grown row elsewhere:
  - **Target choice.** ACE walks the table's FreeMap (slot 1 of its usage-map page) in ascending page order. It clears the bit of each page it finds without room and takes the first page with room. With none, it takes the lowest free page of the global map (page 1), even when that page belonged to another table. With no free page inside the file, the file grows by one page.
  - **Root page.** The row's slot becomes `0x4000 | (old offset + old length - 4)`: the row's last 4 bytes hold the locator, target slot (1 byte) then target page (3 bytes LE). The rows stored below it (lower offsets) move up by old length - 4, and their slot offsets rise by that amount. The vacated bytes at the bottom keep their old content. The free-space word (bytes 2-3) rises by old length - 4. Nothing else on the page changes.
  - **Target on a page of the table.** A new slot is appended (deleted slots are not reused), word `0x8000 | offset`, deleted flag only. The row sits directly below the page's lowest row, and the free word falls by length + 2. The maps and page 1 are not changed. Bits the walk did not reach stay set.
  - **Target on a free page, reused or new.** The whole page is rewritten: `01 01`, the free word, the owning TDEF page (4 bytes LE), 4 zero bytes, slot count 1, slot 0 `0x8000 | (4096 - length)`, the row at the end of the page. Every other byte is zero, and a reused page's old rows are erased. The page is set in the table's owned map (slot 0) and cleared in page 1, and it is set in the table's FreeMap (slot 1) when room is left on it: the targets here left 2,956 and 3,016 bytes free and joined it, while ACE's own overflow page 54 of W in `evidence/r-cplx-rebuild/create-rb-rich-accdb`, with 1,057 bytes free, is not in W's FreeMap (the threshold between them is open). The previous owner's pages are not touched.
  - **Page 0.** Byte 3586 counts +1 for the statement, and +1 more for the session's Close commit when page 1 changed (a free page was taken), as the extent note below says. Page 0 changes at no other byte.
- **Byte values** (fixture r-fmt-2b-w-accdb, 4f42d3fa: Pg rows 1-4 on page 55, 1004 bytes each, 58 bytes free; Pg's maps on page 53, owned {55, 56, 57}, FreeMap {57}; page 122 the only free page inside the 123-page file; T1 of row 2 grown by 30 characters, so the row is 1064 bytes):
  - Page 55: bytes 2-3 `3a 00` > `22 04` (1058). Slot 1 (bytes 16-17) `0828` > `4c10`, slot 2 `043c` > `0824`, slot 3 `0050` > `0438`. Locator `00 7a 00 00` at 3088. Rows 3 and 4 now lie at 2084 and 1080, and 80-1079 keep old bytes.
  - Page 122: `01 01 c8 0b 34 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 01 00 d8 8b`, the row at 3032-4095, the rest zero.
  - Page 53: owned map +122 (byte 4047 `00` > `04`). FreeMap {57} > {122}: byte 3970 `02` > `00` (57 had 58 bytes free, no room), 3978 `00` > `04`.
  - Page 1: byte 4047 `fc` > `f8`. Page 0: byte 3586 `00` > `02`.
  - Changed pages: 0, 1, 53, 55 and 122 only. The file's hash, 6f22c95a, equals r-fmt-2b pg-grow-row2-plus-30's, so this is the complete after-image.
  - Row 2 grown by 60 characters (1124 bytes) gives the same page 55, and page 122 with free `8c 0b` and slot `9c 8b`. Row 4 (slot 3, the lowest row) moves no other row: slot 3 becomes `4438`, the locator sits at 1080, and 80-1079 keep old bytes.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, DAO `Database.Execute` UPDATE with dbFailOnError, shared sessions closed before the pages were read. The before-state was set up by earlier sessions in the same question. The FreeMap rows are type-0 bitmaps starting at page 0.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q16-overflow/` (9 questions, every page's hash after each session, pages 0 3584-3591, 1, 52-57, 106-110 and 119-125 kept inline):
  - grow-row2-plus-30, grow-row2-plus-60, grow-row4-plus-60 repeat r-fmt-2b's after-hashes exactly (6f22c95a, df7b3622, b5db2da5).
  - Discriminators of the target choice:
    - no-free-grow-row2-plus-30: an earlier session's insert into Au4 takes page 122. The target is new page 123, the file is 507904 bytes, and page 1 4047 becomes `f0`.
    - lower-free-grow-row2-plus-30: an earlier DELETE FROM Au1 frees page 110. The target is 110, and 122 stays free.
    - reserved-grow-row2-plus-30: the insert in the same session. Every kept page equals no-free's, and only the counter differs: 3 for the one session against 4 for two.
  - Discriminators of the FreeMap walk:
    - room-last: rows 11 and 12 deleted, so 57 has 2066 bytes free. The target is 57 slot 4 (`8400`, locator `04 39 00 00`), and the FreeMap stays {57}.
    - room-middle: rows 5 and 6 deleted, so 56 has room. The target is 56 slot 4, and the FreeMap stays {56, 57} although 57 has no room. This rules out a reset of the FreeMap to the target.
    - noroom-middle-room-last: rows 5, 11 and 12 deleted, so 56 has 1062 bytes free, no room for 1064. The target is 57, and 56 is cleared from the FreeMap.
  - Every target page, reused 122 or 110 or new 123, is byte for byte the same.
- **Open**:
  - The room test's exact bound, with or without the 2-byte slot: 1062 against 1064 does not decide it.
  - The free space below which a new target page stays out of the FreeMap (between 1,057 and 2,956 bytes; see Target on a free page).
  - Whether the walk starts at the FreeMap's first page or at the root page.
  - "Lowest free page" and "first free page of the session's extent" coincide here, because the extent starts at the byte of the first free page.
  - A page freed earlier in the same session.
  - A row that is already an overflow row growing again, or shrinking back.
  - DAO recordset Edit/Update instead of SQL UPDATE.
  - MDB (r-fmt-2b mdb-pg-grow-row2-plus-30).
  - The extent note's Open on free pages inside the file is answered for the net result only: a session that takes page 110 leaves 122 free after Close. Page 1 was not read inside the session.
- **Checked further (2026-11-21, r-q17-growth: the FreeMap threshold, Open item 2).** Records `evidence/r-q17-growth/freemap-free-*/` (16 questions): one UPDATE grows Pg row 2 to L bytes, T1..T8 filled with U+20AC in turn, so that the overflow target, free page 122, keeps F = 4080 - L bytes free. Everything else matches the grow-row2 rule above: 57 is cleared from the FreeMap (page 53 byte 3970 `02` > `00`), 122 is set owned (4047) and cleared in page 1 (4047 `fc` > `f8`), and page 122's slot is `0x8000 | (4096 - L)`.
  - 122 joins the FreeMap (page 53 byte 3978 `00` > `04`) at F = 2042 (L = 2038) and at every larger F asked: 2044, 2046, 2048, 2050, 2060, 2100, 2250 and 2500.
  - 122 stays out at F = 2040 (L = 2040) and at every smaller F asked: 2038, 2036, 2020, 2000, 1500 and 1250.
  - So a new target page holding one row joins the FreeMap exactly when its free space exceeds 2,040 bytes, which here is the same as exceeding the row's own length.
  - Open: a fixed bound and a bound relative to the row cannot be told apart while one row sits on a fresh page (F = 4080 - L). The same bound applies to a page an INSERT takes (r-q19-growth-open insert-free-*: an INSERT of a Pg row that leaves free page 122 with 2,042 or 3,080 bytes free sets it in the FreeMap, with 2,040 or 1,080 it stays out; 57 is cleared as above).

### R-FMT-2 Overflow / deletion (W-05): a row made smaller is rewritten in place, ending where it ended, and the rows below it move up
- **Claim**: An UPDATE that shrinks a variable-width row by d bytes rewrites it on its own page:
  - The row now starts at old offset + d and ends where it ended before.
  - The rows stored below it (lower offsets) move up by d, and their slot offsets rise by d.
  - The free-space word (bytes 2-3) rises by d. The vacated d bytes at the bottom keep their old content. The slot's flags do not change.
  - Page 0 byte 3586 counts +1. The maps and page 1 do not change.
  - This is the same shape as the root of a row that moves out (the overflow note above, with d = old length - 4).
- **Byte values** (fixture r-fmt-2b-w-accdb; T1 set to 31 U+20AC, so d = 60):
  - Row 2 (shrink-row2-minus-30): page 55 slot 1 `0828` > `0864`, slot 2 `043c` > `0478`, slot 3 `0050` > `008c`; bytes 2-3 `3a 00` > `76 00` (58 > 118). Rows 3 and 4 now lie at 1144 and 140, and bytes 80-139 keep their old bytes.
  - Row 4, the lowest row (shrink-row4-minus-30): only slot 3 changes, `0050` > `008c`, and the free word as above.
  - Of the kept pages (0 bytes 3584-3591, pages 1, 53 and 55), only page 0 byte 3586 (`00` > `01`) and page 55 change.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, DAO `Execute` UPDATE with dbFailOnError, a shared session closed before the pages are read.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q17-growth/shrink-row2-minus-30/`, `shrink-row4-minus-30/`. Discriminator: the row that has rows below it against the lowest row.
- **Open**: an overflow row that shrinks back; a shrink by a recordset Edit; MDB.

### R-FMT-2 Overflow / deletion and Map ownership (W-05): DELETE without WHERE releases every data page unwritten; a row-by-row delete releases an emptied data page (type 01 > 09) only when the table's FreeMap holds a lower page
- **Claim**:
  - **DELETE without WHERE.** `DELETE FROM T` releases the table's data pages without writing them: their rows and slots stay as they were (multi-page, multi-index and long-value tables are covered in the DELETE-without-WHERE note below).
    - In the statement: each page's bits are cleared in the table's owned map and FreeMap, the TableDef's row count and its index counters drop to 0, and the index leaves are emptied.
    - At `Database.Close`: page 1 marks the released pages free, with the Close commit (`page 0` byte 3586 `+1`).
  - **Row-by-row delete (`DELETE ... WHERE` and DAO dynaset `Delete`).** Each deleted row is removed and the page is compacted (see the deleted-slot note below), raising its free word to `4096 - 14 - 2 * slots` with every slot `0xd000` once empty. When the last live row of data page `P` is deleted, ACE releases `P` if and only if the table's FreeMap (slot 1 of its usage-map page) already has a page strictly below `P` (`Q < P`):
    - **Released (`Q < P` in FreeMap).** `P`'s type byte goes from `01` to `09` (with its compacted `0xd000` slots and free word written), and `P` is cleared in the table's owned map and FreeMap during the statement (or the dynaset `Delete`'s write-behind commit). Page 1 marks `P` free at `Database.Close` (`page 0` byte 3586 `+1`), not during the statement or at `Recordset.Close`. `Q` need not be empty (3 rows / 1,062 bytes free on `Pg` page 55, or 92 rows on `L` page 47, suffice), need not be the table's first data page (`L` page 47 with 45 and 46 full), and may have joined FreeMap in an earlier session.
    - **Kept owned (no `Q < P` in FreeMap).** `P` stays owned with type byte `01`, joins FreeMap if not already in it, and page 1 is untouched (`Database.Close` writes nothing). Higher pages `Q > P` in FreeMap — even already-empty ones — do not cause `P` to be released. Consequently, deleting all rows of a three-page table (`Pg`, pages 55-57) ascending (`WHERE ID > 0`, or dynaset `ORDER BY ID`) keeps 55 and releases 56 and 57, while deleting them descending (dynaset `ORDER BY ID DESC`) releases none and leaves all three pages owned and in FreeMap.
  - **Counters.** The TableDef's row count follows the delete (for a dynaset, written at `Recordset.Close` as its own commit). Its index DistinctCount word is unchanged by row-by-row deletes.
- **Byte values**:
  - Single-page table `Au1` (fixture `r-fmt-2b-w-accdb`: tdef 107, maps 108, PK root 109, ten rows of 21-23 bytes on data page 110):
    - `DELETE FROM Au1` (`retire-delete-au1`, after-hash `5f2c29b3`): page 107 bytes 16 and 67 `0a > 00`; page 108 bytes 4045 (owned) and 3976 (FreeMap) `40 > 00`; page 109 bytes 2-3 `c6 0d > 20 0e` and bytes 28-38 zeroed; page 1 byte 4045 `00 > 40`; page 0 byte 3586 `00 > 02`; page 110 unchanged. Read open before Close (`retire-delete-au1-read-open`): same except page 1 unchanged and 3586 `01`; Close writes page 1 and `02`.
    - `WHERE ID > 0` (`retire-delete-au1-where-all`), nine rows then `WHERE ID = 10` (`retire-delete-au1-nine-then-last`), and ten dynaset Deletes (`retire-rs-delete-au1`): page 110 stays type `01`, free `de 0f` (4062), slots 14-33 all `00 d0`; page 107 byte 16 `0a > 00` (byte 67 unchanged); pages 108 and 1 unchanged. In `retire-rs-delete-au1`, each Delete is one commit (3586 `01..0a`), `Recordset.Close` writes page 107 byte 16 (`0b`), and `Database.Close` writes nothing.
  - Three-page table `Pg` (fixture `r-fmt-2b-w-accdb`: tdef 52, maps 53 owned `{55, 56, 57}` at bytes 4038 `80`, 4039 `03` and FreeMap `{57}` at bytes 3969 `00`, 3970 `02`; PK root 54; data pages 55-57 each holding 4 × 1004-byte rows, free `3a 00` = 58):
    - `where-empty-first` (`ID <= 4`): page 55 stays `01`, free `ea 0f` (4074), slots `00 d0` × 4; page 53 FreeMap byte 3969 `00 > 80` (`{55, 57}`); owned and page 1 unchanged; page 0 byte 3586 `00 > 01`.
    - `where-empty-middle` (`ID >= 5 AND ID <= 8`): page 56 stays `01`, free `ea 0f`, slots `00 d0` × 4; page 53 FreeMap byte 3970 `02 > 03` (`{56, 57}`); owned and page 1 unchanged; 3586 `00 > 01`.
    - `where-empty-last` and `where-empty-last-read-open` (`ID >= 9`): page 57 stays `01`, free `ea 0f`, slots `00 d0` × 4; page 53 and page 1 unchanged; 3586 `00 > 01`. In `where-empty-last-read-open`, open (after 1.5 s) and post-Close images are identical (`dca4a342bf24`).
    - `where-empty-all` (`ID > 0`): page 55 stays `01` (`ea 0f`, `00 d0` × 4); pages 56 and 57 type `01 > 09` (`ea 0f`, `00 d0` × 4); page 53 FreeMap bytes 3969-3970 `00 02 > 80 00` (`{55}`), owned byte 4039 `03 > 00` (`{55}`); page 1 byte 4039 `00 > 03`; page 0 byte 3586 `00 > 02`.
    - `where-empty-last-after-partial-first` (session 1 `ID = 1`: page 55 free `26 04` = 1,062, FreeMap byte 3969 `00 > 80`, 3586 `01`; session 2 `ID >= 9`) and `where-empty-last-after-empty-first` (session 1 `ID <= 4`: page 55 empty, FreeMap `{55, 57}`; session 2 `ID >= 9`): in session 2, page 57 type `01 > 09`, page 53 FreeMap byte 3970 `02 > 00` (`{55}`), owned byte 4039 `03 > 01` (`{55, 56}`), page 1 byte 4039 `00 > 02`, 3586 `01 > 03`.
    - `dynaset-empty-all-asc` (`SELECT * FROM Pg ORDER BY ID`, 12 Deletes, read after 4, 8, 12, `rs.Close` and `db.Close`): after Delete 4, page 55 stays `01` and joins FreeMap (`3969: 00 > 80`, 3586 `01`); after Delete 8, page 56 is `01 > 09` and cleared from owned (`4039: 03 > 02`, 3586 `02`, page 1 unchanged); after Delete 12, page 57 is `01 > 09` and cleared from owned and FreeMap (`4039: 02 > 00`, `3970: 02 > 00`, 3586 `03`, page 1 unchanged); after `rs.Close`, page 52 byte 16 `0c > 00` (3586 `04`, page 1 unchanged); after `db.Close`, page 1 byte 4039 `00 > 03` (3586 `05`).
    - `dynaset-empty-all-desc` (`SELECT * FROM Pg ORDER BY ID DESC`): after Deletes 4, 8 and 12, pages 57, 56 and 55 each stay `01`, with FreeMap becoming `{57}`, `{56, 57}` and `{55, 56, 57}` (bytes 3969-3970 `00 02 > 80 03`); `rs.Close` writes page 52 byte 16 `0c > 00` (3586 `04`); `db.Close` writes nothing and page 1 is unchanged.
  - Five-page table `L` (`r-q19-growth-open`, owned `{45, 46, 47, 50, 51}`): `split-empty-right-leaf` (`k > 602`) leaves 92 rows on page 47 (adding 47 to FreeMap) and releases 50 and 51 (`01 > 09`, out of maps, freed in page 1); `split-empty-left-leaf` (`k <= 602`) keeps 45 (`01`, added to FreeMap) and releases 46 (`01 > 09`, out of maps, freed in page 1).
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, shared DAO sessions; SQL with dbFailOnError; dynaset `Delete` then `MoveNext`; pages 0, 1, TableDef, maps, PK root and data pages kept before and after (and every page hashed after Close).
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q20-delete-pages/where-empty-first/`, `where-empty-middle/`, `where-empty-last/`, `where-empty-last-read-open/`, `where-empty-all/`, `where-empty-last-after-partial-first/`, `where-empty-last-after-empty-first/`, `dynaset-empty-all-asc/`, `dynaset-empty-all-desc/`; `evidence/r-q19-growth-open/split-empty-right-leaf/`, `split-empty-left-leaf/`; `evidence/r-q17-growth/retire-delete-au1/`, `retire-delete-au1-read-open/`, `retire-delete-au1-nine-then-last/`, `retire-delete-au1-where-all/`, `retire-rs-delete-au1/`; `evidence/r-q16-overflow/lower-free-grow-row2-plus-30/`. Discriminators: WHERE-less against WHERE; emptying first, middle, last or all pages; ascending against descending dynaset order (higher empty FreeMap pages do not trigger release); cross-session partial (1,062 free) and empty (4,074 free) lower FreeMap page against full lower pages; non-first lower FreeMap page (`L` page 47); open read against Close.
- **Open**:
  - The free-space threshold at which a partial delete adds a data page to FreeMap (seen: 1,062 bytes free joins in `slot-delete-bottom`; 118 bytes free after a 60-byte shrink stays out in `shrink-row2-minus-30` and `slot-delete-middle-after-shrink`).
  - Emptying a data page when FreeMap is initially empty (expected to stay owned by the same rule, not directly asked).
  - Whether a later INSERT or Compact reuses or frees an empty owned page; table-type recordset Deletes emptying a multi-page table; MDB.

### R-FMT-2 Overflow / deletion (W-05): a deleted row's slot word is 0xc000 | (offset + length), and every later slot shifts up by its length
- **Claim**: When a row at slot `i` (byte offset `o`, byte length `len`, ending at `end = o + len`) is deleted on a plain data page (no long values):
  - **Deleted slot word.** Slot `i` becomes `0xc000 | end` (`0x8000` deleted | `0x4000` unused/empty | 13-bit end offset in `0..4096`). For the top row (`end = 4096 = 0x1000`), this is `0xc000 | 0x1000 = 0xd000` (so `0xd000` carries no `0x5000` flag: bit 12 is the offset `4096`).
  - **Compaction and slot shift.** Every live row stored below slot `i` (slots `j > i`) moves up by `len` bytes, and **every** later slot `j > i` — live and already-deleted alike — has its offset raised by `len` (`slot[j] += len`, preserving `0xc000` on deleted slots). The free word (bytes 2-3) rises by `len`, and the vacated bottom bytes keep their old content.
  - **Order independence and length invariant.** Every slot `k` (live or deleted) keeps the upper boundary of slot `k + 1`, and a deleted slot spans length 0 (`(slot[k - 1] & 0x1fff) - (slot[k] & 0x1fff) == 0`, with virtual slot `-1` at `0x1000`). Deleting the same rows in ascending or descending order yields identical slot words (and all `0xd000` once empty; only the vacated bottom bytes reflect which live rows moved).
  - **After an earlier shrink.** Shrinking row 1 compacts rows below it upward without leaving a gap; deleting row 2 below the shorter row 1 writes `0xc000 | (o_2 + len_2)` = `0xc000 | o_1`.
- **Byte values** (fixture `r-fmt-2b-w-accdb`, `Pg` page 55: initially free `3a 00` = 58, slots 0-3 `14 0c 28 08 3c 04 50 00` = `0x0c14`, `0x0828`, `0x043c`, `0x0050`, each row 1,004 = `0x03ec` bytes):
  - `slot-delete-top` (`ID = 1`, slot 0): free `26 04` (1,062), slots `00 d0 14 0c 28 08 3c 04` (`0xd000 = 0xc000 | 0x1000`); page 53 FreeMap byte 3969 `00 > 80`.
  - `slot-delete-middle` (`ID = 2`, slot 1): free `26 04`, slots `14 0c 14 cc 28 08 3c 04` (`0xcc14 = 0xc000 | 0x0c14`).
  - `slot-delete-bottom` (`ID = 4`, slot 3): free `26 04`, slots `14 0c 28 08 3c 04 3c c4` (`0xc43c = 0xc000 | 0x043c`); no row bytes move.
  - `slot-delete-two-adjacent-asc` (`ID = 2 OR ID = 3`): free `12 08` (2,066), slots `14 0c 14 cc 14 cc 28 08`.
  - `slot-delete-two-adjacent-desc`: session 1 (`ID = 3`) gives free `26 04`, slots `14 0c 28 08 28 c8 3c 04` (`0xc828 = 0xc000 | 0x0828`); session 2 (`ID = 2`) gives free `12 08`, slots `14 0c 14 cc 14 cc 28 08` (already-deleted slot 2 shifts `28 c8 > 14 cc`, `+0x03ec`).
  - `slot-delete-middle-then-top`: session 1 (`ID = 2`) gives slots `14 0c 14 cc 28 08 3c 04`; session 2 (`ID = 1`) gives free `12 08`, slots `00 d0 00 d0 14 0c 28 08` (already-deleted slot 1 shifts `14 cc > 00 d0`, `0xcc14 + 0x03ec = 0xd000`).
  - `slot-delete-middle-after-shrink`: session 1 (`UPDATE Pg SET T1 = 31 U+20AC WHERE ID = 1`, -60 bytes to 944) gives free `76 00` (118), slots `50 0c 64 08 78 04 8c 00` (no gap, FreeMap unchanged); session 2 (`ID = 2`, 1,004 bytes) gives free `62 04` (1,122), slots `50 0c 50 cc 64 08 78 04` (`0xcc50 = 0xc000 | 0x0c50`), FreeMap `+55`.
  - Consistent earlier records: `r-q19-growth-open/lv-op-delete-row-chain` (slot 2 `b5 0f` length 25 > `ce cf` = `0xc000 | 0x0fce`), `r-q16-overflow/noroom-middle-room-last-grow-row2-plus-30` (page 57 slots 2-3 `3c 04 50 00 > 28 c8 28 c8`), and `dynaset-empty-all-desc` (bottom-up deletes shift every slot to `00 d0` without moving any row body bytes).
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, shared DAO sessions, SQL `DELETE ... WHERE` (and `UPDATE` in session 1 of `-after-shrink`) with dbFailOnError, plain rows without long values, pages 0, 1, 52-57 kept before and after.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q20-delete-pages/slot-delete-top/`, `slot-delete-middle/`, `slot-delete-bottom/`, `slot-delete-two-adjacent-asc/`, `slot-delete-two-adjacent-desc/`, `slot-delete-middle-then-top/`, `slot-delete-middle-after-shrink/`, `dynaset-empty-all-desc/`; `evidence/r-q19-growth-open/lv-op-delete-row-chain/`; `evidence/r-q16-overflow/noroom-middle-room-last-grow-row2-plus-30/`. Discriminators: top, middle and bottom positions; two adjacent rows ascending against descending (proving already-deleted slots shift); middle then top (proving `0xd000` is `0xc000 | 0x1000`); unequal row lengths after shrink.
- **Open**:
  - Deleting a row on a page that actually holds a non-contiguous gap (no recorded ACE write produces one: shrinking, overflowing and deleting all compact lower rows upward).
  - Deleting an overflow root slot (`0x4000 | offset`) or overflow target slot (`0x8000 | offset`); deleting a non-top row on an LVAL page; MDB.

### R-FMT-2 LV form selection and Map ownership (W-05): in a new file, values up to 2,000 bytes share one LVAL page as single rows, and 4,076 and 5,000 bytes are chained over pages of their own
- **Claim**: For a Memo and a Long Binary column alike (byte-identical layouts for the same sizes), each value added by its own session:
  - **Placement.**
    - The first value takes a new LVAL page (45) before the row's data page (46).
    - Values of 100 and 2,000 bytes are single rows on that page, none inline: slot 0 at 3996 (length 100) and slot 1 at 1996 (length 2000), 1978 bytes left free.
    - Values of 4,076 and 5,000 bytes are chained on newly taken pages, not on page 45. 4076 takes 47 and 48: on 47 a row of 4076 at offset 20, the next locator `00 30 00 00` followed by 4072 bytes; on 48 a row of 8 at 4088, a zero locator followed by 4 bytes. 5000 takes 49 and 50: on 49 a row of 4076 linking to 50; on 50 a row of 932 (4 + 928).
  - **Row reference** (row bytes 6-17, after the column count and the ID):
    - Length word: `64 00 00 40` and `d0 07 00 40` (0x40000000 | length) for the single rows; `ec 0f 00 00` and `88 13 00 00` (no flag) for the chained values.
    - Locator: slot, then page in 3 bytes: `00 2d 00 00`, `01 2d 00 00`, `00 2f 00 00`, `00 31 00 00`.
    - The +8 word: 0 for a single-row value. For a chained value it is the millisecond tick stamp, equal to bytes 8-11 of its first LVAL page. Continuation pages and page 45 hold 0 there.
  - **LVAL page header.** `01 01`, the free word, `4c 56 41 4c` ("LVAL") at 4-7, the stamp at 8-11, the row count at 12-13, the slots from 14 (no flag bits).
  - **Maps.**
    - Page 43 holds five 69-byte usage-map rows (slots 4027, 3958, 3889, 3820, 3751): the table's owned map and FreeMap, the index's map, and V's long-value owned and free maps.
    - The TableDef (page 42) names them: 0x38-0x3f `00 2b 00 00` and `01 2b 00 00`; 0xa9 `02 2b 00 00` (then first index page 44); for the column, bytes 0xe1-0xec are `01 00`, `03 2b 00 00` (owned), `04 2b 00 00` (free), `ff ff`.
    - The long-value owned map gains 45 at the first insert, 47 and 48 at the third, and 49 and 50 at the fourth (byte 3830 `20` > `a0 01` > `a0 07`).
    - The long-value free map holds only 45 throughout (byte 3761 `20`), even though chain pages 48 and 50 keep 4072 and 3148 bytes free.
    - Data page 46 is set in the table's owned map and FreeMap (bytes 4037 and 3968 `40`).
    - TableDef bytes 16 and 67 count 1 to 4.
  - **Page 0.** Byte 3586 counts +2 for a session that took pages (the statement and the Close commit) and +1 for the second insert, which took none.
- **Repeat.** lval-memo a1 and a2 differ, over all kept bytes (page 0 bytes 3584-3591 and pages 42-53 at five points), only in the two chained stamps: the row words and first-page words `ac d7 2a 13` and `cb d7 2a 13` in a1, `a4 18 30 13` and `c3 18 30 13` in a2. Within a run the two stamps are 31 ms apart.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB made by CreateDatabase (dbLangGeneral, dbVersion120); `CREATE TABLE V (ID LONG PRIMARY KEY, V MEMO | LONGBINARY)`; each value added by a table recordset AddNew/Update in its own shared session; pages 42-53 read after each Close. The Memo values are U+20AC text, 2 bytes per character, not compressed.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q17-growth/lval-memo/` (a1, a2), `lval-ole/`. FieldSize reads back 100, 2000, 4076 and 5000. Discriminators: size varied with type fixed; Memo against OLE; a repeated run.
- **Open**:
  - Answered by r-q19-growth-open and r-q22-lv-open (the notes below): the single-row bound (3,816 bytes, also for Memo and with an existing LVAL page), compressed Memo (single-row only), 3- and 4-page chains, when a single row takes a new page and how two LV free-map pages are chosen, and replaced or deleted values including page-1 free timing.
  - MDB.

### R-FMT-2 LV form selection (W-05): a Memo or OLE value of up to 64 bytes is stored in the row unless the row would exceed 4,060 bytes, and from 65 bytes on an LVAL page; the 64-byte bound is per column
- **Claim**: In a new file, a Memo or Long Binary value of n bytes, n at most 64, lies in the data row when it fits:
  - The 12-byte reference has length word n | 0x80000000 (`01 00 00 80` for 1 byte, `40 00 00 80` for 64), then 8 zero bytes, then the n bytes. The row is 25 + n bytes (26 to 89 here).
  - At 65 bytes and more the value is a single LVAL row. The row is 25 bytes, with length word n | 0x40000000 and the locator of slot 0 on a new LVAL page (46, after data page 45, which the first insert took). That page is set in the LV owned and LV free maps (page 43 bytes 3830 and 3761 `40`).
  - Memo and Long Binary give the same rows. The bound is the catalog LvProp's (the CAT-P note below).
  - **Row-size ceiling (4,060 bytes).** A value of at most 64 bytes is pushed out to a single LVAL row when inlining it would make the data row exceed 4,060 bytes (leaving under 20 bytes free on a 4,096-byte data page). In `R (ID LONG PRIMARY KEY, T1..T8 TEXT(255), M MEMO)` with M = 60 bytes (30 U+20AC):
    - 3,958 bytes across T1..T8 gives a 4,060-byte inline row on data page 45 (`slot 0 = 36`, free 20, ref `3c 00 00 80`, no LVAL page taken).
    - 3,960 bytes across T1..T8 (4,062 bytes if inline) pushes M to a single LVAL row on page 45 (`slot 0 = 4036`, length 60, free 4,020, page 45 set in LV owned and LV free maps `3830` and `3761` `20`); the data row on page 46 is 4,002 bytes (`slot 0 = 94`, free 78, ref `3c 00 00 40 00 2d 00 00 00 00 00 00`).
  - **Per-column bound.** In `V (ID LONG PRIMARY KEY, M1 MEMO, M2 MEMO)` with 60 bytes in each Memo (sum 120 > 64), both values lie inline in the 159-byte row on data page 45 (`slot 0 = 3937`, free 3,921, both refs `3c 00 00 80`, no LVAL page taken).
- **Conditions**: ACCDB made by CreateDatabase, `V (ID LONG PRIMARY KEY, V MEMO | LONGBINARY)`, `R (ID LONG PRIMARY KEY, T1..T8 TEXT(255), M MEMO)`, or `V (ID LONG PRIMARY KEY, M1 MEMO, M2 MEMO)`, one value per shared session through a table recordset AddNew/Update, pages 42-53 read after each Close. Memo values are U+20AC text (2 bytes a character).
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q19-growth-open/lv-inline-memo/` (2, 50, 64, 66, 98 bytes), `lv-inline-ole/` (1, 50, 64, 65, 99); `evidence/r-q22-lv-open/lv-inline-tight-3958/`, `lv-inline-tight-3960/`, `lv-inline-two-memos-60/`. FieldSize reads back each size. Discriminators: 64 against 65 and 66; Memo against OLE; 4,060 against 4,062 inline row size; two 60-byte Memo columns summing to 120 > 64.
- **Open**: whether inline compressed Memo tests the 64-byte bound on uncompressed or compressed length; spill order when two `<= 64`-byte long-value columns together push the row over 4,060 bytes; MDB.

### R-FMT-2 LV form selection (W-05): a value of up to 3,816 bytes is one LVAL row; from 3,817 bytes it is chained, and one chain page holds up to 4,072 bytes (also for Memo, with an existing LVAL page, and across 3- and 4-page chains)
- **Claim**: Whether the column has no LVAL page yet or already has an LVAL page with room, and for Long Binary and Memo alike:
  - **n up to 3,816: a single row.** On a new LVAL page 45, the row of n bytes sits at 4096 - n. Its length word is n | 0x40000000, and both its +8 word and the page's stamp (bytes 8-11) are 0. The page is set in the LV free map (page 43 byte 3761 `20`), even with 264 bytes left (n = 3,816).
  - **n from 3,817: the chained form, even when the value fits one page or an existing LVAL page has room.** The length word is n with no flag. The +8 word is the tick stamp, equal to bytes 8-11 of the first LVAL page. Each chain page holds a row of 4 + chunk bytes: the 4-byte next-page locator (`00 page 00 00`, or `00 00 00 00` on the last page), then the chunk.
    - 3,817 to 4,072 bytes take one page; at 4,072 the 4,076-byte row starts at offset 20 and leaves 4 bytes free.
    - Multi-page chains link forward in ascending page order, 4,072 payload bytes per full page and the remainder on the last page, with the data page taken after the chain:
      - 4,074 bytes (2 pages): 45 (`4,076` at 20 -> 46) -> 46 (`6` at 4,090 -> 0); data page 47.
      - 10,000 bytes (3 pages, `lv-chain-3-pages`): 45 (`4,076` at 20, free 4 -> `00 2e 00 00`) -> 46 (`4,076` at 20, free 4 -> `00 2f 00 00`) -> 47 (`1,860` at 2,236, free 2,220 -> 0); data page 48. Only head page 45 holds the tick stamp; 46 and 47 hold 0. LV owned `3830`: `e0` ({45, 46, 47}).
      - 14,000 bytes (4 pages, `lv-chain-4-pages`): 45 -> 46 -> 47 (each `4,076` at 20, free 4) -> 48 (`1,788` at 2,308, free 2,292 -> 0); data page 49. Only head page 45 holds the tick stamp; 46-48 hold 0. LV owned `3830..3831`: `e0 01` ({45..48}).
    - A chain page is never set in the LV free map (byte 3761 unchanged).
  - **Existing LVAL page with room.** The 3,816 / 3,817 bound is fixed regardless of existing free space:
    - With page 45 holding 1,978 bytes free (after 100 and 2,000 bytes), 2,500 bytes takes new page 47 as a single LVAL row (`c4 09 00 40`, stamp 0, free 1,580, set in LV free map), while 3,900 bytes takes page 47 as a chain (`3c 0f 00 00`, tick stamp, free 176, not in LV free map).
    - With page 45 holding 3,980 bytes free (after 100 bytes — room for 3,900 + 2 = 3,902 bytes), 3,900 bytes still leaves page 45 untouched and takes new page 47 as a chain (`3c 0f 00 00`, tick stamp, not in LV free map).
  - **Memo and `MEMO WITH COMPRESSION`.**
    - Plain `MEMO` (uncompressed U+20AC, 2 bytes/char) obeys the same bound: 1,908 chars (3,816 bytes) is a single LVAL row (`e8 0e 00 40`, stamp 0, page 45 free 264, in LV free map); 1,909 chars (3,818 bytes) is chained (`ea 0e 00 00`, tick stamp, page 45 free 258, row 3,822, not in LV free map).
    - `MEMO WITH COMPRESSION` (created by ADO `CREATE TABLE`, TableDef column `ExtendedFlags = 0x01`, ASCII `'a'` written by DAO AddNew/Update): ACE tests the 3,816-byte single-row bound on the **uncompressed UTF-16 byte length** (`2 * chars`), and **compresses only single-row Memo values, never chained ones**:
      - 100 `'a'` (200 UTF-16 bytes) -> single LVAL row of 102 bytes (`ff fe` + 100 `'a'`, ref `66 00 00 40`, stamp 0, page 45 free 3,978, in LV free map; FieldSize 200), leaving stale uncompressed `61 00 ...` bytes at `0f38..0f99` below the compressed row (`0f9a..0fff`).
      - 1,908 `'a'` (3,816 UTF-16 bytes) -> single LVAL row of 1,910 bytes (`ff fe` + 1,908 `'a'`, ref `76 07 00 40`, stamp 0, page 45 free 2,170, in LV free map; FieldSize 3,816), leaving stale uncompressed `61 00 ...` bytes from `0118` (`4096 - 3816`) to `0889` below the compressed row (`088a..0fff`).
      - 1,909 `'a'` (3,818 UTF-16 bytes) -> **not compressed**, chained on page 45 (row 3,822 = 4 + 3,818 UTF-16LE bytes at `0112`, ref `ea 0e 00 00`, tick stamp, free 258, not in LV free map; FieldSize 3,818).
- **Conditions**: as the note above, ACCDB; `lv-memo-comp-*` uses ADO `Microsoft.ACE.OLEDB.12.0` for `CREATE TABLE ... MEMO WITH COMPRESSION` and DAO table recordset AddNew/Update for the insert.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q19-growth-open/lv-single-*` (single rows 2002..3816, chained 3817..4074); `evidence/r-q22-lv-open/lv-bound-room2000-2500/`, `lv-bound-room2000-3900/`, `lv-bound-room3980-3900/`, `lv-memo-bound-3816/`, `lv-memo-bound-3818/`, `lv-memo-comp-100/`, `lv-memo-comp-1908/`, `lv-memo-comp-1909/`, `lv-chain-3-pages/`, `lv-chain-4-pages/`. Discriminators: 3,816 against 3,817 (OLE) and 3,816 against 3,818 (plain Memo and compressed Memo); existing LVAL page with 1,978 or 3,980 free; 2-, 3- and 4-page chains.
- **Open**: `MEMO WITH COMPRESSION` inserted through ADO SQL/recordset instead of DAO; chains taking non-contiguous free pages from page 1; MDB.

### R-FMT-2 LV form selection and Map ownership (W-05): a single LVAL row goes to a free-map page with room for the row and its slot, else to a new page; with two free-map pages both having room, placement is neither pure lowest-page nor pure best-fit; a page with 178 or fewer bytes free leaves the LV free map
- **Claim**: Values of 100 and 2,000 bytes leave LVAL page 45 with 1,978 bytes free, in the LV free map. Then:
  - **Fit on one free-map page.**
    - **1,976 bytes** (with its 2-byte slot, exactly 1,978) land on page 45: slot 2, free word 0. Page 45 is cleared from the LV free map (page 43 byte 3761 `20` > `00`). The next value, 100 bytes, takes new page 47, set in the LV owned and free maps.
    - **1,978 and 2,000 bytes** do not fit with their slot. They take new page 47 (owned and free maps, 3830 and 3761 `20` > `a0`), and page 45 stays in the free map. With 2,000 bytes on page 47 (free 2,080), the next value of 100 bytes lands on page 45 (slot 2, free 1,978 > 1,876).
  - **Two LV free-map pages that both have room (lower page the looser fit).** With 100 and 2,000 bytes on page 45 (1,978 free) and 2,500 bytes on page 47 (1,580 free; LV free map `{45, 47}`, byte 3761 `a0`), varying only the 4th value's length in a fresh session (and confirmed in one shared session for 1,500 bytes):
    - **100 and 250 bytes** land on lower, looser **page 45** (slot 2, free `1,978` > `1,876` and `1,726`; page 47 stays at 1,580 free).
    - **400, 1,400 and 1,500 bytes** land on higher, tighter **page 47** (slot 1, free `1,580` > `1,178`, `178` and `78`; page 45 stays untouched at 1,978 free). `lv-two-free-lower-looser-same-session` (all four values in one session, 4th = 1,500) gives the same page-47 placement.
    - Thus the choice is neither pure lowest-page first-fit (400-1,500 skip page 45 for page 47, even when page 47 stays in the free map at 1,178 free) nor pure best-fit (100 and 250 pick looser page 45 over tighter page 47).
  - **Leaving the LV free map.** Page 47 is cleared from the LV free map (`3761`: `a0` > `20`) when left with **178** bytes free (`1,400`-byte insert), **78** bytes free (`1,500`-byte insert) or **0** bytes free (`1,976`-byte insert), and stays in the LV free map when left with **264** bytes free (`3,816`-byte single row).
  - Page 0 byte 3586 counts +2 for a session that took a page and +1 for one that did not.
- **Conditions**: as the notes above (Long Binary, one value per session, plus one all-in-one-session control).
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q19-growth-open/lv-fill-2000-1976/`, `lv-fill-2000-1978/`, `lv-fill-2000-2000/`; `evidence/r-q22-lv-open/lv-two-free-lower-looser-100/`, `lv-two-free-lower-looser-250/`, `lv-two-free-lower-looser-400/`, `lv-two-free-lower-looser-1400/`, `lv-two-free-lower-looser/`, `lv-two-free-lower-looser-same-session/`. Discriminators: an exact fit with the slot, a fit without the slot, no fit; 4th value length 100 and 250 against 400, 1,400 and 1,500 on identical two-page state (1,978 free on lower page 45, 1,580 on higher page 47); fresh session against same session; remaining free 178 against 264.
- **Open**: the exact rule switching between lower page 45 and tighter page 47 between 250 and 400 bytes (and with three or more LV free-map pages); the exact free-space threshold between 178 and 264 bytes below which a single-row page leaves the LV free map; MDB.

### R-FMT-2 LV form selection and Overflow / deletion (W-05): a replaced long value is written anew; an old single row is removed from its page, an old chain's pages are freed in page 1 during the statement without being written, and Null stores no reference
- **Before-state** (one CreateDatabase session, three SQL INSERTs into `V (ID LONG PRIMARY KEY, V MEMO)`):
  - Rows 1 and 2 hold 100 and 2,000 bytes as single rows on LVAL page 45: slot 0 at 3996, slot 1 at 1996, 1,978 bytes free.
  - Row 3 holds 5,000 bytes chained on pages 47 and 48. Data page 46 holds the three rows.
  - The LV owned map holds {45, 47, 48}, the LV free map {45}.
- **Claim**: One UPDATE or DELETE in a shared session:
  - **The new value is placed first, then the old one is removed** (corrected 2026-11-21: the page bytes show it). In `lv-op-single-larger` the new 200-byte row is written at 1796..1995, below the page's lowest row, and stays there as stale bytes after the old row's removal moves it up by 100 to 1896..2095; in `lv-op-single-smaller` the new 80-byte row leaves stale bytes at 1916..1995 in the same way. Removing the old value first would have left those bytes zero.
    - An old single row is removed as a deleted data row is: its slot becomes `00 d0`, the rows below it (the new one included) move up by its length (slot 1 `cc 07` > `30 08`), and the free word rises by its length.
    - An old chain's pages are released, cleared in the LV owned map and marked free in page 1. Their bytes are not written.
  - **Page-1 and LV-map timing (during the statement, not at Close).** Unlike an emptied data page (which stays allocated in page 1 until Close, `r-q20-delete-pages/where-empty-last-read-open`), freed chain pages 47 and 48 are cleared in the LV owned map and marked free in page 1 **during the statement**, visible 1.5 s later while the session is still open:
    - `lv-free-timing-delete-chain` (`DELETE FROM V WHERE ID = 3`): while open (`open-after-op`), page 0 byte 3586 is `00` > `01`, page 42 row count `03` > `02`, page 43 LV owned `3830..3831` `a0 01` > `20 00`, page 1 `4037..4038` `00 fe` > `80 ff` (47 and 48 already free), and data page 46 slot 2 `b5 0f` > `ce cf`. `after-close` is byte-for-byte identical (`3586` stays `01`, no write at Close).
    - `lv-free-timing-update-chain-smaller` (5,000 > 4,500 on new pages 49 and 50): while open, `3586` `00` > `01`, LV owned `a0 01` > `20 06`, and page 1 `4037..4041` `00 fe ff ff ff` > `80 01 00 00 00` (47 and 48 already free; pages 49-79 cleared as a session allocation reservation); at Close, `3586` `01` > `02` and page 1 becomes `80 f9 ff ff ff` (returning unused reservation 51-79 to `1`, leaving 49 and 50 used).
    - `lv-free-timing-update-chain-to-single` (5,000 > 100 on page 45, taking no new page): while open, `3586` `00` > `01`, LV owned `a0 01` > `20 00`, and page 1 `4037..4041` `00 fe ff ff ff` > `80 03 00 00 00` (47 and 48 already free, 49 stays `1`, pages 50-79 reserved as `0`); at Close, `3586` `01` > `02` and page 1 becomes `80 ff ff ff ff` (returning 50-79 to `1`). This explains why UPDATE freeing a chain counts `3586` +2 (returning the reservation at Close) while DELETE freeing the same chain counts +1 (no reservation, no Close write).
  - **The new value is placed by the ordinary rules.**
    - A single row goes as a new slot on a free-map page with room; deleted slots are not reused. 100 > 200 bytes gives slot 2, free 1978 > 1876. 100 > 80 bytes gives free > 1996. 5,000 > 100 bytes gives slot 2 on page 45, free 1876.
    - A chain is never rewritten in place: 5,000 > 6,000, 5,000 > 4,500 and 100 > 5,000 all write a new chain on newly taken pages 49 and 50, with a new tick stamp.
  - **The data row's 12-byte reference is rewritten in place**: length word, locator, +8 word.
  - **Null** removes the reference from the row. Row 1 goes from 25 to 13 bytes, and its null-mask byte from `03` to `01`. The rows below it move up 12. The old single row or chain goes as above.
  - **DELETE of the row** removes the value as Null does (the single row from page 45; chain pages 47 and 48 freed), removes the data row and its index entry, and lowers the TableDef row count (3 > 2).
  - Page 0 byte 3586 counts +2 for an UPDATE that took or freed a page and +1 for the others. Both DELETEs count +1, also lv-op-delete-row-chain, which freed two pages.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB made by CreateDatabase, Memo of U+20AC text, SQL UPDATE or DELETE with dbFailOnError, pages 0, 1 and 42-57 read after the setup session, 1.5 s after the statement while open (`lv-free-timing-*`), and after the statement's Close. FieldSize reads back the new sizes.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q19-growth-open/lv-op-single-larger`, `-single-smaller`, `-single-to-chain`, `-chain-larger`, `-chain-smaller`, `-chain-to-single`, `-single-null`, `-chain-null`, `-delete-row-single`, `-delete-row-chain`; `evidence/r-q22-lv-open/lv-free-timing-update-chain-smaller/`, `lv-free-timing-update-chain-to-single/`, `lv-free-timing-delete-chain/`. Discriminators: single against chained old value; larger, smaller, crossing form; a value against Null against a deleted row; 1.5 s open read against after-Close read; UPDATE freeing a chain with and without taking new pages against DELETE freeing a chain.
- **Open**:
  - A recordset Edit/Update instead of SQL; Long Binary; MDB.
  - A replacement that no longer fits the data row's page (the reference is the same size, so only a Null-to-value change can grow the row).
  - Deleting a non-top single row on an LVAL page (here only slot 0 on page 45 was removed, giving `00 d0`; the data row's `ce cf` in `lv-op-delete-row-chain` follows the deleted-slot rule above).
  - Whether freeing the last single row on an LVAL page retires that LVAL page during the statement or at Close (or keeps it empty).

### R-FMT-2 Overflow / deletion and Map ownership (W-05): DELETE without WHERE releases every data page, every long-value page and every non-root index page of the table, and empties each index root in place
- **Claim**: `DELETE FROM D` in a shared session, on a table of several data pages:
  - **Data pages.** Every one is released: cleared in the table's owned map and FreeMap and marked free in page 1 (at Close, as the retirement note above found for one page). Their bytes are not written; rows and slots stay.
  - **LVAL pages** of the table's long-value columns, single-row pages and chain pages alike, are released the same way, cleared in the column's LV owned and LV free maps. Their bytes are not written.
  - **Index roots.** Each index keeps its root page, rewritten as an empty leaf: free word `20 0e` (3616), entry bitmap zeroed, entry bytes left.
    - An intermediate root becomes a leaf: type byte `03` > `04`, tail child (bytes 20-23) zeroed, level byte 26 `01` > `00`.
    - Its leaf pages are released: cleared in the index's usage map and marked free in page 1. Their bytes are not written.
  - **TableDef.** The row count (bytes 16-19) and each index's count (byte 67 for the first, 79 for the second) go to 0.
  - **Changed pages.** Page 0 (byte 3586 +2), page 1, the TableDef, the usage-map page and the index roots, and no other (every page's hash compared).
- **Byte values** (new files; page 43 holds the usage maps, page 1 bits from byte 4032):
  - No index (data 44-48, 30 rows of about 530 bytes): page 43 owned 4037-4038 `f0 01` > `00 00`, FreeMap 3969 `01` > `00`; page 1 4037-4038 `00 fe` > `f0 ff`; page 42 byte 16 `1e` > `00`.
  - Primary key (data 45-49): root 44 free `12 0d` > `20 0e` and bitmap bytes 28-60 zeroed; page 42 bytes 16 and 67 `1e` > `00`; page 1 `00 fc` > `e0 ff`.
  - Two indexes (data 46-50): roots 44 and 45 emptied alike; page 42 bytes 16, 67 and 79 `1e` > `00`; page 1 `00 f8` > `c0 ff`.
  - Long values (data 47 and 51; LVAL 45, 46, 48, 49, 50, 52 and 53: one single-row page and three two-page chains). Page 43: LV owned 3830-3831 `60 37` > `00 00`, LV free 3762 `01` > `00` (page 48), table owned 4037-4038 `80 08` > `00 00`, FreeMap 3969 `08` > `00`. Page 1: `00 c0` > `e0 ff`.
  - Intermediate root (1,200 rows, leaves 48 and 49):
    - Root 44: `03` > `04`, free `13 0e` > `20 0e`, tail `31` > `00`, byte 26 `01` > `00`, bitmap byte 28 `20` > `00`.
    - The leaves are cleared in the index map (page 43 byte 3900 `03` > `00`) and freed. Data 45, 46, 47, 50 and 51 are freed: page 1 `00 f0` > `e0 ff`.
    - Page 42 bytes 16 and 67 `b0 04` > `00 00`.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB new files, the table populated in the CreateDatabase session and closed. One shared session runs DELETE FROM D with dbFailOnError (RecordsAffected 30 or 1200) and closes. Pages 0, 1 and 42-81 kept, 0-81 hashed, after both sessions.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q19-growth-open/delete-all-no-index/`, `delete-all-pk/`, `delete-all-two-indexes/`, `delete-all-long-values/`, `delete-all-intermediate-root/`; with `evidence/r-q17-growth/retire-delete-au1/` (one data page, a one-leaf index). Discriminators: no, one and two indexes; a long-value column; a leaf root against an intermediate root.
- **Open**: a three-level index; DELETE without WHERE inside a transaction; which pages a later INSERT takes from those released; MDB.

### R-FMT-2 and R-CURSOR-1 (W-05): a table-type Delete writes the TableDef row count at the recordset's Close, as a dynaset's does; TableDef.RecordCount keeps its first read in the session until TableDefs.Refresh
- **Claim**:
  - **Table-type Delete.** Each Delete removes the row and its index entry as a write group of its own (page 0 byte 3586 +1 per Delete, read after a 1.5 s pause). The TableDef's row count (page 107 byte 16) stays `0a` until the recordset closes, and is then written as one more commit. This is the dynaset's behaviour (the retirement note above).
  - **A burst.** Three dynaset Deletes with no pause between them are one write group (3586 +1).
  - **Counts while the count is pending.** The recordset's own RecordCount falls with each Delete (10, 9, 8, 7). A table-type recordset opened then reads 7 and walks IDs 4-10, and `SELECT Count(*)` gives 7, while the page still holds 10.
  - **TableDef.RecordCount** is the value of its first read in the session. Read before the Deletes, it reads 10 after them and after the recordset's Close, for table-type and dynaset alike. First read after the Deletes, it reads 7. After `TableDefs.Refresh` it reads the live count (10, 9, 8, 7), before the count reaches the page.
- **Conditions**: copies of r-fmt-2b-w-accdb (Au1: ten rows on page 110, tdef 107), one shared DAO session, `TableDefs("Au1")` taken afresh for each read; pages read 1.5 s after the last Delete, after the recordset's Close (and a pause) and after the database's Close.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q19-growth-open/rs-table-delete-au1/`, `rs-table-delete-au1-td-after/`, `rs-table-delete-au1-td-refresh/`, `rs-dynaset-delete-au1-counts/`, `rs-dynaset-delete-au1-td-before/`; `evidence/r-q17-growth/retire-rs-delete-au1/`. Discriminators: table-type against dynaset; the first TableDef read before against after the Deletes; Refresh.
- **Open**: whether a new Database object or a reopen reads the live count; a Delete inside a transaction; MDB.

### R-FMT-2 Index page splits (W-05, zfrp stretch 4): an append past the last key of a full rightmost leaf starts a one-entry leaf, also below the root and whether or not the leaf's prefix covers the key
- **Claim**: An INSERT whose key is above every key of the tree, when the rightmost leaf is full:
  - The full leaf keeps its entries and stored prefix. Only its next link (bytes 16-19) changes, to the new page.
  - A newly taken page becomes the rightmost leaf. It holds the one entry and stores prefix 0, with prev set to the full leaf and next 0.
  - The parent gains one entry: the full leaf's last key, its row locator and the full leaf as child. The parent's tail child (bytes 20-23) becomes the new page.
  - A key the full leaf's prefix covers (1205 under `7f 80 00`) and one it does not (100000) give the same pages, apart from the key and its row locator.
  - This is the rule r-q7 long-split found for a full root leaf and long-split-insert-wider for a leaf that was not full (gqv4 note).
- **Byte values** (append-full-rightmost-covered): leaf 49 holds 603..1204, 602 entries, prefix 3, free 1. After key 1205:
  - Page 49 byte 16 `00` > `34`.
  - New page 52: `04 01 17 0e 2a 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 31 00 00 00`, prefix length (bytes 24-25) 0, the entry `7f 80 00 04 b5` (data 51 slot 184) at 480.
  - Root 44: free `13 0e` > `06 0e`, tail `31` > `34`, the entry `7f 80 00 04 b4` (data 51 slot 183, child 49) at 493-505, bitmap byte 30 `00` > `04`.
  - The new leaf goes on filling under prefix 0: after Close, 52 holds 1205..1210.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB new file, L(k LONG PRIMARY KEY), ascending one INSERT each in a shared session under `SetOption(dbImplicitCommitSync, "Yes")`. The first 28 bytes of pages 44-63 were read after every statement from key 1190, and pages 44-59 whole around the append.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q19-growth-open/append-full-rightmost-covered/`, `append-full-rightmost-uncovered/`; `evidence/r-q7-index-prefix/long-split/`, `long-split-insert-wider/`. Discriminators: a full non-root leaf against a full root (r-q7) and a non-full leaf (r-q7); a covered against an uncovered key.
- **Open**: TEXT keys; a full rightmost leaf under a non-root intermediate page.

### R-FMT-2 Index page splits (W-05, zfrp stretch 4): a key a leaf's stored prefix does not cover splits that leaf at once, even with room; the half that begins with the inserted key stores 0; an odd leaf keeps the smaller half
- **Claim**:
  - **Routing.** An intermediate entry holds its child's last key. A key above it goes to the next child, and a key above every entry goes to the tail child. In split-uncovered-between-leaves, 70000 goes to the rightmost leaf (first key 100000, prefix 0), not to the leaf before it (603..1200, prefix 3), which is untouched.
  - **An uncovered key splits the leaf.** A leaf whose stored prefix the inserted key does not share is split whatever its free space. With prefix `7f 80 01`, key 65535 (`7f 80 00 ff ff`) split each of these:
    - a root leaf of 600 entries with 13 bytes free;
    - a non-root leaf of 451 entries with 907 bytes free;
    - the same leaf after deletes left it 10 entries with 3,553 bytes free.
    - A covered key below a leaf's first entry does not split it before it is full (r-q17 split-inner-below).
  - **Split point.** The n entries are halved, the lower half taking floor(n/2): 600 into 300 and 300, 451 into 225 and 226, 10 into 5 and 5, and the TEXT leaf of 401 into 200 and 201. The new key joins the half its key falls in. At the boundary it joins the upper half: abcd0401, between the 200th and 201st entries, made it 202.
  - **Prefix.** The half whose first entry is the inserted key stores 0, and the other half keeps the leaf's stored prefix. In split-uncovered-right-leaf-below the leaf is not leftmost and 65535 is not the tree's lowest key (keys 1-602 lie to its left), yet its lower half stores 0. This settles the gqv4 question between "new lowest key" and "first entry of the leftmost leaf": neither, it is the half's first entry.
  - **Pages**, as in the gqv4 note:
    - The lower half stays on the old page, and the upper half goes on a newly taken page linked after it.
    - The parent gains one entry (the lower half's last key, child the old page), and its tail moves to the new page when the split leaf was the tail.
    - A root leaf keeps the root page as the intermediate (type `03`, byte 26 `01`), and the halves go on two new pages, the lower half on the lower page.
- **Byte values**:
  - split-uncovered-right-leaf-below: before 65535, leaf 49 (65536..65986) has prefix 3 and free `8b 03`. After it, 49 has free `2e 06`, prefix 0 and next `34`, and new page 52 has free `d1 08`, prefix 3 and prev `31`. Root 44: free `13 0e` > `06 0e`, tail `31` > `34`. After 65534 too, 49 holds 227 entries and 52 holds 226.
  - split-uncovered-sparse-leaf-below: after the delete (441 rows), 49 has 10 entries, free `e1 0d` and prefix 3. After 65535, 49 has 6 entries and prefix 0, and new page 53 has 5 and prefix 3.
  - split-uncovered-root-below: root 44 (600 keys 65536..66135, prefix 3, free `0d 00`) becomes the intermediate. Its halves are 48 (65535 and 300 keys, prefix 0) and 49 (300 keys, prefix 3).
  - split-text-odd-count: K(k TEXT(20)) with even keys abcd0002..abcd0820. The root splits at the 402nd key, leaving leaf 49 full with 401 entries (prefix 6, `7f 4a 4c 4d 4f 36`) and a one-entry leaf 50. abcd0401 splits 49 into 49 (200 entries, free 1810) and new page 51 (202, free 1792), both with prefix 6, linked 49 > 51 > 50.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB new files, L(k LONG PRIMARY KEY) or K(id LONG, k TEXT(20)) with a non-unique index, one INSERT each in a shared session under dbImplicitCommitSync, index pages whole after the statements that decide.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q19-growth-open/split-uncovered-between-leaves/`, `split-uncovered-root-below/`, `split-uncovered-right-leaf-below/`, `split-uncovered-sparse-leaf-below/`, `split-text-odd-count/`; `evidence/r-q17-growth/split-inner-below/`. Discriminators: free space (13, 907 and 3,553 bytes); root against non-root; leftmost against not; covered against uncovered; odd against even n.
- **Observation, one case** (split-text-deep-descending: 500 TEXT(255) keys of about 200 bytes, descending, in the CreateDatabase session):
  - The tree has three levels. Root 44 is level 2, stores prefix 0 and holds 4 entries and a tail.
  - The level-1 pages have prev and next links. They store prefix 3, except the leftmost (78), which holds the lowest keys and stores 0. Right halves hold 9 entries, as the leaves do.
  - The leaves hold 9 entries (the leftmost 14), with stored prefix 3 or 4.
  - This is consistent with leaves' split rules applied to intermediate pages. It is not qualified: one run, read only after Close.
- **Open**: an uncovered key in a TEXT index; the split of an intermediate page asked with designed probes; how each leaf of the deep tree came by its prefix, statement by statement.

### R-FMT-2 Text grammar (public round trip, corrected): Text and Memo store UTF-16 units as given, including NUL and lone surrogates, within the declared length, but a leading U+FEFF is not reliably kept

- **Claim**: "wide €", a mixed narrow/wide string, a surrogate pair, a pair written reversed, a lone high and a lone low surrogate (alone and between letters), "a" NUL "b", U+0001, 254 letters plus a lone high surrogate, 253 letters plus a pair and letters around a pair are stored and read back with exactly the stored units, in Text and Memo, ACCDB and MDB (the bytes `00d8`, `00dc`, `00dc00d8` and `610000d86200` are read back; LEN counts each lone unit as 1 and a pair as 2; AscW of the lone surrogates reads -10240 and -9216). 50 narrow or 50 wide characters are accepted and 51 narrow characters are refused with 0x800A0C5B (3163). A string of U+FEFF followed by "x" reads back as "x" with LEN 1 in both columns and both formats, while U+FEFF followed by "bom first" read back intact in `text-grammar`; the lone-U+FEFF case reads back empty (R-STRING-1). An SQL UPDATE of a Text(20) column with 25 characters is not refused and stores the first 20.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB, counted BSTR puts through a table recordset; stored units read through SQL AscW and LEN and through a recordset.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/text-units-accdb/` and `-mdb/` (re-read: identical in both formats, row 7 reads "x"); `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/text-grammar/`, `alt-drop-ladder/`; `evidence/r-text-fffe/`. Discriminators: lone versus paired surrogates, each position; U+FEFF followed by a short and by a long string.
- **Open**: which following content keeps a leading U+FEFF (a compression-header explanation is a guess, not tested); compression, segments and the exact grammar are bytes (T).

### R-FMT-2 Binary and OLE (public side): every byte value round-trips and an empty array reads back as Null

- **Claim**: a Long Binary value of all 256 byte values (256 bytes), one of 4000 patterned bytes, a Binary(255) of bytes 0 to 254, 255 zero bytes and one 0xFF byte read back with exactly the bytes written; empty arrays written to Binary and to Long Binary read back Null; a Null stays Null; a 256-byte array into Binary(255) is refused with 3163.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, table-recordset puts of VT_ARRAY|VT_UI1, read back through a recordset and through `IS NULL`.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/binary-bytes-accdb/` (re-read in the JSON: hex lengths 510 and 512 for the 255- and 256-byte arrays, ending `fdfeff`); `evidence/r-fmt-1/read-*-binary/`, `read-*-longbinary/`. Discriminators: the empty array versus Null; the length limit.
- **Open**: Long Binary forms and chains are bytes (T).

### R-FMT-2 Generated values: AutoNumber takes its value at AddNew or insert, consumes it on cancel, refusal and rollback, follows an explicit ID even when refused, and is reset only by Compact & Repair; a generated GUID is reached by ADO DDL and ADOX; random AutoNumber has no engine-side route

- **Claim**: after deleting the last rows, a middle row or all rows, the next insert gets the highest key ever issued plus one (11 for ten rows). A recordset `AddNew` already shows its ID (4 for a table holding 1 to 3); `CancelUpdate` consumes it (the next insert gets 5); an INSERT refused for a duplicate in another unique index consumes one (the next insert gets 7, where 6 was taken); an `Update` refused for a duplicate keeps the ID shown at AddNew and the retry stores the row under that ID; a rolled-back transaction consumes its value (9 taken, the next insert gets 10); an explicit ID sets the counter even when the insert is refused (an explicit duplicate ID 3 refused with 3022 made the next insert get 4). After an explicit ID 100 the next automatic ID is 101; `ALTER TABLE ... COUNTER(200, 5)` returns hr 0 and the next two inserts get 200 and 205. DBEngine.CompactDatabase resets the counter to the highest remaining key plus one (8 after deleting rows 8 to 10, 1 after deleting all, 12 for a table with rows up to 11; 11 before compaction). A generated GUID: ADO DDL (Provider Microsoft.ACE.OLEDB.12.0) accepts `G UNIQUEIDENTIFIER DEFAULT GenGUID()`, `G GUID DEFAULT GenGUID()`, `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN H UNIQUEIDENTIFIER DEFAULT GenGUID()` and `ALTER COLUMN G SET DEFAULT GenGUID()`, and inserts that omit the column store a different GUID per row (read as `{guid {...}}`; the field reports Type 15, Size 16, Attributes 2 for the default form); ADOX accepts an adGUID column whose `Jet OLEDB:AutoGenerate` is set True (Type 15, Attributes 1) and stores a different GUID per row, and an Integer column with `Autoincrement` True reads Attributes 17. The same default forms through DAO `Execute` are refused with 3292 and 3290, and a DAO GUID field with Attributes 16 is refused at `TableDefs.Append` with 3001. For a random AutoNumber: `NewValues` is not a built-in property of the DAO AutoNumber field (Item fails with 3270), appending it to an existing field's Properties is accepted but has no effect (IDs 1 to 5), appending it to a field not yet appended is refused with 3219, and tables made with Attributes 16 and no property, or with a Text "Random" or a Long 1 property attempted before append, all issue IDs 1 to 6; the ADOX column properties list no random mode.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, SQL INSERT without the ID and `@@IDENTITY` after each; Workspaces(0) for the transaction; ADO DDL and ADOX over an ADO connection on the closed file, then DAO reads.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/auto-compact-accdb/`, `auto-cancel-fail-accdb/`, `auto-guid-random-accdb/`; `evidence/r-q2-fmt-sort/ado-ddl-guid-accdb/` (re-read: the G and H columns hold distinct GUIDs per row), `adox-autogenerate-accdb/` (re-read: AX2 GUIDs and Attributes), `dao-newvalues-accdb/` (re-read: 3270, 3219, IDs); `evidence/r-fmt-2b/au-after-tail-delete/`, `au-after-middle-delete/`, `au-after-all-deleted/`, `au-explicit-high-ladder/`; `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/fmt2b-au-explicit-high-ladder/`, `fmt2b-au-reseed-ladder/`. Discriminators: cancel, refused insert, refused update, rollback and explicit duplicate ID each in its own step with the counter read after each; compaction against a no-compaction control; four GUID DDL forms and the ADOX route each on its own table.
- **Open**: a random AutoNumber is an Access (NewValues) property and no DAO, ADO or ADOX route reached it: it needs the Access host (T). The ADOX Autoincrement probe wrote explicit IDs, so only its Attributes 17 (not generated values) were observed for that column.

### R-FMT-2 II-05 Statistics word (public DistinctCount): DistinctCount is a counter maintained at insertion, not the number of distinct keys

- **Claim**: an index's DAO `DistinctCount` rises by one when an entry whose key is not yet in the index is inserted and does not rise for a key already present (an index on a column holding one value stays 1 through 1100 rows); it does not fall when rows are deleted (601 rows still read 602, 302 rows still 602) or when the last row of a key is deleted; deleting a key and inserting it again raises it again; an UPDATE that changes the key, to a new key and back, does not change it; a refused insert still raises the indexes ahead of the refusing one (a primary key and a non-unique index went up, the unique index that refused did not); AddNew with CancelUpdate and a refused UPDATE change nothing; the value is the same after a read-only reopen. CompactDatabase recomputes the exact value (3 rows remained after the deletes and refusals: the compacted file reads 2, 3 and 3 for the three indexes, against 4, 6 and 5 before). ACCDB and MDB give the same sequence.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB, Indexes refreshed before each read, a primary key, a non-unique and a unique index, SQL and recordset writes.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/dc-rules-accdb/` and `-mdb/` (re-read: the counts after each of 11 steps, after reopen and after compaction are identical in both formats); `evidence/r-fmt-2b/ix-delete-max-key/`, `ix-delete-low-range/`, `ix-delete-reinsert-ladder/`, `ix-dup-ladder/`, `dr-reinsert-same-key-same-values/`, `compact-after-index-growth/`; `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/idx-key-ladder/`, `upd-indexed-value/`. Discriminators: duplicate, delete of a duplicate, delete of the last row of a key, reinsert, update away and back, refused insert, cancel and refused update each a separate step.
- **Open**: refusal order for indexes other than these three; `DistinctCount` is never used as a row count. The physical words are the next note.

### R-FMT-2 II-05 statistics words of a bulk-built index: word0 counts entries down, word1 is clamped to it
- **Claim**: An index's 12-byte counter block (II-05 §1.1) holds word0, word1 (UniqueEntryCount, what DAO's `DistinctCount` reads) and word2. An index built on an empty table has word0 = 0 and follows the previous note: word1 never falls. An index built by `CREATE INDEX` on a populated table starts at word0 = its entry count and word1 = its distinct keys ((8, 7, 0) for 8 rows with 7 keys), and then:
  - each removal of an entry lowers word0 by one; an insert never raises it;
  - a new key raises word1 by one, and a key-changing UPDATE does not;
  - clamping `word1 <= word0` applies on deletion (`(8, 7) -> (6, 6)` when two unique keys are deleted), whereas inserting a new key after bulk creation increments `word1` past `word0` (`(10, 10) -> (10, 11)` on `r-q51-codecs-props-compact/dec-desc-keys-accdb` p42 and `dec-desc-keys-mdb` p19).
  So: deleting a duplicate gives (7, 7); deleting a unique key gives (7, 7); deleting two unique keys gives (6, 6); an UPDATE from 30 to 35 gives (7, 7); inserting the new key 80 gives (8, 8); inserting the present key 50 leaves (8, 7); deleting every row gives (0, 0). DAO's DistinctCount equals word1 each time. r-cursor-1b/editmode-by-type fits: its four bulk-built indexes went from (8, 7) and (8, 6) to (4, 4) and (6, 6).
- **Conditions**: ACCDB created by DAO; X(id LONG, k LONG), 8 rows, IX_K built after the rows; one statement per question; the TableDef page kept in each record and read with `calcdb-oracle inspect` (counter=).
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q10-statistics-words/` (none, delete-duplicate, delete-unique, delete-two-unique, update-key, insert-new, insert-duplicate, delete-all); `evidence/r-q51-codecs-props-compact/dec-desc-keys-{accdb,mdb}/` (`after-ix-desc` `(10, 10)` -> `after-inc-insert` `(10, 11)` on ACCDB p42 / MDB p19); a Gemini 4 Neon sub-session found the bulk-built case in compare-set growth-1 and `bigint-dt2-codecs` (`dd4b7b4c`) refined the insert vs deletion distinction.
- **Open**: word2; a CompactDatabase-built index (word0 = rows, as the 276 retained blocks with word0 > 0 suggest); word0 once it reaches 0 and rows are added again; MDB.

### R-FMT-2 Compact & Repair diagnostic and R-PRESERVE-1 PRES-COMPACT: CompactDatabase leaves the source unchanged and the copy reads the same public data, except for the counters it recomputes

- **Claim**: `DBEngine.CompactDatabase(source, work file)` returns hr 0, leaves the source file's hash unchanged, and the compacted file reads the same typed rows, field metadata and index lists as the source in eight probes; compacted sizes vary (475136 B from 503808 B after a Memo shrink, equal after growth). It recomputes two counters: the index `DistinctCount` and the AutoNumber seed (see their notes). In the catalog view the only differences are `Database.Name` and, later, `LastUpdated` of some tables and their Containers documents.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB (MDB for the statistics probe), work file written next to the attempt, source closed.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fmt-2b/compact-after-fixed-update/`, `compact-after-page-growth/`, `compact-after-page-emptied/`, `compact-after-memo-shrink/`, `compact-after-memo-growth/`, `compact-after-ole-insert/`, `compact-after-index-growth/`; `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/pres-compact/`; `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/fmt2b-compact-after-fixed-update/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/auto-compact-accdb/`, `dc-rules-accdb/`, `dc-rules-mdb/`.
- **Open**: Compact after a rolled-back or refused operation; repair-marked Compact & Repair (Access).

### R-FMT-2 Failed operations (refusal identity): duplicate, validation, NOT NULL and foreign-key refusals have stable numbers

- **Claim**: a duplicate in a unique or primary index is refused with 0x800A0BCE (3022), a Null in an index that disallows Nulls or in a primary key with 3058, a Null in a NOT NULL column with 3314, a validation-rule violation with 3316 and the rule's ValidationText ("P must be positive"), a missing parent row with 3201 ("The related record is required in table 'Parent'"). An omitted column takes its default.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB, `Execute` with dbFailOnError and per-statement Errors read.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/failed-ladder/`, `pres-txn-failed-ladder/`; `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/sort3-unique-corpus-accdb/`, `sort3-np-accdb/`, `sort3b-nulls-refusals/`, `sort3b-dao-index-flags/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/fail-hash-unique/` (3022), `fail-hash-fk/` (3201), `fail-hash-validation/` (3316), `dao-index-primary-accdb/`. Discriminators: each kind on its own statement; both formats.
- **Open**: none that block.

### R-PRESERVE-1 PRES-DATA-EDITS: ordinary data edits leave every DAO-visible catalog object and property untouched

- **Claim**: a row edit, a row growth that relocates it, an LV replacement in a chain, an LV delete with retirement and a pressure insert of 400 rows change the file hash and change nothing in the DAO catalog view (table, query, relation and Containers names, properties, `LastUpdated`), apart from the edited table's `RecordCount`. A read/write open and close with no statement leaves the hash and the view identical.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, an engine-created baseline with tables, relations, saved queries, user and unknown properties; the view taken before and after.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/pres-row-edit/`, `pres-grow-relocate/`, `pres-lv-replace-chain/`, `pres-lv-delete-retire/`, `pres-ins-pressure/`, `pres-noop/` (pres4 attempts; the pres3 ones are stale); `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/noop-rw-accdb/`. Discriminator: the same view does show property and DDL edits (next note).
- **Open**: protected-range byte comparison (W-04, T); an Access-created front-end baseline (T).

### R-PRESERVE-1 PRES-PROPERTIES and PRES-CATALOG (public view): each property edit and each DDL edit shows as exactly its own change
