- **Claim**: four property edits made one at a time are each visible in the view and change no other property and not `T.LastUpdated`; `CREATE INDEX` grows the file 4096 B and changes nothing visible; add and drop column change only the changed table's `LastUpdated`; `CREATE TABLE` grows the file 12288 B, adds one TableDef, one Containers document, and increments the MSysACEs and MSysObjects record counts.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB; DAO property edits and SQL DDL each as its own step, view before and after.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/pres-property-ladder/`, `pres-catalog-ladder/` (pres4 attempts).
- **Open**: none that block.

### R-PRESERVE-1 PRES-QUERY-PROPS: a row-returning single-table SELECT lists the table's properties among its own; a join and an action query do not

- **Claim**: with a table T that has two table properties (`Description` and an unknown `CalcDbUnknown`), the saved queries `SELECT * FROM T`, with a WHERE, with ORDER BY, `SELECT DISTINCT` and an aggregate (`COUNT(*)`) each list 16 properties including both, while a join of T and U, an UPDATE of T and a SELECT over U (which has none) list 14 properties without them.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, DAO `CreateQueryDef`, Properties of each QueryDef enumerated after Refresh.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/query-props-accdb/` (re-read: names and counts); `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/pres-property-ladder/`. Discriminators: WHERE, ORDER BY, DISTINCT, aggregate, join, action query each as its own query.
- **Open**: a single-table SELECT over a table that has properties other than T (only one such table was asked).

### R-PRESERVE-1 PRES-RENAME: an engine-only table rename follows into relationships but not into saved queries

- **Claim**: renaming a table through `TableDef.Name` updates the `Table` of a relationship that references it (R1 to R1x, R5 to R5x) and leaves every saved query's SQL unchanged, so a query over the renamed table then cannot be opened (`OpenRecordset` of `SELECT * FROM R3;` fails after R3 is renamed, and the one over R5 after R5); renaming a table that nothing references changes nothing else; the queries over tables not yet renamed still open.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, each of four renames its own step (referenced by a relationship only, by a query only, by nothing, by both); relations, query SQL, table names and an open of each query read after each step.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/rename-variants-accdb/` (re-read); `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/pres-rename-referenced/`. Discriminators: the four referencing kinds one at a time.
- **Open**: Access Name AutoCorrect (Access only; T).

### R-PRESERVE-1 PRES-ROLLBACK-FAILED: a refusal after index work and a rolled-back insert change the file bytes; a validation refusal, a NOT NULL refusal and a rolled-back update do not

- **Claim**: on copies of one fixture, a refused duplicate in a unique index and a refused foreign-key insert each change the file hash (size constant), the same refused statement again changes it again, and an open and close afterwards changes nothing; a refused validation-rule insert changes nothing, twice. A transaction that inserts one row and is rolled back changes the hash, a rolled-back bulk insert of 3600 rows of about 200 characters changes it again (size constant, row count 0); the committed bulk insert grows the file from 282624 B to 1945600 B. The earlier results fit: the NOT NULL and validation refusals left the hash equal, and a rolled-back UPDATE left it equal.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, copies of one fixture, each step a read/write open, the statement, close and a hash; Workspaces(0) for the transactions.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/fail-hash-unique/`, `fail-hash-fk/`, `fail-hash-validation/`, `rollback-size-accdb/` (re-read: hashes 7fdc... then 57af..., c2fd... for the unique refusal; e133..., 7c52... for the foreign key; unchanged for validation; 19ed..., f910... for the rollbacks); `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/failed-ladder/`, `pres-txn-failed-ladder/`, `txn-ladder/`. Discriminators: refusal kind varied with the fixture fixed; insert versus update rollback; one row versus 3600.
- **Open**: the cause (a statistics word or counter written by the index work is the candidate that fits all records; needs page images of a refusal or rollback, W-04, T; the sampling recorder `evidence/r-sample-rec/` captured successful inserts only: data pages first, then pages 0, 1 and 45-48 at commit, an implicit INSERT written after it returned).

### R-SORT-3 Descriptor dispatch (file version): dbVersion 64 and 128 files share one order, a dbVersion 256 file orders supplementary-plane characters differently

- **Claim**: with the same locale, corpus and index, the dbVersion 64 MDB and the dbVersion 128 ACCDB give identical GROUP BY classes, ORDER BY order and index walks (18 of 40 values, among them the supplementary-plane characters U+1D11E, U+1D400, U+1F600, U+20000, U+20001, U+10000, U+10FFFF, U+3400, U+9FFF, U+FFFD, U+FFFF, U+D7FF, U+1E9E and the lone surrogates, equal the empty string); the dbVersion 256 file ("14.0") gives the supplementary-plane characters, U+3400, U+E000 and the lone surrogates their own classes, while "", " ", U+9FFF, U+FFFD, U+FFFF, U+D7FF and U+1E9E still equal one another; the order differs correspondingly. In all three files an indexed table and an unindexed copy agree, and CollatingOrder is 1033.
- **Conditions**: one 41-value corpus, files made with 64, 128 and 256, the same locale; reads by GROUP BY, ORDER BY K, ID, and index walks forward and backward.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/v64-corpus-mdb/`, `v128-corpus-accdb/`, `v256-corpus-accdb/` (re-read); `evidence/r-sort-3/create-version-256/`, `equality-accdb/`. Discriminators: file version varied with everything else fixed; indexed versus unindexed.
- **Open**: which other characters differ between 128 and 256 (only this corpus); General97 and General - Legacy selection (T).

### R-SORT-3 Text levels: General Text keys fall into stable equality classes, ordered by a compare that SQL, Seek, GROUP BY and unique indexes share (dbVersion 64 and 128)

- **Claim**: on a General-sorted dbVersion 64 or 128 file, SQL `=`, GROUP BY, DISTINCT, Seek, a unique index and the index walk partition Text values into the same classes: case and kana/width variants are one class (A, a, U+FF21, U+FF41; U+3042, U+30A2, U+FF71), é = É = e + U+0301, ligatures equal their expansions (U+FB01 = "fi", U+0153 = "oe", æ = "ae"), "ss" = "SS" but ß is distinct from "ss", è, å, ö, ø, ü, ı and İ are their own classes, Null and "" are separate. Order is Null first, then the class order (" a" < "1" < "10" < "2" < a-class < å < "a b" < aa < ab < "a-b" < ae-class < b < c < ch < cz < d < e < é-class < è < ... < ss-class < u < ü < ue < z < kana < Han), identical on an indexed and an unindexed table, between fresh and incrementally maintained indexes, between ACCDB and MDB and between 0x0409 and 0x0809. A unique index keeps the first row of each class. Within a class the index walk lists rows in insertion order forward and the exact reverse backward; SQL ORDER BY K without ID lists a class's rows in the reverse of insertion order and ORDER BY K DESC keeps that same relative order (see the tie-order note).
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB with General collation; DAO table walks, SQL, ADO; two corpora (203 rows with 125 classes; 54 rows with the card's list).
- **Records**: `evidence/r-sort-3/equality-accdb/`, `equality-mdb/`, `order-index-*/`, `order-sql-*/`, `seek-*/`, `maintain-*/`, `ado-order-seek-accdb/`; `evidence/r-sort-3b/lg-walk/`, `lg-sql-order/`, `lg-unique-survivors/`, `lu-*/`; `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/sort3-unique-corpus-*/`, `sort3b-unique-refusals-lg/`, `-lu/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/v64-corpus-mdb/`, `v128-corpus-accdb/` (indexed equals unindexed, MDB equals ACCDB). Discriminators: indexed versus unindexed, fresh versus incremental, unique versus non-unique, two locales, two formats.
- **Open**: LIKE depends on the index (own note). Weights and bytes are T.

### R-SORT-3 SORT-CHECK key bytes (b999): leading modifiers, flags against positions and secondary sums follow the encoder; General – Legacy writes a placeholder per primary byte; the prolonged sound mark takes its neighbour's weight

- **Claim**: in ACE's General – Legacy (1033/0) index entries of designed values, a combining mark or modifier letter before the first base letter (U+0301a, U+0301U+0301a, U+0308a, U+02B0a), upper and accented letters in either order (Aé, aÉ, ÀB, àB, AÉ, Abé, aBÉ) and a letter with 1 to 40 combining acutes or graves, with and without a following b, have the bytes CalcDb's encoder writes (src/CalcDb.Format/TextKeys.cs): no secondary sum wraps to 00 in that range. A multi-byte character record writes one secondary placeholder per primary byte, so an expansion before an accent keeps two placeholders (Æé, æé, Œé, ße, ĳé: …01 02 02 0E); with no accent after it the trailing placeholders are dropped and nothing shows (Æ, Æe, Ǣe). Under General (1033/1) the two-byte é record writes one (r-sort-3/create-version-256). The katakana-hiragana prolonged sound mark (U+30FC, U+FF70) has no weight of its own in General – Legacy: at the start of a value it is FF FF (also each mark of a leading run); after a kana it takes the primary of that kana's vowel, the kana primaries running in rows of eight from 02 (キ 7F 0B → イ 7F 03, ッ 7F 1C → ウ 7F 04, ャ 7F 3A → ア 7F 02; ン 7F 80 keeps 7F 80; hiragana, halfwidth, small and voiced kana alike), copies the kana's later flag (ャー: A8) and adds a kana-level stream after the later flags' FF, packed like them with each flagged character 1 and the mark 3, trailing 1s dropped (キー 9C, キイー 97, キーー 9F, キーア 9C); after any other character it repeats that character's primary (1ー 38 38, bー 4C 4C, éー 51 51); its secondary is a placeholder.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB created with dbLangGeneral (1033/0), CREATE INDEX after the rows; keys read from the frozen files by CalcDb's format reader (`NativeTextKeyTests.Designed_b999_entries_equal_the_encoder`: 221 of 221 entries; `Native_General_family_entries_equal_the_encoder`: 15,133 of 15,133, the former 36-entry residual included).
- **Records**: `evidence/r-sort-3c/create-accdb/`, `create-mdb/` (fixtures r-sort-3c-accdb, r-sort-3c-mdb), `order-accdb/`, `order-mdb/` (ACE's index order and Seek equality); `evidence/r-sort-3d/create-accdb/`, `order-accdb/` (the mark after a, k, t, h, y rows, ン, small, voiced, hiragana, halfwidth, doubled, Latin, digit, leading); the corpus test's 15,133 entries as the control. Discriminating: キー against アー (vowel, not always ア), 1ー against aー (repeat, not A's weight), ーキ against キー (no kana level for a leading mark).
- **Open**: the s, n, m, r and w rows and ヴ (the row-of-eight rule is not asked there); a mark after a non-kana flagged character and a mark in a value mixing kana with flagged Latin (the kana level of those characters); whether a mark counts in the suffix's r over long values; General 1033/1's mark (no fixture); General 97 (no CreateDatabase locale or version known to give it); expansions under General 1033/1.

### R-SORT-3 Positions and controls (public equality): NUL and trailing spaces carry no weight, most other controls and white space do

- **Claim**: in a General key (dbVersion 128) an embedded, leading or trailing U+0000 is stored as given but ignored ("a" NUL "b" = "ab"; "a" NUL and NUL "a" equal "a"); trailing ordinary spaces are ignored ("a", "a ", "a  ") while a leading space or a leading tab is a different key sorting before "a"; U+0001, U+001F, U+007F after "a", the soft hyphen, a no-break space, tab, LF, CR, CR LF, a zero-width space, U+2028 and U+3000 after "a" each make their own class (the controls sort just after "a", then the soft hyphen; the ideographic space, "a b", the no-break space, tab, LF, CR and U+2028 sort among the punctuation before "ab"); a decomposed acute equals the precomposed one and case ("a" + U+0301 = "A" + U+0301 = á); two different combining marks give the same class in either order; a leading combining mark is its own class. An indexed table and an unindexed copy agree.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB (controls probe) and ACCDB/MDB (equality probes), counted BSTRs.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/controls-accdb/` (re-read: 32 values, GROUP BY classes and ORDER BY on both tables); `evidence/r-sort-3/equality-accdb/`, `equality-mdb/`; `evidence/r-sort-3b/create-lg/`. Discriminators: each unit appended to the same base letter one at a time.
- **Open**: which unit advances a position or sets the secondary weight is bytes (T).

### R-SORT-3 UTF-16 boundaries (field length and stored units): the 255-unit Text limit counts UTF-16 units and lone surrogates are stored as given

- **Claim**: a Text(255) key of 255 UTF-16 units (253 letters plus a pair, or 254 letters plus one unit, including a lone high surrogate) is accepted and one of 256 units is refused with 3163; the refused row stays as an ID-only row. A pair counts two units, a lone surrogate one (LEN), and the stored units are the units written. A Seek `=` for a 256-unit key gives NoMatch. In dbVersion 64 and 128 files A x 253 equals A x 253 + U+20000 and A x 254 equals A x 254 + U+D840, while A x 253, A x 254 and A x 255 are mutually distinct; in a dbVersion 256 file the supplementary character has weight (see Descriptor dispatch, file version).
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB, counted BSTR puts through a table recordset.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-sort-3/equality-accdb/`, `equality-mdb/`, `seek-accdb/`, `create-corpus-*/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/text-units-accdb/`, `-mdb/` (rows 9 and 10: LEN 255); `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/v256-corpus-accdb/`.
- **Open**: mixed row compression is bytes (T).

### R-SORT-3 Typed key order (Integer and Money, Floating and DATE, NUMERIC / GUID): typed index walks follow numeric order with Nulls first; -0 and +0 are separate keys, and a -0 predicate depends on the index

- **Claim**: forward walks of single-column indexes list Null rows first and then Byte 0, 1, 127, 128, 255; Integer -32768 to 32767; Long minimum to maximum; Currency in numeric order; Decimal(18,4) in numeric order; Date by its double value (-1.5 < -1 < -0.5 < -0 < +0 < 0.5 < 1.5). Single and Double follow the sign-magnitude IEEE order: the negative-sign NaN first, -Infinity, finite values with -0 before +0, +Infinity, then positive-sign NaNs (Double; Single's one NaN sorts first). GUIDs order by the textual hexadecimal digits left to right: 16 GUIDs with the byte 0x01 at each of the 16 byte positions rank from the last textual byte (smallest) to the first (largest), a byte 0x80 at a position ranks just above 0x01 at the same position, all-zero is smallest and all-FF largest, and SQL ORDER BY G gives the same order. -0 and +0 are two values in GROUP BY, DISTINCT, ORDER BY and the index, but without an index the predicates `D = 0`, `D > 0` and `D < 0` treat them as equal (`= 0` returns both rows, `< 0` none), and with an index on D `= 0` returns only +0 and `< 0` returns -0; Single compares them equal (no index). The backward walks of Double and Currency are the exact reverses. ACCDB and MDB agree.
- **Conditions**: one index per column built after the rows, table-recordset walk reading ID; values by bit pattern; the -0 probe reads the same predicates before and after `CREATE INDEX`.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-sort-3/create-nums-accdb/` (re-read: the forward walks and the backward IX_D and IX_C), `create-nums-mdb/`; `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/sort3-nums-decimal-accdb/`, `-mdb/`; `evidence/r-sort-3b/mix-order-n/`, `mix-order-r/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/guid-order-accdb/` (re-read: walk 1 17 23 16 15 14 ... 2 18 24 and the same SQL order), `neg-zero-accdb/` (re-read). Discriminators: NaN of both signs and -0 separately; every GUID byte position one at a time; the same predicate before and after the index.
- **Built (`w21-expr-find-sort`, `e9b3bb97`/`8622648f`, 2026-11-28)**: Implemented SQL bare GUID `ORDER BY` (name/ordinal, `ScalarCodecs.GuidTextOrder`), 510B `IndexKeys.FinishComposite` SQL `ORDER BY` encoding (`r-sort-3/keys-order-sql-{accdb,mdb}`), sign-magnitude `Double.IsNaN` ordering in `RelationalOrder`, missing-operator `WHERE` prefix clause excerpts (`r-cursor-1b/filter-expression-forms` #203), and 0-mismatch executor replay across `mix-order-{b,c,d,n,r,y}`, `lg-sql-order`, and `memo-order-sql` (`CalcDb.Sql.Tests` `1,761/1,761`, `166,520` values matched). Open: `NotModelled.RowOrder` >9-row snapshot ties, derived GUID `ORDER BY`, mixed-scale `Decimal` 510B truncation, and `NotModelled.FindIndexedText`.
- **Open**: the DATETIME2 (extended date) index does not order chronologically: with values written by two routes the walk and ORDER BY V give the order 14 15 | 101 102 103 1 2 3 | 104..110 | 111..113 | 4..10 | 11..13, rows of the SQL-literal route ahead of equal or later values of the put route; the cause is not established and needs key bytes (T). Descending numeric indexes; key bytes and the legacy versus corrected NUMERIC mode are T.

### R-SORT-3 / R-FMT-2 NUMERIC, GUID, Binary and Extended Date/Time bytes: ascending keys are II-05 §4's forms; the NUMERIC payload is II-04 §5.3's; the Extended Date/Time digits are not II-04 §5.5's

- **Claim**: every entry of every physical index with a NUMERIC, GUID, Binary or Extended Date/Time column in the retained database fixtures equals II-05 §4's ascending form built from the row: NUMERIC `7F`, then `FF` and the 16-byte big-endian magnitude at the column scale for a nonnegative value or `00` and its complement for a negative one; GUID, Binary and Extended Date/Time `7F` then 8-byte chunks, `09` after each but the last, the last zero-padded and ended by its byte count, a GUID in its text order (the row's first three components reversed), an Extended Date/Time as its 42 row bytes. 1,861 entries in 272 indexes: Decimal(18,4) from -12345678901234.5678 to 12345678901234.5678 and Decimal(38,0) -1, 0, 1, 7, ACCDB and MDB alike; (Text, GUID) and GUID indexes; MSysQueries' (Long, Byte, Binary) index in every database. The 17-byte NUMERIC payload is a sign byte (00, 80) and four little-endian words most significant first, scale and precision in the column block at 11 and 12; the values equal the inserted literals. An Extended Date/Time payload is 15 day digits, a colon, 12 second and 7 fraction digits, `:7` and five NULs, not the pinned reference's 19-digit form (book-proposals.md, II-04 §5.5).
- **Conditions**: ascending single and compound indexes only; fixtures built by DAO, SQL literals and ADO DDL; no empty Binary value.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/sort3-nums-decimal-accdb/`, `-mdb/`; `evidence/r-sort-3/create-nums-accdb/`, `create-nums-mdb/` (IX_X, IX_GU, IX_DX), `create-keys-accdb/` (IX_KG); `evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/create-cx-types-accdb/` (complex value tables); every retained database for MSysQueries. Tests: `NativeTextKeyTests.Native_NUMERIC_GUID_Binary_and_extended_date_entries_equal_the_encoder`, `NativeScalarValueTests`. Discriminators: negative, zero and multi-word magnitudes; both formats.
- **Open**: Extended Date/Time layout across write routes (`r-q49` closes the SQL 19-digit vs DAO 15-digit encoding); noncanonical NUMERIC sign bytes; non-null empty Binary value (DAO `Bytes()`/`""` store Null).
- **Descending `NUMERIC`/`Binary` & `Seek` arguments (`r-q51-codecs-props-compact`, `jqv9`)**:
  - *Descending `DECIMAL(18,4)` (`0x10`) & `BINARY`/`VARBINARY(16)` (`0x09`)*: ACE `16.0.20625.20044` writes **identical** corrected descending `NUMERIC` keys (`80 00 || ~M` nonnegative, `80 FF || M` negative, `FF` Null) on both ACCDB and Jet 4 MDB (closing `DescendingNotDecided` on NUMERIC/Binary; `dec-desc-keys-{accdb,mdb}`). `Bytes()` (0 B) and `""` store Null (`00` ASC / `FF` DESC); `BINARY(16)` zero-pads to 16 B (19 B key); `VARBINARY(16)` writes 1..16 B chunks (`bin-desc-keys-{accdb,mdb}`), and DESC complements data, pad (`00->FF`), and final count (`01..08->FE..F7`) while keeping continuation `0x09` uncomplemented.
  - *DAO `Seek("=", ...)` argument forms*: `GUID` accepts only `{guid {...}}` (case-insensitive), `{...}`, `Null`, and `Empty` (unbraced strings, 16-byte arrays, empty arrays, invalid strings, and `I4` fail `3421`, `seek-args-guid-{accdb,mdb}`); `BINARY(16)` / `VARBINARY(16)` emit zero `3421`s (`Null`/`Empty`/`Bytes()`/`""` seek Null; `BINARY(16)` matches exact 16 B zero-padded array; `VARBINARY(16)` truncates odd byte lengths `len & ~1` so 9 B `01..09` matches 8 B `01..08`, and BSTR `"ABC"` matches UTF-16LE `41 00 42 00 43 00`, `seek-args-binary-{accdb,mdb}`); `DECIMAL(18,4)` coerces **every** argument (including `VT_DECIMAL` Field/strings/numbers) to `Null` with zero `3421`s (`seek-args-decimal-{accdb,mdb}`).

### R-SORT-3 IndexKeyType DDL (`r-q44-ddl-alter-types/ixtype-*`)

- **Claim**: `CREATE [UNIQUE] INDEX` succeeds on `Double` (`0x07`, ASC/DESC; `-0.0` `7f7f...` and `+0.0` `7f80...` produce distinct keys and coexist in a UNIQUE index), `DateTime` (`0x08`, ASC/DESC), `GUID` (`0x0F`, ASC `7f <chunk0:8B> 09 <chunk1:8B> 08`, DESC `80 <~chunk0:8B> 09 <~chunk1:8B> f7` — continuation byte `0x09` is NOT complemented while final count byte `0x08 -> 0xF7` IS complemented), and `Memo` (`0x0C`, ACCDB & MDB: indexes first 255 UTF-16 units via General sort weights `7f <weights> 01 00`; `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX` fails `3022` `0x800A0BCE` when two memos share their first 255 characters, stepping `0xE02` +2). (`OLE`/`LONGBINARY` `0x0B` still fails `3409`.)
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q44-ddl-alter-types/ixtype-*` (12 probes + `a2` repeats, ACCDB & MDB, DAO & ADO).
- **Built (`alta/r-q44-ddl-types`, `3044559e`)**: single-column DAO/SQL `CREATE [UNIQUE] INDEX` on `Double`, `DateTime`, `GUID` (ASC/DESC), and `Memo` (ASC; single/chained LVAL key reads; `UNIQUE` 255-char prefix collision `3022` with `0xE02 +2`; MDB sole free-map LVAL append `>= 264 B` free) across ACCDB & Jet 4 MDB (`12/12` `ixtype-*` Matched).
- **Open**: `Memo DESC`; succeeding `UNIQUE Memo` with a Null row; ADO / multi-column / `PRIMARY` / `DISALLOW NULL` / `IGNORE NULL` / `ALTER TABLE ADD CONSTRAINT` on `Double`/`DateTime`/`GUID`/`Memo`; `Memo` sort keys with non-default collation flags or Jet 4 legacy text sort outside General sort weights.

### R-SORT-3 Null policy / aliases (public): a Null never collides, an all-Null tuple can be left out of the index, and the refusals are 3022 and 3058

- **Claim**: a plain unique index on K keeps every Null-K row; a compound unique (K, N) keeps rows with a Null in either field and refuses only repeated non-null tuples; `IGNORE NULL` on a single column keeps Null rows out of the index walk, on a compound it leaves out only all-Null tuples; `DISALLOW NULL` and a primary key refuse a Null with 3058 (also at creation on existing Null rows, where no index is left); duplicates refuse with 3022; a NOT NULL column refuses with 3314. DAO indexes made by property combination, setting each property only when True: Primary reads back Unique and Required as well; Unique + Required refuses a duplicate (3022) and a Null (3058); Unique + IgnoreNulls admits Nulls and refuses a duplicate; Required alone admits duplicates and refuses Nulls; Unique + Required + IgnoreNulls still refuses Nulls (Required wins); a Primary index appended to a table that already has a primary key is refused with 3283.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB (and MDB for the NP tables); refusals read with dbFailOnError and a marked Errors.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/sort3b-nulls-refusals/`, `sort3b-existing-nulls-isolated/`, `sort3b-dao-index-flags/`, `sort3-np-accdb/`, `sort3-np-mdb/`; `evidence/r-sort-3b/nulls-walk-*`, `nulls-survivors/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/dao-index-primary-accdb/` (re-read: seven tables, flags and survivors). Discriminators: each property combination on its own table; before-rows versus after-rows builds.
- **Open**: plan eligibility of IGNORE NULL (needs a plan view, T). Aliases sharing one tree are bytes (T).

### R-SORT-3 Traversal consequences (Seek operators): Seek lands on the first or last row of a group by a fixed rule

- **Claim**: on a Text index with duplicate classes, Seek `=`, `>=` and `<=` land on the first row of the equal group in index order, `>` on the first row of the next group, `<` on the last row of the previous group, `=` on an absent key sets NoMatch (a later read fails with 3021), and `>=` of an absent key lands on the next present key; a Long, Integer, Byte, Double, Single or Currency key equal to 1 finds the text key "1", Empty and Null find the Null row, Boolean True and Date 1.5 with `=` give NoMatch, two keys on a one-column index give 0x800A0CB2. ADO adSeekFirstEQ lands on the lowest class ID, adSeekLastEQ the highest. A Null component for a Yes/No key raises 0x800A0C5A.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB (and MDB twin), table-type recordset on a named index, ADO 10.0 over Microsoft.ACE.OLEDB.12.0 for the ADO lane. The "first row of the group" is the first in the index's tie order, that is insertion order (tie-order note); the earlier "lowest ID" equals it only because those tables were filled in ID order.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-sort-3/seek-accdb/`, `seek-mdb/`, `ado-order-seek-accdb/`; `evidence/r-sort-3b/lg-seek-ops/`, `lg-seek-coercion/`, `lu-seek-ops/`, `mix-seek-*/`. Discriminators: each operator and key type separately; descending index varied.
- **Open**: Seek on a long key (the earlier skipped `>=` for the é x 255 group) belongs to the Length510 note; cursor state transitions are R-CURSOR-1.

### R-SORT-3 Traversal consequences (tie order): equal keys walk in insertion order, backward in the reverse, and SQL ORDER BY lists them in the reverse of insertion order

- **Reopened and narrowed (2026-11-20, W-06/parser-binder-scan replay; r-q3-sql)**: `evidence/r-sql-1/order-nulls-text/` lists equal ORDER BY keys in insertion order where the claim above gives reverse insertion order. The discriminator (`evidence/r-q3-sql/ties-*`: the tie-order rows, the same rows inserted in ID order, equal keys of two cases, Null keys, and 50, 200 and 1000 scrambled rows, each ascending and descending, as snapshot and dynaset) shows the recordset type decides: a **dynaset** lists the rows of equal keys in insertion (storage) order ascending and in its reverse descending, at every size asked (9 to 1000 rows); a **snapshot** lists them in reverse insertion order on 9 rows in both directions (the claim above, which was asked as a snapshot) and in a permutation that is neither order on 50, 200 and 1000 rows, not identified. Case variants and Null keys order as equal texts do. Open: the snapshot permutation beyond 9 rows (vary the row count between 9 and 50 one at a time); whether storage order or insertion order decides after a delete and reinsert on a dynaset.

- **Claim**: with equal keys inserted with IDs in a scrambled order (5, 3, 9, 1, 7 for key "a"; 8, 2, 6 for "b"), the index walk lists 5 3 9 1 7 8 2 6 4 (insertion order within each key), the backward walk is the exact reverse, an index built before the rows and one built after give the same walk, and SQL `ORDER BY K` on the indexed tables and on an unindexed copy lists 7 1 9 3 5 6 2 8 4 (each key's rows in reverse insertion order) with `ORDER BY K DESC` keeping the same relative order inside a key (4 6 2 8 7 1 9 3 5) and `ORDER BY K, ID` ordering by ID. After deleting IDs 3 and 7 and inserting IDs 3 and 11 (key "a"), the walk is 5 9 1 3 11: the surviving rows keep their order and the new rows follow in insertion order. ACCDB and MDB give identical sequences. ID plays no part.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB, SQL inserts in the stated order, walk by table-recordset on the named index.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/tie-order-accdb/`, `tie-order-mdb/` (re-read: the walk and SQL sequences, identical in both formats); `evidence/r-sort-3/maintain-*/`; `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/sort3b-image-drop-rebuild/`. Discriminators: insertion order scrambled against ID order; index before versus after the rows; delete and reinsert.
- **Open**: whether a reinserted row can take the position of a deleted one when pages are reused (not seen here); SQL's reverse-insertion tie order on a larger table.

### R-SORT-3 Compound framing (ordinary lengths): a compound index orders by its fields in turn, each with its own direction, and the index walk equals SQL ORDER BY modulo ties

- **Claim**: for each of five compound indexes (K,V1), (V1,K), (K desc,V1), (K,V1 desc), (K desc,V1 desc), over keys a, A, b, é and Null and partner values Long, Date, Currency, Double, Yes/No and Byte (including Null, negative, zero and extremes), the forward walk lists the same order as the SQL ORDER BY of the same fields except for ties (same class and same partner value), which the index lists in insertion order (these tables were filled in ID order); the backward walk is the exact reverse; an incrementally built index walks the same as a fresh one. -0 and +0 are different partner keys in the Double table and Seek separates them.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, keys at most 20 units; read-only reopen.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-sort-3b/mix-order-n/` (re-read: the five walks equal their SQL orders modulo the a/A ties), `mix-order-r/`, `mix-order-d/`, `mix-order-c/`, `mix-order-y/`, `mix-order-b/`, `mix-seek-*/`; `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/sort3-fr-accdb/`, `-mdb/`.
- **Open**: keys near 255 units (next note). Field-framing bytes are T.

### R-SORT-3 Length510 boundary and Shortened-key collisions: a key whose primary weights reach about 509 bytes loses the order of its tail, and its last unit is ordered by a digest, not by the comparator

- **Claim**: for K = "a" x L in a non-unique index on (K, N), rows inserted N = 2 then N = 1, the walk puts N = 1 first at every L from 100 to 255, in ACCDB and MDB. For é, ß and Han the walk puts N = 1 first up to L = 252 and N = 2 (the earlier inserted row) first from L = 253 (é: also at 255 but not at 254; ß: at 253, 254 and 255; Han: at 253 only, while for 254 and 255 the N order is right and the 255-unit group sorts before the 254-unit group). SQL `ORDER BY K, N` on the same table equals the index walk at every length. A unique index on (K, N) accepts both (K, 1) and (K, 2) at every length and refuses only the true duplicate, and a unique index on K accepts K x (L-1) + 'x' and + 'y' for all four characters, refusing only the exact duplicate (3022); the unique index in the collision probes also kept all five rows. A pair that differs only in its last unit collides when the General primary weights of the key reach about 509 bytes: ß or æ (2 weights each) repeated 254 times + 'x' / 'y' (255 units), and U+FB03 (3 weights) already at 200 units; ß x 253 + 'x' / 'y' (254 units) and every shorter ß length from 200 do not; a decomposed e + U+0301 x 127 (254 units, one primary weight each) does not. For a colliding pair: `ORDER BY K` on the indexed table and on an unindexed copy and the index walk list the 'y' row before the 'x' row (for U+FB03 also the control pair at 200 units), while `A.K < B.K` says x < y (the comparator is right); the equality join `A.K = B.K` returns the pair on the indexed table but not on the unindexed copy (ß, æ and, for U+FB03, the 200-unit pair); and for U+FB03 `WHERE K = (SELECT K ... WHERE ID = 1)` on the indexed table returns no row at all, while the unindexed copy returns the row. Seek `=` finds each of the two rows by its own key (ß, æ, U+FB03, decomposed e); Seek `>` follows the index order. The ß result is the same in ACCDB and MDB.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB (a, é, ß, Han, æ, U+FB03, e + U+0301) and MDB (a, ß), General dbVersion 128/64; tables LA (non-unique (K,N)), LB (unique (K,N) plus a duplicate), LC and UL (unique K), LD and L (non-unique K, SQL pairs, Seek), L0 (unindexed copy); every refusal read with a marked Errors.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/len-series-a-accdb/`, `len-series-a-mdb-mdb/`, `len-series-e-acute-accdb/`, `len-series-sharp-s-accdb/`, `len-series-han-accdb/`; `evidence/r-q2-fmt-sort/ntail-bracket-e-acute-accdb/`, `ntail-bracket-sharp-s-accdb/`, `ntail-bracket-han-accdb/` (re-read: lengths 248 to 255, walk, backward walk and SQL order), `collide-bracket-sharp-s-accdb/` (re-read: heads 200 to 254, only the 254 pair collides), `collide-expansion-sharp-s/`, `collide-expansion-sharp-s-mdb/`, `collide-expansion-ae-ligature/`, `collide-expansion-ffi-ligature/`, `collide-expansion-decomposed-e/` (re-read); `evidence/r-sort-3/keys-order-index-*/`, `keys-uniqueness-meta-*/`, `keys-seek-*/`. Discriminators: character kind and expansion factor varied with length fixed; length varied from 100 to 255 with the character fixed; indexed table against its unindexed copy; equality, `<`, unique index and Seek on the same pair.
- **Open**: the digest and the retained key bytes, and why é at 254 and Han at 254 and 255 behave differently from 253, need key bytes (T). The earlier statement that the N tail "falls to insertion order" is withdrawn (Corrections 7): a colliding pair is ordered y before x though x was inserted first, so the keys differ and the order is not insertion order.

### R-SORT-3 Length510 boundary, Memo keys: a Memo index, unique index, GROUP BY and DistinctCount compare only the first 255 units

- **Claim**: a unique index on a Memo column is created on an empty table; two Memo values equal in their first 255 UTF-16 units are one key: m x 255 + 'x' (256 units) collides with + 'y' and with all longer values with the same 255-unit prefix, refused with 3022 (ten rows refused), while m x 254 + 'x' and + 'y' (255 units) and a value changed at the first unit are distinct. The non-unique index on the same 15 rows reads DistinctCount 6, GROUP BY on the Memo column gives the same six classes, and the index walk lists the colliding rows together. A join on a Memo column is refused (0x80020009).
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, ASCII 'm' only, lengths 100, 254, 255, 256, 257, 300, 1000.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/memo-prefix-accdb/` (re-read: refusals for IDs 5 to 14, DistinctCount 6 for IX_M and UX_M, GROUP BY classes); `evidence/r-sort-3b/memo-index-ddl/`, `memo-text255-key/`. Discriminators: change at the last unit versus the first, at 254 and 255.
- **Open**: wide characters (255 units versus 255 characters); Memo values in a compound key.

### R-SORT-3 LIKE-RANGE-01/05: a LIKE result set depends on whether an ascending index on the column exists

- **Claim**: on one table, the same LIKE patterns give different row sets with and without an ascending index on K, in ACCDB: with the index, `K LIKE 'a*'` also returns æ (ID 8) and `'e*'` loses the decomposed é (ID 13) while `'é*'` gains it, `'s*'` gains ß, `'f*'` the fi ligature, `'o*'` the œ ligature (the range of a collation-order scan); without it, the pattern is matched unit by unit. A descending-only index changes nothing; dropping the index restores the first results and creating it again restores the second (a repeat); `'a?'` is the same in all states; `K = 'a'` returns IDs 1 and 2 in all states.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, one 30-row table whose only changing factor is the index (none, IX_K, none, IX_KD descending, none, IX_K), the same ten patterns after each step.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/like-index-accdb/` (re-read); `evidence/r-sort-3b/lg-like-vs-range/`, `lu-like-vs-range/`, `lg-ado-like/`; `evidence/r-sort-3/like-prefix-accdb/`. Discriminators: the index added and dropped on one table, descending versus ascending, a repeat of the state.
- **Open**: unique indexes and compound indexes with K first; plan views are T.
- **Built (`w17-sql-like-range`, `6170f9e3`, 2026-11-27):** `CollationLikePrefix` (`GeneralLegacy` `1033/0` single-column non-unique ascending Text index for `<prefix>*` across primary, unstripped secondary, level-3 small-kana flags, and level-4 `firstOffset == 0`), with descending-only and non-prefix patterns unit-by-unit (`r-q-fmt-sort/like-index-accdb`, `r-sort-3/like-prefix-accdb`, `r-sort-3b/lg-like-vs-range`, `r-cursor-1b/filter-expression-forms`); unique, compound, `IgnoreNulls`, Memo, `General` (`1033/1`), and unrecorded pattern shapes stay refused (`NotModelled.IndexedLike`).

## R-CAT-1, R-TXN-1, R-LOCK-1 to R-LOCK-4 and R-CRASH-1

### R-CAT-1 CAT-NEW: the Option, not the name, chooses the format; refusals are fixed
- **Claim**: `CreateDatabase` gives Version "12.0" for Option 128, as VT_I2, BSTR "128" or VT_R8 128, and when Option is omitted, for `.accdb` and `.mdb` names alike (an `.mdb` name with 128 also gives "12.0"); Option 64 and 192 (64+128) give "4.0" (`Workspace.CreateDatabase` the same); Option 2 (dbEncrypt alone) fails 3001; 32 fails 3170; an existing destination 3204 (file unchanged); a call inside `BeginTrans` 3246 (no file). CollatingOrder is 1033 for the General string used.
- **Conditions**: DAO `DBEngine` and `Workspace.CreateDatabase`, locale `;LANGID=0x0409;CP=1252;COUNTRY=0`, ACE 16.0.20527.20196.
- **Records**: q1/cat-new-argument-forms/ (every form; the .mdb-with-omitted and .mdb-with-128 variants discriminate name from option); evidence/r-cat-1/create-accdb-empty/, create-accdb-option-omitted/, create-accdb-workspace-route/, create-accdb-ext-v40/, create-mdb-empty/, create-mdb-version30/, create-accdb-existing-destination/, create-accdb-in-transaction/.
- **Open**: file sizes differ by version (172,032 B for 12.0, 77,824 B for 4.0, byte classes are W-04); locale strings beyond General, passwords (T).

### R-CAT-1 CAT-B: name rules in every namespace; TableDefs.Item is the odd lookup
- **Claim**: Table, field, index, relation and query names compare without case: duplicate table 3010, field 3191 through an attached `Fields.Append`, index 3375, relation 3012, query 3012 (`Tbl`/`TBL`, `Aa`/`aA`, U+00E9/U+00C9 pairs, `IxA`/`ixa`). A duplicate field through SQL `CREATE TABLE` fails 3380, recorded for exact and ASCII-case pairs (r-def-expr-sql/ddl-column-clauses Cc20 and Cc21, r-cat-1/names C2). Accents are not ignored: `Tä` and `Té` are distinct table names, and no record tests `e` against `é` (corrected 2026-11-21, f8g8 review: the claim said accent-insensitively); a leading space and `. ! [ ]` or a backquote fail 3125; a trailing space is kept; 64 characters are accepted, 65 fail 3001 at `CreateTableDef`, `CreateField` and `CreateRelation` but 3125 for an index name. Precomposed U+00E9 and decomposed `e`+U+0301 are one name for Append, `CREATE TABLE` (3010), `SELECT * FROM [..]` and `DROP TABLE`, but `TableDefs.Item("..")` finds only the spelling stored (3265 for the other), in both creation orders.
- **Conditions**: DAO/SQL on ACCDB, collections refreshed.
- **Records**: q1/cat-b-names-fields-indexes-relations/, q1/cat-b-names-normalization/ (both orders discriminate); evidence/r-cat-1/names/.
- **Open**: MSys rows and IDs (3112, W-04); non-General collations; other accented pairs; field pairs through `CREATE TABLE`, draft `TableDefs.Append` and ADO that differ in non-ASCII case, NFC/NFD form, an accent or a non-ASCII letter (`Fé`/`FÉ`, `e`/`é`, `o`/`ø`, `ß`/`ss`), which SchemaWriter refuses by name; the duplicate-field error of draft `TableDefs.Append` and of ADO.

### R-CAT-1 CAT-P: custom properties on every owner, and their rollback
- **Claim**: A custom Long property appended to the Database, a TableDef, a Field, an Index and a QueryDef is read back (Type 4, value) after closing and reopening read-only. Under `BeginTrans`, setting the Database property to 999 and appending a second property are visible in the level; after `Rollback` and `Properties.Refresh` the original value (123456) is back and the new property gives 3270, on the same handle and on a second Workspace. Input grammar: Null/Empty/`""`/empty bytes 3385, missing value 3386, duplicate 3367, type 99 3259, 65-character name 3001, `Type` change 3219, deleting a built-in 3384; Boolean coerces from numbers; types Boolean..GUID round-trip.
- **Records**: q1/cat-p-custom-properties-all-owners-rollback/ (all owners; Rollback with Refresh); evidence/r-cat-1/properties/, property-inputs/, property-edits/; evidence/r-cat-1b/field-access-property-append/.
- **Open**: LvProp bytes, flag80 (T).

### R-CAT-1 CAT-Q: saved-query public view and the save-time rendering
- **Claim**: On save, `CreateQueryDef` re-renders SQL: keywords upper-cased, one clause per line with CR LF, a closing `;` plus CR LF, spaces collapsed, identifier case kept (`t.id` stays), `ORDER BY t.id DESC , t.a`, `DELETE` becomes `DELETE *`, `INSERT INTO U ( ID, X )`, a two-predicate `ON T.ID = U.ID AND T.A = U.A` becomes `ON (T.A = U.A) AND (T.ID = U.ID)` (one sample, order reversed), a UNION text is kept as written plus `;`, an embedded tab and a string with `;` survive. The reopened text equals the creating handle's. After reopen `Type` is select 0, union 128, update 48, delete 32, insert 64, crosstab 16 (0 on the creating handle for action queries as before); `ReturnsRecords` -1 for every shape; `Execute` on a select 3065, `OpenRecordset` on an action 3219; getter text set back is stored verbatim until reopen; no U+0000; `PARAMETERS` order fixes Parameters/Fields order; invalid SQL is refused at create (3129/3142/3075).
- **Records**: q1/cat-q-sql-reformat-matrix/ (16 shapes, created and reopened); evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/qd-view-writable-reopen/, qd-create-invalid-sql-errors/; evidence/r-cat-1/querydefs/, sql-round-trip/, order-variants/; evidence/r-cat-1b/qd-execute-on-row-returning/, qd-openrecordset-on-action/.
- **Open**: whether the ON reordering is a rule (one sample); MSysQueries Order bytes (W-04); pass-through execution (ODBC forbidden). Not a universal serializer.

### R-CAT-1 CAT-Q (catalog side): MSysQueries role rows and the SQL a reopened file shows
- **Claim**: A saved query is an MSysObjects row of Type 5 whose Flags masked with 0xF0 is DAO `QueryDef.Type` itself (select 0, crosstab 0x10, delete 0x20, update 0x30, append 0x40, make-table 0x50, DDL 0x60, pass-through 0x70, union 0x80), with MSysQueries rows by ObjectId: Attribute (Byte), Expression (Memo), Flag (Integer), LvExtra (Long), Name1 and Name2 (Text), Order (Binary, four bytes 00000001, 00000002 ... per attribute in encounter order). Every query has a start row (0) and an end row (255); a SELECT has no type row (attribute 1). The reopened `.SQL` is a rendering of those rows: an optional `PARAMETERS n T, ...;` line (Integer as `Short`, Long, `Text ( n )` with n from LvExtra), `SELECT [DISTINCT |DISTINCTROW |TOP n [PERCENT] ]` from option bits 2, 8, 0x10, 0x20 with the TOP count in the options row's Name1, outputs `expr[ AS Name1]` joined by `, `, `INTO target` (make-table, type-row Name1), `INSERT INTO t ( c1, c2 )` (append, output Name2 as target columns), `UPDATE t SET c = e` (update, output Name2), `DELETE *`, `FROM t` or `FROM a INNER|LEFT JOIN b ON e` (join Flag 1 or 2), `WHERE`, `GROUP BY`, `HAVING`, crosstab `TRANSFORM v AS a` (output Flag 0), group outputs (Flag 2) and `PIVOT p` (Flag 1), `ORDER BY` with Name1 `d` for DESC written `x DESC , y`, each clause on its own line after CR LF, then `;` CR LF. DDL and pass-through show the type-row Expression alone; pass-through `Connect` is the type-row Name1. UNION is the two fragment rows (Name2 `X7YZ_____1`, `X7YZ_____2`) joined by ` UNION ALL ` (no option bit 2), the first fragment's trailing blank dropped, then `;` CR LF. Stored text is shown verbatim (an alias stored as `Bä€` stays unbracketed).
- **Conditions**: ACCDB made by DAO `CreateQueryDef` and reopened read-only; 28 shapes in one fixture.
- **Records**: evidence/r-cat-1/querydefs (every query's Type and SQL; `SavedQueryTests` decodes the fixture's rows and reproduces all 28 strings).
- **Open**: separators and spellings not shown by these 28 shapes, each refused by name in `QuerySql` until a probe shows it: several GROUP BY terms, two ascending ORDER BY terms, a descending last term, several UPDATE assignments, a source alias, several sources without a join, RIGHT JOIN, `SELECT *` beside other outputs, OWNERACCESS, parameter types other than Short, Long and Text, UNION ALL versus UNION through bit 2 alone (only the ALL case is in this fixture).

### R-CAT-1 CAT-P (blob side): MR2 property blobs on the catalog rows
- **Claim**: `LvProp` holds the F-40 grammar: `MR2\0`, then sections (4-byte length, 2-byte kind): a names section (0x80) of length-prefixed UTF-16 names, then value sections (0x0000 the object's own map, 0x0001 a column's map named by the column) each with a map-name block and records (2-byte length, flags, native type, 2-byte name ordinal, 2-byte payload length, payload). The Database's own stored properties are on the `MSysDb` row (Type 2) under the `Databases` container; a table's column properties (Required, AllowZeroLength, DefaultValue, ValidationRule, ValidationText, Description) are in its column maps; a QueryDef stores ODBCTimeout (Integer 60) and MaxRecords (Long 0) with flag 01 in its own map; user properties have flag 00. A stored property's native type code is its DAO `Property.Type`; Text payloads are uncompressed UTF-16, Boolean one byte.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB made by DAO (r-dao-1b rich fixture), read through the bounded directory reader.
- **Records**: evidence/r-dao-1b/db-properties-enumerated-accdb (UPText ... UPMemo after the built-ins), tabledef-properties-enumerated (Parent's Description), field-properties-enumerated (TText DefaultValue `"dflt"`, AllowZeroLength True; Parent.Name lists 25); `PropertyBlobTests` decodes the same fixture's blobs.
- **Open**: index maps (kind 2) and repeated names sections are not in any retained file read so far; the trailing U+0000 of a stored ValidationRule (TInt `>= -10` plus NUL) as DAO returns it.

### R-CAT-1 CAT-P, II-04 §7.1: a table row's LvProp is stored inline up to 64 bytes and on an LVAL page from 65
- **Claim**: The MSysObjects row of a new table keeps its `LvProp` blob inline (the 12-byte long-value header with the inline flag, then the payload in the row) when the blob is at most 64 bytes, and on a new LVAL page (one more data page, the file one page longer, the row 77 bytes) when it is 65 bytes or more. The row's own length does not decide: a 40-letter table name makes the row 78 bytes longer and leaves every case on the same side. The DAO route's blob for one Long field is 49 + 2 x (field name length) bytes (MR2, names section `Required`, one column map named by the field, Required False); `CREATE TABLE ... NOT NULL` writes the same blob, and plain `CREATE TABLE` writes none (row 65 bytes). Inline row lengths: 128 (51), 130 (53), 134 (57), 136 (59), 138 (61), 140 (63); 64 bytes inline (row 141) and 65, 66 on an LVAL page on the rewrite path (a custom property appended to the stored field).
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, copies of r-cat-1-accdb-empty, DAO `TableDefs.Append` and `Database.Execute`, one session each; the row read from MSysObjects page 17 slot 18.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q15-ddl-open/` lv-dao-name-2, -4, -5, -6, -7 (inline; -7 twice), -8 (LVAL, twice), -16, -32, -64; lv-dao-name-1, -4, -5, -6, -7-table-40 (inline: the row-size discriminator); lv-dao-fields-2, -3, -4, -6 (74 bytes and up, LVAL); lv-sql-notnull-1, -4 (inline), -8, -16, -64 (LVAL); lv-sql-plain-64 (no blob); lv-rewrite-byte-n (64, inline, twice), lv-rewrite-byte-nn (66), lv-rewrite-text-n (65). A draft Field refuses `Properties.Append` with 3219 (lv-dao-byte-property-1, -2), so 64 bytes is reached only on the rewrite path; with Long fields the create path gives odd sizes for one field and 70 + 2 x (both lengths) for two, so it never gives 64.
- **Open**: the rule for other long values (Memo, OLE) and for blobs of other field types; the LVAL page's layout for these blobs is in the kept pages but not decoded here; MDB.

### R-CAT-1 CAT-X, R-PRESERVE-1: a TableDefs.Append or CREATE TABLE refused with 3010 changes only the commit counter, by two
- **Claim**: A refused duplicate table (TableDefs.Append of `T` with one Long field, or `db.Execute CREATE TABLE T (n LONG)` with dbFailOnError, both 3010) in a session of its own changes one byte of the file: page 0 offset 3586 rises by 2 (2 to 4). Page 1 is rewritten with its own bytes; no other page is written. The route changes only the timing: the SQL route writes page 1 and the counter at the Execute and again at Close; the DAO route writes nothing at the Append and both commits at Close. A second session that changes nothing writes nothing.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB copy of r-cat-1-accdb-empty; session 1 created T and closed; pages kept after each session; ETW file-I/O trace.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q15-ddl-open/` dup-append-dao, dup-create-sql (each twice), second-session-nothing. Agrees with r-def-cursor-dao/err-dup-tabledef-append (same length, another hash).
- **Open**: what page 1 holds inside the refused session (only the closed file was kept).

### R-CAT-1 CAT-B, R-DAO-1 DAO-DRAFT: a TableDef draft without fields fails 3264 before its name is checked; an empty name fails 3125; DateCreated is taken at Append
- **Claim**: `TableDefs.Append` of a draft without fields fails 3264 whatever its name: `a.b`, ` lead`, `T`, and `T` when T exists (3264, not 3125 or 3010). With one field, `a.b` fails 3125. `CreateTableDef("")` and `CreateTableDef()` give a draft whose Name is `""`; its Append with one Long field fails 3125 and the file is unchanged. Before Append, the draft's `DateCreated` and `LastUpdated` fail 3219. After Append, `DateCreated` is the time of the Append, not of `CreateTableDef`: with 5 s between them, 46347.142676006944 (03:25:27.207 local) inside the Append's bracket 01:25:27.189-.227 UTC, and in the repeat 03:35:49.395 inside 01:35:49.387-.406. The value has milliseconds; it equals MSysObjects DateCreate, and DAO `LastUpdated` returns the same value while the row's DateUpdate is 2 ms later (46347.14987728009). `TableDefs("T")` after Refresh returns the held draft object.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, DAO, one session; time zone UTC+2.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q15-ddl-open/` refusal-precedence, empty-name-append, missing-name-append, date-create-vs-append (twice).
- **Open**: 3001 for a 65-character name at CreateTableDef (CAT-B) against a draft without fields was not asked.

### R-CAT-1 CAT-X, II-04: a new table's columns are numbered in declaration order; pages freed by DROP TABLE return at Close and are reused by the next session
- **Claim**: `CREATE TABLE T (z LONG, a LONG)` writes the same column blocks as `(a LONG, z LONG)` (column 0 at fixed offset 0, column 1 at 4) with the names in declaration order (`z`, `a`); the DAO route with z appended before a numbers them the same way (its blocks differ from SQL only in each block's 2-byte field that SQL sets to `09 04` and DAO to `00 00`, as between r-q11 create-one-long and append-dao). A table dropped in a session gives its pages back at that session's Close (page-1 bits), not at the DROP: a CREATE TABLE later in the same session, also 4 s later, takes new pages (U at 44, the file 46 pages, page 42 left with kind byte 08 and page 1 byte 4037 `cc` after Close), and a CREATE TABLE in the next session reuses them (U at 42, 44 pages, `f0`).
- **Conditions**: ACCDB copies of r-cat-1-accdb-empty; pages kept after Close.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q15-ddl-open/` columns-z-a, columns-a-z, columns-z-a-dao; drop-then-create, drop-pause-create (twice), drop-then-create-next-session, counter-drop-pages (page 1 unchanged after the DROP, `fc` after Close).
- **Open**: reuse when the freed pages are not the first free ones; a dropped table with rows, indexes or LVAL pages.

### R-CAT-1 CAT-X, R-FMT-1, II-04: new-table column blocks, names, numbering, offsets, DAO LvProp and reopened Fields all follow definition order
- **Claim**: On ACCDB, every newly created user table — through both SQL `db.Execute("CREATE TABLE ...")` and DAO `CreateTableDef / CreateField / Fields.Append / TableDefs.Append` — writes its 25-byte TableDef column blocks and its UTF-16LE column names in exact definition (declaration / `Fields.Append`) order, never sorted by name and never grouped by fixed vs variable. For column `i` in definition order (`0..n-1`): block position is `i` (offset `0x3F + 25*i` with `RealIndexCount = 0`), name position is `i` (preserving exact case), `PhysicalNumber` (`+5..+6`) is `i`, `ColumnId` (`+9..+10`) is `i`, `VariableIndex` (`+7..+8`) is the count of variable-width columns strictly before `i` for both variable and fixed columns (e.g., `(B TEXT(10), C LONG, A TEXT(10))` writes `VarIdx = 0, 1, 1` and `VariableColumns = 2`), and `FixedOffset` (`+21..+22`) is cumulative fixed bytes before `i` (`0` for variable columns; `0` for `LONG` preceded only by `TEXT(10)`). On read-only reopen, DAO `TableDef.Fields(i)` enumerates in definition order with `OrdinalPosition = i`. Case and prefix pairs (`(b, A)`, `(B, a)`, `(AB, A)`) keep definition order because no sorting occurs at creation. Repeating `sql-bca-mixed` and `dao-bca-mixed` (`a1`, `a2`) produces byte-identical pages everywhere outside the 16 MSysObjects `DateCreate`/`DateUpdate` bytes on page 17. Side findings on DAO: TableDef `+11..+14` `TypeInfo` is `00 00 00 00` on `LONG` columns but `09 04 00 00` on `TEXT(10)` columns (SQL writes `09 04 00 00` on both); DAO `LvProp` LVAL page 44 stores its MR2 column property maps in definition order (`B`, `C`, `A` on `dao-bca-mixed`; `B`, `A` on `dao-ba-long`), while `MSysObjects` (page 2 of `r-cat-1-accdb-empty`) remains an example of an already-compacted/system TableDef whose blocks are in name order with out-of-order `PhysicalNumber`.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB copies of `r-cat-1-accdb-empty`, DAO `db.Execute(..., dbFailOnError)` and DAO `TableDefs.Append`, one write session followed by a read-only reopen, pages kept after Close.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q26-column-order/` `sql-ab-long`, `sql-ba-long`, `sql-ab-text`, `sql-ba-text`, `sql-var-fixed-ab`, `sql-var-fixed-ba`, `sql-fixed-var-ab`, `sql-fixed-var-ba`, `sql-bca-long`, `sql-cba-long`, `sql-bca-mixed` (`a1`, `a2`), `sql-case-lower-b-A`, `sql-case-B-a`, `sql-prefix-AB-A`, `dao-ab-long`, `dao-ba-long`, `dao-ba-text`, `dao-var-fixed-ba`, `dao-fixed-var-ba`, `dao-bca-long`, `dao-cba-long`, `dao-bca-mixed` (`a1`, `a2`), `dao-case-b-A`, `dao-prefix-AB-A`; corroborates earlier `evidence/r-q15-ddl-open/` `columns-a-z`, `columns-z-a`, `columns-z-a-dao`. Discriminators: `sql-bca-mixed` & `dao-bca-mixed` (3-column non-monotone order, mixed variable/fixed/variable, `VariableIndex` on fixed after variable, DAO `TypeInfo` on `TEXT` vs `LONG`, DAO `LvProp` map order, `a1`/`a2` stability); `sql-var-fixed-ba` vs `sql-fixed-var-ba` (no fixed/variable regrouping); `sql-case-lower-b-A`, `sql-case-B-a`, `dao-case-b-A`, `sql-prefix-AB-A`, `dao-prefix-AB-A` (no case or prefix sorting).
- **Open**: what operation reorders TableDef column blocks into name order (e.g. `CompactDatabase` or Access Table Designer, as seen in `MSysObjects`); `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN` / post-`Append` `Fields.Append`; explicit `Field.OrdinalPosition` reassignment; tables with inline indexes or LVAL (`MEMO`/`OLE`) columns out of name order; Jet 4 MDB.

### R-CAT-1 CAT-X, W-07: field-name equivalence and duplicate-field errors on every CREATE route
- **Claim**: SQL `CREATE TABLE`, draft DAO `TableDefs.Append` and ADO `CREATE TABLE` (`Microsoft.ACE.OLEDB.12.0`) treat field names as one name under the same case-insensitive General equivalence. `Aa`/`aA`, `Fé`/`FÉ`, NFC/NFD `Fé`, `æ`/`ae` and `ß`/`ss` collide; `e`/`é`, `Tä`/`Té` and `o`/`ø` are two fields, both spellings kept. A collision through DAO SQL fails 3380 (`0x800A0D34`, `DAO.Database`, "Field '<second>' already exists in table 'T'.", help context 5003380). Through ADO it fails `0x80040E21` (`Microsoft Access Database Engine`, the same text, help context 5003191, SQLState `3380`, NativeError -539297252). Both leave 42 pages and change only page 0 at 3586 (`00` to `02` after Close). Draft DAO `Fields.Append` accepts duplicates, even exact `a`/`a` (Count 2, no error); `TableDefs.Append` then fails 3191 (`0x800A0C77`, `DAO.TableDef`, "Cannot define field more than once.", help context 5003191). It leaves page 0 at 3586 `03`, 44 pages with 42 and 43 freed in page 1 (`fc`), and a deleted MSysObjects slot 18 on page 17 holding `T` with field 1's inline LvProp.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB copies of `r-fmt-1/create-accdb-empty`, `dbLangGeneral`; DAO 12.0 and the ACE OLE DB provider; pages kept after Close.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q28-schema-write-open/` `pair-{sql,dao,ado}-{Aa-aA,fe-FE,nfc-nfd,ae-lig,sz-ss,e-eacute,ta-te,o-oslash}`, `pair-dao-a-a`. Discriminator: `pair-dao-a-a` against `pair-dao-Aa-aA` (draft Fields.Append accepts even an exact duplicate; the error belongs to TableDefs.Append).
- **Open**: other sort orders than General; pairs that differ only in width or kana.

### R-CAT-1 CAT-X, R-FMT-1, W-07: DAO TableDefs.Append writes LvProp inline up to 64 bytes, then compacts one LVAL slot on page 44 and spills past a full page
- **Claim**: DAO Append of N Long fields writes N+1 commits; page 0 at 3586 reads N+2 after Close (N = 5, 7, 12, 16 to 20, 24 to 27). Commit 2's LvProp (`49 + 2 * len(first field name)` bytes) stays inline on page 17 up to 64 bytes (first-name length 3 and 7). Commit 3 then moves it to page 44 slot 0, leaving commit 2's vacated inline bytes on page 17 just before 2287. From 65 bytes (length 8 and 30), commit 2 already writes page 44 slot 0 and page 17 has no vacated prefix. Each later commit writes the new LvProp below the live one, marks the old slot deleted (`00 d0`) and compacts, so the one live slot ends at 0x1000; the copy from before compaction stays unzeroed below it. With 1-character names page 44 holds all of N = 5 to 20 (45 pages). With 30-character names it holds N = 25 and spills at N = 26, where `14 + 2 * rowCount + L(k-1) + L(k)` first exceeds 4096. Then the new LvProp goes to page 45 slot 0, page 44 is rewritten as kind `0x09` with every slot deleted, page 44 is freed in page 1 (`fc` to `d0` at 4037), and both page-6 LvProp maps move from bit 44 to bit 45 (3278 and 3347, `20`). At N = 27 ACE takes page 46 without reusing 44 (page 1 `b0`, page-6 maps `40`). Inline LvProp changes nothing on page 6; LvProp on page 44 sets bit 44 (`10`) in the owned map (3347) and the free map (3278) of MSysObjects' LvProp column. At the return of TableDefs.Append (N = 4) page 0 reads N and pages 6, 17 and 44 are final; about 500 ms later commit N+1 raises page 0 by one; Close raises it again and settles pages 1, 2 and 3.
- **Conditions**: as above; Long fields; boundary and timing questions asked twice (`a1`, `a2`), equal outside the clock fields (also those in page 17's vacated copy).
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q28-schema-write-open/` `dao-fields-{5,7,12,16,17,18,19,20}`, `dao-long30-24` to `-27`, `dao-first{3,7,8,30}-2fields`, `dao-first8-3fields`, `dao-fields-{1,2,6}-p6`, `dao-name-8-p6`, `dao-fields-4-timing`. Discriminators: `dao-long30-25` against `-26` and `-27` (the spill and the freed page); `dao-first7-2fields` against `dao-first8-2fields` (the 64-byte inline limit).
- **Open**: the MSysObjects row between commits 1 and N (the worker cannot read pages inside one Invoke, and `etw-check` refuses an attempt folder outside the integration checkout); an LvProp longer than one page (over 4080 bytes); the spill formula is fitted to N = 24 to 27 only.

### R-TXN-1, W-07: DDL inside BeginTrans writes nothing until it ends; CommitTrans is one commit, Rollback leaves only counters
- **Claim**: SQL CREATE TABLE and DAO TableDefs.Append (1 and 3 fields) inside BeginTrans write no byte before the level ends, and a second Workspace does not see `T` until CommitTrans. CommitTrans writes every page at once as one commit (page 0 at 3586 `00` to `01`, no deferred commit after 1500 ms; `02` after Close), with the same page-17 and page-44 bytes as DAO Append outside a transaction; page 1 at 4037 reads `00000000` while open and `f0` (1 field, SQL) or `e0` (3 fields) after Close. Rollback, the same on all three routes, keeps 42 pages: before Close, page 0 at 3586 `01` and page 1 at 4037 `00000000`; after Close, `02` and `fcffffff`. `T` is absent on reopen, and Close writes the raised TableDef counts of MSysObjects and MSysACEs (page 2 at 16, 67, 79: `12` to `13`; page 3 at 16 `24` to `26`, at 67 `12` to `13`).
- **Conditions**: as above; one Workspace, depth 1.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q28-schema-write-open/` `txn-dao-append-1-{commit,rollback}`, `txn-dao-append-3-{commit,rollback}` (`a1`, `a2`), `txn-sql-create-{commit,rollback}`, `txn-dao-append-second-ws`; `evidence/r-q53-conv-abspos-subq-loc/txnddl-commit-rollback-{accdb,mdb}/` (`4nfc`, ACCDB & Jet 4 MDB: inside `BeginTrans`, `CREATE TABLE` + `INSERT` + `SELECT` + `DROP TABLE` write 0 bytes to page 0 before resolution; `Rollback` steps `0xE02` `+1` live (`03->04`) & `+2` on `Close` (`04->06`); `CommitTrans` steps `0xE02` `+1` live (`06->07`) & `+1` on `Close` (`07->08`)).
- **Built (`w13-isam-txnddl-56cs`, `166552ae`, 2026-11-27)**: `TransactionalDdlOverlay` in `Catalog`, `Isam`, `Sql`, and `Com.Dao` admits depth-1 shared-session SQL (`CREATE TABLE` + same-transaction `DROP TABLE`) and DAO (`TableDefs.Append`, attached `Long` `Fields.Append`, `Relations.Append`/`Delete`) with 0 B written before resolution, exact `0x0E02` / page-1 reservation / page 2–3 `RowCount` deltas on `CommitTrans` vs allocating `Rollback` vs 0-allocation `Rollback` carry-over, and two-WS `DROP TABLE` Close-commit transfer (`r-q53` +4 -> `18/32`, `r-q28` +3 -> `48/54`).
- **Open**: nesting depth 2 or more, exclusive sessions, pending pre-transaction `DeferredCatalogCommit`/published global-map changes, failing `Relations.Append` rollbacks inside `BeginTrans`, `ALTER TABLE`/`CREATE INDEX`/`CREATE VIEW`/`MakeTable`/SQL FKs, and DDL/DROP on a pre-existing persisted table inside a level (all refused by name).

### R-CAT-1 CAT-X, II-04: SQL CREATE TABLE column descriptors for scalar, Binary/VarBinary, GUID and Decimal types
- **Claim**: On ACCDB (`r-cat-1-accdb-empty`), SQL `CREATE TABLE T (...)` writes the exact same catalog and header changes as `r-q11-ddl-closure/create-one-long` (pages 0, 1, 2, 3, 7, 8, 10, 17, 41 — no `LvProp` blob on MSysObjects page 17, page 0 offset 3586 `00 -> 02`, page 1 offset 4037 `fc -> f0`, 44 pages total, zero unkept pages changed; repeating `col-memo` confirms only MSysObjects `DateCreate`/`DateUpdate` at page 17 offsets `0x907` and `0x90F` vary), and differs only on TableDef page 42 (and map page 43 for LVAL columns). Every 25-byte column block writes `+1..+4` `59 06 00 00`, `+16` ExtendedFlags `0x00`, and `+17..+20` `00 00 00 00`; every non-Decimal type writes `+11..+14` TypeInfo `09 04 00 00` (General – Legacy sort order). Per-type rules:
  - Fixed scalar (`Flags = 0x03`, `VariableColumns` unchanged): `YESNO`/`BIT` (`TypeCode = 0x01`, `Length = 1`, advances subsequent `FixedOffset` by `0`, not `1`, because Boolean values live in the row's null bitmap); `BYTE` (`0x02`, `Length = 1`); `SHORT` (`0x03`, `Length = 2`); `INTEGER`/`LONG` (`0x04`, `Length = 4` — SQL `INTEGER` is 4-byte Long); `CURRENCY` (`0x05`, `Length = 8`); `SINGLE` (`0x06`, `Length = 4`); `DOUBLE` (`0x07`, `Length = 8`); `DATETIME` (`0x08`, `Length = 8`).
  - `BINARY(n)` vs `VARBINARY(n)`: both write `TypeCode = 0x09` and byte length `n` in `+23..+24`; `BINARY(n)` is fixed (`Flags = 0x03`, `VariableColumns` unchanged, advances next `FixedOffset` by `n`: `BINARY(1)` `1`, `BINARY(16)` `16`, bare `BINARY` defaults to `510` = `0x01FE`, `BINARY(16)` before `LONG` places `LONG` at `FixedOffset = 16`), while `VARBINARY(16)` is variable (`Flags = 0x02`, `VariableColumns` incremented, `FixedOffset = 0`, `Length = 16`).
  - `GUID`: variable-length (`TypeCode = 0x0F`, `Flags = 0x02`, `VariableColumns` incremented, `FixedOffset = 0`, `Length = 16` = `0x0010`, no LVAL maps).
  - `DECIMAL(p, s)`: DAO `db.Execute` refuses `CREATE TABLE T (n DECIMAL(18, 4))` with `0x800A0CDC` (3292, `"Syntax error in field definition."`) and leaves `0` bytes of the file changed (including page 0 offset 3586). ADO (`Microsoft.ACE.OLEDB.12.0`, whose `LONG` control `col-long-ado` is byte-identical to DAO `col-integer` outside clock bytes) accepts it and writes `TypeCode = 0x10`, `Flags = 0x03` (fixed), `Length = 17` (`0x0011`, advancing next `FixedOffset` by `17`), and `+11..+14` `TypeInfo = precision, scale, 0x00, 0x00` (`DECIMAL(18, 4)` `12 04 00 00`, `DECIMAL(28, 0)` `1c 00 00 00`, bare `DECIMAL` defaults to `(18, 0)` `12 00 00 00`).
  - Mixed fixed/variable numbering: across `a LONG, b GUID, c MEMO` and `a MEMO, b LONG`, `+7..+8` `VariableIndex` is the count of variable columns before that column (`GUID` `0`, `MEMO` `1`; fixed `LONG` after `MEMO` gets `1`).
- **Conditions**: ACCDB copies of `r-cat-1-accdb-empty`, DAO `db.Execute(..., dbFailOnError)` and ADO `ADODB.Connection.Execute` (`Microsoft.ACE.OLEDB.12.0`), one session, pages kept after Close and pages 0–49 hashed.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q21-ddl-types/` `col-byte`, `col-short`, `col-integer`, `col-single`, `col-double`, `col-currency`, `col-datetime`, `col-yesno`, `col-bit`, `col-yesno-long`, `col-guid`, `col-fixed-guid-memo`, `col-binary-1`, `col-binary-16`, `col-binary-bare`, `col-varbinary-16`, `col-binary-long`, `col-long-ado`, `col-decimal-dao`, `col-decimal-ado`, `col-decimal-ado-28-0`, `col-decimal-ado-bare`, `col-decimal-long-ado`, `col-memo` (a1, a2 repeat). Discriminators: `SHORT` vs `INTEGER`; `YESNO` vs `BIT` and `col-yesno-long`; `BINARY(1)`/`(16)`/bare vs `VARBINARY(16)` and `col-binary-long`; `DECIMAL(18, 4)`/`(28, 0)`/bare and `col-decimal-long-ado`; `col-long-ado` vs `col-integer`.
- **Open**: DAO `TableDefs.Append` (`LvProp` blobs and `TypeInfo`) for non-Long types; bare `VARBINARY` default length and `BINARY(n)`/`VARBINARY(n)` upper bounds; `DECIMAL` precision/scale limits and refusal codes; `CHAR(n)` / bare `TEXT`; MDB.

### R-CAT-1 CAT-X, II-04: LVAL columns (MEMO/LONGTEXT, LONGBINARY/OLEOBJECT) add two map-page rows and a 10-byte trailing TDEF entry each
- **Claim**: `MEMO` and `LONGTEXT` produce byte-identical files (outside clock bytes), as do `LONGBINARY` and `OLEOBJECT`. Each writes `TypeCode = 0x0C` (`MEMO`/`LONGTEXT`) or `0x0B` (`LONGBINARY`/`OLEOBJECT`), `Flags = 0x02` (variable), `VariableColumns` incremented, `TypeInfo = 09 04 00 00`, `FixedOffset = 0`, `Length = 0`, and no `LvProp`. For each LVAL column in declaration order, ACE appends two empty 69-byte type-0 inline usage maps to map page 43 (rows 2–3 for the first LVAL column, rows 4–5 for the second; page 43 free space drops from `0x0F64` to `0x0ED6` for 1 and `0x0E48` for 2) and writes one 10-byte trailing entry (`u16LE physCol`, `4B ownedMap`, `4B freeMap`, e.g. `00 00 02 2b 00 00 03 2b 00 00`) in TableDef page 42 after the logical index names and before `FF FF` (`defLen` grows by 10 per LVAL column: `0x68` for 1, `0x8F` for 2). When `CREATE INDEX` runs later on a table with an LVAL column (`a MEMO, b LONG`), the index's owned map takes the next row on page 43 (row 4, `04 2b 00 00`) while the LVAL entry (`00 00 02 2b 00 00 03 2b 00 00`) stays after the logical index name before `FF FF`.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB copies of `r-cat-1-accdb-empty`, DAO `db.Execute(..., dbFailOnError)`, one session, pages kept after Close and pages 0–49 hashed.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q21-ddl-types/` `col-memo` (a1, a2 repeat), `col-longtext`, `col-two-memos`, `col-longbinary`, `col-oleobject`, `col-fixed-guid-memo`, `ix-on-table-with-memo`. Discriminators: `col-memo` vs `col-longtext` and `col-longbinary` vs `col-oleobject` (synonyms); `col-two-memos` (rows 2–5, two trailing entries); `col-fixed-guid-memo` (`physCol = 2`); `ix-on-table-with-memo` (trailing section order and map slot 4).
- **Open**: `CREATE TABLE` with an LVAL column and an inline `PRIMARY KEY` constraint (whether the PK map row precedes or follows the LVAL rows on page 43); DAO `TableDefs.Append` for Memo/OLE; MDB.

### R-CAT-1 CAT-X, II-04: COUNTER/AUTOINCREMENT sets column flag 0x04 and writes seed - increment and signed increment at TDEF header 0x14..0x1B
- **Claim**: `COUNTER` and `AUTOINCREMENT` produce byte-identical files (outside clock bytes), and `COUNTER(10, 5)` produces byte-identical files through DAO and ADO (`Microsoft.ACE.OLEDB.12.0`). The column block writes `TypeCode = 0x04` (Long), `Length = 4`, `Flags = 0x07` (`0x01` fixed | `0x02` | `0x04` AutoNumber), `ExtendedFlags = 0x00`, and no `LvProp`. TableDef header bytes `0x14..0x17` (int32LE, `AutoNumber`) hold `seed - increment` (last-issued value before the first insert) and bytes `0x18..0x1B` (int32LE, previously unconfirmed `0x00000001`) hold the signed increment: default `COUNTER`/`AUTOINCREMENT` writes `0, 1` (`00 00 00 00 01 00 00 00`); `COUNTER(10, 5)` writes `5, 5` (`05 00 00 00 05 00 00 00`); `COUNTER(100, 7)` writes `93, 7` (`5d 00 00 00 07 00 00 00`); `COUNTER(10, -2)` is accepted and writes `12, -2` (`0c 00 00 00 fe ff ff ff`).
- **Conditions**: ACCDB copies of `r-cat-1-accdb-empty`, DAO `db.Execute(..., dbFailOnError)` and ADO `ADODB.Connection.Execute`, one session, pages kept after Close and pages 0–49 hashed.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q21-ddl-types/` `col-counter`, `col-autoincrement`, `col-counter-seed-inc`, `col-counter-seed-inc-ado`, `col-counter-100-7`, `col-counter-neg-inc`. Discriminators: `col-counter-100-7` separates `seed - inc` (`93`) from `seed` (`100`) and `inc` (`7`); `col-counter-neg-inc` proves signed int32 increment (`-2`); `col-counter-seed-inc` vs `-ado` proves DAO/ADO parity.
- **Open**: `COUNTER(seed, 0)` refusal; random AutoNumber; MDB. Row insertion is the next note.

### R-CAT-1 CAT-X, R-FMT-2, II-04: each insert issues the last value plus the signed increment and writes it to TDEF 0x14..0x17
- **Claim**: an insert into a table with an AutoNumber column issues TDEF `0x14..0x17` (the last-issued value) plus `0x18..0x1B` (the signed increment), and writes the issued value back to `0x14..0x17`; `0x18..0x1B` is unchanged, and the row count at `0x10..0x13` counts the row. `COUNTER(100, 5)`: one insert issues 100 and leaves `0x14 = 100` (`64 00 00 00`); two issue 100, 105 and leave `105` (`69`). `COUNTER(10, -2)`: one insert issues 10 and leaves `10`; two issue 10, 8 and leave `8` (`08 00 00 00`, increment `fe ff ff ff`). `COUNTER(100, 1)` behaves the same way (100, `0x14 = 100`). A DAO `AddNew` on a dynaset shows the value at `AddNew` (100) and leaves the same TDEF bytes as SQL `INSERT`.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB copies of `r-cat-1-accdb-empty`, DAO, `CREATE TABLE T (n COUNTER(seed, inc), v LONG)` then one or two `INSERT INTO T (v) VALUES (...)` with `dbFailOnError`, or one `AddNew`/`Update`, in the same session; T's TDEF is page 42; pages kept after Close.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q23-counter-step/` `sql-100-5-ins1`, `sql-100-5-ins2`, `sql-100-1-ins1`, `sql-10-neg2-ins1`, `sql-10-neg2-ins2`, `dao-100-5-addnew1`. Discriminators: one against two inserts (the written value follows the last issue, not the seed); increment 5 against 1 (the step is the stored increment); `-2` (signed, the value goes down); SQL against DAO `AddNew` (same bytes). The issued values match `r-def-expr-sql/ddl-counter-seed-increment` and the `au-reseed-ladder` records.
- **Open**: overflow past `int.MaxValue` or below `int.MinValue`; an explicit value under a non-1 increment (whether the next issue steps from it); a cancelled `AddNew` under a non-1 increment; MDB.

### R-CAT-1 CAT-X, II-04: CREATE INDEX option flags, logical types, column sort bits, Text and two-column keys
- **Claim**: On ACCDB (`CREATE TABLE T (...)` then `CREATE [UNIQUE] INDEX IX_T ON T (...) [WITH ...]`), every single-index probe writes the exact same catalog, map-page and leaf-page structure as `r-q11-ddl-closure/create-then-index` (45 pages total, page 0 offset 3586 `00 -> 03`, page 1 offset 4037 `fc -> e0`, empty leaf page 44 `04 01 20 0e 2a 00 00 00`, index owned map in the next row of page 43 with bit 44 set at byte +10 `0x10`, zero unkept pages changed), varying only the TableDef's 52-byte physical index block and 28-byte logical index block:
  - Physical `IndexFlags` (byte +46 of the physical index block) and logical `IndexType` (byte +23 of the logical index block): plain `CREATE INDEX` `flags = 0x80`, `type = 0x00`; `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX` `flags = 0x81` (`0x80 | 0x01`), `type = 0x00`; `WITH IGNORE NULL` `flags = 0x82` (`0x80 | 0x02`), `type = 0x00`; `UNIQUE` + `WITH IGNORE NULL` `flags = 0x83` (`0x80 | 0x01 | 0x02`), `type = 0x00`; `WITH DISALLOW NULL` `flags = 0x88` (`0x80 | 0x08`), `type = 0x00`; `UNIQUE` + `WITH DISALLOW NULL` `flags = 0x89` (`0x80 | 0x01 | 0x08`), `type = 0x00`; `WITH PRIMARY` (with or without `UNIQUE` — byte-identical outside clock bytes) `flags = 0x89`, `type = 0x01` (matching `r-q11 create-primary-key`).
  - Column slots (`+4..+33` of the physical index block, 10 × 3-byte slots): each used slot is `u16LE physCol` + `u8 colFlags` (`0x01` for `ASC`/default, `0x00` for `DESC`); unused slots are `FF FF 00`. A `TEXT(50)` index (`ix-text`) writes the same index blocks as a `LONG` index. Two-column indexes populate slots 0 and 1 in key-list order with per-column sort bits (`ix-two-col`: `00 00 01 01 00 01`; `ix-two-col-mixed-desc` on `(a LONG, b TEXT(50))` with `(a, b DESC)`: `00 00 01 01 00 00`).
- **Conditions**: ACCDB copies of `r-cat-1-accdb-empty`, DAO `db.Execute(..., dbFailOnError)`, empty table, one session, pages kept after Close and pages 0–49 hashed.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q21-ddl-types/` `ix-unique`, `ix-with-primary`, `ix-unique-with-primary`, `ix-disallow-null`, `ix-ignore-null`, `ix-unique-disallow-null`, `ix-unique-ignore-null`, `ix-desc`, `ix-text`, `ix-two-col`, `ix-two-col-mixed-desc`, `ix-on-table-with-memo`; baseline `evidence/r-q11-ddl-closure/create-then-index`, `create-primary-key`. Discriminators: `ix-unique-disallow-null` (`0x89`, `type 0x00`) vs `ix-with-primary` (`0x89`, `type 0x01`) separates `IndexType` from `IndexFlags`; the 2×2 `UNIQUE` × (`DISALLOW NULL`/`IGNORE NULL`) matrix separates bits `0x01`, `0x02`, `0x08`; `ix-desc` and `ix-two-col-mixed-desc` separate per-column sort bits `0x01`/`0x00`.
- **Open**: adding a second index to a table that already has one; indexing a non-empty table (leaf population and counter block); indexing `BINARY`, `DECIMAL`, `GUID`, `YESNO` or 3–10 columns; DAO `TableDef.Indexes.Append`; MDB.

### R-CAT-1 CAT-D (catalog side): MSysRelationships rows
- **Claim**: A relation is one MSysRelationships row per column: szRelationship its name, szReferencedObject DAO `Relation.Table` (the primary side), szObject `ForeignTable`, szReferencedColumn the relation field's `Name`, szColumn its `ForeignName`, grbit `Attributes` as DAO reports it (0, and 256 dbRelationUpdateCascade), icolumn the column ordinal and ccolumn the column count. `Relations` lists them in name order; a relation shows 5 built-in properties and PartialReplica gives 3032; a relation field answers Name, ForeignName and CollectionIndex, FieldSize 3267, OriginalValue and VisibleValue 3251, and every other Field member 3219.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, two single-column relations.
- **Records**: evidence/r-dao-1b/relation-members-each, relationfield-members-each, relation-properties-enumerated; the rows decoded from the same fixture.
- **Open**: compound relations (ccolumn above 1) and the other grbit bits as stored.

### R-CAT-1 CAT-V: held-object lifetime, parameter state and Requery()
- **Claim**: A held QueryDef whose saved query is deleted answers 3420; recreating the name does not revive it; renaming makes the old name unresolvable (3265); after `Database.Close` held children answer 3420; `Workspace.Close` (also with an open transaction) leaves `Database`, recordset, TableDef, QueryDef and Workspace answering 3420 while `Workspaces.Count` stays 1; `Database.Close` leaves the Workspace usable and its children 3420. `QueryDefs.Item("QP")` twice on one Database returns one shared instance (a value set through one reads through the other); an instance from another Database of the same Workspace or from another Workspace has its own, empty parameter values (open fails 3061 until set). Parameter values persist across executions and after a `SQL` put; a held `Parameters` collection keeps its order after a `SQL` put (reopen reorders). `Recordset.Requery()` with no argument re-runs the open as it was: with a dynaset and a snapshot opened at pV = 20, after pV was set to 40 and after the saved SQL was put to `V < [pV]`, Requery() returned the pV = 20 rows (3, 4, 5) both times; a recordset opened afresh then returned the new definition's rows (1, 2, 3).
- **Records**: q2/cat-v-requery-after-parameter-change/; q1/cat-v-two-instances-parameters/ (four instances), q1/txn-close-workspace-close-held-objects/ (three Close kinds; resolves evidence/r-def-cat-txn/cat-workspace-close-held-objects/, whose Database was opened through the default Workspace); evidence/r-cat-1/held-querydef-lifetime/, held-sql-replace/, collection-refresh/.
- **Open**: `Requery(QueryDef)` crashes ACE and is never run.

### R-CAT-1 CAT-D: relationship semantics through DAO
- **Claim**: Each enforced relation adds its own Foreign index on the child, named like the relation; two relations over the same foreign-key column give two indexes (`R1`, `R2`), no sharing, each enforced independently (an insert needs a parent in both; deleting either parent key gives 3200); `Relations.Delete("R1")` removes its index and its enforcement. Also: relation `Attributes` reads back as created; no-enforce adds no index; orphans 3201 (compound keys, self-reference), parent delete/update 3200 unless cascading (also self-reference and a cycle); `Append` over existing orphans 3201 when enforced; type pairs LONG→LONG, COUNTER→LONG, TEXT10→TEXT20 accepted, others 3368; renames propagate; `DROP TABLE` of a parent 3281, `DROP COLUMN` in a relation 3383, DDL under a held recordset 3211 (same process) or 3262 (other process).
- **Records**: q1/cat-d-two-relations-shared-fk-columns/; evidence/r-cat-1b/rel-orphan-insert/, rel-parent-delete-by-attributes/, rel-index-ownership/, rename-field-in-relation/; evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/cat-drop-table-in-relation/, cat-rel-type-pairs-complete/, rel-orphan-insert-errors/; evidence/r-def-cat-txn/cat-rel-self-reference-cascade/, cat-rel-cascade-cycle/, cat-rel-compound-key-enforcement/.
- **Open**: which relation's message an insert violating both names (one sample names the first); MSys/ACL rows, Name AutoCorrect (T).

### R-CAT-1 CAT-I: saved queries bind by name at each open; rule changes bite at the next Execute
- **Claim**: A saved query holds text only: renames do not rewrite it; the next open fails 3061 (unresolved identifier becomes an implicit parameter) or 3078; dropping a used column succeeds silently and breaks dependents at the next open; the column coming back resolves the parameter; changed nested output changes the outer open; held `Fields`/`Parameters` are snapshots until reopen; wildcard mode follows the client (DAO `*`, ACE ADO `%`). A held saved parameter INSERT and a fresh instance behave the same after each rule change: a field `ValidationRule` gives 3316 with its text, `Required` with Null gives 3314, a unique index with a duplicate gives 3022; a Caption property changes nothing.
- **Records**: q1/cat-i-saved-append-after-rule-change/ (held vs fresh instance, four rule kinds); evidence/r-cat-1/select-star/, rename-column/, drop-column/, unresolved-identifier/, nested-querydef/, recreate-table/, column-type-change/, wildcard-mode/; evidence/r-cat-1b/rename-query-dependent-query/.
- **Open**: function registry, sandbox, cache hit/miss (not observable from DAO).

### R-CAT-1 II-11: CHECK, VIEW, PROCEDURE, OWNERACCESS through the ACE OLE DB provider
- **Claim**: ADO (ANSI-92) accepts `ALTER TABLE ADD CONSTRAINT ..
