    (ins-nested-inner-rb-outer-rb).
  - *Explicit AutoNumber.* With unit increment, an explicit value accepted below the counter lowers the word to it
    (counter 10, ID 5 into a gap: 20 becomes 5, next ID 6; exp-gap-5, a1 and a2), and one equal to it leaves it
    (exp-gap-10, next 11). Refused 3022 by the primary key it sets the word the same way (exp-pk-dup-5, a1 and a2: 10 to
    5, next 6; exp-pk-dup-10) and the field rises by 1. Refused 3022 by another unique index, it sets the word
    (exp-uk-dup-40: 40, next 41; exp-uk-dup-5: 5, next 6), the primary key's count rises by 1, the row count and the
    other index's count stay, and the field rises by 2.
  - *Other refusals in INSERT…SELECT, `dbFailOnError`.* On a page with room the 3022 refusal adds 2, not 3
    (ins-dup-fail-existing). A 3201 at row 3 leaves row count 2, AutoNumber 3, primary key count 3 and foreign key
    index count 2 (ins-fk-3201-fail); 3314, 3316 and a 3464 conversion at row 3 reach neither index (row count 2,
    AutoNumber 3, both index counts 2: ins-req-3314-fail, ins-val-3316-fail, ins-conv-3464-fail); each adds 3. A refusal
    at row 6 of rows that fill three data pages when accepted (ins-clean-two-pages) writes no data or index page and
    takes none: pages 0 and the TableDef change, +3 (ins-dup-fail-two-pages). Jet 4 MDB writes the same counters on its
    TableDef page (ins-dup-fail-mdb; its repeat differs only in an MSysObjects date).
  - *The group inside `Execute` comes with an AutoNumber column or a page taken.* At external depth 0 under
    `dbFailOnError` (tasks qhs8 and k64d):
    - **No AutoNumber column, existing page: +1.** 3022, 3314, 3201 and 3316 write no group inside `Execute` and
      nothing at `Close`; only the deferred group writes the counters reached. ACCDB and Jet 4 MDB (TableDef page 19).
      A first-row 3022 by a second unique index raises only the primary key's count; one accepted row then 3022 gives
      row count +1, primary key +2, unique +1.
    - **No AutoNumber column, a new page: +3.** Rows on one or several new pages give the same, ACCDB and MDB, with
      page 1 unchanged after `Close`. Pages read beside ACE's open handle split it. Inside `Execute`: +1, the 32-page
      extent reserved in page 1 at 4038, the TableDef untouched. Deferred: +1, the rolled-back page's bit freed and
      the TableDef counters written. At `Close`: +1, the unused extent returned.
    - **The refused row's own index entries.** 3022 by the primary key, 3314 and 3316 count no index entry for the
      refused row. 3022 by a second unique index and 3201 count its primary key (+1).
    - **AutoNumber table, explicit values, existing ACCDB page: +2.** As for generated values: inside `Execute`, then
      deferred, nothing at `Close`. This holds for values below the counter, equal to it (the word stays) and above
      it. The AutoNumber word takes the refused row's explicit value: 10 becomes 5 or 1, stays 10, or becomes 30.
    The earlier reading of r-txn-1b/implicit-unique-first-failonerror and r-txn-1/stmt-unique-middle-fail-d0 (final
    hashes only), that the group inside `Execute` goes with a *consumed* AutoNumber, is wrong: an explicit value
    equal to the counter consumes none and still writes it.
    - **Records:** `evidence/r-q29-stmt-rollback-counters/`, 20 questions, 15 of them asked twice and equal byte for byte
      in the kept pages. `noan-dup-row1-uk-existing`, `noan-dup-row2-uk-existing`, `noan-req-3314-existing`,
      `noan-fk-3201-existing`, `noan-val-3316-existing`, `noan-dup-row2-mdb-existing`; `noan-dup-row2-newpage`,
      `noan-dup-twopages`, `noan-req-3314-newpage`, `noan-fk-3201-newpage`, `noan-val-3316-newpage`,
      `noan-dup-row2-mdb-newpage`; `exp-sel-below-dup2-existing`, `exp-sel-below-pk-dup2-existing`,
      `exp-sel-eq-dup2-existing`, `exp-sel-eq-uk-dup1-existing`, `exp-sel-above-dup2-existing`.
    - **Discriminators:** the three `*-live` questions (`noan-dup-row2-existing-live`, `noan-dup-row2-newpage-live`,
      `exp-sel-eq-dup2-live`) separate the groups. `exp-sel-eq-*` separates "AutoNumber column" from "consumed
      AutoNumber".
    - **Open:** explicit values on a new page, in MDB, inside `BeginTrans` or under another increment; MDB 3314, 3201
      and 3316 without an AutoNumber column; MDB with a generated AutoNumber on an existing page; 3464 without an
      AutoNumber column; a first-row 3314 or 3316 on an empty table. These were asked without ETW, which `etw-check`
      refuses from a worktree.
  - *Memo AllowZeroLength.* A Memo made by DAO `CreateField` reads `AllowZeroLength` False when it is not set; '' into it
    fails 3315 under `dbFailOnError` and consumes the AutoNumber (+1; memo-zerolength-unset-fail,
    memo-zerolength-false-fail a1 and a2). With True it stores '' (memo-zerolength-true-fail).
  - *UPDATE making two rows equal.* `UPDATE U SET K = 9 WHERE ID IN (2, 3)` sets ID 2 and refuses ID 3, in key order: `IN (3, 2)` gives the same, and `WHERE ID >= 2` changes only ID 2. Without the flag `RecordsAffected` is 1 and no error is raised. With `dbFailOnError`: 3022, `RecordsAffected` 0, and ID 2 stays 9 (SQL-ACT-FAIL). Both write one deferred group (data page, unique index page; no TableDef change): +1.
  - *Read-only database.* `Execute` of an INSERT on a database opened read-only fails 3073 "Operation must use an updateable query.", with `RecordsAffected` 0 and one `Errors` entry. This holds shared or exclusive, with or without `dbFailOnError`, with no read/write handle in the process. The file hash is unchanged, and ETW shows no write to the database.
  - *Failed value, no flag.* Text 'x43' into the Long K, or `1 / (K - 33)` = 1/0 into D, in `INSERT…SELECT` stores that row with Null in the field. `RecordsAffected` is 3 for 4 stored rows, and `Errors` is empty. The counters count the row (row count, AutoNumber and index counts 4). A one-row `INSERT … VALUES ('x', …)` or `(…, 1 / 0)` stores its row with Null and reports `RecordsAffected` 0.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, DAO `Database.Execute`, one process. Each question builds An (COUNTER primary key, unique K), Src and U (unique K) on a copy of r-fmt-1-accdb-empty in a setup session. Then one statement session runs, with a 1500 ms pause before `Close` except in ins-clean-fail-nopause. Pages are read after each closed session, and the ETW recorder is on.
- **Implemented** (w6z8, b035e2ed): the cases above as recorded; the MDB commit counter after a normal INSERT + Close is not modelled (R-CRASH-1 open).
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q27-autonumber-txn/` (27 questions, 10 repeated; task nxj9) for the transaction, explicit,
  other-refusal and Memo cases. `evidence/r-q18-statements/` (27 questions). Clean insert: ins-clean-{fail,plain,fail-txn,plain-txn,fail-txn-rollback,fail-nopause}. Duplicate: ins-dup-{fail,plain,fail-txn,plain-txn,fail-count,plain-count}. Allocation: ins-one-newpage-fail, ins-one-existing-fail, ins-clean-existing-fail. UPDATE: upd-dup-{fail,plain,plain-desc,plain-three}. Failed values: ins-conv-plain, ins-conv-values-plain, ins-div0-{plain,fail,values-plain}. Read-only: ro-ins-{fail,plain}, ro-excl-ins-fail.
- **Discriminators**: a new page against a page with room, and one row against four (the allocation probes); the pause; the IN order; the public RecordCount against its no-flag control; the transaction variants.
- **Contradicts**: ERR-PARTIAL's "constraint refusals under dbFailOnError change nothing". A refused `INSERT…SELECT` leaves the rows unchanged, but it changes the TableDef counters and the public `RecordCount` (ins-dup-fail-count). This also explains PRES-ROLLBACK-FAILED's changed hash and ERR-PARTIAL's open d1e hash.
- **Open**:
  - The values of the three 3586 writes within a group: ETW gives offsets only, and the closed file the net.
  - Whether Compact & Repair resets the drifted row count.
  - The first ro-ins-fail attempt's `Execute` took 3.15 s. Its repeat (a2, retained) took 11 ms.
  - After the word was lowered (exp-gap-5), a generated value that already exists as a row: 3022, or skipped.
  - Explicit values under an increment other than 1.

### R-TXN-1 TXN-MULTI: files and Workspaces
- **Claim**: Within one Workspace, changes in files A and B resolve together by one `Rollback`/`CommitTrans` in either touch order; a file opened after the first `BeginTrans` joins; a statement failure in B after a change in A is statement-only; closing B inside the level still rolls back both; three handles to one file roll back together. Two Workspaces touching the same two files in opposite order (w1: A then B, w2: B then A) resolve independently: on rows that do not share a page (unindexed field, all four updates admitted) the three resolution orders (w1 Rollback then w2 CommitTrans; w2 CommitTrans then w1 CommitTrans; w2 Rollback then w1 CommitTrans) each leave exactly the committing Workspace's changes on both files and nothing of the rolled-back one (A and B rows 1/64: (0,10), (1,20), (2,20)), in each Workspace's own reads and in a third Workspace after a pause and Idle; no deadlock. An update colliding with the other Workspace's page (three rows on one page) is refused 3188. A refused second-file admission (`OpenDatabase` on a file another process holds exclusively, 3045) leaves the level usable: later updates and `CommitTrans` succeed and both main-file changes are committed (independent final read), and the file opens after the other process closes.
- **Records**: q2/txn-multi-two-workspaces-far-rows-orders/, q1/txn-multi-two-workspaces-opposite-order/, q1/txn-multi-second-file-held-exclusive/; evidence/r-txn-1b/multi-a-then-b-rollback/, multi-b-then-a-rollback/, multi-b-fails-after-a-commit/, multi-close-b-in-txn/, multi-same-file-handles/; evidence/r-txn-1/nest-late-file-b/.
- **Open**: none that blocks.

### R-TXN-1 TXN-LOCAL: lockers inside and across processes
- **Claim**: With a transaction (or pessimistic Edit) on row 1, a write to row 1 or to row 2 (same page) fails after the retry time with 3260 across processes and 3188 across Workspaces of one process (ADO Connections: `0x80004005 "Could not update; currently locked."`), `RecordsAffected` 0, nothing changed; a far row (ID 64) updated through an unindexed field and another table are admitted, and that change survives the first locker's Rollback and CommitTrans; an update of the indexed N on a far row is refused 3188 in-process (an index leaf is a conflict resource); after the holder's resolution the retried write succeeds. In-process (two Workspaces, far rows 1 and 64, unindexed V) all six orders (A:C B:C, A:R B:C, B:C A:C, B:C A:R, A:C B:R, B:R A:C) keep exactly each owner's committed change: final fresh read row 1 V=4 (A committed four times), row 64 V=8 (B committed four times). Two ADO Connections in one process: same row and same page refused, far row (V) and another table admitted, and cn2's committed changes (row 64 V=4, U N=14 after two phases) are kept. The refusal time is `dbLockRetry` x `dbLockDelay` plus 0.01-0.23 s: default 2.01-2.03 s (second card, 3 repeats; 2.21-2.23 s in the first), retry 1 x delay 1 15 ms (3), retry 20 x delay 250 5.08-5.11 s (2), retry 3 x delay 300 0.904 s (3); `dbPageTimeout` alone (100 or 10000) leaves the 2.0 s unchanged (3 each). Three DBEngine objects in one process (CreateObject, CoCreateInstance, ITypeInfo::CreateInstance) are one engine: the same `#Default Workspace#`, an exclusive open beside an exclusive open admitted, `BeginTrans` levels nest across them (three levels), updates of the same row admitted and committed innermost first.
- **Records**: q2/txn-local-inproc-far-rows-unindexed-orders/, q2/txn-local-pagetimeout-alone-timing/ (timestamped brackets), q2/txn-local-two-ado-connections-events/, q2/engine-instances-in-process-admission/, q1/txn-local-conflict-timing/, q1/txn-local-inproc-far-rows-and-orders/ (indexed N refused 7 of 8), q1/txn-range-far-row-final-read/; evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/txn-local-xproc-same-row/, -same-page/, -far-row/, -other-table/; evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/txn-local-xproc-same-page-retry/, local-two-rows-far-b-commit-a-rollback/.
- **Open**: ADO resolution orders (cn1 never resolved its outer level; ADO's rollback member is `RollbackTrans`, not called); leaf/map byte footprints (W-04); COM threads (T).
- **Open (W-05/local-lockers-rebase, each a candidate probe; CalcDb's choice named in its code)**: (1) the time an in-process refusal takes: every timed record is cross-process (3260); CalcDb waits the same dbLockRetry × dbLockDelay schedule (`LockWaitPolicy`) — probe: the conflict-timing question with participant b as a second Workspace of a's process, default and retry 1 × delay 1. (2) Lock release at CancelUpdate of a pessimistic Edit inside a transaction (WRITE-TIMING records it outside one; CalcDb releases). (3) A pessimistic Edit of two Databases of one Workspace on one page (only the optimistic pair is recorded; CalcDb's lock owner is the Workspace, so they never conflict). (4) A recordset Update meeting another Workspace's page (an optimistic edit, or a key change whose index leaf is held): TXN-LOCAL records 3188 for Execute only; CalcDb names it (`MutationOpen.Conflict`). (5) Whether an inner Rollback releases the level's pages (TXN-RANGE "lock release at an inner level"; CalcDb keeps them to the outer resolution). (6) The page an uncommitted AddNew locks in-process (WRITE-ALLOC is cross-process; CalcDb's landing page is chosen at publication, W-05/same-width-mutation).

### R-TXN-1 TXN-VIEW: transaction visibility and the refresh boundary
- **Claim**: Uncommitted insert, delete, update and DDL of one Workspace are never visible to another Workspace or process; after a commit another process's held dynaset and table cursors and new snapshots show the value only after a delay: with the default `dbPageTimeout` the first new look is past (4.55, 4.80] s after the update (looks every 250 ms; two earlier samples (4.08, 5.59] and (4.0, 5.5] agree); with `SetOption(dbPageTimeout, 2000)` it was (3.54, 4.04] s and with 1000 (2.07, 4.08] s, so the setting moves the boundary down but not in proportion; the held snapshot stays old until `Requery`, which moves it (and the dynaset) to the new value; `Transactions` is True for dynaset/table, False for snapshot/forward-only; a held dynaset shows a deleted row as a placeholder (3167).
- **Records**: q2/read-refresh-fine-grid/; q1/txn-view-refresh-boundary/, q1/read-refresh-grid/; evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/txn-view-readers-late/, xproc-uncommitted-membership-late/, xproc-held-cursors-membership-late/, xproc-reader-own-level-late/; evidence/r-txn-1b/ws2-reader-after-other-rollback/, rs-types-after-rollback/.
- **Open**: what fixes the boundary (about 5 s with the default); each look is an observation that may itself refresh.

### R-TXN-1 TXN-CLOSE: what ends and releases a level; Close of a recordset
- **Claim**: `Workspace.Close` with 1, 2 or 3 levels (also after an inner commit, with a pending Edit, with two Databases, or with an update inside the level) returns without error, rolls back everything and leaves every held child 3420; releasing Workspace and Database without `Close` also rolls back; `Database.Close` with the level open keeps the level (Rollback works) and leaves children 3420; a pending Edit/AddNew is dropped by Rollback (`EditMode` 0, `Update` 3020) and survives `CommitTrans`. `Close` of a recordset or clone opened BEFORE `BeginTrans`, called inside the level, raises 3246 in 6 of 7 variants (plain dynaset without any edit, after Edit/Update, original with a clone, clone, clone of an edited original, also on `Workspaces(0)`); the table-type recordset (Seek, Edit/Update) closes without error; the level is not ended: `Rollback` then succeeds in every variant and the row is back at its old value. A recordset opened inside the level closes without error in all eight variants (plain, Edit+Update, clone closed after the original's Update, original with a clone, clone, table-type, Edit+CancelUpdate, AddNew+Update).
- **Claim (the recordset after the resolution)**: `Rollback` of the level makes a recordset opened inside it invalid. `EditMode` still answers 0, but Field.Value, `Move 0`, `MoveFirst` and `Bookmark` raise 3420, and `LastModified` returns a null array. This holds whether the level changed anything or not (no edit; only Edit+put+CancelUpdate; Edit+Update), for dynaset and table type alike. A dynaset opened before `BeginTrans` stays valid after the same Rollback but loses its current record: Field.Value and `Move 0` raise 3021, `MoveFirst` repositions and reads the rolled-back value, `LastModified` is a null array and `Bookmark` answers. `CommitTrans` instead keeps a recordset opened inside the level valid and on its row: Field.Value reads, and `LastModified` is the committed row's bookmark.
- **Records**: evidence/r-cursor-1/edit-cancel-rollback/, edit-rollback-no-change/, edit-rollback-cancel-only/, edit-rollback-table/, edit-rollback-opened-before/, edit-commit-opened-inside/; q2/txn-close-recordset-opened-before-level/, q1/txn-close-recordset-close-variants/, q1/txn-close-workspace-close-held-objects/; evidence/r-txn-1b/wsclose-pending-edit-depth-1/, wsclose-two-databases-depth-1/, wsclose-after-inner-commit-depth-2/, wsrelease-no-close-depth-1/; evidence/r-txn-1/workspace-close-depth-1/, workspace-close-depth-3/, close-db-held-recordset/; evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/wsrelease-no-close-read-first/; evidence/r-def-cat-txn/txn-pending-addnew-*/.
- **Open**: what ended the level in evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/clone-close-read-between/ and r-def-cat-txn/txn-close-original-recordset-while-clone-holds-level/ (Rollback then 3034, row at its old value): those programs read the table through a new snapshot inside the level between the Update and the failed Close; the new probe did not. Callbacks (T). Nested inner CommitTrans/Rollback of held Edit/AddNew buffers and OpenRecordset options are closed below (`r-q50`).

### R-TXN-1 / R-CURSOR-1 / R-DAO-1 (`r-q50-jk44-ddl-vis` Part C `wbd6`): `Database.BeginTrans` `3251`, `OpenRecordset` option matrix, & held edit buffers across nested partial rollback
- **Claim**:
  - *`Database.BeginTrans`/`CommitTrans`/`Rollback` & `Workspace` txn sharing (ACCDB & Jet 4 MDB)*: `Database.{BeginTrans,CommitTrans,Rollback}` exist on dispatch and fail `3251` (`0x800A0CB3`, not `DISP_E_UNKNOWNNAME`). `DBEngine.BeginTrans` and `Workspaces(0).CommitTrans`/`Rollback` share one transaction level across open `Database` handles (`db1`/`db2`) and open recordsets (`rs1`/`rs2`/`rs3`).
  - *`OpenRecordset` option matrix (ACCDB & MDB)*: `dbDenyWrite` (`1`) on table-type or dynaset blocks both `ws2` AND same-Workspace `Execute UPDATE` with `3008` (`0x800A0BC0`), while allowing `ws2` snapshot/dynaset opens. `dbDenyRead` (`2`) on table-type blocks both `ws2` AND same-Workspace snapshot reads with `3008` (`dbDenyRead` on dynaset opens). `dbAppendOnly` (`8`) opens at `RecordCount=0` and admits `AddNew` + `Edit` of newly added rows. On `P INNER JOIN C`, editing join key `PID` fails `3164` (`0x800A0C5C`) at `Field.Value` put under `dbConsistent` (`32`) vs `3200` (`0x800A0C80`) at `Update` (RI) under `dbInconsistent` (`16`); `dbConsistent | dbInconsistent` fails `3001` (`0x800A0BB9`); `dbSeeChanges` (`512`) fails `3197` (`0x800A0C7D`) at `Edit()`.
  - *Held `Edit()`/`AddNew()` across nested levels & Close*: held `Edit()`/`AddNew()` buffers survive both outer `CommitTrans` and nested inner `CommitTrans` (on pre-populated `rsOut`) and post on later `Update()`; both outer `Rollback` AND nested inner `Rollback` drop held `Edit()`/`AddNew()` buffers (`EditMode=0`, `Update` -> `3020` `0x800A0BCC`); `Recordset.Close` outside txn discards cleanly; `Database.Close` discards and invalidates held recordsets (`3420` `0x800A0D5C`). Nested inner `Rollback` + outer `CommitTrans` writes 0 bytes before outer `CommitTrans`, which persists outer + inner-committed rows while keeping the rolled-back inner `INSERT`'s TableDef `rowCount`/`PK` bump (`4 -> 5` on page 42 `@16/@67`).
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q50-jk44-ddl-vis/{dao-txn-surface-sharing,dao-opt-deny-append,dao-opt-join-seechanges,dao-edit-buf-commit-rb,dao-edit-buf-nested-partial}-{accdb,mdb}` (10 probes + `.a2` repeats, `nativeRead keep:true`).

### R-TXN-1 TXN-FLUSH-COMMIT: an explicit commit is in the file at return
- **Claim**: After the outer `CommitTrans` with the default `dbUserCommitSync` (and with "Yes") the file image is final at the call's return (17.2 MB, then 68.0 MB, unchanged at every look for 500 ms) and a process killed 0 ms after the return leaves the rows visible to the next opener (ACCDB and MDB, 3 of 3); with `SetOption(dbUserCommitSync, "No")` the file has its final size at the return (34.1 MB) but its content still changes at the looks 200 and 250 ms later before it is stable, and the kill 0 ms after the return lost the commit (n=1 per format). An inner forced commit followed by outer Rollback leaves the file unchanged. A small transaction leaves the main file unchanged until the commit; a large one (4,096-row insert) writes pages before the commit (13.1 MB, 28.5 MB, 64.4 MB inside the level) yet a process killed 1.5 s after the Execute, before CommitTrans, leaves the next opener the original 2,048 rows in a 13.0 MB file. In a sampler trace of an explicit transaction (new data pages 49-2188 in the first sample, pages 0, 1, 45-48 and 634 on in the second, none afterwards; one record) the new pages come first. The ETW traces show the mechanism: `CommitTrans` issues the transaction's writes itself, on the calling thread, before it returns; with "No" it writes only the growth bytes and an ACE background thread writes the pages about 508 ms later. Neither issues `FlushFileBuffers`; that comes from the timer of TXN-FLUSH-BEHIND.
- **Conditions**: process-termination model with a live OS cache and public file-image reads; cannot show power-loss durability. The ETW traces see ACE's WriteFile and FlushFileBuffers calls, not the disk.
- **Records**: q2/txn-flush-usercommitsync-no-timeline/ (default, No, Yes), q1/txn-flush-implicit-file-image-timeline/ (txn-inside, txn-committed-0), q1/crash-txn-kill-between-execute-and-commit/; evidence/r-txn-flush/accdb-explicit-wait-0ms/ and mdb-explicit-wait-0ms/ (3 each), -usercommitsync-no/; evidence/r-crash-1-midcall/*-txn-kill-after-100ms, -400ms; evidence/r-crash-1-kill/*-txn-committed-ace-first, *-txn-committed-forceflush-ace-first; evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/txn-flush-live-images/; evidence/r-sample-rec/pages-large-explicit/.
- **Open**: `dbForceOSFlush` against default: the ETW recorder can now answer it (F).

### R-TXN-1 TXN-FLUSH-OP: an implicit statement is not in the file at return unless ImplicitCommitSync is Yes
- **Claim**: An implicit (non-transactional) `Execute` returns before its change is in the file: killed 0 or 250 ms after return the next opener sees the old count (3 of 3 each, ACCDB and MDB), and the file image read in-process after the return is still the post-statement size at 0 and 250 ms. With `SetOption(dbImplicitCommitSync, "Yes")` the file image is final at the return (17,166,336 B at the first look and unchanged for 500 ms) and a process killed 0 ms after the return leaves the rows present (n=1 per format); the same statement under "No" is not final at the return (29.0 MB at the first look, 34.1 MB at the 200 ms look, a further content change at 250 ms). In the ETW traces the shared-session statement returns with its pages unwritten and an ACE background thread writes them 500 to 545 ms later (INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE alike); with "Yes" Execute writes them itself before it returns. In an exclusive session (CreateDatabase's among them) the statement is deferred too, and the next Execute writes the pending pages before its own, so back-to-back statements there look synchronous (r-q41-create-session-counter, corrected 2026-11-25 from "every statement writes synchronously").
- **Records**: evidence/r-etw-rec/insert-implicit-256/, insert-implicit-4096/, update-in-place-4096/, delete-indexed-4096/, option-implicitcommitsync-yes/; q2/txn-flush-implicitcommitsync-yes-timeline/; evidence/r-txn-flush/*-implicit-wait-0ms/, *-wait-250ms/, *-implicitcommitsync-yes/; q1/txn-flush-implicit-file-image-timeline/ (t0, t250).
- **Open**: none that blocks.

### R-TXN-1 TXN-FLUSH-BEHIND: the write-behind window ends about 0.5 s after the return
- **Claim**: After an implicit `Execute` returns, the main file keeps its post-statement image until about 0.5 s later and then gets its final image (and size) in one burst: in three repeats on the big fixture the image was unchanged at 0.43-0.55 s after the return and final by 0.54-0.74 s (signal timestamps); a sampler trace of a 4,096-row implicit INSERT (2,189 pages) showed nothing changed at +0.50 s and all changed pages (0, 1, 46, 49, 50, ...) in the sample at +0.54 s, none later; killed 300 ms (+0.37 s) and 400 ms (+0.48 s) after the return the next opener sees 2,048 rows, at 500 ms (+0.56 s) 4,096, at 1 s 3 of 3, at 250 ms 0 of 3. A small implicit INSERT (6 ms statement) left the file unchanged for the whole 0.47 s sampled and changed it at `Close` (one record). `Database.Close` right after the Execute makes the file final at once (135.8 MB at the first look) and the kill 0 ms after leaves the rows present (n=1 each); `DBEngine.Idle(dbRefreshCache)` and `Idle(dbFreeLocks)` do not (the image is not final at the first look, final at the usual time; kill after Idle(dbRefreshCache): 2,048 rows); opening and closing a snapshot recordset after the statement leaves the file final at the next look (n=1). The same window explains why another process's `Idle(dbRefreshCache)` right after five rapid implicit updates shows the fourth, not the fifth (q1/read-refresh-grid).
- **Records**: q2/txn-flush-boundary-fine-timeline/ (three runs), q2/txn-flush-idle-and-close-timeline/, q2/txn-flush-implicit-kill-after-300ms-return/, -400ms-return/, q2/txn-flush-implicitcommitsync-yes-timeline/ (No run); q1/txn-flush-implicit-kill-after-500ms-return/, -750ms-return/, -idle-refresh-then-kill/, -db-close-then-kill/, -file-image-timeline/; evidence/r-txn-flush/*-implicit-wait-0ms/, -250ms/, -1000ms/ (3 attempts each), *-wait-3000ms/, *-wait-6000ms/; evidence/r-crash-1-midcall/*-implicit-kill-after-100ms, -400ms; evidence/r-sample-rec/pages-large-implicit/, insert-implicit/.
- **Open**: what sets the 0.5 s (`dbSharedAsyncDelay` 0 left it unchanged, `dbFlushTransactionTimeout` 1 was mixed; each n=1 to 3); the Idle(dbFreeLocks) and open/close-recordset effects are single samples. The ETW traces name the window: it is the background writer thread's delay (500 to 545 ms), and `Close` writes the pending pages itself on the calling thread.

### R-CRASH-1 CRASH-ORDER (issued order) and R-TXN-1 TXN-FLUSH: ACE flushes once per burst, from a timer thread; Close, CompactDatabase and the exclusive creation session never flush
- **Claim**: In every write probe, ACE issues one `FlushFileBuffers` on the database, from an ACE thread of its own, 2.33 to 2.89 s after the burst of writes, whether a statement, a `CommitTrans` or the background writer issued it, and under `ImplicitCommitSync` Yes and `UserCommitSync` No alike. No call flushes by itself. `Close` writes pending pages, page 1 and a 2-byte field of page 0, and does not flush; a session closed within the delay, the whole exclusive `CreateDatabase` session and `CompactDatabase` (source and new file) are never flushed by ACE. Within a statement the writes are: 2 bytes of page 0 (offset 3584 in the exclusive session, 3586 in shared ones), the pages, the same 2 bytes again. A new page is preceded by a 1-byte write at its last byte.
- **Disk writes (2b3c)**: ACE's flush puts the whole dirty range on disk as one paging write from the flushing thread (insert-implicit-256 a3: `FLUSH`, then p0–p52, 217,088 B, in one call). Pages a session leaves unflushed reach the disk only through the system's lazy writer (pid 4), at a delay ACE does not control and not in ACE's issued order. Observed delays after `Close`: 1.92 s for the creation session (p0–p48 in one ascending write) and 9.93 s for the shared session (p49–p52, then p45–p48, in two calls; lazy-writer-window a3). The shared session's page 1 and its 2-byte page-0 field were not on disk 15.4 s after its `Close`. In lazy-writer-window a2, no page of the shared session reached the disk within 15 s of its `Close`. So the file a crash leaves can be any mix of on-disk pages and lost cached writes, and a crash state is rebuilt from the paging writes, not from the issued writes.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, DAO, shared and exclusive sessions; ETW Microsoft-Windows-Kernel-File with each operation's process and thread. The recorder names an event by FileKey, so it attributes paging writes, the lazy writer's included. `calcdb-oracle etw-check` proves this: a cached 64 KB write closed with no flush is named as a pid-4 paging write after 8.9 s and after 1.9 s in two runs. Events with no attempt file are only counted (lazy-writer-window a2: 6,827 unnamed writes system-wide).
- **Records**: evidence/r-etw-rec/ (12 records; `lazy-writer-window` decides that Close does not flush; its a2 and a3 bracket the lazy writer's delay; insert-implicit-256 a3 shows the flush's paging write).
- **Open**: what triggers the timer (a period or an idle delay) and its value under continuous load; flushes inside a long transaction; the 2-byte field's meaning is the next note; the lazy writer's delay is the operating system's, so a crash model treats it as unbounded instead of measuring it further; torn writes and power loss stay T.

### R-CRASH-1 CRASH-ORDER, W-04: the page-0 field at offset 3586 counts the session's commits
- **Claim**: The byte at page-0 offset 3586, the field ACE rewrites before and after a statement's pages, rises by one for each commit of a write: each `Database.Execute` statement that changes rows, and each outer `CommitTrans` with changes (three DELETE statements inside one transaction count once). Recordset edits are gathered into one lazy commit per burst: one dynaset with 1, 2 or 4 Deletes, one Edit/Update, and two dynasets each with one Delete, all count once. Two Deletes count once when 100 to 485 ms apart (three runs each at 300 to 485 ms) and twice when 1 s or more apart (2 to 4 s, two runs each); at 500 ms they counted twice in three runs of four. That fits ACE's deferred write of an automatic operation 500–545 ms after it (TXN-FLUSH-OP), not the flush timer of 2.33–2.89 s (an earlier text of this note took that timer as the boundary). A Rollback, and an open and close with no write, count nothing. The count carries across sessions: two sessions of one Delete each leave 1, then 2. The bytes around it (3584–3585 `1b 01`, 3587–3591 `01 00 01 00 01` in this fixture) did not change.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, copies of the r-cursor-1-nav fixture (field 0 at the start), DAO in one process; the bytes 3584..3591 read from the closed file and kept in each record.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q8-header-counter/` (14 questions: no-write, one-delete, two-deletes, four-deletes, one-update, one-insert-sql, two-statements, transaction-three-deletes, rolled-back-delete, two-sessions-one-delete-each, two-recordsets-one-delete-each, delete-then-execute, two-deletes-4s-apart, two-statements-4s-apart). The compare-set growth-1 file differences agree: r-cursor-1/pos-delete-ends (four Deletes) 0 to 1; edit-validation-order 3; editmode-by-type 1; edit-validation-errors-per-failure 4.
- **Burst boundaries (r-q10-commit-counter-edits, 10 questions on r-cursor-1-edit):** an AddNew's consumed AutoNumber is a write of its own burst. AddNew with CancelUpdate, once or twice, counts 1, and so does a rolled-back AddNew, since the AutoNumber stays consumed. A refused Update for Required (3314) or a field rule (3316) adds nothing beyond the AddNew's burst, but a refused unique insert (3022) commits on its own and counts 2. Opening a snapshot ends the burst: two edits count 1, and with a snapshot read between them 2. A snapshot alone counts 0. This gives the 3 of r-cursor-1/edit-validation-order and the 4 of r-fix-cursor-dao/edit-validation-errors-per-failure, which differs by a snapshot between edits.
- **Open**: the second byte's role, and what happens past 255; the gap between 500 ms and 1 s, where the deferred write's timing decides; whether other reads (a dynaset or table-type open, a Find) end a burst; MDB.

### R-CRASH-1 CRASH-ORDER, W-04, W-07: a session reserves a 32-page extent in page 1 at its first allocation, and Close gives back what it did not use in one more commit
- **Claim**: The global usage map on page 1 holds one bit per page from byte 4032 (page p at byte 4032 + p/8, bit p mod 8; 1 = free). A session's first statement that allocates a page (CREATE TABLE, or the INSERT of a table's first row) clears the 4 bytes starting with the byte that holds the first free page: pages 40-71 (bytes 4037-4040) when the file ends at page 42 or 44, pages 48-79 (4038-4041) when it ends at 50. Its pages come from that extent; the statement that needs a page past it clears the next 4 bytes (4041-4044 at the 16th CREATE TABLE) with no extra counter step. `Close` then sets the bits again for the unused pages from the file's end to the extent's end (4037 `f0` for a 44-page file, `e0` for 45, 4041 `c0` after 78 pages) and for the pages of tables dropped in the session, as one more commit written by Close on the calling thread: `@3586+2`, pages 1-3, `@3586+2`, and page 0 offset 3586 +1. That is the "+1 more in a session whose changes touched the global map": it is written at Close, never at the first allocation, once per session however many extents (19 for 18 CREATE TABLE). A session that allocates nothing has no Close commit: a TableDef rename (+1 at the statement, which writes pages 7, 17 and 42, page 42 with its own bytes) and the deletes of r-q8 leave Close writing nothing. A SQL DDL statement in a shared session is written at its return on the calling thread (16 writes, +1), not by the deferred writer; the INSERT is written about 545 ms later by another thread (TXN-FLUSH-OP). The DAO TableDefs.Append commits twice: at the return (+1, the pages with page 1) and again about 505-520 ms later from another thread (page 42 rewritten with the same bytes, +1); it leaves the MSysObjects and MSysACEs TDEF counts (page 2 bytes 16, 67, 79 `12`>`13`; page 3 byte 16 `24`>`26`, 67 `12`>`13`) to the Close commit, where the SQL route writes them at the statement.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB copies of r-cat-1-accdb-empty, DAO, shared sessions; page 0 bytes 3584-3591, page hashes and pages 0-3 read beside ACE's open handle right after each step, 1.5 s later and after Close; ETW file-I/O trace.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q15-ddl-open/` counter-one-create (twice), counter-two-creates, counter-create-then-read (a snapshot read adds nothing), counter-dao-append (twice), counter-dao-append-pages, counter-create-second-session, counter-insert-second-session, counter-rename-second-session, counter-close-pages, counter-close-pages-insert, counter-close-pages-rename (0 bytes change at Close), counter-reserve-later-start, counter-reserve-overflow, counter-drop-pages. Totals agree with r-q11-ddl-closure (create-one-long 2, two-tables 3, append-dao 3).
- **Open**: the extent when free pages lie inside the file (after a drop in an earlier session) or at a byte the file end does not start; whether the extent size is fixed at 32; MDB (r-q11 mdb-create-one-long differs at page 0).

### R-LOCK-2 RO-NATIVE: a read-only session writes nothing to the database
- **Claim**: `OpenDatabase` read-only and shared opens the database with share read/write, creates or opens the lock file and writes its 64-byte record at offset 0, reads the database and writes and flushes nothing in it; `Close` deletes the lock file.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, DAO, one process; ETW trace.
- **Records**: evidence/r-etw-rec/readonly-session/.
- **Open**: an MDB, a second reader, and an exclusive read-only open.

### R-SQL-2 / R-LOCK-1 (r-q43-schema-lval-index): DDL and DML on a read-only Database handle
- **Claim**: On a read-only `Database` handle alone, `DROP TABLE <missing>` raises 3376 before checking read-only; DDL on existing or new targets (`DROP TABLE T`, `CREATE TABLE U1`, `CREATE INDEX`, `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN`) fails 3370 (`"Cannot modify the design of table '<T>'. It is in a read-only database."`), `UPDATE` fails 3073, and `DELETE` fails 3086, leaving the file unchanged. When a writable handle to the same database is open in the same process, `DROP TABLE <missing>` still raises 3376 while `CREATE TABLE`, `CREATE INDEX`, and `DROP TABLE` through the read-only handle succeed.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, DAO `Database.Execute`, one process (`ro-ddl-disc-accdb`).
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q43-schema-lval-index/ro-ddl-disc-accdb/`.
- **Built (`CalcDb.Sql` slice `alta/nk97-bool-roddl`, `6827a95c`, and `CalcDb.Com.Dao` slice `alta/com-dao-tabledef-roddl`, `77d8e0d8`)**: `DdlExecutor` checks missing-table `DROP TABLE` -> `3376` before checking `database.ReadOnly`; `DdlExecutor` and `ActionExecutor` attach structured `SqlFailure.ReadOnlyInfo` (`3370` DDL, `3073` `INSERT ... VALUES`/`UPDATE`, `3086` `DELETE`). In `CalcDb.Com.Dao`, `ActionRoute` surfaces `ReadOnlyInfo` from `DAO.Database` (`RecordsAffected = 0`, no `IErrorInfo`) for recorded read-only ACCDB alone modes (`INSERT ... VALUES` shared `0`/`128` or exclusive `128`, DDL/`UPDATE`/`DELETE` shared `Options = 0`), while `SharedDatabaseSession` ref-counts `ComDatabaseSession` so a shared read-only ACCDB opened beside a same-`Workspace` shared writable peer admits recorded peer-RW `Database.Execute` `CREATE TABLE` / `CREATE INDEX` / single-table `DROP TABLE` (`Options = 0`) and `INSERT ... VALUES` (`Options = 0`/`128`) and coordinates `SharedHold` and last-holder `CloseCommit`.
- **Open**: `INSERT ... SELECT`, invalid-DDL precedence, `QueryDef.Execute`, ADO `Mode=Read` DDL, Jet 4 MDB read-only `Execute`/peer-RW, exclusive read-only DDL/`UPDATE`/`DELETE`, peer-RW `UPDATE`/`DELETE`/`ALTER TABLE`/`DROP INDEX`, cross-workspace / RO-before-RW peers, `dro.Updatable` inheritance and post-`drw.Close()` behavior (to be reconciled with `r-q46` `6xzx`).

### R-TXN-1 TXN-TEMP: lock-count exhaustion only
- **Claim**: With `SetOption(dbMaxLocksPerFile, 5)` a bulk `UPDATE` raises 3035 with `RecordsAffected` 0, earlier changes stay, the level stays usable (`CommitTrans` keeps earlier changes, `Rollback` reverses them, `Database.Close` leaves them uncommitted); implicit statements leave partial progress (COUNT 512, SUM 510) with or without `dbFailOnError`.
- **Records**: evidence/r-txn-1/exhaust-locks-commit/, -rollback/, -close/; evidence/r-txn-1b/implicit-exhaustion-failonerror/, implicit-exhaustion-nofail/.
- **Open**: TEMP/disk faults (T).

### R-TXN-1 TXN-DDL: catalog changes are transactional
- **Claim**: CREATE/ALTER/DROP TABLE, index add/drop, DAO TableDef append, SELECT INTO, relation drop, QueryDef create/SQL change and database property create are reversed by Rollback (also an outer rollback after inner commit), judged on a fresh Workspace after `Refresh`; a held `TableDef` shows old counts until `Refresh`; DROP under an open recordset 3211; another Workspace or process never lists uncommitted objects; AutoNumber values consumed in the level are not restored.
- **Records**: evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/ddl-alter-index-drop-rollback-catalog/, ddl-relation-querydef-property-rollback-refresh/, ddl-second-workspace-idle/; evidence/r-txn-1b/ddl-drop-recreate-rollback/, ddl-dao-tabledef-append-rollback/, ddl-inner-commit-outer-rollback/, ddl-held-tabledef-after-rollback/, temp-make-table-rollback/; evidence/r-txn-1/ddl-create-table-rollback/, ddl-nested-failure-outer-rollback/; q1/cat-p-custom-properties-all-owners-rollback/ (database properties).
- **Open**: counter restoration across a crash.

### R-TXN-1 TXN-SUPPORT: ADO nesting values and events (partial)
- **Claim**: Through the ACE OLE DB provider `Connection.BeginTrans` returns the level (1, 2, 3), `Attributes` reads 0; setting `adXactCommitRetaining` (131072) or `adXactAbortRetaining` (262144) is accepted, and the next `BeginTrans` returns a level one higher after a `CommitTrans` (2 → 3, 4 → 5); `Close` inside a transaction raises 0x800A0CAE; provider properties `Transaction DDL` 16 and `Isolation Levels` 4096; with the outermost level never resolved no change reached the file (independent DAO read: rows unchanged). The recording event sink sees, on the calling thread, WillConnect/ConnectComplete (dispids 6, 7), BeginTransComplete (1) per `BeginTrans`, CommitTransComplete (3) per `CommitTrans`, WillExecute (4) and ExecuteComplete (5) per `Execute` with the command text and the `RecordsAffected` (0 for a refused statement, 1 or 4 for a successful one), Disconnect (8) at `Close`; an `Execute` of an UPDATE returns a closed recordset (`Close` on it 0x800A0E78).
- **Records**: q2/txn-support-ado-rollback-control-events/, q2/txn-local-two-ado-connections-events/ (two Connections), q1/txn-support-ado-nesting/; evidence/r-ops/ado-connection-events/.
- **Open**: rollback through ADO: our probes called `Rollback`, which is not an ADO member (ADO ran it as a command named "Rollback" and refused it, 0x800A0BB9, every time); `RollbackTrans` was never called. ISAM/ODBC participants (T).

### R-LOCK-1 EX-OPEN: share admission matrix
- **Claim**: Across processes, DAO exclusive read/write refuses every second mode (3045); exclusive read-only admits exclusive/shared read-only and refuses read/write; shared read-only admits shared read-only/read-write and exclusive read-only, refuses exclusive read/write (3704 ACCDB, 3045 MDB); shared read/write admits shared modes and refuses exclusive; native `GENERIC_READ|FILE_SHARE_READ` opens beside DAO read-only modes and is refused (Win32 32) beside DAO read/write; native read/write share-zero is refused beside every DAO mode and refuses every DAO mode (3045) in both orders; same pattern in ACCDB and MDB.
- **Records**: evidence/r-lock-1-2/{accdb,mdb}-dao-*-then-dao-* (32 pairs), *-native-r-shareread-then-dao-*, *-native-rw-share0-then-dao-*, *-dao-*-then-native-*.
- **Open**: the 3704/3045 split; actual access/share flags (W-PROBE).

### R-LOCK-1 EX-DEFAULT: omitted arguments are shared read/write
- **Claim**: `OpenDatabase(path)` with every optional argument omitted, in two processes, opens in both and reads in both (2,048 rows); the lock file grows 64 → 128 B; after one closes, a default open by the other still works. Same as the explicit shared read/write pair.
- **Records**: q1/ex-default-omitted-arguments/; evidence/r-lock-1-2/*-dao-shared-rw-then-dao-shared-rw.
- **Open**: an exclusive-only open beside a default open across processes (the probe's attempt was admitted in-process after b's own open; EX-PUBLIC rules).

### R-LOCK-1 EX-CREATE: creation beside other processes
- **Claim**: `CreateDatabase` of a path that exists is refused 3204 and does not touch the file, whether the other process created it a moment before (a started 0.25 s earlier, b called during that window), holds it open (exclusive: `OpenDatabase` by the other process 3045) or has closed it; the winning creator's file opens (Version 12.0, 172,032 B) in an independent final read. While the first creator is frozen inside `CreateDatabase` (100 and 200 ms after it started) no file exists (opens 3024), a second creator of the same path succeeds and holds it exclusively (a native read open Win32 32), and the frozen creator, once resumed, gets 3204; the finished file opens with its five system tables.
- **Records**: q2/crash-new-suspend-mid-create-100ms/, -200ms/; q1/ex-create-race-two-creators/ (one overlap), q1/ex-create-over-held-destination/ (held, then closed); evidence/r-cat-1/create-accdb-existing-destination/.
- **Open**: cleanup with pending I/O (T).

### R-LOCK-1 EX-ALIAS: path spellings across processes; the extended spelling is refused
- **Claim**: With A holding the plain path exclusive, B opening the upper-case, `.\` or slash spelling shared read/write is refused 3045 as for the plain path; unheld, those spellings open.
- **Records**: evidence/r-lock-1-2/*-alias-xproc-upper/, -dot/, -slashes/; q1/ex-alias-extended-path-unheld-control/ (plain, slashes, dot, upper open unheld; the absolute `\\?\C:\...` spelling gives 3043 with nobody holding the file, so ACE refuses that spelling itself, as in evidence/r-lock-1-2/*-alias-*-extended/).
- **Open**: hard links and 8.3 names (T); UNC extended forms.

### R-LOCK-1 EX-PROBE: metadata probing beside every DAO mode
- **Claim**: A `FILE_READ_ATTRIBUTES` open with all sharing succeeds beside every DAO mode (also exclusive read/write); a data read through it gives Win32 5; the lock file opens for read with all sharing when present (64 B shared, absent under exclusive opens, Win32 2).
- **Records**: evidence/r-lock-1-2/accdb-dao-excl-rw-then-metadata-probe/, mdb-dao-shared-ro-then-metadata-probe/ (representative of 16).
- **Open**: `FileIdInfo` itself is not requested by the driver.

### R-LOCK-1 EX-RACE: an opener frozen before it has the file reserves nothing
- **Claim**: Process a calls `OpenDatabase` exclusive and is frozen 5 ms or 25 ms later (its call not returned): process b opens the same file shared and then exclusive, both succeed, a native `GENERIC_READ|FILE_SHARE_READ` open fails Win32 32 (b holds it exclusive); after b closes and a is resumed, a's open returns a Database and reads 2,048 rows, and a final open by b succeeds. Nothing of the half-finished open was visible to b.
- **Conditions**: whole-process suspension (SuspendParticipant), local NTFS; where inside the call a stopped is not visible.
- **Records**: q2/ex-race-suspend-opener-mid-open-5ms/, q2/ex-race-suspend-opener-mid-open-25ms/.
- **Open**: path replacement and injected failure after identity probing (T).

### R-LOCK-1 EX-PUBLIC and R-LOCK-2 RO-INPROC: in-process admission
- **Claim**: In one process (same or second Workspace) a second binding is admitted more widely than across processes: beside an exclusive or shared read/write first binding all four modes open; beside exclusive read-only, exclusive and shared read/write are refused (3045); beside shared read-only, exclusive read/write is refused (3704 ACCDB, 3045 MDB); reads succeed in both, also after the first closes. Several DBEngine objects of one process are one engine (see TXN-LOCAL), so their opens follow the same in-process rules.
- **Records**: evidence/r-lock-1-2/{accdb,mdb}-inproc-ws1-*-then-*, accdb-inproc-ws2-*-then-*; q2/engine-instances-in-process-admission/.
- **Open**: alias spellings in-process do not discriminate.

### R-LOCK-1 EX-PUBLIC, R-LOCK-2 RO-INPROC, W-05 `6xzx` (`r-q46-dao-ddl-multi`, `r-q47-bigint-expr-edges`): in-process `OpenDatabase` sharing, RW+RO open-order inheritance, Exclusive 3045/3704, `TableDefs.Refresh`, & post-close/post-drop lifetimes
- **Claim**:
  - *RW+RW in-process sharing (same WS & two WS)*: shared in-process page cache makes `db1` `INSERT` (0 bytes on disk at return) immediately visible to `db2` fresh snapshots (same WS and two WS, ACCDB & Jet 4 MDB); interleaved `db2` `INSERT` flushes `+1` (`04->05`), `db1` dynaset `Edit`/`Update` bumps `+1` (`05->06`), and a 1500 ms pause flushes deferred `+1` (`06->07`). Under `ws0.BeginTrans`, same-WS `db2` sees `db1`'s uncommitted row and `UPDATE`s the same page (`CommitTrans` `07->08`); two-WS `db2` does NOT see the uncommitted row and same-page `UPDATE` fails `3188` (`0x800A0C74`, `CommitTrans` `07->09`). Closing `db1` while `db2` stays open writes 0 bytes and keeps `.laccdb`/`.ldb` (64 B); closing the last handle releases `.laccdb`/`.ldb` and any reserved page-1 extent (`+1`).
  - *RW+ReadOnly open-order asymmetry*: when RW opens FIRST and `ReadOnly=True` opens SECOND (same WS or two WS, ACCDB & Jet 4 MDB), the RO handle inherits RW: `db2.Updatable = True (-1)`, `db2.Execute("INSERT ...")` succeeds, and `rs2d.Edit()` succeeds; `db2` held dynaset sees `db1` updates immediately on `Move 0` (held snapshot keeps old value across `Idle(dbRefreshCache)` + `Move 0` until `Requery`). After `db1` (`RW`) closes (`0xE02 04->05`), surviving `db2` (`ReadOnly=True` opened second) keeps `db2.Updatable = -1`, `db2.Execute("INSERT...")` succeeds (`RecordsAffected = 1`), and dynaset `Edit`/`Update` succeeds (`05->06`, pause `06->07`, `db2.Close()` `07`). When `ReadOnly=True` opens FIRST and RW opens SECOND, `dbRo.Updatable = False (0)`, `dbRw` writes are visible to `dbRo` fresh snapshots, and `dbRo.Execute("INSERT ...")` fails `3073` (`0x800A0C01`, `"Operation must use an updateable query."`).
  - *Exclusive in-process admission & closing order*: beside Exclusive RW `(True, False)` first (same WS or two WS, ACCDB & Jet 4 MDB), Shared RW, Exclusive RW, and Shared RO all succeed, and after exclusive opener `e1` closes surviving `e2srw` stays usable and writable (`INSERT` succeeds). Beside Exclusive RO `(True, True)` first, Shared RW and Exclusive RW fail `3045` (`0x800A0BE5`) while Shared RO succeeds. Beside Shared RO `(False, True)` first, Exclusive RW fails `3704` (`0x800A0E78`) on ACCDB and `3045` (`0x800A0BE5`) on Jet 4 MDB (both same WS and two WS).
  - *DDL, `TableDefs.Refresh`, & held `TableDef` lifetimes*: after `db1` runs `CREATE TABLE U` + `INSERT`, `db2`'s held `TableDefs` (`tds2`) stays stale (`Count` unchanged, `Item("U")` `3265`) until `tds2.Refresh()`, after which `Count` rises `+1`, `Item("U")` returns live `_TableDef` (`RecordCount=1`), and `db2` queries `U` cleanly; after `db1` runs `DROP TABLE U`, **before `tds2.Refresh()`** `tds2` stays stale (`Count` `+1`, `Item("U")` still returns `_TableDef`), held `tdU`/`flsU`/`fV`/`ixsU` cached metadata (`Name`, `RecordCount`, `Attributes`, `Fields.Count`, `fV.Name/Type`, `Indexes.Count`) succeed (`DateCreated`/`LastUpdated` return `VT_DATE 0`), while `OpenRecordset` and `flsU.Append` fail `3078` (`0x800A0C06`); **after `tds2.Refresh()`**, ALL held wrapper calls fail `3420` (`0x800A0D5C`, `"Object invalid or no longer set."`) and `tds2.Item("U")` fails `3265` (`0x800A0CC1`). Page-1 extent and dropped `U` pages stay reserved across `db1.Close` and return only at `db2.Close` (`07->08`). Identical across same WS / two WS and ACCDB / MDB.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q46-dao-ddl-multi/mh-*` (`mh-rwrw-*`, `mh-rwro-*`, `mh-excl-*`, `mh-ddl-refresh-*`; 16 probes + 3 `a2` repeats); `evidence/r-q47-bigint-expr-edges/{mh-rwro-close-rw-first-*,mh-held-tdf-after-drop-*}` (4 probes + 4 `a2` repeats).
- **Open**: `3+` mixed handles; cross-Workspace `Rollback` while second handle holds a cursor; exclusive opener released without `Close`; `NativeOpen` sharing state after `e1` closes while `e2srw` stays open.
- **Built (W-05 `6xzx`, `02c41f9c`)**: `FileSessionPool` / `PooledFileSession` in `CalcDb.Com.Core`, DAO `OpenDatabase`/`Close`/`TableDefs`/`ActionRoute` wiring, and `DBEngine.Workspaces(i)` indexed get; `r-lock-1-2` `*-inproc-ws{1,2}-*` 48/48 and `r-q46` `mh-excl-*` 4/4 match byte-for-byte. Noted from kept page-0 bytes: RO-first + RW-second writes page-0 commit counter slot 2 (`0x0E04` `00->01`, keeping `0x0E02=07`) in all 4 `mh-rwro-*` records; `mh-rwrw-twows-{accdb,mdb}` steps `0x0E02` `07->09` (+2 across `3188` + `CommitTrans`) while `mh-rwrw-samews` steps `07->08`.
- **Discriminators (`r-q52-ddl-make-domain`, ACCDB & Jet 4 MDB, kept pages):**
  - *`mh-twows-3188-step-{accdb,mdb}`*: `ws0.BeginTrans` + `INSERT` leaves `0x0E02 = 07`, failed `db2.Execute` (`3188`) steps `07 -> 08`, and `ws0.CommitTrans()` steps `08 -> 09` (not `07 -> 09` in one jump).
  - *`mh-rofirst-dynaset-edit-{accdb,mdb}`*: `dbRo` opened first stays `Updatable = 0`, both pre-open & post-open dynasets stay `Updatable = 0` and fail `3027` (`0x800A0BD3`) on `Edit`/`AddNew`/`Delete` even after `dbRw` opens and `Requery`; slot-2 counter `0x0E04` steps `00 -> 01` on `dbRo.Close` in same-WS vs `01 -> 02` on `dbRw.Close` in two-WS.
  - *`mh-twows-both-close-commit-{accdb,mdb}`*: when both `ws0` and `ws2` hold page-1 reservations (`CREATE TABLE`+`INSERT`), each flushes its own `+1` at its own `Close` in either close order (`07->08->09`, `0f->10->11`); when both `DROP TABLE` (`ReleasedAtClose`), first close writes 0 bytes (`0b->0b`) and last close emits one `+1` (`0b->0c`) freeing both.

### R-LOCK-1 EX-PERM and R-LOCK-2 RO-PERM: file attributes
- **Claim**: With the database read-only, shared and exclusive read/write opens fail 3051, read-only opens succeed (`Updatable` False; lock file 64 B for shared, none for exclusive); with a read-only lock file, shared modes fail (read/write 3051, read-only 3050) and exclusive modes open; with both the database and a 64-byte lock file read-only, shared read-only 3050 and read/write 3051, exclusive read/write 3051 and exclusive read-only opens (count read, `Updatable` False) with the lock file untouched (hash f5a5fd42d16a unchanged); a stale writable lock file stops no mode and is replaced.
- **Records**: q1/ex-perm-readonly-db-and-lockfile-exclusive/ (completes the matrix); evidence/r-lock-perm/accdb-db-readonly-all-modes/, accdb-readonly-lockfile-all-modes/, accdb-stale-lockfile-all-modes/, accdb-db-and-lockfile-readonly/ and mdb counterparts.
- **Open**: folder ACLs (T).

### R-LOCK-1 EX-LIFE: what keeps an exclusive file held
- **Claim**: After `Recordset.Close`+`Database.Close` or release without `Close`, a second process opens read/write within about 25 ms. With a table recordset and a TableDef held: releasing only the Database keeps the exclusion (3045); closing the recordset (TableDef still held) ends it; releasing the TableDef changes nothing more. With an open transaction on a created Workspace, the exclusion stays after the Database and recordset are released (3045), ends at `Rollback`, and the rolled-back row reads unchanged in an independent open. A killed exclusive holder leaves no lock file and the next opener opens; killed 40 ms into an implicit `INSERT…SELECT` (its call not returned) the file is available to a shared open and to a native read open 0.1 s later, still at 0, 100, 300 and 1000 ms, and the file is unchanged (8,663,040 B, 2,048 rows).
- **Records**: q2/ex-life-kill-mid-statement-exclusive/; q1/ex-life-child-and-transaction-owner/ (seven attempts after seven states); evidence/r-lock-1-2/accdb-life-close/, accdb-life-release/ (+ mdb); evidence/r-crash-1-kill/accdb-kill-open-only-exclusive-ace-first/, mdb-...
- **Open**: pending I/O itself, inherited handles (T); each state sampled once.

### R-LOCK-2 RO-NATIVE and RO-PAIR (lock-file slots): a shared session holds one lock byte per slot and writes its record at the slot

- **Claim.** A DAO shared read-only session holds a one-byte lock on the lock file at 0x10000000 + n for its slot n, counted from 1, and its 64-byte record is at (n − 1) × 64: the computer name and `admin`, each NUL-terminated and space-padded to 32 bytes. A second session in another process takes slot 2 (lock 0x10000002, record at 64; the file grows to 128 B). When the second closes, its lock byte is released and its record stays; the last close deletes the file. The database file carries no byte-range lock at all. An open table-type recordset adds two lock bytes on the lock file, 0x40005401 and 0x50005401.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB; read-only shared opens in one and in two processes; locks found by bisecting [0, 2^62) with exclusive fail-immediately LockFileEx through a separate handle.
- **Records.** w-10-readers-only/lock-ranges-shared-readonly, lock-ranges-two-readers; r-etw-rec/readonly-session (the record write at offset 0, deletion at Close).
- **Open.** The encoding of the recordset's lock bytes (one observation: 0x54 and slot 1 are candidates); MDB `.ldb`; slot reuse after a departed session in a third open; whether the slot lock is exclusive or shared.

### R-LOCK-2 RO-PAIR: readers coexist
- **Claim**: Two and three shared read-only readers coexist and read (2,048 rows each); exclusive read-only is admitted beside a shared read-only reader (against the book's expectation); the lock file grows 64 → 128 B and is gone when all close.
- **Records**: q1/ro-three-readers-and-reads-after-writer/ (a, b, c); evidence/r-lock-1-2/*-dao-shared-ro-then-dao-shared-ro, *-dao-shared-ro-then-dao-excl-ro; evidence/r-lock-perm/accdb-readonly-db-two-readers/, mdb-...
- **Open**: none that blocks.

### R-LOCK-2 RO-ACTUAL: DAO read-only opens hold no database write access
- **Claim**: A DAO shared or exclusive read-only open does not hold write access to the database file: a native `GENERIC_READ|FILE_SHARE_READ` open succeeds and reads 4096 bytes beside it, and fails Win32 32 beside DAO read/write; read-only readers permit writers (share-write).
- **Conditions**: inferred from Windows share semantics and refusals, not from a handle trace.
- **Records**: evidence/r-lock-1-2/{accdb,mdb}-dao-shared-ro-then-native-r-shareread/, -dao-excl-ro-then-native-r-shareread/, -dao-shared-rw-then-native-r-shareread/, -dao-excl-rw-then-native-r-shareread/.
- **Open**: direct handle observation (W-PROBE).

### R-LOCK-2 RO-LATE: writers beside readers
- **Claim**: With DAO shared read-only readers, a DAO shared read/write writer opens and updates (`RecordsAffected` 1) while two or three readers are open, after one closes and after all close; the readers then (7 s, Idle) read the writer's value from a new snapshot and see an intact file; a DAO exclusive writer and a native read/write share-zero writer are refused. With native `GENERIC_READ|FILE_SHARE_READ` readers a and b, the DAO shared read/write open and the DAO exclusive open are refused 3045 and the native share-zero open Win32 32 with both held and with b alone held; after both close the writer opens and updates, native share-zero opens, and the update reads back.
- **Records**: q1/ro-late-native-lease-then-dao-writer/, q1/ro-three-readers-and-reads-after-writer/; evidence/r-lock-1-2/accdb-ro-late/, mdb-ro-late/.
- **Open**: none that blocks (the exclusive-open attempts after the process's own open were admitted in-process and are not evidence).

### R-LOCK-3 / R-TXN-1 (`r-q50-jk44-ddl-vis` Part A `jk44`): cross-engine DAO+ADO and multi-ADO in-process admission, visibility, DDL refresh, & conflicts
- **Claim**:
  - *Simultaneous admission (ACCDB & Jet 4 MDB)*: DAO shared RW + ADO shared RW (`Mode=3`), DAO RO + ADO RW, and ADO RO (`adModeRead=1`) + DAO RW coexist in-process and grow `.laccdb`/`.ldb` to 128 B (2 separate engine slots). DAO Exclusive blocks ADO Shared (`0x80004005`); ADO Exclusive (`Mode=12`) blocks DAO Shared RW & RO (`3045` / `0x800A0BE5`).
  - *Independent page caches & uncommitted/committed visibility*: uncommitted `BeginTrans` DML is invisible across DAO<->ADO and across 2 ADO `Connection`s in both directions. After `CommitTrans` (writes changed data/TDEF/index pages immediately), the other engine's held handle still reads stale cached pages at 0 ms (and ADO even after `Requery` + 500 ms); `DBEngine.Idle(dbRefreshCache)` refreshes DAO immediately (held dynaset shows ADO-deleted row 4 as `3167` / `0x800A0C5F`), and a fresh ADO `Connection` reads committed rows immediately.
  - *Cross-engine DDL refresh*: after ADO `CREATE TABLE CrossAdo`, DAO `TableDefs.Refresh` alone still reads stale Count 9 and `3265` until `DBEngine.Idle(dbRefreshCache)` + `Refresh` (Count 10, `SELECT` succeeds); after DAO `CREATE TABLE CrossDao`, open ADO `cn` immediately fails `0x80040E37` (3011) on `SELECT`/`DROP TABLE`.
  - *Cross-engine & 2× ADO conflict codes*: DAO uncommitted txn on row 1 -> ADO same-row/same-page `Execute UPDATE` fails `0x80004005` (`3218`, native `-72156238`), ADO keyset edit `Update` fails `0x80040E21` (`3218`), `T2` admitted. ADO uncommitted txn on row 1 -> DAO same-row/same-page `Execute`, pessimistic `Edit`, and optimistic `Update` all fail **`3260`** (`0x800A0CBC`, cross-session lock, not `3188`); after ADO `CommitTrans`, DAO optimistic retry fails `3197` (`0x800A0C7D`). Two ADO `Connection`s match (`3218` on `Execute`/`Update`, `T2` admitted, `3197`/`0x80040E21` on stale keyset after `CommitTrans`).
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q50-jk44-ddl-vis/{vis-dao-ado-admit,vis-dao-txn-ado-read,vis-ado-txn-dao-read,vis-cross-cursors-dml,vis-cross-ddl,vis-cross-conflict,vis-two-ado-txn}-{accdb,mdb}` (14 probes + `.a2` repeats, `nativeRead keep:true`).
- **Built (`w17-jk44-vis`, `2ac2fabb`/`ffaba57a`, 2026-11-27; ADO `vis-cross-ddl` 3011 follow-up in `w19-com-dao-core` `e2abcd64`/`644f3875`)**: Implemented cross-engine DAO+ADO and multi-ADO in-process admission (`128 B` `.laccdb`/`.ldb`, exclusive `3045`/`0x80004005`, `RO-PAIR`), independent `PageCache`s/transactions via `ComDatabaseSession` & `SharedBacking`, `DBEngine.Idle(8)` cache+catalog refresh, targeted pre-write table refresh, `3260`/`3218`/`3197` conflict mapping, MDB depth-0 snapshot `ReadView` + `Requery`, and ADO single-table `SELECT` `0x80040E37`/`3011` on an externally dropped table while `cn.Catalog` stays stale (`4,087/4,087` tests across all 7 projects + NativeAOT snapshot `w17-cp1`, `4/14` `vis-*` byte-exact Matched, `19/39` `probes/r-q50-jk44-ddl-vis`). Open: depth-0 lazy write-behind timing (`TransactionCoordinator`), close-commit counter after `DELETE` (`0xE02`/`0xE04`), index-ordered keyset `Requery`, `TransactionCoordinator.cs:1608,1995,2251` `LastConflictHolder`, and `Slot > 2` page-0 counter offset.

### R-LOCK-2 / R-ERR-1 (`r-q54-excl-ddl-ix-cmd` Part D, `lock-page-wait`): Same-process `3188` page-lock conflicts perform zero retry sleeps
- **Claim**: Under `SetOption(57, 3)` (`dbLockRetry=3`) and `SetOption(63, 200)` (`dbLockDelay=200 ms`) with another `Workspace` (`ws2`) holding a pessimistic lock on a 18-row middle data page (`ID=25` of 60-row `LockBig` across 4 pages), ACE performs **0** lock-retry sleeps on same-process `3188` page-lock conflicts: plain no-flag `UPDATE LockBig SET V='u'` finishes in **6 ms ACCDB / 1 ms MDB** (`RecordsAffected=42`, skipping all 18 rows on the locked page), `dbFailOnError` fails `3188` in **3 ms ACCDB / 2 ms MDB** (`RecordsAffected=0`), single-row `WHERE ID=26` finishes in **2 ms / 1 ms**, and `SetOption(57,1)/(63,100)` finishes in **3 ms / 2 ms**.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q54-excl-ddl-ix-cmd/field-meta-and-lockwait-{accdb,mdb}/`.

### R-LOCK-3 READ-ROLE: a pending Edit does not block readers
- **Claim**: While another process holds a pessimistic `Edit` on row 1, readers (snapshot, dynaset, forward-only, table; held and newly opened) read row 1 and the unedited values without error.
- **Records**: evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/read-role-edit-existing-readers/, read-role-edit-then-update-new-reader/, write-timing-pessimistic-matrix/.
- **Open**: native lock purpose (T).

### R-LOCK-3 READ-PATH: a reader beside a writer stopped inside a statement (partial)
- **Claim**: With a writer process frozen (all threads suspended) 60 ms into `INSERT INTO T SELECT ...` of 4,096 rows with 2,000-character memos (its call not returned; the file at 20.1 MB, the lock file 128 B once the reader is in), another process opens read-only without error or wait and reads only the old committed state: COUNT 4,096, SUM/MIN/MAX of N unchanged, memo lengths 2,000, an `IX_N` table cursor (first row, `Seek >= 20000` finds 2000/20000, last row 4096); after the writer is resumed and a pause, a fresh read gives 8,192 rows and the new SUM. The same holds in r-ops (COUNT 0 on the table while the writer is frozen mid-INSERT).
- **Records**: q2/read-path-writer-frozen-mid-insert/; evidence/r-ops/suspend-writer-mid-insert/.
- **Open** (all single samples): a writer frozen 40 ms into `UPDATE T SET M = String(3500,'y')`: the reader's aggregate over `LEN(M)` failed 3624 "Could not read the record; currently locked by another user", other reads old and intact (q2/read-path-writer-frozen-mid-memo-replace); a reader frozen mid-scan while another process updated all 4,096 N: the writer was admitted without waiting and the resumed reader returned the new SUM(N) (q2/read-path-reader-frozen-while-writer-updates, its position in the scan unknown); an index-relocating `UPDATE T SET N = N + 1000000` had already returned when the freeze came (q2/read-path-writer-frozen-mid-indexed-update); native acquisitions and page-level coherence (T).

### R-LOCK-3 READ-REFRESH: the staleness boundary and Idle
- **Claim**: Another process's committed or implicit update becomes visible to a held dynaset, a held table cursor and a new snapshot between 4.55 and 4.80 s after the update with the default `dbPageTimeout` (looks every 250 ms; the earlier samples (4.0, 5.5] and (4.08, 5.59] agree), not to a held snapshot until `Requery`; with `dbPageTimeout` 2000 between 3.54 and 4.04 s; `Idle(dbRefreshCache)` before that time changes nothing. After five rapid implicit updates (+1 each, 100 ms apart), `Idle(dbRefreshCache)` 15 ms after the last shows the fourth value (1014), not the fifth (1015); after 7 s it shows 1015: Idle shows the last flushed state, so the earlier 1001-versus-1002 ladder is the write-behind window (TXN-FLUSH-BEHIND).
- **Records**: q2/read-refresh-fine-grid/ (250 ms looks, then PageTimeout 2000); q1/read-refresh-grid/ (0.25-7 s grid, then the five-update ladder), q1/txn-view-refresh-boundary/; evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/read-refresh-elapsed/, read-refresh-ladder/.
- **Open**: what fixes the boundary (see TXN-VIEW).

### R-LOCK-3 READ-ID: a deleted-and-reinserted row
- **Claim**: After another process deletes row 5 and reinserts the same key and values (twice, the second time with changed values), a held dynaset positioned on it, read after 7 s and Idle, answers 3167 "Record is deleted" for every field at that position (its Bookmark bytes unchanged, 01070000) and neighbours read normally; `Requery` shows the reinserted row (N=51, V=9) at the same place with different bookmark bytes (01030000); a new snapshot shows it.
- **Records**: q1/read-id-delete-reinsert-late/; evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/read-id-delete-reinsert/ (stale, superseded).
- **Open**: a bookmark can differ because Requery rebuilds the keyset, so bookmark equality is not an identity test.

### R-LOCK-3 READ-SCHEMA: held descriptors across another process's DDL
- **Claim**: When the other process holds no cursor on the table, another process's `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN`, `CREATE INDEX`, `DROP TABLE` and `CREATE TABLE` all succeed; the first process's held TableDef still answers (Name, Fields.Count 3 and Indexes.Count 1) after 7 s and Idle, with no error; a refreshed `TableDefs` Item shows the new 4 fields; the held Fields collection shows 4 after its own `Refresh`; a new `SELECT *` reads the recreated table. A held QueryDef is a snapshot of its definition as of the last collection refresh: after another process set the saved SQL (7 s earlier, Idle) the held `qt.SQL` still read the old text and a fresh `Item` from a refreshed collection read the new one; after that process deleted and recreated `QT` with other SQL, the held object still answered (Name "QT", no 3420) with the previous text while a fresh `Item` read the newest and `SELECT * FROM QT` ran the newest; the held QueryDef still opened a recordset.
- **Records**: q2/read-schema-held-querydef-late/; q1/read-schema-held-descriptors-late/; evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/read-schema-held-descriptors/ (3262 with held cursors).
- **Open**: what the held QueryDef's recordset returned (not read).

### R-LOCK-3 READ-SNAPSHOT: unread rows
- **Claim**: A snapshot opened before another process updates rows 20 and 40 shows their open-time values when first read after 7 s and Idle (N=200, 400, old S) and the new values only after `Requery`; a dynaset opened at the same time shows the new values (7777, 8888, 'changed') at first read of those rows; its first row, read before the update, was not re-read.
- **Records**: q1/read-snapshot-unread-rows/ (three rows on separate pages).
- **Open**: lazy materialization of large snapshots; callbacks (T).

### R-LOCK-4 WRITE-TIMING: edit ownership vs writers
- **Claim**: A pending pessimistic `Edit` of an unindexed field (V) of row 1 blocks another process's update of an unindexed field (3260) and of an indexed field (N) of row 2 (same page), with the ADO locking mode first-opened as page (0) and as row (1, error 3218 "currently locked"); it admits V of row 64 and an INSERT; after `CancelUpdate` the row 2 update succeeds. Also: optimistic/`dbSeeChanges` admits all; the block lasts only while `Edit` is pending (admitted after CancelUpdate, Update, recordset Close or release, `Database.Close`); `dbDenyWrite` gives 3262 for UPDATE/table open and `dbDenyRead` 3261.
- **Records**: q1/write-timing-unindexed-field-page-mode/, -row-mode/ (field and mode variants); evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/write-timing-pessimistic-matrix/, write-timing-optimistic-seechanges/, write-timing-release-boundaries/, write-timing-ado-lock-mode-page/, -row/, write-deny-options/.
- **Open**: the release probe never showed the Edit still pending at each step.

### R-LOCK-4 WRITE-ORIGINAL: first Update conflicts, second Update overwrites
- **Claim**: With an optimistic dynaset edit (N = 100) pending, another process changing the same row (N), only another field (V), changes the row so that the first `Update` fails 3197 (`EditMode` stays 1); a second `Update` succeeds (`EditMode` 0) and overwrites the whole row, so the other field's change is lost (final independent read: N=100, V=0); the other process changing N and restoring the original value gives no conflict (first Update succeeds; a second Update gives 3020). Final values read by the other process after a 7 s pause and Idle.
- **Records**: q1/write-original-matrix-final-read/ (three rows on separate pages); evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/write-original-same-row/, write-original-same-row-mdb/, write-original-changed-then-restored/.
- **Open**: a change to another row on the same page.

### R-LOCK-4 WRITE-ALLOC: an uncommitted insert blocks the page it lands on
- **Claim**: While another process holds an uncommitted insert of key 500 into T, an insert of key 501 into T (3260) and an update of row 64 (the last page) (3260) are refused, an insert into another table, an insert into the AutoNumber table and an update of row 1 (first page) are admitted; after the rollback the T insert succeeds.
- **Records**: q1/write-alloc-insert-granularity/; evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/write-alloc-insert-txn/; evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/write-alloc-counter-rollback-readid/.
- **Open**: byte-level page identity (W-04); an earlier insert was admitted while all rows of the table were updated (write-promote), which depends on free space.

### R-LOCK-4 WRITE-ALLOC, W-05 growth: a new row lands on the first page with room, in a new slot
- **Claim**: ACE puts a new row on the first of the table's pages, in storage order, that has room for it. It appends a new slot there and does not reuse a deleted slot. With rows deleted from the first page and from a middle page, the new row went to the first page as slot 7, after the six live and two deleted slots, by recordset AddNew and by SQL INSERT alike. With rows deleted only from the first page, it went to the first page too. With no deletes, only the last page had room, and the row went there, as WRITE-ALLOC's insert into the last page did. The table-type Bookmark (slot, page) of the new row equals LastModified after the AddNew.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB created by DAO; T(n LONG, t TEXT(255)) with 60 rows of 250-character texts, seven rows a page on nine pages, no index; one insert.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q9-insert-landing/` (no-free-but-last, free-first-and-middle-addnew, free-first-and-middle-sql, free-first-only-addnew).
- **Open**: room that only a deleted slot's reuse could give; pages whose free space is fragmented (ACE's delete compacts, so it was contiguous here); a table whose FreeMap and owned map differ in order; MDB.

### R-LOCK-4 WRITE-TREE: concurrent uniqueness candidates
- **Claim**: While another process holds an uncommitted insert, inserts of the same primary key, other keys and the same secondary-unique value fail 3260; after its rollback they insert; after its commit the same key or value gives 3022. With WRITE-ALLOC the block is the page the new row lands on.
- **Records**: evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/write-tree-unique-secondary/; evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/write-tree-unique-candidate/, write-alloc-insert-txn/.

### R-LOCK-4 WRITE-LV: memo replacement is blocked by a recordset that has read the memo
- **Claim**: With another process's dynaset positioned on row 4 of a table of 3,000-character memos and having READ that row's memo `Value`, replacing the memo of row 4 (3,500+ characters, `dbFailOnError`) fails 3186 "Could not save; currently locked by user ..." twice, while rows 3 and 1 are replaced; once the recordset is closed, row 4 is replaced and all lengths read back. A dynaset merely positioned on row 1 (no Edit, no memo read) did not block replacing rows 1 to 4; with `Edit` pending on row 1, replacing rows 1, 2 and 3 fails 3260 (page granularity); after `CancelUpdate` and Close the replacement succeeds (lengths 3,501, 3,500 x3).
- **Records**: q2/write-lv-replace-after-memo-read/ (3186 twice); q1/write-lv-replace-granularity/; evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/write-lv-replace-reuse/ (the earlier 3186).
- **Open**: whether a memo read in the same page blocks neighbours (only the read row was blocked).

### R-LOCK-4 WRITE-DDL: schema changes beside other users
- **Claim**: Another process's `ALTER TABLE`, `CREATE INDEX`, `DROP TABLE` and `DROP INDEX` fail 3262 while a process has cursors open, a pending pessimistic Edit (also after CancelUpdate with the recordset open) or an uncommitted DDL; its `SELECT` of a table under uncommitted DDL gives 3261; all succeed after the other side closes or rolls back, and also while the other process holds only descriptors (TableDef, QueryDef).
- **Records**: evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/write-ddl-vs-pending-edit/, read-schema-held-descriptors/; evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/write-ddl-vs-open-transaction-ddl-first/, cat-xproc-drop-table-under-reader/; q1/read-schema-held-descriptors-late/, q2/read-schema-held-querydef-late/.

### R-LOCK-4 TXN-RANGE: an unrelated admitted change survives the other's resolution
- **Claim**: While process a holds a transaction updating N of row 1, process b updates V of row 64 (far) and U row 1 (admitted) and V of row 2 (same page, 3260); after a's Rollback (phase 1) b reads row 1 N=10, row 64 V=1, U N=11 and a reads the same; after a's CommitTrans (phase 2) both read row 1 N=1010, row 64 V=2, U N=12 (b after 7 s and Idle; a after 7 s and Idle).
- **Records**: q1/txn-range-far-row-final-read/; evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/txn-local-xproc-same-page-retry/, local-two-rows-far-b-commit-a-rollback/ (in-process); q2/txn-local-inproc-far-rows-unindexed-orders/.
- **Open**: lock release at an inner level.

### R-LOCK-4 WRITE-PROMOTE: lock budget
- **Claim**: Exceeding `dbMaxLocksPerFile` raises 3035 (statement fails, `RecordsAffected` 0, transaction usable, Rollback works, other process admitted); no promotion is observed: another table and an INSERT stay admitted while a process holds 64 row updates.
- **Records**: evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/write-promote-bulk-update/; evidence/r-txn-1/exhaust-locks-*/.
- **Open**: the default threshold (no getter).

### R-LOCK-4 WRITE-FAIL: a stopped or killed holder
- **Claim**: With another process holding an uncommitted update of row 1 and a pessimistic Edit on row 40 (first card), or an open transaction after `UPDATE T SET S = 'held'` over all 4,096 rows (second card, the process frozen), writes to its rows fail 3260 (after the full retry wait, frozen or running); 1.5 s after the holder is killed (TerminateProcess) the same writes succeed, its uncommitted changes are gone (row 1 N=10 then +1 by the survivor, S 'row', independent read after 7 s and Idle) and the lock file stays at 128 B.
- **Records**: q2/write-fail-suspend-holder-mid-update/; q1/write-fail-kill-lock-holder/.
- **Open**: the release time inside 1.5 s; a freeze inside the acquiring statement (the update had returned before the freeze); injected failures (T).

### R-CRASH-1 CRASH-NEW: the new file appears all at once
- **Claim**: `DBEngine.CreateDatabase` of a new path (about 250 ms) leaves no file at 5, 40, 150 and 200 ms after it started (killed; opens 3024) and a complete file at 250 and 300 ms (172,032 B, Version 12.0, the five system tables, opened by the next opener; hashes differ between runs); with the creator frozen at 100 and 200 ms no file exists on disk either. A partial or zero-length file was never seen; after a kill before the file appears a later `CreateDatabase` of the path succeeds, after one that left a complete file it gives 3204.
- **Conditions**: process termination or suspension of an owned process, OS and cache alive; kills at the stated delays after the start signal, one attempt each.
- **Records**: q2/crash-new-kill-mid-create-200ms/, -250ms/, -300ms/, q2/crash-new-suspend-mid-create-100ms/, -200ms/; q1/crash-new-kill-mid-create-5ms/, -40ms/, -150ms/.
- **Open**: states between 200 and 250 ms; cut reconstruction of the initial-file writes (T).

### R-CRASH-1 CRASH-KILL: process-termination images open cleanly (serves CRASH-CAPTURE)
- **Claim**: In the live-OS process-termination model, each of 48 frozen images (34 kill points on ACCDB and MDB, 12 mid-call kills, one uncommitted bulk transaction, and a freeze-then-kill 60 ms into a 4,096-row insert, one after a statement that had returned, one after a commit that had returned) opens ACE-first with `hr=0`, no error, no repair request and an unchanged file; Compact-first also succeeds (17.2 MB, 4,096 rows); uncommitted transactions (also a 2,048-row insert in a transaction killed 1.5 s after Execute: 13.0 MB file, 2,048 rows visible), pending AddNew, edited dynaset rows, implicit statements (also a frozen 4,096-row insert at 21.2 MB: 4,096 original rows, SUM(N) 83,906,560, memo lengths 2,000, IX_N cursor first/last row intact), LV replace and bulk insert are absent; committed (default and forced) and closed states are present (a 4,096-row insert committed before the freeze: 8,192 rows, SUM(N) 577,413,120); the lock file stays 64 B after a shared writer is killed and is absent after an exclusive writer.
- **Conditions**: `TerminateProcess` (also after a full-process suspension), OS and cache alive, kill at API boundaries or 20/60/100/250/400/1500 ms into a call; not power loss, no write recording.
- **Records**: q2/crash-control-suspend-then-kill-mid-insert-early/, -late/, crash-control-suspend-mid-commit-then-kill/; q1/crash-txn-kill-between-execute-and-commit/; evidence/r-crash-1-kill/* (32 kill records), evidence/r-crash-1-midcall/* (12).
- **Open**: a freeze inside `CommitTrans` (the call had returned before the 8 ms freeze), Access-first, CalcDb cuts, power loss (T).

## R-CURSOR-1, R-DAO-1 and R-ERR-1

### R-CURSOR-1 CUR-POS: position, BOF/EOF and Move transitions are one rule set across table, dynaset and snapshot; forward-only is a restricted subset

- **Claim.** On table-type, dynaset and snapshot: `MovePrevious` on the first row gives BOF without error, a second gives 3021; `MoveNext` past the last row gives EOF, a second gives 3021; `Move 0` at BOF/EOF gives 3021; `Move n` and `Move -n` past either end give EOF/BOF without error; `Move -1` from EOF and `Move 1` from BOF reach the last/first row. `AbsolutePosition` is -1 at BOF/EOF and 0-based otherwise (table-type: 3251). An empty table has BOF and EOF true and every move gives 3021. Deleting the current row through the recordset itself leaves BOF/EOF false and the next field read fails 3021 on a dynaset (3167 on table-type); a row deleted by another cursor reads 3167 on a dynaset. A failed `FindNext`/`FindPrevious` sets NoMatch and leaves the position; a later `Move` resets NoMatch.
