**Claim.** LIKE treats "æ" as one unit (matches `?`, not `??`, `a?` or `ae`), while `=` and sort treat "æ" = "ae" and "ß" = "ss"; `[æa]e` does not match "æ" and `[æa]?` matches "ae". A decomposed e+U+0301 equals precomposed é under `=` and `StrComp(...,1)` (Len 2 against 1) but `LIKE 'é'` does not match it; dotted/dotless I equal neither "i" nor "I"; half-width ｱ equals ア. Under a traditional Spanish database sort `S > 'cz'` is true for "ch" and ORDER BY places "ch" after "cz"; `?`, `??`, `c?` give identical results in General and Spanish.
**Conditions.** DAO, ACCDB created with the General (0x0409) and the Spanish sort.
**Records.** `evidence/r-expr-1/pat-07-08-expansion-and-classes/r-expr-1.pat-07-08-expansion-and-classes.e0b7df7a06.a1.json`, `evidence/r-expr-1/pat-09-contraction/r-expr-1.pat-09-contraction.604d219d80.a1.json`, `evidence/r-expr-1/pat-18-composition-and-folding/r-expr-1.pat-18-composition-and-folding.ac038a43c0.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/like-collation-ranges/r-sql-13b.like-collation-ranges.4bde1411b7.a1.json`, `evidence/r-expr-1b/str-case-folding/r-expr-1b.str-case-folding.17fdcba221.a1.json`.
**Open.** Binary/Text module comparison profiles need VBA; Spanish `[a-ä]` style ranges.

### R-EXPR-1 PAT-10, PAT-13: UTF-16 units and digit membership
**Claim.** `?` consumes one UTF-16 code unit: a surrogate pair needs `??`, and lone U+D800 and U+DC00 each match `?` (Len 1). `#` matches ASCII digits and full-width U+FF11 but not Arabic-Indic U+0661; `[0-9]` matches only ASCII digits.
**Records.** `evidence/r-expr-1/pat-10-13-character-boundaries/r-expr-1.pat-10-13-character-boundaries.6a181bed64.a1.json`, `evidence/r-expr-1b/str-len-lenb-forms/r-expr-1b.str-len-lenb-forms.3150f4cb9d.a1.json`.

### R-EXPR-1 PAT-12, PAT-14: literal shape
**Claim.** `'A ' = 'A'` is -1 (trailing space trimmed in `=`), `StrComp('A ','A')` is 1, `LIKE 'A'` does not match "A " and neither NBSP nor a tab is trimmed; `LIKE 'MN'` matches only the whole subject, case-insensitively.
**Records.** `evidence/r-expr-1/pat-12-14-17-literal-shape/r-expr-1.pat-12-14-17-literal-shape.c4c2b436d3.a1.json`, `evidence/r-def-expr-sql/str-stored-where-equality/r-def-expr-sql.str-stored-where-equality.f483444341.a1.json`.

### R-SQL-1 SQL-B: source syntax, identifiers and parameters
**Claim.** Bracketed identifiers work (`[Order]`; unbracketed `Order` also opens); backtick quoting and `[x]]y]` fail 3141/0x800A0C45 (corrected 2026-11-20 for backticks: `evidence/r-sql-1/b-brackets/` #103 and all ten forms of `evidence/r-q3-sql/backtick-names/` succeed, a backtick-quoted table, column, qualified column, alias and ORDER BY term, with and without a space, so backticks quote names as brackets do; which record the 3141 rests on is to be re-read). An unresolved name becomes an implicit Text parameter (3061 "Too few parameters" at OpenRecordset; `Parameters` lists it; after assignment it reads back as Text); an ambiguous name fails 3079. `PARAMETERS a Long, a Text` fails at `Parameters.Count` (3060). Unparenthesised join chains fail 3075/0x800A0C03; `(L LEFT JOIN R) LEFT JOIN RX` works while mixed LEFT/INNER nesting fails 3296; FULL/CROSS/NATURAL/bare JOIN, `JOIN ... USING`, `INNER JOIN` without ON are syntax errors; Memo = Memo join fails 3118; Single = Double and Currency = Double joins work. Output names: `SELECT *` over a join keeps names, same-named aliased columns give "a.id","b.id", expressions get Expr1000...; an empty result keeps typed Fields. A saved QueryDef's SQL comes back re-rendered with a trailing `;`. Statement text limits: 64001 characters accepted, 65535 refused (3035) through OpenRecordset and CreateQueryDef; 50 ANDs accepted, 98 give 3072, 100 give 3360; parenthesis depth 16 accepted, 32 gives 3075; 255 output columns accepted, 256 give 3190; 255 parameters accepted; 32 comma-joined tables accepted, 33 give 3360; 50 SELECT keywords accepted, 51 give 3360.
**Conditions.** DAO, ACCDB (limits also MDB for some probes); ANSI-89 syntax.
**Records.** `evidence/r-sql-1/b-brackets/r-sql-1.b-brackets.50f5ba938f.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-1/b-name-resolution/r-sql-1.b-name-resolution.8df84d0c53.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-1/b-parameters/r-sql-1.b-parameters.f46d9b1572.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-1/b-join-nesting/r-sql-1.b-join-nesting.1d7d6cf711.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-1/b-join-bound-types/r-sql-1.b-join-bound-types.2530f40599.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-1/b-output-names/r-sql-1.b-output-names.032255cfb0.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-1/b-limit-length/r-sql-1.b-limit-length.d0e318ce91.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-1/b-limit-and-nesting/r-sql-1.b-limit-and-nesting.e73502f8c6.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/join-keyword-forms/r-sql-13b.join-keyword-forms.65a149c2d7.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/join-nesting-four-tables/r-sql-13b.join-nesting-four-tables.6a467dc80b.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/select-limit-columns/r-sql-13b.select-limit-columns.30ed344e93.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/sub-nesting-depth/r-sql-13b.sub-nesting-depth.1bfa9bd56c.a1.json`, `evidence/r-fix-expr-sql/join-limit-comma-tables/r-fix-expr-sql.join-limit-comma-tables.570f379a7b.a1.json`, `evidence/r-def-expr-sql/sql-text-framing-forms/r-def-expr-sql.sql-text-framing-forms.851f4c3811.a1.json`, `evidence/r-def-expr-sql/sql-select-modifier-order/r-def-expr-sql.sql-select-modifier-order.91a29d217e.a1.json`, `evidence/r-def-expr-sql/sql-foreign-identifier-quoting/r-def-expr-sql.sql-foreign-identifier-quoting.3c65830983.a1.json`.

**Claim (follow-up, limits).** AND chains of 60, 70, 80, 90 and 97 conjuncts open (98 failed earlier with 3072 in `r-sql-1/b-limit-and-nesting`; whether its "98 ANDs" and 97 conjuncts count alike was not checked); parenthesis depth 20, 24 and 28 opens and depth 31 fails 3075 "Expression too complex"; statement text of 64500 and 65000 UTF-16 units opens and 65534 fails 3035 "System resource exceeded" through OpenRecordset and through CreateQueryDef. So the limits lie between 28 and 31 (parentheses) and between 65000 and 65534 (length).
**Records (follow-up).** `evidence/r-q-expr-sql/sqlb-limit-chains/r-q-expr-sql.sqlb-limit-chains.3fe5e6c3b5.a1.json`, `evidence/r-q-expr-sql/sqlb-limit-length/r-q-expr-sql.sqlb-limit-length.cd25787ee9.a1.json`.
**Open.** Exact thresholds (depth 29 and 30, length 65001 to 65533, AND 97/98 with one counting); the 32-table nested INNER JOIN gives 3072 (limit, or that nest form's own answer?); Access query opening and external-file IN need Access or a path in SQL text (T).

### R-SQL-1 SQL-B, R-EXPR-1 EXPR-BIND (query context): how a SELECT reports a failing expression
**Claim.** An expression of a SELECT that fails to parse reports 3075 (0x800A0C03) with the expression service's text and "in query expression '…'" for "in expression": "Syntax error (missing operator) in query expression 'Count(DISTINCT t)'.", "Syntax error in string in query expression ''A'.", "Missing ), ], or Item in query expression 'p IN (UCase('a')'.", "Syntax error in date in query expression …". A call with an argument count its function does not take is also 3075, "Wrong number of arguments used with function in query expression 'CDec(1'." (one-argument CDec, `IIf()`, `Choose(1)`, `Weekday(d, 2, 3)`). A function standalone ACE lacks is 3085 (0x800A0C0D) "Undefined function 'Nz' in expression." with the name as written. A FROM name that is no table or query is 3078 (0x800A0C06) "The Microsoft Access database engine cannot find the input table or query 'ND'. Make sure it exists and that its name is spelled correctly." All fail at OpenRecordset, with source "DAO.Database". The other parse texts follow the same form: "Syntax error in number" (`CDbl(1.8E308)`), "Syntax error (comma)" (`(a, g) IN ((1, 'x'))`), "Invalid use of '.', '!', or '()'." (`Split('a b', ' ')(1)`), "Expression too complex" (parentheses 31 deep, R-SQL-1 SQL-B). A bang name (`Forms!F!x`, `TempVars!x`) is one unresolved name, so 3061. The quoted text is the expression's source from its first unit to about where the error was found, not always the whole expression (`'CDec(1'` for `CDec(1)`, `'JA.a = JB.b ON JA.a = JB.'`); where it ends is not identified.
**Conditions.** DAO `Database.OpenRecordset` with SQL text, ACCDB and MDB.
**Records.** `evidence/r-fix-expr-sql/cdec-argument-forms/r-fix-expr-sql.cdec-argument-forms.952cd70a13.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/agg-distinct-forms/r-sql-13b.agg-distinct-forms.b8a4971273.a1.json`, `evidence/r-expr-1/bind-names-and-literals/r-expr-1.bind-names-and-literals.117c73dbbf.a1.json`, `evidence/r-def-expr-sql/sql-text-framing-forms/r-def-expr-sql.sql-text-framing-forms.851f4c3811.a1.json`, `evidence/r-def-expr-sql/expr-literals-date-forms/r-def-expr-sql.expr-literals-date-forms.0073d8d417.a1.json`, `evidence/r-expr-1b/sandbox-vba-library/r-expr-1b.sandbox-vba-library.55bf0b9648.a1.json`, `evidence/r-expr-1/a-optional-arguments/r-expr-1.a-optional-arguments.39f47b3fcb.a1.json`, `evidence/r-cat-1/catalog-accdb/r-cat-1.catalog-accdb.d7d7c6d683.a1.json` (3078), `evidence/r-expr-1b/conv-range-edges/r-expr-1b.conv-range-edges.01b85c3b66.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/sub-in-list-null-forms/r-sql-13b.sub-in-list-null-forms.7d6e063de7.a1.json`, `evidence/r-expr-1b/route-mdb-battery/r-expr-1b.route-mdb-battery.a443959d11.a1.json`, `evidence/r-expr-1b/sandbox-access-hosted-names/r-expr-1b.sandbox-access-hosted-names.8ac7ffb162.a1.json` (3061 for bang names).
**Open.** The end of the quoted text (the lexer position at the error); CalcDb names it `NotModelled.QueryExpressionExcerpt` and compares the rest of the message.
**Built (`w14-expr-sql-errors`, `deeea785`, 2026-11-27).** `WrongArgumentCount` and `InvalidQualifier` 3075 errors quote the trimmed expression minus its final character (`ExcerptExact = true`); `SELECT FROM ...` reports 3141; `NotModelled.AliasShadowsColumn` defers past expression-bind (`3085`) and open-fold (`5`) failures; `CalcDb.Values`/`Expressions` implement Windows `LCMapStringW` non-ASCII `UCase`/`LCase`, CP-1252 `Chr`/`Chr$`/`Asc`, recorded `Null` argument errors (`3464`/`94`), and `Str$`.

### R-EXPR-1 EXPR-BIND, R-SQL-1 SQL-B (result metadata): the DAO Field.Type of a projected column
**Claim.** A projected field reference reports its column's type (FMT1-FAMILIES). A projected expression reports a static type, and its Field.Value is the inner value converted to that type: `CSng(1)` is 7 with a VT_R8 value, `Round(-2.5)` is 7 with a VT_R8 value although its inner value is Decimal (VarType 14); the bare literal `2147483648` is 20 (Size 16) with a VT_DECIMAL value (r-expr-1b/meta-conversion-field-types, r-wire/expr-literal-tags, r-def-expr-sql/expr-literals-numeric-suffix-radix; an earlier text of this note gave 7), while `2147483647 + 1` is 7 with VT_R8, `CByte(255) + CByte(1)` is 4 with a VT_I4 256 although its inner value is Integer, comparisons and `IsNull` are 3 with VT_I2. The Null literal and an operator with a Null-literal operand report 9 when the value is Null (`Null`, `Null + 1`, `7 + Null`, `Null + '7'`, `Null & Null`, `Null = Null`, `-Null`, `Not Null`, `True And Null`), not otherwise (`7 & Null` is 10 with "7", `False And Null` and `1 <> Null` are 3); a function with a Null result reports its own type (`Len(Null)` 4, `Abs(Null)` 7, `Round(Null)` 7, `Int(Null)` and `Fix(Null)` 0, `UCase(Null)` 10, `StrComp(Null, 'a')` 3).
**Conditions.** DAO snapshot over `SELECT e FROM One` and over stored fields, ACCDB.
**Records.** `evidence/r-expr-1/a-null-empty-error-literals/r-expr-1.a-null-empty-error-literals.f2882098b8.a1.json`, `evidence/r-expr-1/b-plus-versus-concat/r-expr-1.b-plus-versus-concat.963f485c1c.a1.json`, `evidence/r-expr-1/d-logical-bitwise/r-expr-1.d-logical-bitwise.952f06510f.a1.json` (`False And Null` 3), `evidence/r-expr-1/b-integer-widening/r-expr-1.b-integer-widening.52ba244d05.a1.json` (256 as VT_I4), `evidence/r-expr-1/b-integer-conversion/r-expr-1.b-integer-conversion.4a417fa48a.a1.json` (`Round(-2.5)` VT_R8), `evidence/r-expr-1b/null-propagation-functions/r-expr-1b.null-propagation-functions.b2d04aed9f.a1.json`, `evidence/r-expr-1b/meta-conversion-field-types/r-expr-1b.meta-conversion-field-types.bef199cacc.a1.json` (`CSng(1)` 7; `SELECT Null AS v` 9). Discriminating: `Len(Null)` 4 against `Null + 1` 9 (a function's static type, not the value's); `Round(-2.5)` 7 against its inner VarType 14 (the column type is not the inner value's).
**Open.** The rule giving the static type of an operator or function (the R-EXPR-1 Branch B mixed-type note has more pairs): CalcDb names it `NotModelled.ExpressionFieldType` for every computed column but the Null-literal operator, and its replay compares a computed value after converting it to ACE's VARIANT type, counting the conversion as that open item.

### R-SQL-1 SQL-B (select list and DISTINCT): refusals of a SELECT's output
**Claim.** Two items with the same alias fail 3062 "Duplicate output alias 'n'." (`SELECT Count(*) AS n, Count(*) AS n`); an alias holding `!` fails 3125 "'a!b' is not a valid name. Make sure that it does not include invalid characters or punctuation and that it is not too long." (the first bad alias in list order); an alias named in its own expression fails 3103 "Circular reference caused by alias 'd' in query definition's SELECT list." (`CByte(D) AS d`); `[x]]y]` as an alias fails 3141 (SQL-B); an ORDER BY key that is not in a DISTINCT select list fails 3093 "ORDER BY clause (v) conflicts with DISTINCT."; DISTINCT over a Memo column fails 3163 "The field is too small to accept the amount of data you attempted to add. Try inserting or pasting less data." (SQL-EQ). All at OpenRecordset. A NUL in the statement text ends it there (`SELECT 'A\0B' AS v` is "Syntax error in string in query expression ''A'.").
**Conditions.** DAO `Database.OpenRecordset`, ACCDB and MDB.
**Records.** `evidence/r-sql-13b/agg-expression-composition/r-sql-13b.agg-expression-composition.4ffc8d3d9e.a1.json` (3062), `evidence/r-expr-1/bind-names-and-literals/r-expr-1.bind-names-and-literals.117c73dbbf.a1.json` (3125, NUL), `evidence/r-expr-1/b-integer-conversion/r-expr-1.b-integer-conversion.4a417fa48a.a1.json` (3103), `evidence/r-sql-1/b-brackets/r-sql-1.b-brackets.50f5ba938f.a1.json` (3141), `evidence/r-sql-13b/order-expression-forms/r-sql-13b.order-expression-forms.82ceb8279d.a1.json` (3093), `evidence/r-sql-1/eq-distinct-text-mdb/r-sql-1.eq-distinct-text-mdb.4c4c8a3008.a1.json` and `evidence/r-sql-2-3/dr-long-values/r-sql-2-3.dr-long-values.802f6dc088.a1.json` (3163).

### R-SQL-1 SQL-B (select list aliases): unbracketed reserved words after AS fail 3141 before expression parsing and binding
**Claim.** In `SELECT ... AS <alias>`, an unbracketed alias matching a SQL or type reserved word (case-insensitively) fails at `OpenRecordset` with 3141 (`0x800A0C45`, `"The SELECT statement includes a reserved word or an argument name that is misspelled or missing, or the punctuation is incorrect."`, source `"DAO.Database"`). That is why `r-expr-1b/info-stored-null-handling` call 8 fails 3141 instead of 3085 for `Nz`: its fourth item is `IsNull(T) AS inT`. Renaming only `inT` to `x` turns ACE's answer into 3085 (`0x800A0C0D`, `"Undefined function 'Nz' in expression."`), matching standalone `SELECT Nz(N) FROM Mx` and `SELECT Nz(N, -1) FROM Mx`; changing any other alias, 1- vs 2-argument `Nz`, `VarType(Nz(...))`, or `ORDER BY ID` leaves 3141. Bracketing (`AS [inT]`) or backticking (``AS `inT` ``) opens and sets `Fields(0).Name` to `"inT"`; omitting `AS` (`SELECT IsNull(T) inT FROM Mx`) fails 3075 (`"Syntax error (missing operator) in query expression 'IsNull(T) inT'."`). The 3141 check runs per SELECT item at parse time before FROM-table lookup, column/parameter binding, and function resolution: `SELECT Nz(N) AS inT FROM Mx` (3085 when bracketed as `[inT]`), `SELECT 1 AS inT FROM NoSuchTable` (3078 otherwise), `SELECT NoSuchCol AS inT FROM Mx` (3061 otherwise), `SELECT CDec(1) AS inT FROM Mx` (3075 wrong arity otherwise), `SELECT 1 AS inT, (1 + ) FROM Mx`, and `SELECT Nz(N), 1 AS inT FROM Mx` all fail 3141. When an item's own expression has a syntax error (`(1 + )`), `AS inT`, `AS FROM` (missing alias before `FROM`), unclosed `AS [inT`, and `AS OUTER` fail 3141, whereas `AS x`, `AS 5`, `AS [x]]y]` (which fails 3141 on a valid expression `SELECT 1 AS [x]]y] FROM Mx`), a later item's `AS inT` (`SELECT (1 + ), 1 AS inT FROM Mx`), and parenthesized `SELECT CAST(1 AS LONG) AS v FROM Mx` keep the expression's own 3075 failure (`"Extra ) in query expression '(1 + )'."` or missing operator). In `FROM Mx AS <word>`, the same 66 refused words fail at parse time — 65 with 3131 (`0x800A0C3B`, `"Syntax error in FROM clause."`) and `FROM Mx AS UNION` with 3450 (`0x800A0D7A`, `"Syntax error in query. Incomplete query clause."`) — while `AS [inT]` and all 39 accepted words open. In expression or `ORDER BY` position (`SELECT inT FROM Mx`; `SELECT ID AS [inT] FROM Mx ORDER BY inT`), unbracketed `inT` is parsed as an unresolved identifier and fails 3061 (`"Too few parameters. Expected 1."`). Of 105 words tested in both `SELECT 1 AS <word> FROM Mx` and `SELECT ID FROM Mx AS <word>`:
- **Refused — 66 words (3141 in SELECT; 3131 in FROM except `UNION` 3450):**
  - 24 type words (`alias-type-words`): `INT`, `INTEGER`, `LONG`, `SHORT`, `BYTE`, `SINGLE`, `DOUBLE`, `CURRENCY`, `TEXT`, `DATE`, `DATETIME`, `YESNO`, `BIT`, `BINARY`, `GUID`, `CHAR`, `VARCHAR`, `NUMERIC`, `FLOAT`, `REAL`, `VALUE`, `STRING`, `NUMBER`, `TIMESTAMP`.
  - 31 clause/DML words (`alias-clause-words`): `SELECT`, `FROM`, `WHERE`, `ORDER`, `GROUP`, `BY`, `AS`, `IN`, `DISTINCT`, `TOP`, `UNION`, `JOIN`, `ON`, `HAVING`, `INNER`, `LEFT`, `RIGHT`, `ASC`, `DESC`, `ALL`, `INTO`, `TABLE`, `SET`, `VALUES`, `UPDATE`, `DELETE`, `INSERT`, `DISTINCTROW`, `WITH`, `PIVOT`, `TRANSFORM`.
  - 11 additional clause/type/constraint words (`alias-more-words`): `OUTER`, `PERCENT`, `PARAMETERS`, `OWNERACCESS`, `TIME`, `LONGTEXT`, `LONGBINARY`, `VARBINARY`, `MONEY`, `SMALLINT`, `UNIQUE`.
- **Accepted — 39 words (open in both SELECT and FROM):**
  - 6 type words (`alias-type-words`): `MEMO`, `COUNTER`, `DECIMAL`, `BOOLEAN`, `IMAGE`, `OLEOBJECT`.
  - 22 expression-operator, literal, and DDL words (`alias-clause-words`): `AND`, `OR`, `NOT`, `IS`, `NULL`, `LIKE`, `BETWEEN`, `TRUE`, `FALSE`, `ANY`, `EXISTS`, `CREATE`, `DROP`, `ALTER`, `INDEX`, `CONSTRAINT`, `PRIMARY`, `KEY`, `FOREIGN`, `REFERENCES`, `CHECK`, `DEFAULT`.
  - 11 additional words (`alias-more-words`): `CROSS`, `FULL`, `YEAR`, `MONTH`, `DAY`, `TINYINT`, `SOME`, `MOD`, `XOR`, `EQV`, `IMP`.
**Conditions.** DAO `Database.OpenRecordset(..., dbOpenSnapshot)` over ACCDB (`r-expr-1b-main.accdb`, General collating order 1033, ACE 16.0.20527.20196), single process, STA.
**Records.** `evidence/r-q25-nz-3141/baseline/r-q25-nz-3141.baseline.c381e91d17.a1.json`, `evidence/r-q25-nz-3141/baseline/r-q25-nz-3141.baseline.c381e91d17.a2.json`, `evidence/r-q25-nz-3141/alias-int/r-q25-nz-3141.alias-int.11635d7779.a1.json`, `evidence/r-q25-nz-3141/alias-int/r-q25-nz-3141.alias-int.11635d7779.a2.json`, `evidence/r-q25-nz-3141/alias-int-alone/r-q25-nz-3141.alias-int-alone.8ddc60c17c.a1.json`, `evidence/r-q25-nz-3141/alias-int-precedence/r-q25-nz-3141.alias-int-precedence.3bf84f22e5.a1.json`, `evidence/r-q25-nz-3141/alias-precedence-forms/r-q25-nz-3141.alias-precedence-forms.187e552933.a1.json`, `evidence/r-q25-nz-3141/alias-int-positions/r-q25-nz-3141.alias-int-positions.b948abd1e2.a1.json`, `evidence/r-q25-nz-3141/alias-type-words/r-q25-nz-3141.alias-type-words.59c50e9fe2.a1.json`, `evidence/r-q25-nz-3141/alias-clause-words/r-q25-nz-3141.alias-clause-words.6117bb22c3.a1.json`, `evidence/r-q25-nz-3141/alias-from-words/r-q25-nz-3141.alias-from-words.14a6bfd486.a1.json`, `evidence/r-q25-nz-3141/alias-more-words/r-q25-nz-3141.alias-more-words.d455001fc4.a1.json`, `evidence/r-q25-nz-3141/minimal-nz-1arg/r-q25-nz-3141.minimal-nz-1arg.54e439a0a3.a1.json`, `evidence/r-q25-nz-3141/minimal-nz-2arg/r-q25-nz-3141.minimal-nz-2arg.c1165bcbd2.a1.json`, plus the 12 single-element controls under `evidence/r-q25-nz-3141/` (`alias-inn`, `alias-nzn`, `alias-nzt`, `alias-nzn1`, `alias-nzt1`, `alias-lt`, `alias-vn`, `alias-vt`, `nz-one-arg-only`, `nz-two-arg-only`, `vartype-plain`, `no-order-by`).
**Discriminators.** `baseline` (3141) against `alias-int` (3085) differing only in `AS inT` vs `AS x`, both repeated once and retained (`evidence/r-q25-nz-3141/baseline/r-q25-nz-3141.baseline.c381e91d17.a2.json` and `evidence/r-q25-nz-3141/alias-int/r-q25-nz-3141.alias-int.11635d7779.a2.json`, identical outcomes); 12 single-element edits of call 8 staying 3141; `SELECT IsNull(T) AS inT FROM Mx` (3141, no `Nz`) against `AS [inT]` / ``AS `inT` `` (succeeds); `SELECT Nz(N) AS inT FROM Mx` (3141) against `SELECT Nz(N) AS [inT] FROM Mx` (3085) and `(1 + ) AS inT` / `CDec(1) AS inT` / `FROM NoSuchTable` / `NoSuchCol` (all 3141); `alias-precedence-forms` distinguishing `(1 + ) AS inT` / `AS FROM` / `AS [inT` / `AS OUTER` / `1 AS inT, (1 + )` / `Nz(N), 1 AS inT` (3141) from `(1 + ) AS x` / `AS 5` / `AS [x]]y]` / `(1 + ), 1 AS inT` / `CAST(1 AS LONG) AS v` (3075); the 105-word split in both SELECT and FROM across `alias-type-words`, `alias-clause-words`, `alias-from-words` (including `FROM Mx AS UNION` 3450), and `alias-more-words`.
**Open.** Exhaustive membership of ACE's `AS <alias>` reserved-word table beyond the 105 words tested (e.g., `OPTION`, `PROCEDURE`, `VIEW`, `DATABASE`, `SCHEMA`, `BIGINT`, `IEEESINGLE`, `IEEEDOUBLE`, `VARIANT`, `CHARACTER`, `NCHAR`, `NVARCHAR`, `AUTOINCREMENT`, `IDENTITY`, `ALIKE`, `CASE`, `WHEN`, `THEN`, `ELSE`, `END`, `EXCEPT`, `INTERSECT`, `MINUS`, `CORRESPONDING`, `NATURAL`, `USING`, `DISALLOW`, `IGNORE`, `NO`); `CreateQueryDef` and MDB parity; whether implicit aliases without `AS` are refused for non-reserved words as well; whether bare `FROM Mx <word>` without `AS` uses the same 66-word set.

### R-EXPR-1 Branch D (Between) and Branch B (precedence): Between takes its bounds in either order; unary minus binds tighter than ^
**Claim.** `x BETWEEN a AND b` is `x BETWEEN b AND a`: `n BETWEEN 5 AND 1` keeps the rows of `n BETWEEN 1 AND 5`, likewise for Text, Date, Double and Memo bounds, and NOT BETWEEN with reversed bounds keeps their complement; a projected `5 Between 9 And 1` is -1. `-2 ^ 2` is 4 and `-2 ^ 2 = 4` is -1: the minus applies to 2 before ^, unlike VBA.
**Conditions.** DAO snapshot, ACCDB.
**Records.** `evidence/r-sql-13b/between-bound-order/r-sql-13b.between-bound-order.168da76eda.a1.json` (designed: bounds in order and reversed for each type), `evidence/r-expr-1b/cmp-between-in/r-expr-1b.cmp-between-in.059527a48b.a1.json`, `evidence/r-expr-1b/op-precedence/r-expr-1b.op-precedence.bcc86300c3.a1.json`.
**Open.** Null bounds in the reversed order; whether a Find criteria orders Between the same way (no Find record asks it).

### R-SQL-1 SQL-J: outer joins and ON predicates
**Claim.** `LEFT JOIN ... WHERE R.qty > 0` returns the single matching pair; every ON clause with a non-column-equality conjunct (`ON a = b AND h = 'p'`, `ON a = 1`, `ON ... AND qty>0`, `ON True`) is refused as a whole query with 3296, so ON-versus-WHERE placement cannot be compared; `ON a = b OR ...`, `<`, `<>`, `+`, `IN (...)` are accepted. A Null key never matches and is Null-extended once; a left row with several matches gets all of them. The row order of a join, comma joins included, is not identified: `FROM CX, CY` and `FROM CY, CX` both vary x fastest (r-sql-13b/join-cross-forms), so the listed order does not decide it; an earlier text of this note said the first listed table varied fastest.
**Records.** `evidence/r-sql-1/j-where-vs-on/r-sql-1.j-where-vs-on.43d308a986.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-1/j-on-variants/r-sql-1.j-on-variants.df5ed6b170.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-1/j-null-keys-dups/r-sql-1.j-null-keys-dups.695a04b8f5.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/join-outer-null-keys/r-sql-13b.join-outer-null-keys.ca890e4b39.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/join-on-predicate-forms/r-sql-13b.join-on-predicate-forms.aaf58287b0.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/join-cross-forms/r-sql-13b.join-cross-forms.445d60f12e.a1.json`.
**Open.** The whole-query refusal of `JOIN ... AND` is unexplained. Interleaved pulls and plan comparison are not asked. A join's row order is the next note.

### R-SQL-1 SQL-J, PAR-ORDER: the row order of a two-table join without ORDER BY
**Claim.** ACE runs a two-table join as an outer loop over one table read in storage order (insertion order, not primary-key order) and an inner loop over the other; which table is outer depends on the join's form, not on the order the tables are listed in, their names or their creation order:
- **A cross join** (a comma list with no join condition): the table with more rows is outer, and the smaller varies fastest (P 2 rows with Q 3 gives `1 a, 2 a, 1 b, ...` in both FROM orders). With equal row counts the second-listed table is outer and the first-listed varies fastest.
- **An equality join** (INNER JOIN ... ON, or a comma list with WHERE a = b): if exactly one side has an index on the key, the unindexed side is outer and the indexed side is probed through its index, whatever the sizes (KA, primary key, 3 rows, with KB, no index, 4 rows: KB drives). If both sides or neither side is indexed, the table with fewer rows is outer, against the cross join (LE 4 rows created first with LF 3 rows: LF drives; KE 3 with KF 4: KE drives). With equal row counts the second-listed table is outer.
- **An outer join** (LEFT or RIGHT): the preserved side is always outer, with or without indexes.
- **The inner side's matches for one outer row:** through an index, in index order (equal keys in insertion order: b2 before b4). Without an index, in reverse storage order for tables of up to 6 rows (three duplicates of a key come b4, b3, b1), for INNER, LEFT and WHERE joins alike; with 16 and 32 rows it is another order, not identified (r-par-1/order-join-duplicate-matches: JR's eight matches of k = 1 come 95, 91, 87, 99, 83, 79, 75, 71, against storage 99 down to 71; JL's four 11, 16, 1, 6; a TEXT key in r-sql-1/eq-distinct-text gave forward storage order).
**Conditions.** DAO snapshot over a fresh ACCDB (General), each table on one data page, 1 to 6 rows; LONG keys; primary keys and non-unique indexes made by DDL. Cross joins are measured on unindexed tables.
**Records.** `evidence/r-q5-join-order/` (cross-sizes, cross-creation-names, cross-storage-order, inner-index, outer-direction, inner-both-neither-sizes, unindexed-duplicates); the cross forms agree with `evidence/r-sql-13b/join-cross-forms/`. Discriminators: sizes 2×3, 3×2, 3×3, 1×4 and 4×1 in both listing orders; creation order against name order; an index on either side, both and neither; equal sizes for both and neither; three duplicates on the unindexed side.
**Checked further (2026-11-20, W-06 join order, a Gemini 4 Neon sub-session).** `evidence/r-par-1/order-join-duplicate-matches` (JL and JLI 16 rows, JR and JRI 32 rows, each on one data page, read by GetRows): the outer-table choice and its storage-order scan hold in all 20 statements, so "fewer rows" is the row count (both sides one page, the 16-row table outer); the indexed inner side's index order holds in all 13 statements that have one; the unindexed inner order does not (above).
**Open.** The unindexed inner order above 6 rows (a hash-like order); tables on more than one data page; non-LONG keys; three or more tables; a join over an indexed table read through a key range; a cross join where one side has an index.

### R-CURSOR-1 CUR-POP, R-SQL-1 SQL-J, PAR-ORDER: the row order of an unordered dynaset
**Claim.** An unordered DAO dynaset comes in the order of the access path ACE picks for it, and that path differs from the snapshot's. The snapshot of the same text, the control in every question, kept storage order for one table and the join order of the note above.
- **One table, no WHERE:** the order of one index. The primary key wins if there is one, including over a unique index made before it (XC3). Otherwise a unique index wins over an equally distinct non-unique one whatever the names (XE1, XE2). Otherwise the index with more distinct keys wins whatever its name or creation order: N, 8 values, beat G, 2 values, in XN, XC1, XC4 and XC5. Among equals the last by name wins, never the first or last created: N DESC against N in XD2 to XD5 follows the names. A lone non-unique index on a column of duplicates still serves (XC2: G order). A descending index gives descending order (XD1). A table with no index comes in storage order (XH).
- **One table, WHERE that is one range on one column with an ascending index:** that index's order, primary key or not. Ranges include `<`, `<=`, `>`, `>=`, `BETWEEN`, two bounds `N >= 3 AND N <= 6`, `NOT (N < 3)`, a Text range `c >= 'x3'`, and LIKE with a literal prefix (`'x*'`, `'x[3-6]'`). `ID > 2` gives primary-key order. An equality (`N = 40`, `G = 1`, `N = 2` on 700 rows) comes in storage order either way, because index ties are in storage order (next item). So whether `=` takes the index is not observable.
- **One table, any other WHERE:** storage order, even with a primary key. That covers a WHERE on an unindexed column, `<>`, `IN`, `OR`, `Is Not Null`, an expression (`N + 0 >= 3`), and a range on a column whose only index is descending. It also covers a range with any second conjunct: an indexed one (`ID > 2 AND N >= 3`, `N >= 3 AND G >= 0`), an unindexed one (`... AND s <> 'zz'`, also on a 2-row range and on 27 of 700 rows over many pages), and the same conjunct twice (`N >= 3 AND N >= 3`).
- **Ties in an index walk** come in storage (row-locator) order, not entry order. After `UPDATE ... SET G = 1` on the second-stored row it comes second in `G = 1`, through the dynaset and through a table-type `Index` walk. This sharpens R-SORT-3's "insertion order" for an updated row.
- **A two-table equality join** (INNER, LEFT, RIGHT, or a comma list whose WHERE is the join condition only) **with an index on the key on both sides:** join-key order through the indexes, ties in storage order. The driving table, varying slowest, is the preserved side of an outer join and the second-listed table of an INNER join, whatever the sizes. MA (3 rows) INNER JOIN MB (4 rows) drives MB, and MB INNER JOIN MA drives MA, where the snapshot drives the smaller MA both times. Its primary key does not decide (QA: k order, not id order).
- **Any other two-table join dynaset** comes in its snapshot's order (the note above): one side indexed (KA/KB, RA/RB), neither indexed (KG/KH), and a join over two indexed keys with a WHERE beyond the join condition (`MB.u <> 'zz'` unindexed, `YA.k >= 1` on the indexed key). The driver is read in storage order even when it has a primary key: KA's is on the key, RA's on another column, also under `WHERE RA.id > 0`.
- The rule holds over 700 rows on many pages and past the 500-row population chunk (rows 495 to 506 continue each order). The stopping cases follow it: r-def-cursor-dao/prov-missing-projected-key (`WHERE N = 40`, IX_TN ascending, IDs 4, 5 in storage order) and query 2 of join-field-names-and-lookup (PA primary key, CH's foreign-key index on PID: CH drives in PID order).

**Conditions.** ACCDB made by CreateDatabase (General, dbVersion120) in each question. LONG and TEXT(4) columns, indexes made by DDL before the rows. One data page for 8-row tables; W has 700 rows of about 220 bytes. Rows read field by field or by GetRows; one process, one STA.
**Records.** `evidence/r-q14-dynaset-order/`. Single tables: single-pk, single-pk-indexes, single-heap, single-nopk-indexes, single-unique-n, index-choice, index-names, unique-vs-name, where-forms, where-forms-2, text-forms, tie-after-update, multi-page, multi-page-chunk, multi-page-residual. The description of multi-page-residual says 13 of 700 rows; GetRows returned 27. Joins: join-pk-noindex, join-both-indexed, join-neither-indexed, join-parent-child, join-left-both-indexed, join-dups-both-indexed, join-pk-off-key, join-outer-dups, join-repeat-and-where. The last repeats join-dups-both-indexed and single-pk-indexes under other names with the same orders. Earlier agreement: `evidence/r-par-1/order-recordset-types` (OT3: primary-key order without WHERE, k-index ties with `WHERE k = 2`).
**Discriminators.** Storage, primary-key and N orders differ by construction, also within `G = 1` and `N >= 3`. Creation order against name order (XC1 against XN, XD2 against XD3). Name against distinct keys (XC4, XC5). Name against uniqueness (XE1, XE2). Primary against unique (XC3). Each WHERE form changed by one element (one bound or two, a second conjunct, the same conjunct twice). Listing order against size in joins (MA/MB both ways). Driver primary key against key order (QA/QB, RA/RB).
**Open.** How the number of distinct keys is measured: the true count, the DistinctCount counter or index statistics; for an index made after the rows; compound indexes. Whether a parameter in place of a literal keeps the range. `OR` between two equalities on one indexed column was asked only where both orders agree (text-forms). Join dynasets with three or more tables, a descending key index, or both sides on many pages. The comma-list join's driving table with duplicates on both sides. A dynaset over a saved QueryDef.

### R-SQL-1 SQL-EQ-01, SQL-EQ-02, SQL-EQ-03, SQL-KEY-01, SQL-KEY-02, SQL-KEY-03: grouping and DISTINCT equality
**Claim.** GROUP BY and DISTINCT over Text merge values that compare equal under the database sort: "abc", "abc ", "ABC", "Abc" form one group, "ae" and "æ" one group, "ß" and "ss" one; " abc", "äbc", `abc\0x` and `abc\0y` and 55-character prefixes stay separate; Null and "" stay separate; Null is one group sorted first. ACCDB and MDB give identical groups; a join `a.t = b.t` pairs the same classes. GROUP BY over Memo works (compared on the first 255 characters; values differing only after 255 merge), DISTINCT over Memo, OLE or Binary is refused (3163, 3114). Numerics: Double -0 and 0 are separate groups; 0.1 as Double and Currency merge; BIGINT 2^53 and 2^53+1 stay distinct alone and merge after UNION with Double. A GROUP BY or DISTINCT over a failing conversion fails at OpenRecordset (3464).
**Conditions.** DAO, ACCDB and MDB.
**Records.** `evidence/r-sql-1/eq-distinct-text/r-sql-1.eq-distinct-text.63e61077f3.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-1/eq-distinct-text-mdb/r-sql-1.eq-distinct-text-mdb.4c4c8a3008.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-1/eq-cross-type/r-sql-1.eq-cross-type.6be1a0daf9.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-1/eq-numeric-domains/r-sql-1.eq-numeric-domains.86439dd58d.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-1/eq-distinct-error/r-sql-1.eq-distinct-error.3aba2117fa.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/group-null-keys/r-sql-13b.group-null-keys.2b3167e7ae.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/agg-min-max-text/r-sql-13b.agg-min-max-text.3c20621bb8.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/memo-distinct-scope/r-sql-13b.memo-distinct-scope.0fdf906886.a1.json`, `evidence/r-fix-expr-sql/eq-decimal-bigint-groups/r-fix-expr-sql.eq-decimal-bigint-groups.c56046f9b8.a1.json`, `evidence/r-par-1/order-groupby-text-representative/r-par-1.order-groupby-text-representative.9175419765.a1.json`.

**Claim (follow-up, representative).** The spelling shown for a group of values that compare equal is the first row's in scan order: Eq1 inserts 'ae', U+00E6, 'AE' and shows "ae" for DISTINCT, GROUP BY (count 3), Min, Max, First and UNION; Eq2 inserts U+00E6, 'ae', 'AE' and shows U+00E6 for the same statements; an index on t (Eq3) does not change it; Last is "AE"; ORDER BY t lists the three tied rows in descending id (3, 2, 1).
**Records (follow-up).** `evidence/r-q-expr-sql/eq-repr-order/r-q-expr-sql.eq-repr-order.681e982a07.a1.json`.
**Open.** The earlier records showed U+00E6 for DISTINCT and "ae" for GROUP BY on another fixture; that difference did not reproduce here and was not re-examined. Hash and partition agreement is CalcDb's.

### R-SQL-1 SQL-AGG: empty, Null, Count, result types, singletons
**Claim.** With no GROUP BY an aggregate over an empty or all-Null input returns one row: Count(*) 0, Count(x) 0, Sum/Avg/Min/Max/First/Last Null; with GROUP BY and no rows there are no rows; `HAVING Count(*)=0` returns (0, Null). `Count(*)` counts rows, `Count(d)` non-Null, `Count(Null)` is 0 and `Count('A & B')` counts rows; Count is VT_I4. Sum and Avg of Byte/Integer/Long/Single/Date are Double (no overflow below 1E308); Sum(Currency) is Currency and overflows at open (error 6) when a group exceeds the Currency range; `Sum(2.5)` and Sum/Avg/Min/Max of a Decimal column are Decimal; Min/Max keep the source type (Byte becomes Long); Sum(Yes/No) is -n. Var and StDev of one value are Null, VarP and StDevP are 0; none are Null for two values ((1,3): Var 2, VarP 1, StDev 1.4142135623730951); statistics are Double for every input type. `First` and `Last` return Null rows (they do not skip them). `Min/Max/Sum(Memo)` fail 3115, `Count(DISTINCT x)` is a syntax error. The same aggregate text may appear in SELECT, HAVING and ORDER BY.
**Conditions.** DAO, ACCDB; Decimal columns made by ADO DDL; GROUP BY results read Precision/Scale (28/20, 22/4), ungrouped aggregates cannot (3021).
**Records.** `evidence/r-sql-1/agg-empty/r-sql-1.agg-empty.f77ed3cf54.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-1/agg-count/r-sql-1.agg-count.2a6bfd656d.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-1/agg-result-type/r-sql-1.agg-result-type.f98de5fd4d.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-1/agg-arg-kinds/r-sql-1.agg-arg-kinds.439ac83cbf.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-1/agg-single/r-sql-1.agg-single.f85311d358.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-1/agg-first-last/r-sql-1.agg-first-last.9d008bdb10.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-1/agg-occur/r-sql-1.agg-occur.50068df1e2.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/agg-sum-boundary/r-sql-13b.agg-sum-boundary.d88bac0645.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/agg-stat-argument-types/r-sql-13b.agg-stat-argument-types.ee4ed8dd0f.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/agg-yesno-memo-guid/r-sql-13b.agg-yesno-memo-guid.dc2357037f.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/agg-distinct-forms/r-sql-13b.agg-distinct-forms.b8a4971273.a1.json`, `evidence/r-fix-expr-sql/agg-result-type-decimal/r-fix-expr-sql.agg-result-type-decimal.e3851e0c91.a1.json`, `evidence/r-fix-expr-sql/agg-stat-argument-types-renamed/r-fix-expr-sql.agg-stat-argument-types-renamed.9bec1c3cc6.a1.json`, `evidence/r-fix-expr-sql/agg-sum-boundary-per-group/r-fix-expr-sql.agg-sum-boundary-per-group.208b46ede7.a1.json`, `evidence/r-par-1/fold-double-null-mixed/r-par-1.fold-double-null-mixed.184d3eec19.a1.json`.

**Open.** Count overflow needs more than 2^31 rows; Precision and Scale of an ungrouped aggregate cannot be read (3021). First and Last follow the feed (scan) order of the access path (see the Double fold order note). The variance recurrence is in its own note.

### R-SQL-1 SQL-UNION-01..04: arms
**Claim.** Output names come from the first arm; arity mismatch fails 3307 at open; Long with Text gives Text(255), Date with Double gives Text ("1899/12/31"), the numeric ladder widens b to i to l to d and Currency stays Currency (t5); Decimal-first arm gives Decimal (Precision 22, Scale 4), Currency-first gives Currency, also through ADO. `UNION` removes duplicates under the text sort ('a' and 'A', 'a' and 'a ' collapse) and Null sorts first; `CSng(0.1)` and `CDbl(0.1)` stay two rows. Tokens are kept in the saved SQL: `A UNION B UNION ALL C` returns two rows and `A UNION ALL B UNION C` one. ORDER BY may name only the first arm's alias or position; `UNION DISTINCT`, `CORRESPONDING`, `INTERSECT`, `EXCEPT` are refused; `IN (... UNION ...)` is refused (3787); 50 arms are accepted. A failing conversion in an arm fails at open for UNION (3464) but for UNION ALL it opens and the bad row raises 3071 on MoveNext then reads as Null and iteration continues.
**Records.** `evidence/r-sql-1/union-binding/r-sql-1.union-binding.968666d2bf.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-1/union-types/r-sql-1.union-types.319d8d2279.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-1/union-mixed-tokens/r-sql-1.union-mixed-tokens.1112a9592f.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-1/union-error-order/r-sql-1.union-error-order.706a77244a.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/union-all-numeric-ladder/r-sql-13b.union-all-numeric-ladder.dc860cdbc9.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/union-dedup-cross-type/r-sql-13b.union-dedup-cross-type.6e6a279cbe.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/union-order-by-forms/r-sql-13b.union-order-by-forms.41938a9671.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/union-syntax-forms/r-sql-13b.union-syntax-forms.4aebdb5d51.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/union-arm-count/r-sql-13b.union-arm-count.4cc8acfe76.a1.json`, `evidence/r-fix-expr-sql/union-types-decimal/r-fix-expr-sql.union-types-decimal.fdd2696d83.a1.json`.
**Built (`w19-sql-clauses`, `914c2cba`/`72fd8afa`, 2026-11-27).** Implemented `TABLE <name>` arms & trailing `UNION` `3450`, `UNION ORDER BY` ordinals/`3070`/`3351`/pre-`UNION` `ORDER BY` + `*` over derived table (`w19-sql-clauses` also landed `TOP &H`/non-numeric `3141`, unprojected `GROUP BY` exprs/`Null`, alias-as-parameter in `GROUP BY`/`HAVING`/grouped `ORDER BY`, aggregate-free `HAVING`, `3096`/`3098`/`3122` excerpts, `IN ()`/`CommaSyntax` excerpts, and ordered `BETWEEN` open `3464`).

**Claim (follow-up, Null first arm).** `SELECT Null AS v FROM One UNION SELECT col FROM Ut` (distinct UNION) with a later arm of type Long, Double, Date, Currency or Yes/No returns a Text(255) column whose values are the raw bytes of the typed value (Long 7 gives U+0007 U+0000, Currency 1.5 gives U+3A98 U+0000 U+0000 U+0000, Double and Date their eight raw bytes). With UNION ALL the type is kept (Long 4, Double, Date 8, Currency 5, Yes/No 1) although Field.Size reads 510 for the Null-first column (4 or 8 when the typed arm is first). With the typed arm first, UNION and UNION ALL keep the type; a Text later arm stays Text in all orders.
**Records (follow-up).** `evidence/r-q-expr-sql/union-null-first/r-q-expr-sql.union-null-first.79a485d401.a1.json`.
**Open.** Accepted effects in an earlier arm need a host route. The Yes/No rows displayed as empty text, so that case does not show the bytes.

### R-SQL-1 SQL-SUB-01, SQL-SUB-02: scalar subqueries and Null folds
**Claim.** A scalar subquery with 0 rows is Null; with 2 rows it fails 3354 at open; with 2 columns 3306. Over an empty set `IN` is 0, `NOT IN` is -1, `> ALL` is -1 even for a Null outer value; over a set containing Null `NOT IN` is Null for non-matching values and 0 for matches; `a NOT IN (1, Null)` returns only the non-1 non-Null rows; `= ALL` and `<> ALL` over an empty child return every row including Null; `= ANY` over an empty child returns none. `IN (1,'2')` opens and fails on MoveNext (3071) after the first row. EXISTS is I2 -1/0.
**Records.** `evidence/r-sql-1/sub-scalar/r-sql-1.sub-scalar.f063a4852e.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-1/sub-in-quantified/r-sql-1.sub-in-quantified.e0c1fcc3df.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/sub-in-list-null-forms/r-sql-13b.sub-in-list-null-forms.7d6e063de7.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/sub-quantified-operators/r-sql-13b.sub-quantified-operators.3faceda688.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/sub-exists-vs-in-null/r-sql-13b.sub-exists-vs-in-null.361a54ca60.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/sub-correlated-select-list/r-sql-13b.sub-correlated-select-list.440d4f7149.a1.json`.
**Open.** Correlation precedence and EXISTS shapes are in the SQL-SUB-03, SQL-SUB-04 note.

### R-SQL-3 SQL-TOP-01..04: TOP
**Claim.** TOP keeps every row tied at the cutoff (`TOP 3 ... ORDER BY K,T` returned 5 rows; TOP 1/5/36 gave 36 tied rows over 256 rows). Admission: `TOP +2`, `&H2`, `2E0`, `2.0`, 2147483647 are accepted, 0, -1, `TOP 0 PERCENT`, >100 PERCENT, `-0`, expressions, parameters and strings are refused (3141, parameters at CreateQueryDef); `TOP 11` over 10 rows returns 10; `TOP 2.5` returns 2. PERCENT returns the ceiling of n% of the row count (10 rows: 15 gives 2, 25 gives 3, 2.5 gives 1; 9 rows: 11 gives 1, 12 gives 2, 34 gives 4, 99 gives 9), ties kept. Null sorts first ascending. Without ORDER BY TOP n returns the first n rows in scan order. A failing sort key fails at open; a failing projection beyond the cutoff raises nothing, a failing row inside the cutoff raises on the Value read.
**Conditions.** DAO, ACCDB and MDB (ties on MDB identical).
**Records.** `evidence/r-sql-2-3/top-arguments/r-sql-2-3.top-arguments.d9074eabd8.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-2-3/top-ties-order/r-sql-2-3.top-ties-order.98f67f5e07.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-2-3/top-demand/r-sql-2-3.top-demand.a543dfd216.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/top-expression-forms/r-sql-13b.top-expression-forms.1ffa7c03ff.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/top-parameter-forms/r-sql-13b.top-parameter-forms.df1829c1f7.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/top-percent-counts/r-sql-13b.top-percent-counts.d558f3ccec.a1.json`, `evidence/r-par-1/order-top-n-ties-asc/r-par-1.order-top-n-ties-asc.f00129dc89.a1.json`, `evidence/r-par-1/order-top-n-ties-asc-mdb/r-par-1.order-top-n-ties-asc-mdb.890f172449.a1.json`, `evidence/r-par-1/order-top-n-ties-desc/r-par-1.order-top-n-ties-desc.8116a2142c.a1.json`, `evidence/r-par-1/order-top-percent-ties/r-par-1.order-top-percent-ties.2c388425b2.a1.json`, `evidence/r-par-1/order-top-without-order-by/r-par-1.order-top-without-order-by.a0aad8ada5.a1.json`, `evidence/r-par-1/err-top-stops-before-bad-row/r-par-1.err-top-stops-before-bad-row.db1df3b225.a1.json`.
**Open.** Order of ties inside the kept group is deterministic but unexplained; spill is CalcDb's.

### R-SQL-3 SQL-DR-01..05: DISTINCTROW
**Claim.** DISTINCTROW deduplicates by source-row identity of every contributing table, not by projected values or whole-row value equality. `DISTINCTROW A.Label` over A (a1 and a2 identical in every field, a3 differing in an unprojected field) joined to B returned 3 rows (plain 6, DISTINCT 1); `B.Tag` alone returned 2 (the fan-out of B collapsed); projecting both families gives 6; a constant projection gives 6, `UCase(A.Label)` 3; a saved query boundary gives 6; a LEFT JOIN keeps an all-Null row. A single-table DISTINCTROW never merges equal rows (Memo/Binary included, 4 rows for 4). `SELECT DISTINCTROW A.Label, A.Hidden ... JOIN B` is updatable: Edit changed one occurrence and Delete removed one physical row. DISTINCT over Memo fails 3163 and Binary 3114.
**Records.** `evidence/r-sql-2-3/dr-whole-row/r-sql-2-3.dr-whole-row.40add761b9.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-2-3/dr-participation/r-sql-2-3.dr-participation.6f14df4458.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-2-3/dr-calc-saved-outer/r-sql-2-3.dr-calc-saved-outer.9a9ae182d9.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-2-3/dr-updatable/r-sql-2-3.dr-updatable.f2a07c3654.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-2-3/dr-long-values/r-sql-2-3.dr-long-values.802f6dc088.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/agg-distinct-forms/r-sql-13b.agg-distinct-forms.b8a4971273.a1.json`, `evidence/r-par-1/order-distinct-without-order-by/r-par-1.order-distinct-without-order-by.4a7aac4372.a1.json`, `evidence/r-fix-expr-sql/order-distinct-full-read/r-fix-expr-sql.order-distinct-full-read.0d563de4a9.a1.json`.
**Open.** The refused provisional choice (whole logical row, 2 classes) is a CalcDb design finding. A direct test of unprojected Memo content is not discriminating because physical identity already separates the rows.

### R-SQL-3 SQL-PIVOT-01..03, -05, -07: crosstab
**Claim.** Fixed IN headings keep list order (`IN ('c','b','a','zz')` publishes Grp, c, b, a, zz); a duplicate or equivalent value in IN ('A','a') fails 0x800A0C20; dynamic headings are the distinct values, Null first as `<>`, numeric headings sort numerically and text as text, 'a' and 'A' merge, a long heading is cut to 64 characters, `.`, `!`, `]` become `_`, `3.0` becomes "3_0", dates render "2024/01/02". `IN ('')` and `IN (Null)` both fill `<>`; a declared parameter in IN is a literal heading. Empty input: dynamic gives 1 field and 0 rows, fixed gives its headings with 0 rows; outside-list-only still returns every row group with Null cells. A missing cell is Null for every value function; a seen cell of all-Null values gives Count(x) 0 and Null Sum/Avg/First. The fixed crosstab's QueryDef.Fields has its headings before any open; a dynamic one has 0 fields until OpenRecordset and then keeps its schema through MoveNext and MoveLast and after an insert. Heading limit: 254 headings (255 fields) accepted, 255 headings fail "Too many crosstab column headers" (0x800A0C85); `Requery` on a crosstab dynaset or snapshot fails (3251/0x800A0CB3).
**Records.** `evidence/r-sql-2-3/pivot-headings/r-sql-2-3.pivot-headings.68d1c68c44.a1.json`, `evidence/r-fix-expr-sql/pivot-headings-clean/r-fix-expr-sql.pivot-headings-clean.77f1d9ddd8.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-2-3/pivot-empty-null/r-sql-2-3.pivot-empty-null.382879f2a4.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-2-3/pivot-cells/r-sql-2-3.pivot-cells.7e6eb2df0a.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-2-3/pivot-schema-lifetime/r-sql-2-3.pivot-schema-lifetime.27ebaee52e.a1.json`, `evidence/r-fix-expr-sql/pivot-dynaset-requery/r-fix-expr-sql.pivot-dynaset-requery.5b56a896c6.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-2-3/pivot-limit/r-sql-2-3.pivot-limit.8c88c64eb9.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/pivot-clause-forms/r-sql-13b.pivot-clause-forms.2c2071ebfb.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/pivot-heading-sort-order/r-sql-13b.pivot-heading-sort-order.77fabece6d.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/pivot-in-list-forms/r-sql-13b.pivot-in-list-forms.b2ca805610.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/pivot-parameter-positions/r-sql-13b.pivot-parameter-positions.b3329bc546.a1.json`.

**Claim (follow-up, rendered names).** Numeric headings 1, 1.5, 10, -1 sort numerically and render `-1`, `1`, `1_5`, `10` (rows with 1 and 1 sum to 6); text headings '1', '1.0', '01' render "1", "1_0", "01" and stay distinct (with a fixed IN list in written order, cells 1, 2, 3); 'a' and 'A' merge (11). A leading-space ' 1' and '1' render to one column "1" that holds 4, the ' 1' row's value, not the sum 5: two distinct keys with one rendered name collapse silently to a single column.
**Records (follow-up).** `evidence/r-q-expr-sql/pivot-typed-collisions/r-q-expr-sql.pivot-typed-collisions.68ca3bd422.a1.json`.
**Open.** The mechanism of the ' 1' collision (overwrite or first-name wins); a typed number against a text with the same rendering cannot share one pivot column; locale rendering needs a settings change (T); `PIVOT IN (1.0, 1)` returning zero rows is unexplained.

### R-SQL-3 SQL-META (parameters): declaration, types and value adaptation
**Claim.** Declared types map as Bit 1, Byte 2, Short 3, Long 4 (also Integer, Int), Currency 5, IEEESingle/Single/Real 6, IEEEDouble/Double/Float/Numeric 7, DateTime 8, Binary 9, Text/Char/VarChar/Value 10, LongBinary 11, LongText 12, Guid 15, BigInt 19; `Decimal`, `Memo`, `Counter`, `Boolean`, `Variant`, `TinyInt` are refused (3139). A trailing `;` is required after PARAMETERS; two PARAMETERS clauses fail 3129; duplicate names are accepted at CreateQueryDef and fail at `Parameters.Count` (3060); 64- and 65-character names open. A parameter in an ON clause is refused (3296), every other position opens. Assigned values adapt: Long and Short round half to even (2.5 gives 2, 2.6 gives 3, 3.5 gives 4, -2.5 gives -2), Byte rejects -2.5, 255.5, 300 and text (3421), Bit becomes -1 for any non-Null number, DateTime rejects "abc" and "5" (3421) but takes I4 70000, Text keeps text longer than its declared length (10 and 26 characters read in full), Currency/Single/Double/DateTime keep the value; a rejected assignment leaves the old value; an unassigned parameter fails 3061 at OpenRecordset; Null makes `x = p` Null and `p Is Null` -1. `QueryDef.Fields` of a saved parameter query is readable before assignment.
**Records.** `evidence/r-sql-13b/params-declaration-syntax/r-sql-13b.params-declaration-syntax.d11177e387.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/params-declared-type-names/r-sql-13b.params-declared-type-names.def4e28133.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/params-positions/r-sql-13b.params-positions.5c6047d0f4.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/params-count-limit/r-sql-13b.params-count-limit.dd1f5a3b5e.a1.json`, `evidence/r-fix-expr-sql/params-values-rounding-and-length/r-fix-expr-sql.params-values-rounding-and-length.99dc9925f0.a1.json`, `evidence/r-fix-expr-sql/parameters-values-set/r-fix-expr-sql.parameters-values-set.55c1458fde.a1.json`, `evidence/r-fix-expr-sql/nz-free-forms/r-fix-expr-sql.nz-free-forms.4105e37dd6.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-2-3/params-saved/r-sql-2-3.params-saved.a78be0cf53.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-2-3/params-implicit-temp/r-sql-2-3.params-implicit-temp.38ef68ab0e.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/params-value-readback/r-sql-13b.params-value-readback.1f3eed4e11.a1.json`.
**Open.** `Parameters.Count` fails when 255 or more parameters are all used (unexplained). Parameter lifecycle on a held recordset is in the EXPR-BIND note.

### R-SQL-2 ACT-UPD-01, ACT-UPD-03, ACT-UPD-04, ACT-INS-02, ACT-INS-03: assignment views, joined targets, counts, INSERT SELECT
**Claim.** UPDATE assignments read the original row: `SET V=W, W=V` swaps in either textual order and `V=W+1, W=V+1, S=V&'-'&W` uses the old values; two assignments to one field fail 3063 with nothing changed; `SET N=(SELECT...)` and `SET ID=ID+1` (collision) are refused. A join UPDATE counts joined occurrences (P1 matched by two children counts 3), `SET P.Name=C.Tag` leaves the last child's value, DISTINCTROW counts 2, a DISTINCT saved query as target fails 3073, LEFT JOIN with the outer side as target fails 3058, a two-target SET changes both tables, dbConsistent/dbInconsistent both succeed. RecordsAffected counts no-op assignments (`SET V=V` gives 4), is not counted for cascades, is set on the QueryDef that executed and `Database.RecordsAffected` stays 0 until `Database.Execute` runs. INSERT SELECT from the target table is finite (4 rows gives 8 rows; appended rows do not re-enter), a SELECT without a column list maps by name, a short select fails 3314, one extra value 3346; omitted column gets its default (5) and explicit Null stays Null; insertion through an updatable saved query or a join succeeds.
**Records.** `evidence/r-sql-2-3/act-upd-assign/r-sql-2-3.act-upd-assign.ac3d38f712.a1.json`, `evidence/r-fix-expr-sql/update-assignment-reseeded/r-fix-expr-sql.update-assignment-reseeded.92fbe9f025.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-2-3/act-upd-join/r-sql-2-3.act-upd-join.9e423c7a63.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-2-3/act-records-affected/r-sql-2-3.act-records-affected.53ac1b81d4.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-2-3/act-cascade/r-sql-2-3.act-cascade.9c205eff72.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-2-3/act-qdef-routes/r-sql-2-3.act-qdef-routes.297136c51e.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-2-3/act-ins-self/r-sql-2-3.act-ins-self.29656dc32f.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-2-3/act-ins-mapping/r-sql-2-3.act-ins-mapping.9e73b54376.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-2-3/act-ins-defaults/r-sql-2-3.act-ins-defaults.f84f17d4f5.a1.json`, `evidence/r-def-expr-sql/sql-insert-values-forms/r-def-expr-sql.sql-insert-values-forms.7ffaec6fa3.a1.json`.

**Claim (follow-up, joined target layout).** A joined `UPDATE PJ INNER JOIN Cx ON PJ.ID = Cx.PID SET PJ.Name = Cx.Tag` with two children leaves the value of the last child in scan (insertion) order: children inserted 10 then 11 leave "c11", inserted 11 then 10 leave "c10", with and without an index on the foreign key; RecordsAffected is 2 each time.
**Records (follow-up).** `evidence/r-q-expr-sql/act-upd-join-layout/r-q-expr-sql.act-upd-join-layout.105930ad58.a1.json`.
**Open.** Joined target with an effectful expression needs a host route; stale `RecordsAffected` after DDL; reentry needs a host route.

### R-SQL-2 SQL-CONTINUE: row failure without dbFailOnError
**Claim.** Without dbFailOnError a failing candidate is skipped without an error and later candidates continue, for unique (3022), validation (3316), relationship (3200/3201), required (3314) and conversion (3464) failures; the rows that changed are counted in RecordsAffected and Errors stays unchanged (a single-row failing INSERT gives RecordsAffected 0 and no error). A locked row (second Workspace pessimistic Edit) gives success with RecordsAffected 0; with dbFailOnError 3188. A conversion failure stores Null for that row and counts it not. These hold on MDB for the unique and lock cases.
**Conditions.** DAO Database.Execute and QueryDef.Execute, ACCDB and MDB.
**Records.** `evidence/r-sql-2-3/act-fail-unique/r-sql-2-3.act-fail-unique.8d251e639e.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-2-3/mdb-fail-unique/r-sql-2-3.mdb-fail-unique.29f2e5ac58.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-2-3/act-fail-validation/r-sql-2-3.act-fail-validation.6ad741c4ef.a1.json`, `evidence/r-fix-expr-sql/fail-relationship-update-isolated/r-fix-expr-sql.fail-relationship-update-isolated.1f9c1bdf98.a1.json`, `evidence/r-fix-expr-sql/fail-insert-required-cross-table/r-fix-expr-sql.fail-insert-required-cross-table.010320f5e9.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-2-3/act-fail-conversion/r-sql-2-3.act-fail-conversion.0dcbbf3a73.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-2-3/act-lock-blocked/r-sql-2-3.act-lock-blocked.c1f4d15854.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-2-3/mdb-lock-blocked/r-sql-2-3.mdb-lock-blocked.833f67bbeb.a1.json`, `evidence/r-expr-1b/route-action-query-expressions/r-expr-1b.route-action-query-expressions.92a71c40cb.a1.json`.

**Claim (follow-up, locks).** A pessimistic Edit by a second Workspace on any one row of a 3-row table (ID 2, ID 1 or ID 3), or on all three, blocks the whole UPDATE: without dbFailOnError the call succeeds with RecordsAffected 0 and no row changes, including the unblocked rows; with dbFailOnError it fails 3188 with no row changed. A successful no-flag Execute neither clears nor adds to Errors (the marker's 3376 entry is still the only one). The lock therefore covers a page (all rows here share one), not a row.
**Records (follow-up).** `evidence/r-q-expr-sql/cont-blocked-positions/r-q-expr-sql.cont-blocked-positions.ba95f08bc3.a1.json`.

**Claim (round 2, other pages).** In Big (65,536 rows, 18 rows per page, ID order = physical order) a pessimistic Edit by a second Workspace on one row blocks only the rows of its page. Without dbFailOnError the UPDATE succeeds, skips the locked page and changes every other row: blocking ID 0 leaves IDs 0..17 unchanged (RecordsAffected 65518), blocking ID 30000 leaves IDs 29988..30005 unchanged (65518), blocking ID 65535 leaves IDs 65520..65535 unchanged (65520); Errors is unchanged. With dbFailOnError the statement stops at the first locked page with 3188 and RecordsAffected 0, and the rows before it stay changed: blocking ID 0 changes no row, blocking ID 30000 changes IDs 0..29987 (Sum(V) 29988), blocking ID 65535 changes IDs 0..65519.
**Records (round 2).** `evidence/r-q2-expr-sql/cont-multipage-noflag/r-q2-expr-sql.cont-multipage-noflag.b76c53bc7b.a1.json`, `evidence/r-q2-expr-sql/cont-multipage-flag/r-q2-expr-sql.cont-multipage-flag.ae9313e97a.a1.json`.
**Open.** Several locked pages in one statement; first/last/all blocked on single-page tables is the earlier note.

### R-PAR-1 PAR-FOLD: Currency, Decimal and Single accumulation
**Claim.** Sum and Avg over Currency overflow at OpenRecordset (error 6) whenever the exact running total leaves the Currency range, even if the final sum would be in range (g1, g2, g4..g6, g11, g12), and succeed at the boundary values (g3 922337203685477.5807, g8 0.9999); Var/StDev over Currency overflow in 10 of 12 groups. Avg(Currency) = the Double of the exact total divided by the count in Double, rounded exactly half-to-even into Currency (`fold-currency-avg-minmax` g3 `307445734561825.875`, g9 `0.0001`). `Sum(CDbl(c))` of the same group is Double 922337203685477.6. A Decimal column sums as Decimal at full precision: 28 nines + 1 - 28 nines is 1; 28 nines + 1 and a group of 28 nines overflows with 3761; Sum(x*1) stays Decimal; Avg is Decimal with the scale needed (0.3333333333333333333333333333); Var/StDev are Double or overflow (6). Single sums are Double (g3 6.805646932770577E+38, no overflow).
**Records.** `evidence/r-par-1/fold-currency-sum-groups/r-par-1.fold-currency-sum-groups.ac7abebfe6.a1.json`, `evidence/r-par-1/fold-currency-avg-minmax/r-par-1.fold-currency-avg-minmax.4b4330c5d6.a1.json`, `evidence/r-par-1/fold-currency-var-stdev/r-par-1.fold-currency-var-stdev.decaedc844.a1.json`, `evidence/r-par-1/fold-currency-argument-forms/r-par-1.fold-currency-argument-forms.efd4183936.a1.json`, `evidence/r-par-1/fold-decimal-sum-groups/r-par-1.fold-decimal-sum-groups.5dc68e238f.a1.json`, `evidence/r-fix-expr-sql/fold-decimal-28-digits/r-fix-expr-sql.fold-decimal-28-digits.430d58abfd.a1.json`, `evidence/r-fix-expr-sql/fold-decimal-avg-var-forms/r-fix-expr-sql.fold-decimal-avg-var-forms.04deca2ebd.a1.json`, `evidence/r-par-1/fold-single-groups/r-par-1.fold-single-groups.12570a2f84.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-13b/agg-sum-boundary/r-sql-13b.agg-sum-boundary.d88bac0645.a1.json`, `evidence/r-fix-expr-sql/agg-sum-boundary-per-group/r-fix-expr-sql.agg-sum-boundary-per-group.208b46ede7.a1.json`.

**Open.** Whether Currency accumulates as exact 64-bit integers or in a wider intermediate is not distinguished by these records. The feed order of an aggregate over a derived table with ORDER BY is neither the ORDER BY nor its reverse (`agg-order`, `fold-double-large-reordered`, `agg-first-last` c26). The Double order is in the Double fold order note.

### R-PAR-1 PAR-ORDER: scan order, grouping order and ties
**Claim.** An unordered table scan returns insertion order, not primary-key order (OT5/OT2/OT3/OT4 identical, filtered scans identical); a dynaset of the table without a WHERE returns primary-key order while snapshot and forward-only return insertion order (the dynaset's whole rule is the R-SQL-1 note on the row order of an unordered dynaset); GROUP BY and DISTINCT output is ascending by key; UNION returns ascending distinct values and UNION ALL keeps arm order; ORDER BY ties come out in a deterministic order that is not insertion order, identical across tables with different indexes, and it changes after DELETE/INSERT/UPDATE; TOP n and TOP n PERCENT keep the whole tie group (TOP 5 over a join returned 24 rows). The order of join duplicate matches depends on the table pairing and indexes.
**Records.** `evidence/r-par-1/order-scan-storage-order/r-par-1.order-scan-storage-order.7c47b25e2c.a1.json`, `evidence/r-par-1/order-scan-filter-routes/r-par-1.order-scan-filter-routes.83879b1a70.a1.json`, `evidence/r-par-1/order-recordset-types/r-par-1.order-recordset-types.e232632274.a1.json`, `evidence/r-par-1/order-groupby-output-order/r-par-1.order-groupby-output-order.b48c25d6be.a1.json`, `evidence/r-par-1/order-groupby-output-order-mdb/r-par-1.order-groupby-output-order-mdb.ef422953ef.a1.json`, `evidence/r-fix-expr-sql/order-distinct-full-read/r-fix-expr-sql.order-distinct-full-read.0d563de4a9.a1.json`, `evidence/r-par-1/order-union-without-order-by/r-par-1.order-union-without-order-by.8c1f7f4de4.a1.json`, `evidence/r-par-1/order-ties-order-by-k/r-par-1.order-ties-order-by-k.29d06704d8.a1.json`, `evidence/r-par-1/order-after-delete-insert-update/r-par-1.order-after-delete-insert-update.6d90c07ccb.a1.json`, `evidence/r-par-1/order-join-duplicate-matches/r-par-1.order-join-duplicate-matches.eb3683de21.a1.json`.
**Built (`w20-sql-order-like-dist`, `03d4872b`/`8399d88f`, 2026-11-28).** Implemented `ORDER BY` numeric positions/constants, `Expr1000+colIndex`, circular alias `3103`, `ORDER BY` alias-shadowing, `ASC DESC` `3138`, mixed Text/Number `IIf` ordering, grouped `SELECT DISTINCT`, aggregate `DISTINCT`/`ALL` & `Extra )` `3075` excerpts, unsupported `JOIN` keywords `3131`/dup `ON` `3075`, derived `Filter` dynaset order, `LIKE` `[^a-c]`/Yes/No/ordered WHERE open `93`/`94`, and ungrouped `ORDER BY Left(m, 0..255)`/`Len(m)` (`CalcDb.Sql.Tests` `1,747/1,747`, `147,498` values matched).
**Open.** The tie order itself has no explanation (a sort algorithm artefact); no claim about it beyond "deterministic, not insertion order".

### R-PAR-1 PAR-FOLD, R-SQL-1 SQL-AGG-ORDER, SQL-AGG-FIRST: Double Sum follows the feed order, which an index maintained during insert scrambles
**Claim.** Sum and Avg of Double are a strictly sequential left-to-right accumulation in the order the rows reach the aggregate. In all eleven tables the Sum for group 3 (2^53, 4096 ones, -2^53 by id 8195, 8196..12291, 12292) equals the sum recomputed offline in IEEE doubles from the retained scan order: FL, FLD (index on d only), FLN (primary key only), FLX (no key), FLC (make-table copy) and the three post-load tables (index on g, on d, on both created after the rows exist) read group 3 in id order (8195..12292) and sum 0; FLR (copy inserted ORDER BY id DESC) reads 12292 down to 8195 and sums 4096; FLI (indexes on g and d built before INSERT SELECT) reads group 3 as 9701..10282, 8195..9700, 10283..12292 and sums 584; FLG (index on g only, built before the insert) reads 8343..9506, 8195..8342, 9507..12292 and sums 1164. WHERE g = 3 and GROUP BY g give the same Sum; First and Last of id equal the first and last ids of the retained order (FLI 9701 and 12292); the whole-table scan starts 1..40 in every table except FLR (12292 downward). So an index on g that was maintained during the insert delivers an equal-key run in a rotated order of id runs, an index on g built after the load, an index on d only, no index, a make-table copy and a reversed insert give plain scan order, and the Sum is whatever the sequential sum of that order is. The earlier 1360 for FLI did not repeat: the rebuilt FLI (same recipe) gives 584 with a different rotation, so the rotation point depends on the build, not the recipe.
**Conditions.** DAO snapshot, ACCDB, one process; offline sums recomputed in awk (IEEE doubles) from the arrays retained by GetRows.
**Records.** `evidence/r-q-expr-sql/fold-scan-FL/r-q-expr-sql.fold-scan-FL.afd432826c.a1.json`, `evidence/r-q-expr-sql/fold-scan-FLI/r-q-expr-sql.fold-scan-FLI.b10577fe82.a1.json`, `evidence/r-q-expr-sql/fold-scan-FLG/r-q-expr-sql.fold-scan-FLG.6d952e77cf.a1.json`, `evidence/r-q-expr-sql/fold-scan-FLD/r-q-expr-sql.fold-scan-FLD.6838b79f00.a1.json`, `evidence/r-q-expr-sql/fold-scan-FLN/r-q-expr-sql.fold-scan-FLN.f8d7f77466.a1.json`, `evidence/r-q-expr-sql/fold-scan-FLX/r-q-expr-sql.fold-scan-FLX.5a76eac0b6.a1.json`, `evidence/r-q-expr-sql/fold-scan-FLR/r-q-expr-sql.fold-scan-FLR.be5581ea58.a1.json`, `evidence/r-q-expr-sql/fold-scan-FLC/r-q-expr-sql.fold-scan-FLC.6afd4dd7a1.a1.json`, `evidence/r-q-expr-sql/fold-post-g/r-q-expr-sql.fold-post-g.3a471b968a.a1.json`, `evidence/r-q-expr-sql/fold-post-d/r-q-expr-sql.fold-post-d.ea93514e2a.a1.json`, `evidence/r-q-expr-sql/fold-post-both/r-q-expr-sql.fold-post-both.79ca5ef774.a1.json`; earlier: `evidence/r-par-1/fold-double-large-groups/r-par-1.fold-double-large-groups.b518c9662a.a1.json`, `evidence/r-fix-expr-sql/fold-double-routes-materialized/r-fix-expr-sql.fold-double-routes-materialized.765f09d5d6.a1.json`.

**Claim (round 2, many groups).** The same holds for 4096 groups of [2^53, 1, -2^53]: on FG (primary key only), FGI (index on g built before the insert) and FG with an index on g built after the load, one `GROUP BY g` with `Sum(d)`, `First(id)` and `Last(id)` gives, for every one of the 4096 groups, First = the 2^53 row (id g), Last = the -2^53 row (id 8192 + g) and Sum 0, which is the sequential sum of the id order; single-group reads (g = 1, 151, 1000, 4096) return the ids g, 4096 + g, 8192 + g in that scan order, and the whole-table scan starts at id 1..40. So with many groups the feed order is the scan order in all three layouts; the earlier FG result of 1164 groups summing 1 (groups 151 to 1314) did not reproduce on this rebuild (the rebuild's groups 151, 1000 and 4096 are in id order with Sum 0).
**Records (round 2).** `evidence/r-q2-expr-sql/fold-many-FG/r-q2-expr-sql.fold-many-FG.a59a345e6a.a1.json`, `evidence/r-q2-expr-sql/fold-many-FGI/r-q2-expr-sql.fold-many-FGI.2c63a19193.a1.json`, `evidence/r-q2-expr-sql/fold-many-FGP/r-q2-expr-sql.fold-many-FGP.9e51d6cfa3.a1.json`.
**Open.** The earlier 1164-group result and the earlier 1360 (FLI) are build-dependent layouts (the rebuilt FLI gave 584, the rebuilt FG gave none): why an index on g maintained during insert, or an earlier build of FG, delivers a rotated order is not explained, and no probe made a scrambled many-group layout on demand.

### R-SQL-1 SQL-AGG-NUMERIC: Var, VarP, StDev and StDevP come from single-pass Double sums
**Claim.** ACE computes the variance family from Σx and Σx² accumulated in Double, not from deviations. For 1e9 + (4, 7, 13, 16) (exact sample variance 30) ACE returns Var 170.66666666666666 and VarP 128; for 1e15 + (1, 2, 3) (exact 1) 187649984473770.66 and 125099989649180.44; for 1e8 + (0.1, 0.2, 0.3) (exact 0.01) 2.6666666666666665 and 1.7777777777777777; for 1e16 + (0, 2, 4) (exact 4) 24019198012642644 and 16012798675095096. These magnitudes are reproduced exactly offline in IEEE doubles by (n·Σx² − (Σx)²) / (n·(n−1)) for Var and / n² for VarP; ACE returns them positive although that expression is negative for these inputs (the sign handling is not determined). StDev and StDevP are their square roots (13.063945294843617 and 11.313708498984761 for the first group). 1e150 · (1, 2, 3) gives Var 1.0000000000000006E+300 (exact 1e300) and 1e154 · (1, 2, 3) fails with Overflow (6) at OpenRecordset because the squares leave the Double range although the variance is representable.
**Conditions.** DAO snapshot, ACCDB, Double column, GROUP BY and WHERE g = k.
**Records.** `evidence/r-q-expr-sql/agg-var-recurrence/r-q-expr-sql.agg-var-recurrence.d29f892304.a1.json`, `evidence/r-fix-expr-sql/agg-numeric-per-group/r-fix-expr-sql.agg-numeric-per-group.4cfc915b42.a1.json`.
**Built (`w18-sql-stat-aggs`, `1953034d`/`89c02fb0`, 2026-11-27).** Implemented `Var`/`VarP`/`StDev`/`StDevP` across Double/Single/Currency/Decimal (scale `<=4`/20)/Byte/Integer/Long/DateTime/Yes/No/Text/Double derived inputs (with `FeedOrderOpen` guards), nested-aggregate `3095`, and `Sum` `BooleanLiteral`/`DecimalLiteral`/`Text` literal/scalar param arg kinds (`CalcDb.Sql.Tests` `1,737/1,737`, `636/636` citations).
**Open.** Sign handling; accumulation order for sums that cancel (the order dependence is in the Double fold order note); Decimal variance scales `5..19`/`21..28` (`NotModelled.DecimalVarianceScale`); `Sum(StoredDecimal expr)`; non-zero Currency variance `Finish` overflow boundary; unprobed `Var` bare types (`Memo`/`Ole`/`Binary`/`Guid`/`BigInt`/`ExtendedDateTime`) and `Var(expr)`.

### R-SQL-1 SQL-SUB-03, SQL-SUB-04: correlation names and EXISTS shapes
**Claim.** A correlated count through an alias (`e2.Id <= Ev.Id`) gives 1, 2, 3; when the inner table has the outer table's name (`FROM Ev WHERE Id <= Ev.Id`) the inner name shadows the outer and every row gives 3; three nested correlated EXISTS levels return all rows; EXISTS over `SELECT DISTINCT` and over a GROUP BY subquery returns all rows; EXISTS ignores a failing projection (`1/x.Denominator` with Denominator 0 at Id 2 returns Ids 1, 2, 3); `EXISTS (SELECT TOP 1 x.Id FROM Ev AS x WHERE x.Id = Ev.Id)` returned only Id 1, so TOP in a correlated subquery does not behave per outer row. Earlier: an alias `L2` with `L.id` inside failed 3061, and a declared parameter `lid` won over column `R.lid`.
**Conditions.** DAO snapshot, ACCDB, ANSI-89 syntax.
**Records.** `evidence/r-q-expr-sql/sub-corr-shapes/r-q-expr-sql.sub-corr-shapes.e9e8b6b82d.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-1/sub-correlated/r-sql-1.sub-correlated.df79dc512e.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-1/sub-exists-demand/r-sql-1.sub-exists-demand.2e3c35d3b4.a1.json`.
**Open.** Why TOP 1 returns once (a TOP applied before the correlation?); nested parameter-name reuse; callbacks in the child need a host route (SQL-SUB-05, T).

### R-SQL-1 SQL-SUB-03/04, SQL-AGG-NUMERIC (`r-q53-conv-abspos-subq-loc` Part C): Subqueries (`IN`/`EXISTS`/scalar/correlated/`ANY`/`ALL`), `GROUP BY` expressions, and `DECIMAL(18,4)` aggregates
- **Claim**: Across ACCDB & Jet 4 MDB (DAO & ADO), `IN`/`NOT IN` (3VL `Null` & empty), correlated `EXISTS`/`NOT EXISTS`, scalar subqueries in `WHERE`/`SELECT` (0-row -> `Null`), correlated `WHERE`, and `ANY`/`ALL` match; multi-col `IN` fails `3306` and multi-row scalar fails `3354` (ADO `0x80004005`, native `-201133054`). `GROUP BY` expressions (`Left(S,1)` `Type 10/Size 0`, `Year(D)` `Type 3/Size 2`, `N \ 10` `Type 4/Size 4`), multi-expr, and `HAVING` succeed while ungrouped expressions fail `3122`. `DECIMAL(18,4)` `SUM`/`AVG`/`MIN`/`MAX`/`FIRST`/`LAST` return DAO `Type=20, Size=16` (`Properties("Precision"/"Scale").Value` fails `3021` on query field), ADO `adNumeric(131), DefinedSize=19` (`SUM` `28,4`; `AVG` `28,10`; `MIN`/`MAX` `18,4`), and exact `VT_DECIMAL` values (`SUM` widens past 18 digits to `299999999999999.9997`; `AVG` uses full 28-digit Decimal division `1.3333333333333333333333333333`; 96-bit overflow fails `6` DAO / `0x80040E57` ADO).
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q53-conv-abspos-subq-loc/{subq-in-exists-dao,subq-scalar-corr-dao,groupby-expr-decagg-dao,subq-grp-decagg-ado}-{accdb,mdb}/`.

### R-SQL-2 SQL-ACT-FAIL: failure disposition with dbFailOnError by statement kind
**Claim.** With dbFailOnError a failure in a later candidate leaves a different state by statement kind. UPDATE stops at the failing candidate and keeps the earlier changes: unique (3022) changed IDs 1 and 2 (1 to 11, 2 to 12) and left ID 3 and 4; conversion (3464, `CLng(S)` failing at ID 3) set V for IDs 1 and 2 and left 3 and 4 Null; validation (3316) and relationship (3201) behave the same in the earlier records. INSERT ... SELECT with a Null for a required field at the third source row (3314) appends no row; DELETE whose third candidate has a child (3200) deletes none (IDs 1 and 2, which had no child, remain). RecordsAffected reads 0 after each failure although UPDATE rows changed. Without the flag each skips the failing candidate and continues (SQL-CONTINUE note).
**Conditions.** DAO Database.Execute outside an explicit transaction, ACCDB, four candidates, the failing one third.
**Records.** `evidence/r-q-expr-sql/fail-late-candidate/r-q-expr-sql.fail-late-candidate.7b269cfdc0.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-2-3/act-fail-unique/r-sql-2-3.act-fail-unique.8d251e639e.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-2-3/act-fail-validation/r-sql-2-3.act-fail-validation.6ad741c4ef.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-2-3/act-fail-conversion/r-sql-2-3.act-fail-conversion.0dcbbf3a73.a1.json`, `evidence/r-fix-expr-sql/fail-relationship-update-isolated/r-fix-expr-sql.fail-relationship-update-isolated.1f9c1bdf98.a1.json`, `evidence/r-fix-expr-sql/fail-insert-required-cross-table/r-fix-expr-sql.fail-insert-required-cross-table.010320f5e9.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-2-3/act-trans-frames/r-sql-2-3.act-trans-frames.636a969421.a1.json`.

**Claim (round 2, ADO lane).** ADO has no dbFailOnError and behaves like the flagged DAO call: the four failing statements each raise an error (UPDATE with a unique collision 0x80004005 "duplicate values", `CLng('x')` 0x80040E07 "Data type mismatch", INSERT ... SELECT with Null for a required field "You must enter a value in the 'Fi.R' field", DELETE with related records "includes related records"), the by-reference RecordsAffected is left at its preset -1, ConnectionEvents raise WillExecute then ExecuteComplete with RecordsAffected 0 and adStatus 2, and the rows read by DAO afterwards are those of the flagged DAO run: the UPDATE keeps IDs 1 and 2 changed (11, 12; conversion: V 1, 2 with 3 and 4 Null), INSERT ... SELECT appends nothing and DELETE deletes nothing. A successful ADO UPDATE writes the count into a by-reference RecordsAffected (2, and 0 for no matching row).
**Records (round 2).** `evidence/r-q2-expr-sql/act-ado-failure-lane/r-q2-expr-sql.act-ado-failure-lane.085b64019c.a1.json`, `evidence/r-ops/byref-ado-execute-records-affected/r-ops.byref-ado-execute-records-affected.fdfa8ae1e0.a1.json`.
**Open.** Whether the INSERT and DELETE reversal is an implicit transaction or a pre-check is not determined; injected I/O, TEMP and allocation failures need other tooling (T). A peer's schema change during a statement is in the ACT-SEL-03/04 note.

### R-SQL-2 ACT-SEL-01, ACT-SEL-02: candidate stability for key-moving updates
**Claim.** An UPDATE that moves the key it selects on changes each qualifying row once, with or without an index. Rows V = 10, 20, 20, 20, 30, 30, 5: `V = V + 100 WHERE V BETWEEN 10 AND 30` affects 6 rows (110, 120 x3, 130 x2; the 5 stays), `V = V - 100 WHERE V >= 100` 6 rows back, `V = V + 5 WHERE V >= 10` 6 rows; the indexed and the unindexed table agree. On 4096 rows (k = n) `k = k + 100000 WHERE k >= 0` affects 4096 rows (min 100001, max 104096) and `k = k - 1 WHERE k > 100` affects 4096 (min 100000, max 104095), with and without an index on k: no row is revisited or skipped.
**Conditions.** DAO Database.Execute with dbFailOnError, ACCDB.
**Records.** `evidence/r-q-expr-sql/act-sel-repeated-keys/r-q-expr-sql.act-sel-repeated-keys.9ccfed59e0.a1.json`, `evidence/r-q-expr-sql/act-sel-leaf-split/r-q-expr-sql.act-sel-leaf-split.148d46c5a0.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-2-3/act-sel-range/r-sql-2-3.act-sel-range.dcd16645d1.a1.json`.
**Open.** That the 4096-entry index split its leaves was not verified (file bytes, W-04); variable rows growing into overflow bodies and relocated roots; callbacks that change unseen rows (T).

### R-SQL-2 ACT-INS-01: AutoNumber consumption by failed inserts
**Claim.** A failed insert consumes an AutoNumber: with An(ID Counter, Req NOT NULL, U unique) and dbFailOnError, ('a',1) gets ID 1, ('b',1) (3022) consumes 2, ('c',2) gets 3, (Null,3) (3314) consumes 4, ('d',3) gets 5. An explicit ID that collides sets the seed to that ID + 1 even though the insert fails: INSERT with ID 1 (3022) is followed by a plain ('f',10) that gets ID 2 (a gap is reused) and a plain ('g',11) that fails 3022 because the seed (3) is taken.
**Conditions.** DAO Database.Execute with dbFailOnError, ACCDB, one sequence.
**Records.** `evidence/r-q-expr-sql/act-ins-autonumber/r-q-expr-sql.act-ins-autonumber.0ac261a7d4.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-2-3/act-ins-defaults/r-sql-2-3.act-ins-defaults.f84f17d4f5.a1.json`.
**Open.** The explicit-ID variant was asked once, in sequence; physical re-preparation must not consume a second value (T).

### R-SQL-2 SQL-OWNERACCESS: WITH OWNERACCESS OPTION under default Admin
**Claim.** The clause is accepted and changes nothing observable under the default Admin account with a blank password and no workgroup file, on ACCDB and on a Jet 4 MDB: a direct OpenRecordset, a temporary QueryDef, a saved QueryDef and an INSERT ... SELECT ... WITH OWNERACCESS OPTION run as without it (rows 1,10 and 2,20; RecordsAffected 2). The QueryDef SQL keeps the clause (re-rendered with line breaks and a trailing `;`), Type is 0 and Updatable is -1 with and without it.
**Records.** `evidence/r-q-expr-sql/act-owneraccess-routes/r-q-expr-sql.act-owneraccess-routes.1e75ddae31.a1.json`, `evidence/r-sql-2-3/act-owneraccess/r-sql-2-3.act-owneraccess.8885d6a8da.a1.json`.
- **Open.** Independent reopen and native bytes (R-CAT-1); named logon, workgroup file and permission checks stay T (out of scope).

### R-SQL-2 SQL-ACT-MAKE (`r-q52-ddl-make-domain` Part C): `SELECT ... INTO` MakeTable kept bytes across DAO & ADO
- **Claim.** Across ACCDB & Jet 4 MDB on both DAO `Database.Execute` and ADO `Connection.Execute` (writing `ByRef RecordsAffected`): populated (`COUNTER->LONG`, `MEMO` LVAL, 0 indexes copied), empty source, `WHERE 1=0`, expressions/`WHERE`/`ORDER BY`, and `LEFT JOIN`/`GROUP BY` aggregates step `0xE02` `+3` per statement + Close. Existing target via ADO (`3010` / `0x80040E14`) is 0-byte invariant (`09->09`); DAO errors `3010` (exact & case-insensitive), `3078`, `3191` (`0x800A0C77` duplicate column), `3125`, and `3027` (read-only). Inside `BeginTrans`, `Rollback` removes the created table (`3078`) while retaining the `MSysObjects`/`MSysACEs` AutoNumber bump (`+1`). Both routes are byte-identical across repeats outside ACE clock fields.
- **Records.** `evidence/r-q52-ddl-make-domain/maketable-{basic-dao,expr-agg-dao,errors-dao,ado}-{accdb,mdb}/` (all pages kept).
